# GLM review 2026-11-03 (glm-5.3): 411 findings, 206 high severity, 13 shard(s)

Revision 0057f288c76a. Shards: agentbox, agentbox#2, agentbox#3, dreamlab-ai-website, estate, nostr-rust-forum, ontomark, solid-pod-rs, visionclaw, visionclaw#2, visionclaw#3, visionclaw#4, visionflow. Unverified hypotheses: reproduce each with a failing test or check (build-with-quality) before acting.

## agentbox / critical

### F-01 — Unauthenticated Chrome DevTools Protocol published to the LAN by the browser sidecar
- Topics: AB-06.4, AB-06.7, AB-06.2, AB-06.12
- Evidence: browsercontainer publishes `0.0.0.0:9222` → container `9223` and the material states "raw CDP is also reachable — published 9222 on the host mapping to container 9223 (docker-compose.browsercontainer.yml:53, SANCTIONED at check-ports-loopback.mjs:99)". The Chrome profile holding Podkey's encrypted vault and the browser's authenticated sessions is a named volume intended to persist (docker-compose.browsercontainer.yml:64, :75-76, "never down -v").
- Failure: any host on the LAN connects to CDP (the protocol has no authentication — inferred from its nature, not stated in the material) and fully drives the browser: reads logged-in sessions, exfiltrates the Podkey vault, and acts as the browser identity. The ADR-2013 gate has blessed the port, so no CI check will ever flag it.
- Confidence: high (exposure and profile persistence are stated; the "no auth" property of CDP is inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (it is listed as SANCTIONED, not as a risk)

### F-02 — The entrypoint re-widens the host Docker socket to world read-write on every boot while isolation is off
- Topics: AB-06.13, AB-02.24, AB-05.16
- Evidence: the host socket is bound rw (docker-compose.override.yml:116); with the shipped `[security].role_isolation = false` (agentbox.toml:2543, setup default agentbox.default.toml:1770) the entrypoint "still runs `chmod o+rw` on every boot" (entrypoint-unified.sh:611-613); the material states the socket is the HOST inode and "only a host-side chmod 0660 narrows it" (docker-compose.override.yml:114-116, risk R2); the sudo drop is baked at build time, so isolation cannot even be trialled without a rebuild (flake.nix:4054-4061, system-manifest.js:97-99).
- Failure: any code executing as devuser — i.e. every agent session — talks to the host Docker daemon, mounts the host filesystem, and becomes host root. Even if an operator narrows the socket, the next container boot re-widens it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open R2 in AB-06.13; invariant notes in AB-02.24)

### F-03 — Persistent arbitrary MCP-server injection through the devuser-writable `.mcp.json` that reconcile deliberately never touches
- Topics: AB-09.3, AB-09.7
- Evidence: the projector's target is `WORKSPACE/.mcp.json`; "a target entry ABSENT from the ledger is treated as bespoke and is never touched, so adopting this revision cannot delete a hand-written server" (project-mcp-servers.mjs:61-64); only ledger-owned names are removed. The harness spawns each entry's `command` with expanded env at session start (AB-09.7). WORKSPACE is chowned to uid 1000 (entrypoint-unified.sh:585-593), so every agent process can write the file.
- Failure: one compromised or runaway agent appends a stdio server entry running an arbitrary command; the ownership ledger preserves it forever as "bespoke"; every later session — including the operator's tab0 — spawns it. Root `settings.json` hooks are boot-rewritten, but this file is reconciled, not rewritten, so the injection is the one persistence point that survives reboots.
- Confidence: medium (the ownership rule and writable target are stated; the injection chain is inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the never-touch rule is presented as an invariant protecting hand-written entries)

### F-04 — Any in-container process can forge any `did:nostr` identity and read every key
- Topics: AB-10.14, AB-11.14, AB-11.16, AB-02.12
- Evidence: `proxyVerifiedPubkey` trusts `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` from any loopback TCP peer (agent-event-auth.js:98-105, :117); the authors state "a process INSIDE the container can still connect over loopback and forge the stamp, the same-UID boundary (TODO-unified X-1)" (agent-event-auth.js:34-39); `resolveAgentDid` shares the rule (action-plane.js:222-233). With role_isolation off, "every agentbox process runs as devuser, so co-resident code can read the agent key and the bridge key alike" (agent-identity.js 0600 files, AB-11.16 divergence; AB-02.12 "not an OS access boundary").
- Failure: any co-resident process emits agent-events and action-plane dispatches as the operator's DID, poisoning governance attribution and the audit chain, and exfiltrates the sovereign signing keys.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, TODO-unified X-1; DIVERGENCE in AB-11.16 and AB-02.12)

### F-05 — The host's `~/.claude` is bound read-write into the container, so container compromise rewrites host credentials
- Topics: AB-06.11, AB-07.11, AB-05.15
- Evidence: host `~/.claude` is bound rw at `/var/lib/agentbox/host-claude` "for that purpose alone" (docker-compose.override.yml:83-85, :88); the authors mark it Open: "the credential bind stays writable both ways, so a compromised in-container tool could still modify the host's .claude" (AB-06.11; BASELINE-container.md:235, Q43); the merge daemon pushes whichever side has the later `expiresAt` (cred_sync.rs:29-62).
- Failure: an in-container attacker crafts a credentials document with a later `expiresAt`; cred-sync pushes it to the host; the host's Claude Code sessions are hijacked or their tokens stolen.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, AB-06.11; Q43 in AB-07.11)

### F-06 — Mandate revocation is never enforced at the pod: a revoked delegate keeps its WAC grant
- Topics: AB-11.7, AB-11.8, AB-11.9
- Evidence: `revokeMandate` only re-signs the record and rewrites the local registry (routes/mandate.js:238-281); "nothing in agentbox removes or rewrites the `.acl`, so a revoked mandate's WAC grant stays in force at the pod until someone replaces it" (AB-11.7 Tension); the revoking event is not even published to a relay (mandate.js:264-269).
- Failure: the operator revokes a compromised agent's mandate through the documented API, sees `revoked: true`, and the agent continues to read and write the pod's corpus under the old ACL indefinitely — revocation is a registry annotation, not an enforcement act.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, AB-11.7)

### F-07 — The backup tool covers exactly one volume: identity keys, pod data and audit state have no backup path
- Topics: AB-07.8, AB-06.11, AB-06.8, AB-03.12
- Evidence: `cmd_backup` tars `agentbox-ruvector-data` ("always included", agentbox.sh:306-309) plus a `pg_dump`; its manifest carries only `ruvector_data` and `ruvector_pg_dump` flags (agentbox.sh:400-401). No other `_volume_tar` call appears. Yet the estate declares `agentbox-solid-data` (docker-compose.yml:175-182), the external `agentbox-claude-home` volume (docker-compose.override.yml:185-187), `codex-packages` (:200-203) and `browsercontainer-profile` — and ADR-2118 made the claude-home volume the container-owned home whose loss destroys the sovereign identity keys (AB-06.11, AB-11.2).
- Failure: disk loss or a bad activation wipes every did:nostr identity the pods' ACLs and mandates reference, all solid-pod contents, and the audit/events state; the DIDs can never sign again and the ruvector restore is the only thing that comes back.
- Confidence: high that only ruvector-data is included (the command flow is explicit); medium (inferred) that this omission is a defect rather than handled by some unstated external backup
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — The privacy filter classifies mutations by method name, so `dispatch`, `claim`, `close`, `del` and `spawnAgent` bypass redaction into an append-only chain
- Topics: AB-04.5, AB-03.12, AB-04.4
- Evidence: the mutation set and prefix rule do not classify those methods (privacy-filter.js:283-313); "the events slot's only write, `dispatch`, therefore reaches the hash-chained JSONL log without redaction, whatever the slot policy says. So do beads `claim` and `close`" (AB-04.5 Tension). The chain's verifier treats any later edit as tamper (audit-chain.js:123-126).
- Failure: identifier- or PII-bearing payloads are written unredacted into a log whose integrity guarantee makes scrubbing impossible without breaking chain verification — the exposure is permanent by construction (permanence inferred from the stated chain semantics).
- Confidence: high (the gap is marked; the irreversibility is inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, AB-04.5)

### F-09 — The execution journal is appendable by any authenticated principal, not only the operator
- Topics: AB-03.1, AB-03.14
- Evidence: `POST /v1/exec/record` is called "operator-gated" (server.js:433) but attaches no `requireOperator` preHandler, unlike mandate routes (mandate.js:311); in hybrid/nip98 modes any NIP-98-verified signer passes `authMiddleware` (auth.js:194-199); the route appends through the journal singleton onto the events-slot hash chain (exec-record.js:128, AB-03.14).
- Failure: any nostr key holder injects fabricated execution records and provenance envelopes into the audit chain, corrupting D2 traceability; the entries cannot be removed without breaking the chain.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, AB-03.1)

### F-10 — The primary `/mgmt` ingress flow may 401 on NIP-98 re-verification, and two panels in the material disagree about it
- Topics: AB-03.15, AB-10.7, AB-10.4
- Evidence: the proxy strips `/mgmt/` and forwards the caller's NIP-98 header verbatim (proxy.mjs:304-310, :886-892); the API rebuilds the URL from the stripped path (auth.js:73); `verifyNip98` accepts only an exact or suffix match of the signed `u` tag (nostr-bridge.js:545-546), and "a tag that keeps `/mgmt/` is not a suffix match of the stripped URL, so this path may answer 401. Only a runtime request through `:9096/mgmt/` can settle it" (AB-03.15 Open). Yet AB-10.7's end-to-end diagram draws the same nip98-passthrough reaching the handler with no failure branch.
- Failure: if the suffix reading holds, every direct-NIP-98 operator call through the one LAN door — mandate creation, system view — fails 401, pushing operators to the break-glass bearer; the documented write path (AB-03.15's own panel) is dead in production.
- Confidence: medium (the authors themselves could not settle it from source)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, AB-03.15) — but the contradiction with AB-10.7's diagram is unmarked

### F-11 — bootstrap-seal has a 120 s budget but a 300 s projection wait inside it: slow boots wedge readiness permanently
- Topics: AB-02.8, AB-02.22, AB-01.3, AB-02.17
- Evidence: the program carries "timeout 120s" (flake.nix:2676) and its status poll runs "up to BOOTSTRAP_SEAL_TIMEOUT=120s" (seal-bootstrap.sh:96-115); `_check_projections` then polls each promised file "up to 300 s" (seal-bootstrap.sh:137) before the sentinel is written (:181). The hub's own bounded wait is 120 s (hub/mod.rs:259). Activation treats /ready failure after 60×2 s as activation failure (runtime-delivery.cjs:267-268).
- Failure: a legitimate slow boot (programs slow to RUNNING, or the hub projection late) overruns the seal's budget; the sentinel is never written; /ready returns 503 forever while every service is actually up; compose reports unhealthy and every subsequent activation times out — a total operational wedge requiring manual intervention.
- Confidence: medium (both numbers are stated; which scope the 120 s timeout governs is not)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — `system-one-proxy` is catalogued boot-class but gated as rebuild-class in the flake, and its absence fails open
- Topics: AB-05.13, AB-01.1, AB-02.6, AB-06.10, AB-05.4
- Evidence: the catalogue entry is `apply_class: boot` (system-manifest.js:274-276, AB-05.13 C13), yet the package set and the supervisor block are both emitted under `lib.optionals`/`lib.optionalString` on `system_one.enabled` (flake.nix:1884, :2134, :3037; AB-02.6 SO1) — exactly the pattern ADR-039's honesty rule reserves for rebuild-class (AB-05.4). The material separately records that consumers "fail open to their built-in paths rather than erroring" when the proxy is absent (AB-06.10 invariant, system-manifest.js:282).
- Failure: an operator enables System One and restarts, as the boot label promises; the catalogue reports `on`, but no proxy program or binary exists in the image; every typed-decision consumer silently bypasses the judge — no routing decisions, no cost attribution — with `/v1/system` showing green the whole time.
- Confidence: high on the classification contradiction; medium on the operator scenario
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — strict-nip98 auto-elevation silently kills the proxy's bearer_env path used by every browser session
- Topics: AB-10.7, AB-10.1, AB-03.15
- Evidence: hybrid "AUTO-ELEVATES to strict-nip98 when AGENTBOX_SOVEREIGN_MESH_ENABLED is true and no mode is set explicitly, and strict-nip98 rejects Bearer unconditionally" (auth.js:172-182, :194-195); the proxy injects `Bearer route.bearer` whenever auth.mode is not nip98 — i.e. for every NIP-07 cookie session and break-glass user (proxy.mjs:893-894); the voice console's Caddyfile routes `/approvals`, `/mgmt`, `/dream` and friends through exactly this proxy (voice/console/Caddyfile:55-106).
- Failure: with sovereign mesh enabled and no explicit auth mode, the entire browser governance cockpit answers 401 for signed-in operators while CLI NIP-98 users work — a config-dependent, hard-to-diagnose outage of the approvals surface.
- Confidence: medium (conditional on an env var whose shipped value the material does not show)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — `agentbox.sh health` exit code is wrong on three paths, in both directions
- Topics: AB-04.16, AB-05.7, AB-06.10
- Evidence: `--json` prints the payload and exits 0 unconditionally (agentbox.sh:1148-1151); without jq it prints raw and "falls off the end with status 0" even when slots are degraded (agentbox.sh:1220-1223); exit 1 is reachable only on the pretty path (agentbox.sh:1217-1218). Separately, the exit condition includes `speech_failed` from unconditional curls to ports 8897/8898 (agentbox.sh:1203-1215), "independent of the [adapters] manifest", while the speech pair is its own compose overlay that a deployment may not run (docker-compose.speech.yml).
- Failure: monitoring that pipes `health --json` never sees degradation (silent incident); conversely, any host without the speech sidecars has health exit 1 permanently, training operators to ignore the alarm. Both failure modes are silent in opposite directions.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes for `--json`/no-jq (Tension, AB-04.16); no for the unconditional speech term

### F-15 — Production ships behaviour whose decision records say it is not implemented or not activated
- Topics: AB-01.1, AB-05.17, AB-08.16, AB-05.13, AB-06.14, AB-05.8
- Evidence: ADR-2151 is `proposed`/`none`/`inactive` (ADR-2151:5-7) while `[voicelink] enabled = true`, `record_events = true` (agentbox.toml:2264, :2271) bake voice-paned (flake.nix:1848-1850, :2129) and register Stop/Notification hooks and a Codex notify line every boot (entrypoint-unified.sh:2156-2198, :904-937). ## agentbox / premortem

# Post-mortem — client-loss incident (data/custody/security class)

Scope note: everything below is drawn only from the mapped material (topics AB-01…AB-11 at verified_commit `c57d9c05`). Where I reason past a cited fact I say "inferred". All line references are the ones the material itself gives; an engineer should re-read each before acting. Diagrams whose internal ordering is not asserted were not treated as assertions.

The three candidates below are ranked by likelihood; each is independently sufficient to have produced an incident of this class.

---

### R-1 — Shipped-default custody posture: the host Docker socket is widened for devuser on every boot and PID 1 still carries every secret
- Topics: AB-02.24, AB-06.13, AB-05.16, AB-11.16, AB-06.3, AB-06.11
- Chain of events:
  1. The box runs with the shipped `[security].role_isolation = false` (`agentbox.toml:2543`, setup default `agentbox.default.toml:1770`).
  2. On every boot the entrypoint runs `chmod o+rw` on the host Docker socket (`entrypoint-unified.sh:611-613`); compose binds that socket read-write into the container (`docker-compose.override.yml:116`). The socket is the *host* inode — earlier boots have already left it `o+rw`, and only a host-side `chmod 0660` narrows it (OPEN R2, `docker-compose.override.yml:114-116`).
  3. With the flag off, the role-env capture and scrub return at their first line, so PID 1 — and therefore every supervised program spawned from it — carries every `.env`/ROLE key (AB-02.24 invariants). `/run/secrets` is chowned back to devuser 0700 (`entrypoint-unified.sh:359-362`).
  4. Agent sessions execute tool calls as devuser. Any one compromised dependency, MCP server or prompt-driven command now runs as devuser.
  5. devuser drives the host Docker daemon. The material states the consequence outright: "The Docker daemon is root on the host, so while devuser can drive it every in-container boundary is void (custody design §0, bypass 1)". Host filesystem, volumes and sibling containers become reachable.
  6. In the same hop the attacker reads the 0600 agent and bridge keys (same-uid only, `agentbox.toml:2543` posture per AB-11.16), the PID-1-inherited secrets, and writes the host's `~/.claude` credentials through the still-writable credential bind (`docker-compose.override.yml:83-85`, AB-06.11 Open). Client data and signing keys leave the estate.
- Evidence: `docker-compose.override.yml:114-116`; `entrypoint-unified.sh:611-613`, `:359-362`; `agentbox.toml:2543`; `agentbox.default.toml:1770`; the custody design statement quoted in AB-06.13; AB-02.24's "PID 1 still carries every `.env` key"; AB-11.16's same-uid 0600-key note; AB-06.3/AB-06.11 on the two-way writable credential bind; `flake.nix:4054-4061` (devuser sudo drop baked from the build-time flag — off in this image).
- Warning signs visible today: the custody design itself names this as bypass 1; OPEN R2 is registered; the flag's own comment says to leave it off "until the rehearsal passes", i.e. today's posture is knowingly the unsafe one; the box self-reports `degraded:devuser-sudo` when the flag is flipped without a rebuild (AB-02.24, AB-05.16); detection is weakened by F-15 below.
- Confidence: high (every hop except the exact entry point of the malicious/errant devuser code is stated in the material; steps 4–6 are inferred but each is the documented behavior).
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Mandate revocation never reaches the pod: a revoked delegation's WAC grant stays in force
- Topics: AB-11.7, AB-11.8, AB-11.9
- Chain of events:
  1. The operator mints a mandate for an external contractor agent's DID, modes including Write; the *caller* PUTs the returned `acl_turtle` to the pod container (`routes/mandate.js:191-231`, `lib/mandate.js:137`).
  2. The engagement ends. The operator calls `POST /v1/mandate/revoke` and receives 200 (`routes/mandate.js:388-430`).
  3. Revoke re-signs the same d-tag with `revoked = true` and updates the local `registry.json` (`routes/mandate.js:254-278`). Nothing is published to any relay — the revoking event is only returned in `signed_event` (`routes/mandate.js:264-269`), and no code in agentbox publishes mandate events at all (AB-11.7 Open).
  4. Critically, nothing in agentbox removes or rewrites the `.acl` at the pod. The material states it: "a revoked mandate's WAC grant stays in force at the pod until someone replaces it" (`routes/mandate.js:238-281`, `lib/mandate.js:129-152`).
  5. The pod keeps enforcing the WAC modes minted at issuance; the contractor's key retains Read/Write on the client's container indefinitely. `isMandateActive` checks only `revoked` and `expires_at` (`lib/mandate.js:191`) — the registry the operator sees says revoked, the enforcement point says allowed.
- Evidence: the Tension paragraph in AB-11.7; `routes/mandate.js:264-269` (nothing sent); `routes/mandate.js:453-457` (the listing shows revoked entries, giving false comfort); AB-11.9's note that signature authenticity is a separate, unchecked concern.
- Warning signs visible today: the revocation gap is written down as a Tension in the governing topic; the "signed mandate distribution" Open records that no publish path exists; the mint flow explicitly leaves the `.acl` PUT to the caller, so revocation was always going to be asymmetric.
- Confidence: high for the defect (it is stated); the end-to-end incident scenario is inferred from the stated mechanism.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The privacy filter never sees the events slot's only write: `dispatch` is not classified as a mutation, so unredacted content lands in the durable hash-chained logs
- Topics: AB-04.4, AB-04.5, AB-03.12, AB-03.13
- Chain of events:
  1. The events slot policy is configured on; every adapter method is wrapped observability → privacy (`metrics.js:125-140`).
  2. Layer 2 gates redaction by method name (`privacy-filter.js:283-313`). The set does not classify `dispatch` — the events slot's only write — nor beads `claim`/`close` (AB-04.5 Tension).
  3. Any emitter reaching `emitAgentAction`/dispatch sends its payload through untouched: pass-through with a marker stamp only (`privacy-filter.js:661-663`). Failure payloads preserve raw text (`failure_detail` "preserved text", AB-03.13); only `role: 'content'` leaves are ever rewritten in place (AB-04.5).
  4. `LocalJsonlEventsAdapter.dispatch` appends the full JSON line to the dated JSONL files on a persistent volume (`local-jsonl.js:61`, `:114`), inside the hash chain.
  5. Client PII and secret-bearing text accumulate in plaintext. Because the files are hash-chained (`audit-chain.js:68-72`), after-the-fact redaction either breaks the chain visibly or is impossible — which is plausibly how the exposure was discovered. The non-strict fail-open branch (`privacy-filter.js:677-682`) compounds the same class of problem for the methods that *are* classified.
- Evidence: the AB-04.5 Tension ("the events slot's only write, `dispatch`, reaches the hash-chained JSONL log without redaction, whatever the slot policy says"); `privacy-filter.js:283-313`, `:661-663`, `:677-682`; `local-jsonl.js:61`, `:114`; `audit-chain.js:6`, `:68-72`.
- Warning signs visible today: the Tension is documented; the privacy-filter contract suite exists (`tests/contract/privacy-filter.contract.spec.js`), so the layer is load-bearing and its gap is a live exposure, not a theoretical one.
- Confidence: high for the mechanism (stated verbatim); the specific payload that carried the client data is inferred.
- Severity: high

## Further latent defects

### F-01 — `POST /v1/exec/record` is described as operator-gated but carries no operator gate
- Topics: AB-03.1
- Evidence: the mount comment says "operator-gated" (`server.js:433`, `routes/exec-record.js:6-7`), but the route attaches no `requireOperator` preHandler, unlike `routes/mandate.js:311`; in `hybrid`/`nip98` modes any NIP-98-verified signer passes `authMiddleware` (`middleware/auth.js:194-199`), and the route appends through the chain-writing singleton (`routes/exec-record.js:128`).
- Failure: any authenticated principal — not just the operator — appends fabricated execution events to the journal and its hash chain; provenance for a disputed client deliverable cannot be trusted afterward.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-02 — `role_isolation` apply-class drift: the governing doc says boot, the catalogue and image say rebuild
- Topics: AB-02.24, AB-05.16, AB-01.2
- Evidence: `BASELINE-container.md:77` calls it boot-class; the catalogue entry is `apply_class: 'rebuild'` (`system-manifest.js:97-99`); the devuser sudo drop is baked from the build-time flag (`flake.nix:4054-4061`), so a boot-time flip reports `degraded:devuser-sudo`; the key's own comment still says "restart to apply" (`agentbox.toml:2536`).
- Failure: an operator follows the governing doc, flips the flag and restarts, believes the box is isolated; devuser keeps passwordless sudo and the widened socket while every dashboard says the gate is on.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, Tension)

### F-03 — In-container loopback forgery of `X-Agentbox-Pubkey`: the stamp authenticates a network position, not a process
- Topics: AB-10.14, AB-11.14
- Evidence: `proxyVerifiedPubkey` accepts the header only from a loopback peer (`agent-event-auth.js:98-105`, `:117`), but any process inside the container can open its own loopback connection (TODO-unified X-1, `agent-event-auth.js:37-39`); `resolveAgentDid` shares the same rule (`action-plane.js:222-233`).
- Failure: agent-spawned code POSTs to `/v1/agent-events/emit` over loopback behind a session/bearer hop and attributes events to an arbitrary `did:nostr`; audit and governance attribution are forged without a key compromise.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-04 — Raw CDP published on 0.0.0.0 into a browser whose profile volume holds the Podkey vault
- Topics: AB-06.7, AB-06.4, AB-06.12, AB-06.2
- Evidence: browsercontainer publishes `0.0.0.0:9222→9223` CDP, sanctioned (`docker-compose.browsercontainer.yml:53`, `check-ports-loopback.mjs:99`); "raw CDP is also reachable" (AB-06.4); the named volume `browsercontainer-profile` holds "Podkey's encrypted vault — never down -v" (`docker-compose.browsercontainer.yml:64`, `:75-76`); `K_browser`'s pubkey is still null and the nip98-proxy roster still admits the house key for it (`config/custody/g5-key-split.json:16-19`, `agentbox.toml:2134`).
- Failure: a LAN peer connects to CDP (the protocol has no authentication), drives the authenticated browser session and reads the profile/vault — client-site sessions and the still-house-key identity are abused. Inferred composition of stated facts.
- Confidence: medium — inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the 0.0.0.0 publish is presented as sanctioned; only the adjacent `K_browser` gap is a marked Open in AB-06.12)

### F-05 — `payment_settlement` is declared zero-tolerance but no route ever passes that class to the authority gate
- Topics: AB-05.13
- Evidence: the class is declared with its own task properties (`agentbox.toml:1429`, `:1477`); `mandate_revoke` is the only class any route names (`routes/llm-marketplace.js:473`).
- Failure: a payment-rail mutation routes without the strictest governance class ever engaging; the "zero-tolerance" label is unenforced decoration.
- Confidence: high (for the gap); the failure scenario is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-06 — Break-glass bearer bypasses NIP-98 on the LAN with default-off expiry and scope, and rides query strings on WS
- Topics: AB-10.3, AB-10.9, AB-11.16
- Evidence: a single shared bearer is accepted while the secret is set (`proxy.mjs:529-533`, `:567`); the expiry and scope bounds are "both bounds default-OFF and opt-in" (`proxy.mjs:534-544` as drawn; AB-11.16); WS upgrades accept it via `?access_token=`/`?bearer=` (`proxy.mjs:960-965`), a carrier that lands in logs; INGRESS-identity tracks both as known divergences.
- Failure: an operator sets the break-glass secret during an outage and forgets it; an unbounded shared secret is a full NIP-98 bypass on the one LAN-published identity door until rotated.
- Confidence: medium (configuration-dependent — the secret must be present)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence / known divergences)

### F-07 — The bootstrap-done projection table is opt-in: nothing forces a new boot-written file into it
- Topics: AB-02.22
- Evidence: `_PROJECTIONS` carries two rows today (`seal-bootstrap.sh:141`) and the Open states "nothing forces a new boot-written file into it"; the sentinel is the sole readiness signal (DDD-001), and ADR-2104 documents the exact prior failure — sentinel written over a boot that skipped projections.
- Failure: a later feature adds a boot projection without a table row; Stage B dies late, `bootstrap.done` is written anyway, `/ready` answers 200 over a half-booted box and orchestrators route client traffic to it.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-08 — System One consumers are all-true in the manifest while activation is staged, and the five external consumers get `_ALLOW_CLOUD=1` by default
- Topics: AB-05.13, AB-08.5
- Evidence: ADR-2155 is `accepted`/`complete`/`staged` (`ADR-2155:5-7`) while `[system_one.consumers]` sets all nineteen true (`agentbox.toml:906-927`); the exports for visionflow, website, solid pod, ontomark and vault rank each carry `_ALLOW_CLOUD=1` with no laya endpoint unless listed (`system_one.rs:553-639`); routing prompts ride the proxy with no client-side key (`lib/skill-route.cjs:418`, `:682-684`).
- Failure: on image activation, prompts and tool content from five external systems egress to the cloud backend by default — a confidentiality incident against a client contract, with no configuration change to blame.
- Confidence: high for the facts; the harm is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open for the staging; the `_ALLOW_CLOUD` default is stated unmarked in the panel)

### F-09 — Unsigned mandates persist and `isMandateActive` never checks a signature
- Topics: AB-11.9, AB-11.8
- Evidence: an entry can sit in `registry.json` with no signed event behind it and `ensureMandate` keeps returning it (`routes/mandate.js:150-153`, `:171`); `isMandateActive` checks only `revoked`/`expires_at` (`lib/mandate.js:191`); a re-mint content-addresses a fresh `issued_at` and produces a new URN rather than signing the old record (`lib/mandate.js:107`, `:113-124`); the registry is a local same-uid-writable file (`routes/mandate.js:52`).
- Failure: grants minted while the signer was down remain usable forever; in-container tamper re-marks a revoked entry active (compounds with F-03).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, Drift)

### F-10 — Pod writes sign under the container's sovereign identity, not the mandate grantee
- Topics: AB-11.13, AB-04.11
- Evidence: the default signer is `loadSovereignSigner` (`pod-signer.js:98-99`); `AGENTBOX_PROFILE` no longer selects a stack (`pod-signer.js:21-26`); the per-session grantee is the profile DID (`routes/sessions-boundary.js:294`).
- Failure: per-agent mandate grants never bind the writes actually made; everything is attributed to and authorized as the container identity, so profile-level revocation changes nothing at the pod.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-11 — A caller-supplied `Authorization` header bypasses the pod signer and `requireSigned` in both modes
- Topics: AB-04.11, AB-11.13
- Evidence: "caller header is trusted, never overwritten" (`_solid-http-base.js:81-82`); the `hasAuth` branch sends it as-is before the signer is consulted, including when `sign_requests` is on; the Open notes "which callers pass one is not traced".
- Failure: any code path that forwards request-controlled headers into a pods write emits unsigned or caller-chosen-identity writes from behind the ADR-2064 fail-closed gate.
- Confidence: medium (callers untraced)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-12 — Capability trust classes are validated and reported but consumed by nothing
- Topics: AB-11.1, AB-11.12
- Evidence: declarations are validated (`capability-scope.js:113-114`) and surfaced (`execution-coverage.js:59`), but "neither the authority gate nor the mandate code reads them"; the acknowledged bypasses (code-mode sub-calls, MCP plugins, background jobs) stand.
- Failure: a `secrets`/`network`-class effect runs under a grant minted as if `pure`; the blast-radius labels are decorative at enforcement time.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Divergence)

### F-13 — The sidechain producer runs under a broader gate than the accepted design specifies
- Topics: AB-05.13, AB-02.6, AB-01.1
- Evidence: the BASELINE's supervised-set table gates `sidestr-producer` on `[sidechain.signer].enabled` (`BASELINE-container.md:403`); the code gates on top-level `[sidechain].enabled` (`flake.nix:322`), shipped true after the ENOSPC recovery (`agentbox.toml:1959`); the `signer`/`bridge` sub-blocks have no schema entry (`BASELINE-container.md:362-363`).
- Failure: an operator tightening `[sidechain.signer]` per the design changes nothing; a chain-signing producer keeps running and signing under a gate narrower review never approved.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-14 — Features are enabled in the manifest while their ADRs are proposed, staged or never booted
- Topics: AB-01.1, AB-05.17, AB-08.16, AB-08.15, AB-05.13
- Evidence: voicelink `enabled = true`/`record_events = true` (`agentbox.toml:2264`, `:2271`) while ADR-2151 is `proposed`/`none`/`inactive`, baking `voice-paned` (`flake.nix:1848-1850`, `:2129`) and registering hooks (`entrypoint-unified.sh:2165`); jev-compaction enabled (`agentbox.toml:741`) while ADR-2121 is staged with no image booted (`ADR-2121:161-163`); ADR-2155 staged vs consumers all-true; isaac-sim dispatches unconditionally (`agentbox.sh:2891`) with an ungated live catalogue entry (`system-manifest.js:178`) while ADR-2149 is proposed; ADR-2154 staged while the Pele probe already runs (`artifact-probes.json:75-78`).
- Failure: production behavior is defined by designs nobody accepted and, for factrail, by code that has never been booted; when one misfires there is no accepted spec to judge against and rollback semantics are undefined.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Open — repeatedly)

### F-15 — Health signals that cannot fail: the adapter gauge is never set and the CLI exits 0 on degraded output
- Topics: AB-04.15, AB-04.16, AB-05.7
- Evidence: `agentbox_adapter_health` is declared (`metrics.js:35-40`) with a setter that has no caller (`metrics.js:202-204`) while BASELINE claims it is surfaced (`BASELINE-container.md:119`); `agentbox.sh health --json` prints and exits 0 regardless (`agentbox.sh:1148-1151`); a host without `jq` returns 0 even when slots are degraded (`agentbox.sh:1220-1223`).
- Failure: during the incident, both dashboards and scripted checks report healthy while slots sit degraded or quarantined; detection of R-1/R-2/R-3 is delayed by construction.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, Tension)

---

Items I checked and am *not* raising as findings because the material resolves them or states no defect: the Fastify-5 URL rebuild in NIP-98 re-verification (`middleware/auth.js:73`), the ADR-2040 code-server/jupyter credential minting, the wrapper URL authority parsing, the session-plane dirty-worktree holds, and the MCP projector's ownership ledger. One item I could not settle from the material and therefore only flag here, not as a finding: whether a `/mgmt/`-prefixed signed `u` tag verifies a second time at the management API is an explicitly open runtime question (AB-03.15).

## agentbox#2 / critical

### F-01 — Default relay consumer admits any signed author from public relays into local handlers
- Topics: AB-13.7, AB-13.1, AB-13.6
- Evidence: With `ingressPolicy = allowlist`, an empty allowlist ADMITS every signed author (`mcp/nostr-bridge/relay-consumer.js:764-768`) — the opposite of the Rust boundary's deny-all (`services/nostr-pod-bridge/src/admission.rs:35-45`). Nothing in `flake.nix`, the entrypoint or the manifest projector sets `AGENTBOX_RELAY_ALLOWED_PUBKEYS` (`management-api/server.js:1434`). The manifest sets `external_fanout = "bidirectional"` (`agentbox.toml:175` → `flake.nix:4515`), which adds `wss://relay.damus.io,wss://relay.primal.net` (`flake.nix:4321`) to the subscription; any signed author whose event p-tags a local npub (I10) passes I07 into the governance, intent and payment handlers and pod inbox files. The authority gate's own responder allowlist (`management-api/lib/authority-consumer.js:259-271`) keeps 31403 decisions closed, so it is not an approvals bypass — it is ungoverned remote event ingestion into state agents consume.
- Failure: anyone with a Nostr key publishes a p-tagged event on damus or primal; it lands in `pods/<npub>/events/inbox/` and intent markers, and local agents act on it as if it came from an authorised peer.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension "JS consumer allowlist" plus an explicit Open on the runtime configuration)

### F-02 — Shipped default leaves every role secret, including the break-glass bearer, ambient in every child process
- Topics: AB-16.13, AB-16.9, AB-16.1, AB-16.7, AB-16.6
- Evidence: With `role_isolation = false` (the shipped default), the capture/scrub functions "return at their first line, so every other ROLE variable stays in PID 1 and reaches every child" (`entrypoint-unified.sh:395`, `entrypoint-unified.sh:439`). The break-glass bearer is one of those ROLE variables (`proxy.mjs:53`) and "is still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4" (AB-16.9 C3) — and BRIDGE_TOKEN must be set for the voice loop's container-to-container path. Break-glass matching alone, with unset expiry and unset scope, is a full NIP-98 identity bypass whose downstream attribution is a sentinel placeholder pubkey (AB-16.6). Compounding it, every supervised program runs as `devuser` uid 1000, so "each can read every other's 0600 key file and the mirror key" (AB-16.1 N4) and uid 1000 is admitted to the session socket by design (`session-plane-acl.json:8-11`).
- Failure: any compromised MCP server, consultant CLI or agent process reads `NIP98_PROXY_ALLOW_BEARER` from its own environment (or any co-resident key file) and authenticates to port 9096 as a full identity bypass over the LAN, with every action attributed to a fake sentinel pubkey.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — the same-uid divergence is marked (AB-16.1 N4); the PID-1 env reach (AB-16.13) and the bearer-equals-BRIDGE_TOKEN fact (AB-16.9 C3) are stated without markers and their combination is unflagged

### F-03 — Nearly all agent-initiated side-effect surfaces bypass the policy pipeline entirely
- Topics: AB-14.1, AB-14.2, AB-14.14
- Evidence: ADR-2041 wired exactly one surface — `POST /v1/tasks` via `dispatchTaskSpawn` (`action-plane.js:254`). "every OTHER surface above (direct tool call, code-mode, ACI shell, consultant, dream, beads, alt harness) still reaches EXEC with no interceptor" (AB-14.1 DIVERGENCE1, citing `GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:359-362`). The dream engine "RECORDS each side effect into the same journal through POST /v1/exec/record (ADR-2071 Phase 1) but nothing there approves or denies". The fully implemented 9-stage pipeline with `NEVER_FAIL_OPEN` (`agent-action-pipeline.js:36`) has no production caller for its approval branch.
- Failure: an agent session (or a compromised one) mutates files, spends money or exfiltrates through Bash, code-mode or a consultant with no approval gate, no journal entry and no capability token — the entire governance apparatus observes nothing.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE "NARROWED by ADR-2041", named the TOP OPEN RISK)

### F-04 — The nostr command gateway signs with the ROOT operator key by default
- Topics: AB-16.5, AB-13.5, AB-13.8
- Evidence: `sk = rawSk — the gateway signs AS the operator` is the default; the derived-child mode is opt-in only via `AGENTBOX_GATEWAY_IDENTITY === 'gateway'` (`gateway.cjs:154-155`). The gateway is the process that accepts remote commands which inject text into the coordinator, spawn sessions and spend LLM calls (`gateway.cjs:269-322`). The panel itself flags this as a DIVERGENCE, and neither derived key appears in the custody register (AB-16.5 note, ADR-2027). Combined with F-02, the root key is held by a network-facing daemon and readable at 0600-by-uid by every co-resident process.
- Failure: compromise of the gateway process (or any devuser process reading its key) yields the root operator Nostr identity — the same identity that is `adminPub`, the only allowed commander (`gateway.cjs:151,157`) — letting an attacker command the fleet as the operator, with no revocation path short of a full rebuild.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in AB-16.5)

### F-05 — Malformed zone metadata silently downgrades dream digests to plaintext publication even with the gate on
- Topics: AB-23.24, AB-23.3
- Evidence: "Malformed or absent zones become empty; unknown section can select first configured zone" (`zone_crypto.rs:86-105`) and "No encrypted match means plaintext plan" (`zone_crypto.rs:222-224`); "Malformed metadata produces plaintext selection even with the gate on" (AB-23.24 Privacy paragraph). The no-key refusal applies "only after zone policy identifies an encrypted zone" (`digest.rs:417-428`).
- Failure: a digest intended for an encrypted forum zone — carrying session-derived summary content — is signed and published in plaintext to the relay because the section/zone tag was malformed, with nothing logged as a policy violation. The material's own caveat: the probe traced the selection logic but "does not publish plaintext or claim the live relay would admit it".
- Confidence: high for the selection behaviour (source-traced); the end-to-end plaintext landing on the relay is inferred one step beyond the probe
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Annexe retention sweep `rm`s by mtime heuristic with errors masked and no lock check
- Topics: AB-23.18, AB-23.2
- Evidence: "SSH retention: find date-like dirs by parent mtime, then rm" (`engine.rs:806-810`); "Shell ends with true; find and deletion errors hidden" (`engine.rs:809`); "The exact retention command deleted a disposable old parent directory containing a freshly written child. It checks neither active jobs nor night locks, and a failing find command still returned success with no stderr."
- Failure: a currently-running night's annexe directory sits under a parent whose mtime looks old (or a concurrent repo-night is mid-clone); the sweep deletes live work — in-progress worktrees, journals, evaluator output — and reports success while doing it, and no error surfaces anywhere because the pipeline ends in `true`.
- Confidence: high (fixture-reproduced in the linked audit)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Dream publication identity is unbound: branch name, not commit; Accept decided by URL presence
- Topics: AB-23.6, AB-23.4
- Evidence: "the PR request carries a branch name, not the verified commit hash" (`seams.rs:341`); "A successful create command returns Some even for empty stdout" (`seams.rs:531`); "the adapter then selects the acceptance notice by Option presence"; "The tested command boundary does not validate the URL or bind the pushed head to the evaluated tree" (AB-23.6 Open, publication identity).
- Failure: the pushed branch already exists from an earlier attempt (branch names are not unique per attempt); the push lands extra commits or the PR points at a mix, the operator reviews and merges a PR whose content was never the tree the gate judged — the human merge gate is the last line of defence and it is inspecting an unbound artefact.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, publication identity)

### F-08 — Evaluator and formatter writes escape the recorded candidate tree, so ACCEPT attests code that was not judged
- Topics: AB-23.4, AB-23.5
- Evidence: "a valid spaced Rust path attribute caused rustfmt to modify an external temporary file"; the same review "reproduced an evaluator changing tracked files after the candidate tree was recorded"; "Worktree separation does not confine formatter or evaluator writes" (AB-23.5 note); "Formatting is not a confinement boundary" (AB-23.4). The gate rechecks the applied patch from the recorded state (`gate.rs:420`), so its ACCEPT is measured against a tree the evaluator has already mutated.
- Failure: an evaluator (or a malicious repo's test script) rewrites source after the tree hash is captured; the gate ACCEPTs the recorded snapshot, the published branch contains the post-mutation files, and the operator merges code the evaluators never ran.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — Execution journal: concurrent appends share seq numbers, restart reuses sequences, and dedupe ignores payloads
- Topics: AB-23.23, AB-14.7
- Evidence: "Concurrent appends can share seq; restart needs hydration" (`execution-journal.js:152-185`); "Caller key becomes deterministic event ID. Duplicate ID returns prior envelope, without payload comparison" (`exec-record.js:52-54`, `execution-journal.js:144-151`); "route probes reproduce changed-payload deduplication, restart sequence reuse and concurrent duplicate sequence numbers while the on-disk hash chain verifies". Yet AB-14.7 states the invariant "session_urn + seq is the journal key, contiguous from 0, assigned HERE" and that the journal is ADR-2071's governance record.
- Failure: two dream-night side effects interleave at the API and both persist under the same (session, seq); after a management-api restart without hydration the sequence restarts; a caller re-posts the same key with different content and the change is silently recorded as the old event — and the hash chain verifies throughout, so the corruption is invisible to the integrity check.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated in AB-23.23's audit prose, no Tension/Drift/Debt marker; the AB-14.7 invariant it contradicts is presented as holding)

### F-10 — The cross-repo event forwarder emits a frame the consumer rejects, and the mirror drops two wire fields
- Topics: AB-17.1, AB-17.10
- Evidence: "the live outbound forwarder does NOT [use the canonical builder]: it sends the bare event (`agent-event-ws-subscriber.js:147`), which VisionClaw ingest rejects as Malformed. see ES-02, finding W2-01" — while the publisher's own comment claims the canonical envelope is kept on those paths (`agent-event-publisher.js:296-303`). Separately, the VisionClaw mirror `AgentActionEnvelope` has "no `failure_mode` or `authority_class` field, so serde drops those two wire fields on ingest" (AB-17.1 Drift note).
- Failure: the ADR-2025 provenance stream to VisionClaw is either entirely rejected (forwarder path) or silently loses failure and authority-class data (every other path); cross-repo consumers build a provenance view that is wrong in ways no error reveals.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, twice, in AB-17.1)

### F-11 — BC20 identity mappings degrade to memory on append failure and are never persisted for blocked candidates
- Topics: AB-17.4, AB-17.6, AB-17.10
- Evidence: "A failed append degrades to in-memory only with a stderr line" (`bc20-provenance-bridge.js:359-368`); "Where the VisionClaw kind is content-addressed (execution, kg) the local is a FRESH sha256-12 and the original urn:agentbox identity is recovered only from the durable UrnMapping store — lose the store, lose the crossing" (B01); no recovery receipts exist (PROTOCOL-registry DIVERGENCE, AB-17.4). Additionally, a blocked elevation "puts a `urn:visionclaw:kg` address on the wire that `toAgentbox` can never resolve back" because the mapping is persisted only inside a successful publish (`elevation-publisher.js:163-173`, AB-17.10 Debt).
- Failure: a disk-full or permissions error on the JSONL mapping file is a stderr line only; every subsequent crossing accumulates in memory, the process restarts, and a whole day of cross-namespace identity bindings becomes permanently unrecoverable — `toAgentbox` returns null for real events.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE on the durable-translation row; Debt on the blocked-candidate URN)

### F-12 — tmux fail-open fallback can double-submit after a session-plane send that already took effect
- Topics: AB-12.5, AB-12.15, AB-12.6
- Evidence: on any `sessionSend` throw the bridge falls back to `tmux send-keys` (`server.mjs:162-168`), and the send failure path includes client timeouts (`client.cjs:18,22`). AB-12.15 states the daemon boundary's narrower guarantee directly: "send can return failure after submission" (`plane.rs`), and the FALL node is annotated "prior submission may be unknown".
- Failure: the socket delivers the text to the coordinator but the reply times out; the bridge logs "session plane unreachable" and types the same text again through tmux — the operator's command-agent receives the prompt twice and executes its side effects (spends, session spawns, file writes) twice, with `pushTurn` recording only one `via` value.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the failure-after-submit ordering and the throw-on-timeout are both stated; the end-to-end duplicate is their direct composition, not an observed incident in the material)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — the fallback is marked FAIL-OPEN (ADR-044 D3) in AB-12.5; the duplicate-submission consequence appears only as unmarked limits prose in AB-12.15

### F-13 — A readiness failure overwrites a completed night's report and appends a HANDOFF row to it
- Topics: AB-23.1, AB-23.2, AB-23.7
- Evidence: "The controls probe reproduced a completed night gaining a HANDOFF row and an overwritten report after its evaluator config became unusable" (AB-23.1); the ordering cause is drawn explicitly: "Persist HANDOFF before checking finished state" (`engine.rs:599`), and "Readiness can append HANDOFF to an already finished night" (AB-23.2 Limits).
- Failure: an operator degrades evaluator config (or annexe health flaps) after a good night; the next tick rewrites the finished night's report with a HANDOFF/BLOCKED-ENV record, destroying the evidence of what the completed run did — while the ledger rows persist, the night's report-level record is lost.
- Confidence: high (reproduced per the material)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile, and the zero-tolerance settlement class is never gated
- Topics: AB-15.6, AB-14.11, AB-15.9
- Evidence: "spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile: the flock-guarded cost_cap ledger file (`ledger.rs:119,136`), the pod-held sats balance behind GET /v1/pay/balance (`payments.js:205`), and the marketplace token_budget carried on a grant (`llm-marketplace.js:85`). No surface sums them, so no single number answers what this container has spent." And: "the authority classification table names payment_settlement zero-tolerance… but no production path passes that actionClass to the gate. The only call sites are tests" (`agentbox.toml:1429`, `authority.js:226`, `tests/sovereign/authority.test.js:129,248`).
- Failure: a runaway agent drains the cost-cap ledger while pod sats and marketplace grants still show headroom (or vice versa); an actual sats settlement — the one class the manifest calls irreversible and zero-tolerance — proceeds with no authority gate ever firing.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, twice in AB-15.6; DEBT again in AB-14.11)

### F-15 — Two topics contradict each other on whether session mirror egress is redacted before it leaves the box
- Topics: AB-16.11, AB-13.9, AB-13.17, AB-13.10
- Evidence: AB-16.11 draws the mirror as "composes UNREDACTED selected text" → gift-wrap → cloud relay, and asserts "ADR-2026 — proposed / partial / INACTIVE for the complete egress policy… one of them unredacted before wrapping." AB-13.9 draws the same hook with mandatory `redactForEgress(body)`, fail-closed on redaction failure (`nostr-live-mirror.cjs:453-457`), and AB-13.17 records "RESOLVED ADR-2026 — the mirror and the kind-30840 digest… now share ONE policy contract: a global AGENTBOX_EGRESS switch… mandatory redaction" with a shared fixture (`tests/fixtures/egress-redaction.v1.json`).
- Failure: whichever panel is wrong misleads the operator at the worst moment: if AB-16.11 reflects the deployed hook, per-turn session text is already leaving unredacted (merely gift-wrapped) to a cloud worker relay and the "resolved" closeout is false; if AB-13.9 is right, the custody topic's core egress claim is stale and an operator auditing from AB-16 would re-design around a non-existent exposure. Neither topic flags the other.
- Confidence: high that the contradiction exists; which side is current is not determinable from the material
- Severity: medium (high if AB-16.11 turns out to describe the running hook)
- Marked by authors: no — each side carries its own note (a DIVERGENCE in AB-16.11, a RESOLVED in AB-13.9/17); the conflict between them is unflagged

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Runtime values of `AGENTBOX_RELAY_ALLOWED_PUBKEYS` and `AGENTBOX_RELAY_FANOUT` on the deployed container** — the material explicitly says this is runtime configuration not visible in source (AB-13.7 Open); F-01's real exposure size depends entirely on it.
2. **The auth posture of the published 8444/8443 voice surfaces** — whether `/embed` and `/api/*` on the 0.0.0.0-published ports require any credential before a LAN caller can open a mic session that reaches the coordinator (`docker-compose.voice.yml:40-41`, `voice/console/Caddyfile`).
3. **The current `nostr-live-mirror.cjs` redaction state and its live configuration** — recipient allowlist contents, `AGENTBOX_LIVE_MIRROR`, `AGENTBOX_MIRROR_DRY_RUN`, and which topic (AB-16.11 or AB-13.9) matches HEAD, to resolve F-15.
4. **Whether the running management-api actually calls `ExecutionJournal.hydrate()` on boot** — the helper is documented (`execution-journal.js:289-300`) but no caller is shown; restart sequence reuse in F-09 depends on it.
5. **Which "staged until rebuild" activations are live in the running image** — ADR-2087 (exit 2, posted 0), the `[system_one]` consumer projections, `role_isolation`, and the dream adapter's 0.4.0 pins all carry activation evidence older than their source pins; several findings above change severity depending on which image is actually running.

## agentbox#2 / premortem

# Post-mortem — client-loss incident (written twelve months on)

Method note: everything below is drawn only from the diagrams corpus and its own citations. Where I reason past what a panel states, Confidence says "inferred". Nothing here asserts an order or concurrency a diagram does not show.

---

### R-1 — One compartment holds every key: with `role_isolation` off (the shipped default), any devuser process can read the operator root key and then command the fleet and approve governance as the operator
- Topics: AB-16.1, AB-16.4, AB-16.5, AB-16.7, AB-16.13, AB-16.14, AB-14.5, AB-14.16, AB-13.5, AB-23.4, AB-23.11
- Chain of events: (1) a devuser (uid 1000) process — an MCP server, a consultant CLI, a skill, or a dream-engine evaluator over a newly nominated external repo — executes attacker-influenced code; (2) it reads `/run/secrets/nostr.key` (0400 devuser), every 0600 profile key, the mirror key, the 36 DEVUSER_CLASS env credentials, and connects to the session socket (uid 1000 admitted by design); (3) with the operator secret it seals a NIP-59 DM as the commander — the gateway's only sender authorisation is `sealed sender == commanderPub` — so `/spawn`, `/tab`, `/say` reach every session; (4) the same key satisfies both NIP-98 and `isApprover` on `POST /v1/approvals/:id/decide` (inferred: the operator is on the approval allowlist), so the attacker approves their own kind-31402 requests; (5) exfiltration rides the sanctioned egress paths (mirror relay, router vendor, consultant CLI, Z.AI) — no egress allowlist constrains a process that legitimately holds env credentials.
- Evidence: AB-16.1 N4 — role_isolation OFF is the shipped default, a same-uid boundary, "each can read every other's 0600 key file and the mirror key, and uid 1000 is admitted to the session socket by design". AB-16.7 — `PEER->KF read SUCCEEDS`, `PEER->SOCK ADMITTED (session-plane-acl.json:8-11)`, `PEER->BSK read SUCCEEDS, the boot writes it 0400 devuser (config/entrypoint-unified.sh:1121-1130)`. AB-16.14 — 36 DEVUSER_CLASS credentials stay in every agent process, Q8 pending (`config/custody/env-classes.json:94`). AB-16.5 — gateway default identity is `'operator'`; "out of the box the gateway holds and signs with the ROOT operator key" (`gateway.cjs:154-155`). AB-13.5 — command gate order, sealed-sender check at `gateway.cjs:673`. AB-14.5 — decide requires NIP-98 (`approvals.js:139-144`) and `isApprover` (`approvals.js:150-155`). AB-16.4 — trust-seed pre-accepts the trust prompt for every checkout under the workspace at boot (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:1801-1814`), an explicit availability-over-confirmation trade; ten Opus panes once sat dead an hour behind that gate. AB-23.4 — a dream evaluator changed tracked files after the candidate tree was recorded; rustfmt escaped the worktree. AB-23.11 — the engine clones and archives nominated external repos, and sibling overlays can replace target source. AB-16.13 — flag off, capture/scrub return at their first line and every ROLE variable stays in PID 1 (`entrypoint-unified.sh:395,439`).
- Warning signs visible today: the governing docs themselves name the same-UID boundary as the residual limit (AB-16.7); the flag cannot yet be turned on — under it JunkieJarvis, the mirror, colloquy publish and the gateway fail closed pending W3b (AB-14.16 OPEN, AB-16 open note); every custody-register row is custodian-UNCONFIRMED (AB-16.9); AB-14.14 Debt names the same boundary for journalled `agent_did`.
- Confidence: high that the compartment is flat and the keys are co-resident; the specific entry vector is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Turn content leaves the box by default: the skill router sends every routed prompt to an external vendor, the mirror ships session text to a public cloud relay, and the two topics contradict each other about whether the mirror redacts
- Topics: AB-22.2, AB-16.11, AB-13.9, AB-13.17, AB-13.10, AB-23.15
- Chain of events: (1) the operator works on client material in a normal turn; (2) `router = jev` is the default and `bypass_projects` is empty by default, so the routing prompt — the turn text plus the operator's synced claude.ai skill descriptions as candidate rubrics — leaves the container to the Jev vendor (backend `typesafe` via the System One proxy); (3) on Stop, the mirror gift-wraps the assistant's final reply to the derived child identity on `wss://dreamlab-nostr-relay.solitary-paper-764d.workers.dev`; (4) on SessionEnd the digest flattens the transcript to Z.AI and publishes a kind-30840 event whose visibility is public; (5) client material now sits at three external parties plus a public relay, and no per-client gate was ever applied — the one gate that exists (`bypass_projects`) ships empty.
- Evidence: AB-22.2 — "router jev is the default … a turn that routes at all and misses the cascade leaves the container unless its project is listed"; `agentbox.toml:1321` empty by default, "today's turns still go out"; ADR-2090's acceptance covers the skill-routing prompt only, not factrail or jev-checkpoints (`ADR-2090-skill-routing-prompt-egress.md:121`), and whether it covers synced claude.ai skill descriptions is OPEN (`ADR-2091-live-skill-router.md:38`); the manifest again selects backend typesafe (`agentbox.toml:891,929`). AB-16.11 — the mirror "composes UNREDACTED selected text", gift-wraps to the child identity, publishes to the cloud worker relay, the only non-LAN egress in the domain; "there is no single answer to 'is session content leaving this box, and redacted how'". AB-13.9 contradicts it: the same hook is drawn with mandatory `redactForEgress` and fail-closed skip (`nostr-live-mirror.cjs:453-457`) — both panels cannot describe the deployed path. AB-13.10 / AB-13.17 — the digest sends flattened (redacted) transcript to Z.AI before publication; "The digest is public kind-30840 visibility". AB-23.15 — Zai is the default primary with Loom fallback; "a provider label alone does not enforce" confinement (`estate.rs:126`).
- Warning signs visible today: the OPEN scope question on ADR-2090; AB-16.11's own consequence line ("two paths, two gates, two encryption postures, one of them unredacted before wrapping"); ADR-2026 recorded proposed/partial/INACTIVE for the complete egress policy; two topics of the same corpus disagreeing about redaction on the identical hook.
- Confidence: high that the defaults egress prompts and replies; inferred that client-identifying content was in them — that is the incident.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — One shared secret is both the tab0-bridge bearer and the NIP-98 break-glass, rides in URLs, and has no expiry
- Topics: AB-16.6, AB-16.9, AB-12.3, AB-12.7, AB-12.9
- Chain of events: (1) `BRIDGE_TOKEN` must be set for the bridge's `0.0.0.0` bind, and until Q4 it equals `NIP98_PROXY_ALLOW_BEARER`; (2) browser WebSocket clients cannot set an Authorization header, so the bridge's `/feed` and the proxy's WS upgrades accept `?token=`, `?auth=`, `?access_token=`, `?bearer=` — the secret lands in browser history and proxy logs; (3) the same value is handed to the Unmute voice backend as `KYUTAI_LLM_API_KEY`, so voice-side containers hold the break-glass credential too; (4) a leak puts a LAN attacker on the 0.0.0.0-published port 9096 with a bearer that bypasses NIP-98 entirely — unset expiry means unbounded lifetime, unset scope means every method and path; (5) accepted requests are attributed to the sentinel `BREAK_GLASS_PUBKEY`, so downstream records carry a placeholder identity; on the docker network the same token authorises `POST /tab0/send` (coordinator injection) and `GET /tabs/:n` (pane capture), and `/feed` hands over a snapshot of the last 50 turns.
- Evidence: AB-16.9 C3 — "still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4". AB-16.6 — unset expiry permits unbounded lifetime, unset scope is unrestricted; the sentinel "is NOT a real pubkey, so every downstream attribution for this request is a placeholder"; accepted via `?access_token=` / `?bearer=` on WS upgrades (`proxy.mjs:53,533-567`); ADR-2027's `verified_paths` is empty and `verified_commit` unpinned — "custody claims … attested by reading, not by a gate" (`ADR-2027 …:193-198`). AB-12.3 — "?token= or ?auth= is the only carrier there (`server.mjs:499,506`)" and "on accept the connection handler sends a snapshot of the last 50 turns (`server.mjs:685-687`)". AB-12.7 — the Unmute backend holds the token as `KYUTAI_LLM_API_KEY` (`voice/README.md:93`). AB-12.7 DIVERGENCE — 8444/8443 are `0.0.0.0`-published outside the loopback CI gate (`docker-compose.voice.yml:40-41`).
- Warning signs visible today: the register row itself; the OPEN lifecycle items in AB-16.6; the CI gate covering only 9096 while three other LAN doors stand open.
- Confidence: high that the credential is shared, URL-carried and unbounded by configuration; inferred that a URL/log/voice-container leak is the entry.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Empty allowlist in the JS relay consumer admits every signed author, and the default `bidirectional` fanout adds public relays
- Topics: AB-13.7, AB-13.16
- Evidence: with `ingressPolicy = allowlist`, an empty allowlist ADMITS every signed author (`mcp/nostr-bridge/relay-consumer.js:764-768`) — the inverse of the Rust boundary's deny-all (`admission.rs:35-45`); nothing sets `AGENTBOX_RELAY_ALLOWED_PUBKEYS` (`server.js:1434`); the manifest's `external_fanout = "bidirectional"` (`agentbox.toml:175` → `flake.nix:4515`) adds `wss://relay.damus.io,wss://relay.primal.net` (`relay-consumer.js:247-255`); intent kinds dispatch a command when `AGENTBOX_INTENT_COMMAND` is set (`default-intent-spec.js:71`).
- Failure: a remote, unauthenticated attacker on a public relay publishes a signed kind-38000+ event p-tagging a local npub; it passes I07/I10 into the intent handler and, with that env set, executes a command as the consumer process — landing in the R-1 compartment. Without it, foreign events still reach pod files and intent markers.
- Confidence: high on the code facts; inferred on whether `AGENTBOX_INTENT_COMMAND` is deployed set (the material's own Open says deployment config is not visible in source).
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension + Open)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — The email raw tier is gated only by a bearer and a capability pubkey that both live in the environment of every agent process
- Topics: AB-16.10, AB-16.14
- Evidence: tier 1 is `Bearer <AGENTBOX_EMAIL_GATEWAY_TOKEN>`, tier 2 checks the pubkey against `PRIVILEGED_NOSTR_PUBKEYS` with the operator pubkey read from `AGENTBOX_X_ONLY_PUBKEY_HEX` "at call time, never hardcoded" (`skills/email-search/SKILL.md`); raw tools return verbatim mail. The ROLE env class holds only 11 names (`env-classes.json:35`); neither email name is in it, so both ride DEVUSER_CLASS and "stay in every agent process by exception" (`env-classes.json:94`).
- Failure: any devuser process — or any agent whose prompt is injected — reads both values from its own environment and calls `fetch_email_raw`, receiving unfiltered client mail from the two Proton accounts.
- Confidence: high on the gating facts; inferred on the class assignment of the two names (not individually listed in the material).
- Marked by authors: yes, partially — the Q8 DEVUSER debt is marked (AB-16.14); the composed email path is not.
- Severity: high

### F-03 — Secrets are backed up as an unencrypted ZIP into the host-visible workspace bind, beside a spend-capable treasury key
- Topics: AB-16.8, AB-16.9, AB-16.14
- Evidence: the register's backup path is `scripts/backup-secrets.sh` to `workspace/secret-backups` ZIP + manifest (AB-16.9 C7); "ordinary zip and unzip … NO encryption flags and no explicit umask or chmod" (AB-16.8); the age-encrypted `agentbox-secret-backup` tool exists but requires "explicit operator invocation" (AB-16.12). Separately the sidestr treasury key sits at `/home/devuser/workspace/sidestr/agents/treasury.key` (`agentbox.toml:1964`), with the legacy twin "copied once and never deleted" (`ADR-2122 …:241-244`).
- Failure: any devuser process, or anything with the host bind mounted, reads the full secrets set from the backup archive; the treasury key is already there without needing the backup.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (partial note AB-16.8; Tension AB-16.14)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — The nostr-gateway signs as the ROOT operator key by default
- Topics: AB-16.5, AB-13.5
- Evidence: default identity `'operator'`, `sk = rawSk — the gateway signs AS the operator` (`gateway.cjs:154-155`); the derived-child mode is opt-in via env; fail-closed by exit when keyless (`gateway.cjs:149`).
- Failure: the long-running gateway process holds the root operator secret in memory for its lifetime; any memory disclosure or compromise of that one process is root-identity compromise, and every relay sees the root pubkey as the acting identity, muddying attribution.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — No rotation exists and relay publisher revocation requires a full nix rebuild
- Topics: AB-16.8, AB-13.3, AB-16.5
- Evidence: ADR-2027 is proposed/none/inactive — "No cadence, no custodian, no incident window, no revocation procedure … for any of the seven register rows" (AB-16.8); the relay allowlist is baked at nix build (`relayAllowedPubkeysCsv`, `flake.nix:1893`), so "publisher revocation needs a full rebuild — the compromise window is one build-deploy cycle"; derived HMAC children have "no revocation of the previously derived child at any relay".
- Failure: once any key leaks (see R-1), the operator cannot revoke a publisher without a rebuild cycle, and cannot revoke a derived child at all — the compromise outlives the response.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes
- Severity: high

### F-06 — The identity port hands the live-mirror child secret to devuser, so the phone's key is uid-1000-readable
- Topics: AB-16.5, AB-16 (port grant notes), AB-14.16
- Evidence: the ACL grants devuser `mirror_key` with `secret true` (`config/custody/identity-port-acl.json:42`) and the port returns the child `secret_hex` (`identity_port/port.rs:635-637`); ADR-2101 "calls a generic sign-this-payload port a bypass" and ER-10 records this as its one named export exception; the wrap-only alternative that would have kept it in ab-identity was not built.
- Failure: a uid-1000 compromise reads the child secret and decrypts every gift-wrapped mirror message it can fetch from the public cloud worker relay — compounding R-2.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION + Drift note)
- Severity: high

### F-07 — Session-plane send can fail after submission, and the tmux fallback then re-injects; the picker targets the most active client, not the invoking one
- Topics: AB-12.5, AB-12.15
- Evidence: "send can return failure after submission" and "the picker chooses a client by activity rather than proving invoking-client identity" (AB-12.15); the fallback node "tmux fallback; prior submission may be unknown (`server.mjs:162-168`)" (AB-12.15 graph); picker at `pick.rs:17,24-26`.
- Failure: a submitted keystroke whose ack is lost is re-sent over tmux — duplicated text into a live agent session (an instruction executed twice), or a command delivered to whichever client was most active rather than the one addressed.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism; medium that it fires in practice (depends on socket failure timing).
- Marked by authors: no — stated in the lifecycle-audit paragraph and a FAIL-OPEN note, not flagged as Tension/Debt/Drift/Open.
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — Attribution is forgeable or fabricated in three distinct ways
- Topics: AB-14.14, AB-17.2, AB-17.10
- Evidence: a same-container process passes the loopback test "and can stamp any pubkey into the journalled `agent_did`" (AB-14.14 Debt); the proxy-stamped `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` can be forged over loopback (AB-17.2 Debt); the env fallback "stamps the CONTAINER identity on the event" for task-token and policy-off requests (`agent-event-publisher.js:146-151`), and kg-elevation can attribute to the operator identity from env (`kg-elevation.js:162`).
- Failure: after any incident, the governance journal and agent-event archive cannot be relied on to say who acted — either forged (in-container) or defaulted (container/operator identity).
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt ×2, Tension, DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — Voice ports 8443/8444 are published 0.0.0.0 outside the loopback CI gate, and /feed hands 50 turns to any token holder
- Topics: AB-12.7, AB-12.3
- Evidence: "port 8444 and port 8443 are published 0.0.0.0 by `docker-compose.voice.yml:40-41`, while only port 9096 is the … sanctioned … LAN door covered by the loopback CI gate"; `/feed` accepts `?token=`/`?auth=` and "sends a snapshot of the last 50 turns (`server.mjs:685-687`)".
- Failure: the voice cockpit surface is exposed to the LAN with no CI-enforced loopback discipline; whoever holds the shared token (R-3) reads recent coordinator turns.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: high

### F-10 — Dream forum decisions are applied without a local authority check, unbound decisions are accepted, and a reopened duplicate can retarget deletion
- Topics: AB-23.17
- Evidence: "Signature validity and rejection of this agent's key do not establish a human decision"; "No local admin or delegated-role lookup (`governance.rs:857-862`)"; the binding check accepts "Request e tag absent" (`governance.rs:879-883`); `mark_published` selects the FIRST item with matching ID, so "a reopened duplicate can update its closed predecessor" and an "old record can then target the new request for deletion" (`inbox.rs:114-130`, `governance.rs:587-601,620-633`).
- Failure: a foreign or forged-but-validly-signed 31403 with a matching d-tag resolves local items or withdraws requests; relay admission policy is the only authority.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no — stated under "Authority"/"Delivery", not flagged with the four markers.
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — Malformed zone metadata selects a plaintext digest plan even with the encryption gate on
- Topics: AB-23.24
- Evidence: "Malformed or absent zones become empty; unknown section can select first configured zone (`zone_crypto.rs:86-105`)"; "Malformed metadata produces plaintext selection even with the gate on"; the no-key refusal applies only after zone policy identifies an encrypted zone.
- Failure: metadata drift (a typo in a section's zone tag) silently publishes a digest the operator believed encrypted.
- Confidence: high on the selection logic; inferred that no live plaintext publish occurred (the probe did not).
- Marked by authors: no (stated under "Privacy")
- Severity: high

### F-12 — Execution-journal integrity: sequence reuse after restart, concurrent duplicate seq, payload-blind dedup
- Topics: AB-23.23, AB-14.7
- Evidence: "Concurrent appends can share seq; restart needs hydration (`execution-journal.js:152-185`)"; "Duplicate ID returns prior envelope, without payload comparison (`exec-record.js:52-54`)"; journal delivery is Phase-1 fail-open, not an approval gate (`journal.rs:11-17`).
- Failure: the journal that ADR-2071 puts around the dream night can silently conflate two different effects under one id, or reuse seq numbers after a restart — an audit record that misstates what happened.
- Confidence: high (reproduced by the journal audit's route probes)
- Marked by authors: no (stated under "Evidence")
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — A manifest-disabled skill's instructions still bake, so frontmatter self-trigger remains reachable
- Topics: AB-22.1, AB-22.4
- Evidence: "instructional file still bakes with the skills tree, only the executable package/supervised process is omitted" (AB-22.1 DIVERGENCE) — and discovery is "skills self-trigger from description frontmatter (ADR-2021)". Also the count authority says "131 active" while the generated table says "126 active, 5 deprecated redirects" (`routing-table.md:12`).
- Failure: a capability believed gated off still injects its instructions into turns; an operator reading the directory believes a retired skill is active.
- Confidence: medium — the harness's behaviour when the executable is missing but SKILL.md bakes is not drawn; the bake itself is stated.
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, TENSION)
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — Spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile, and the payment_settlement authority class never reaches the gate
- Topics: AB-15.6, AB-14.11
- Evidence: "spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile: the flock-guarded cost_cap ledger (`cost_cap/ledger.rs:119,136`), the pod-held sats balance (`routes/payments.js:205`), and the marketplace token_budget (`llm-marketplace.js:85`). No surface sums them"; the classification table names `payment_settlement` zero-tolerance (`agentbox.toml:1429`) but "no production path passes that actionClass … routes/payments.js never reaches management-api/lib/authority.js:226".
- Failure: real money moves with no single number answering what the container has spent and no governance gate on settlement — a custody failure that surfaces only on reconciliation with a counterparty.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT ×2)
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — Dream PR publication is bound to a branch name, not the evaluated commit, and success is inferred from Option presence
- Topics: AB-23.6
- Evidence: "the PR request carries a branch name, not the verified commit hash (`seams.rs:341`)"; "a successful create command returns Some even for empty stdout (`seams.rs:531`)"; the adapter then "selects the acceptance notice by Option presence"; the tested boundary does not validate the URL or bind the pushed head to the evaluated tree.
- Failure: a race between evaluation and push publishes content that was never judged, and the ledger records an Accepted notice for it.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism; medium that the race fires in production.
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: medium

---

Two cross-cutting observations the engineers verifying this will need, both already visible in the material and therefore not counted as findings: the corpus itself disagrees with its sources in places the authors flagged (e.g. AB-16.11 vs AB-13.9 on mirror redaction, folded into R-2), and every "resolved/staged until a rebuild" note means the running image's behaviour at incident time is a separate fact from the pinned source — the ADR-2141/ADR-2081 pair shows the corpus tracks that distinction, and any verification should too.

## agentbox#3 / critical

Only the agentbox topics (AB-24 through AB-40) were provided; findings below are limited to that material.

### F-01 — Every secret in the container is readable by every process, including agent tools, in the shipped configuration
- Topics: AB-36, AB-36.3, AB-36.6, AB-34.3, AB-34.6
- Evidence: ADR-2122 is `proposed`/`partial`/`inactive` and `[security].role_isolation` ships **false** (`agentbox.toml:2543`); with the flag off every program runs as devuser and reads keys at their at-rest paths (`run-producer.sh:70-71`, `run-faucet.sh:27`). 36 names stay ambient in devuser's environment by exception Q8, including `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`, `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, and "the ssh-agent socket path that grants use of every key the agent holds" (`env-classes.json:92, 193-209`). The faucet treasury key's legacy workspace twin "stays on the host-visible bind in both modes until a manual purge" (ADR-2122:241-244).
- Failure: one compromised or mistaken MCP tool running as devuser reads the sovereign Nostr key, the forum house key, the chain signing keys and the management API key — impersonation, unsigned-by-owner forum posts, and full management-API control in a single step.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, Debt and Tension markers across AB-36.6/.7 and AB-34.3)

### F-02 — Role isolation fails OPEN: a flag-on boot on an image missing the artefacts silently reverts to the insecure shape
- Topics: AB-36.2, AB-36.7
- Evidence: "An image that lacks either artefact boots as if the flag were off, and says so loudly" (`role-custody.sh:59`, `entrypoint-unified.sh:328` emits `ROLE-ISOLATION-UNAVAILABLE` then proceeds). ADR-2122 §2 specified the flag as **boot-class** (flip and roll back with a restart) while the catalogue records it rebuild-class precisely because the devuser sudo route is baked (`system-manifest.js:97-98`); the shipped off-build image keeps devuser in `wheel` with a `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in (`flake.nix:4047`, `bake-devuser-privilege.sh:44-47`).
- Failure: the owner follows the ADR's boot-class wording, sets `role_isolation = true` in the manifest and restarts without rebuilding; the old image lacks `/etc/supervisord.roles.conf`, boots today's shape, and now runs with the operator believing isolation is on while all secrets are ambient and the sudo drop-in is present. The only signal is a boot-log marker.
- Confidence: high (fail-open behavior is stated; the restart-without-rebuild scenario is inferred from the boot-class/rebuild-class contradiction)
- Severity: high
