- Marked by authors: no — the fail-open is presented as design ("says so loudly"), and the boot-class/rebuild-class contradiction is stated but not flagged as a trap

### F-03 — The host Docker socket stays world-accessible; the container can only detect and record the degradation
- Topics: AB-36.7 (Open R2)
- Evidence: the socket is a bind of a host inode earlier boots widened; skipping the chmod under the flag does not narrow it (`docker-compose.override.yml:115-116`); the flag-on boot can only record `degraded:docker-socket` (`entrypoint-unified.sh:624`), and the host rehearsal half records the mode "for the owner without gating on it" (`role-isolation-rehearsal.host.sh:18-19`) while ADR-2122 counts the degradation as a failure (ADR-2122:171).
- Failure: with the flag off (as shipped) devuser reaches the Docker socket and is effectively root on the host — any agent tool can mount the host filesystem or start a privileged container, bypassing every boundary in AB-36. Even flag-on, the boundary stays open until someone chmods on the host.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, R2)

### F-04 — A baked `NOPASSWD: ALL` sudo grant for devuser whose safety rests on two revertible guards
- Topics: AB-36.7 (Tension)
- Evidence: built with `role_isolation` off — how it ships — the image keeps devuser in `wheel` (`flake.nix:4047`) and bakes a `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in (`bake-devuser-privilege.sh:44-47`, called at `flake.nix:4061`), while the same flake states the setuid wrapper is gone and there is no runtime escalation path (`flake.nix:3814-3818`). The grant is inert only because `no-new-privileges` is set (`docker-compose.yml:129`) and no setuid sudo ships; "reverting either guard on a flag-off build turns devuser back into root, past every role boundary in this topic".
- Failure: a compose override, host flag change, or image variant that drops `no-new-privileges` or ships a setuid sudo silently converts devuser — and therefore any agent tool — into root inside the container.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-05 — The forum-decision signature-verification repair is merged but deployment is unverified, and the running container may still act on unverifiable decisions
- Topics: AB-40
- Evidence: the repair (verifier exception → reject before side effects, `relay-consumer.js:749`, `:450`, `:549`) is recorded at commit `0ec2956…`, distinct from the `c57d9c0…` pin of every other topic; "It is merged source, awaiting deployment; it does not certify a running container", and "consolidating rosters and ingress remains open" — the independent authority consumer already rejects (`authority-consumer.js:159`) but the relay path did not.
- Failure: if the deployed container predates the repair, a Nostr kind-31403 forum decision whose signature cannot be verified (verifier dependency failure, malformed event) passes into "record event, notify waiter and dispatch orchestrator" (`relay-consumer.js:479, 487`) — a forged or corrupt governance decision drives `applyOntologyDecision` and corpus writes.
- Confidence: medium (the pre-repair failure mode and the deployment state are both inferred from the material's own wording)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — deployment and consolidation)

### F-06 — The identity port authorises an account, not a workload, and checks kind but not catalogue semantics; receipts are not crash-durable
- Topics: AB-36.4, AB-36.5, AB-37.2
- Evidence: the cited mesh audit qualifies the boundary — "UID identifies an account, ordinary event signing checks kind rather than catalogue semantics, and appended receipts are not a crash-durable protocol inbox" (AB-36.4). A synthetic grant for kind 30078 "signs an unrelated namespace with no revision" while the checked-in grants merely omit that kind (`port.rs:534-536`, `port.rs:559-564`, `identity-port-acl.json:39-40`); caller tags, content and timestamp are passed through untouched.
- Failure: under flag-on, any process running as an authorised uid — devuser 1000, where all agents run — can sign arbitrary content within granted kinds (colloquy units 38410-38415, house-account forum posts) and a future kind-grant silently becomes a content-namespace bypass; a crash loses the receipt trail that is the only record of what was signed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — carried by the cited 2026-11-01 mesh audit, not by a Tension/Debt/Drift marker

### F-07 — A pathspec-limited governed commit does not constrain the push: unrelated commits can ride along to the corpus branch
- Topics: AB-25.12 (Tension)
- Evidence: `commitPage` counts commits ahead and pushes only when the governed commit is the sole one (`management-api/lib/ontology-apply.js:868-879`), "but a second writer between that count and the push still rides along; this was reproduced with real local Git".
- Failure: during a governed ontology decision, a concurrent writer (another session, a cron job) commits to the branch between the count and the push; the push publishes both, and ungoverned content lands on the canonical corpus branch and flows into the next Loom generation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, 2026-10-31, reproduced)

### F-08 — Scaffold-on-by-default serves ontology prose about the wrong subject as a confident answer
- Topics: AB-24.11, AB-24.12
- Evidence: ADR-139's recorded incident — "a packet about a test script scored the blockchain class Node at 42 and was served from the corpus in 40 ms with zero completion tokens" (ADR-139:13-16); "absence of the key, or any value other than the boolean false, leaves the scaffold ON" (ADR-139:31). Every existing consumer had to learn to opt out per request.
- Failure: any new consumer of the facade that does not know about `loom_options.scaffold=false` gets corpus-grounded answers for out-of-corpus subjects — fast, cheap, confidently wrong, with `corpus_backed true`.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, AB-24.11)

### F-09 — The Loom health signal can be green while the service serves nothing or black-holes every synthesis
- Topics: AB-24.6, AB-24.7, AB-24.8
- Evidence: the empty-floor trap — "The facade still starts and /health still returns 200, but the log reads 'lexical index NOT loaded … empty floor'" (`loom/README.md:73-76`); and "a stale model-backend route black-holes every synthesis while /health still answers" (AB-24.7 DIVERGENCE note) — while AB-24.6 separately claims `/health` reports "backend/graph/index readiness", an internal inconsistency about what health actually covers.
- Failure: after a bad deploy or staging error, monitoring stays green; condense, retrieval and every loom night silently produce nothing (or empty completions) until a human reads logs.
- Confidence: high on the empty floor; medium on the backend black-hole (the material's two health claims conflict)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE note AB-24.7; the trap is named in AB-24.8)

### F-10 — The entire ontology-grounding tier hangs off one LAN host and one rail, with no chat failover
- Topics: AB-24.1, AB-24.6, AB-24.2
- Evidence: every consumer — retrieval brain, condense, dream loom nights, the loom-raw session seed — holds the single door on machinelearn `.132:8084`, which DNATs to the model "over the 25G rail 10.10.10.0/30" (AB-24.1); the only other deployment, the sidecar, is retrieval-only — "DISTILL_BACKEND_URL blank = retrieval-only, /v1 returns 503" — so the email reasoner's chat path has no second model. AB-24.2 states a configured-but-unreachable Loom is an operational fault, not a fallback condition.
- Failure: `.132` or the rail drops; all ontology retrieval, condensation and loom sessions fail simultaneously with no degradation path, and the email gateway's reasoning returns 503s.
- Confidence: medium (single-host topology is stated; the blast-radius inference is mine)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — the topology is drawn without any redundancy or failure note

### F-11 — The governing identity document and the code disagree on how the spend key is produced: recovery by the book produces the wrong key
- Topics: AB-31.7, AB-31.8, AB-34.4
- Evidence: INGRESS-identity still specifies `k_spend = derive_subkey(k_id, "sidestr/spend/" ‖ chain_id)` and says the binding carries the derived pubkey (`INGRESS-identity.md:507, 520`), while the registry and `sidestr-spend-key.js:7-12` mint an independent key "NEVER derived from k_id", and ADR-2101's D3/D4 (which the module cites) still records `implementation_status: none`.
- Failure: an operator performing key recovery or a custody ceremony following the governing identity document derives a key that is not the deployed one — the binding names a pubkey the recovered ceremony cannot reproduce, and at activation this is a custody-loss path for whatever the chain then holds.
- Confidence: high (the contradiction is explicit)
- Severity: medium (testnet-only value today; high the day the chain is not)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, AB-31.7/.8)

### F-12 — The genesis commits nothing about parent or containment, so the chain's custody facts live only in a swappable document
- Topics: AB-32.2, AB-32.3
- Evidence: upstream commits only chain id, pegs, genesisTime and the signer's witness; parent, cashOut, currencyPin and every containment field are NOT committed, the `pin:` record is unbuilt (ADR-2103:207-211), and D3's claim that they are "bound on-seal" is false (ADR-2103:208 amendment). The CI test checks the document's `containmentDigest` against the document's own containment — self-consistency, not on-chain binding (`sidechain-genesis.test.sh:91-93`).
- Failure: a modified `chain.json` with a different `cashOut`/`currencyPin`/parent and a recomputed digest still matches the same `genesisHash`; anything that trusts the document for custody facts (including the mirror, whose trust rule checks the signer field of that same document) accepts changed facts about the same chain.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension and Open, AB-32.2)

### F-13 — A TUI save silently resets hand-tuned model-routing keys to fixed literals, including the cost ceiling
- Topics: AB-28.10
- Evidence: `tui_sections.rs:193-200` emits `provider "openrouter"`, `quality_bar 0.50`, `cost_ceiling_usd_per_mtok 0`, `privacy_tier "public"`, `trajectory true` as fixed literals; "a TUI save therefore RESETS provider/quality_bar/cost_ceiling/trajectory/assets_dir to these literals even if the operator hand-edited agentbox.toml" — unlike the ADR-2031 read-back-and-preserve path for consultant models.
- Failure: an operator tunes the cost ceiling or provider in the manifest, later saves any unrelated change through the TUI, and routing silently reverts to the literals — including a cost ceiling of 0 against a paid external provider.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, in the AB-28.10 note)

### F-14 — Session-plane delivery can submit the same instruction twice, and restart checks treat a Git error as a clean worktree
- Topics: AB-39.3, AB-39.1, AB-39.2
- Evidence: the bridge "catches send error then uses tmux fallback" which "may submit again" (`server.mjs:157, 162, 168`), while the API's `ok:false` can arrive *after* `tmux` already submitted the text (`plane.rs:411` → `api.rs:106`) and a client timeout leaves completion unknown (`client.cjs:4, 9`); separately, "Git error becomes zero changes" (`plane.rs:325`) so a failing Git inspection makes a dirty worktree pass the clean check before the old pane is killed (`reconcile.rs:82-88`), and send checks fewer blockers than status reports (`plane.rs:405-408` vs `status.rs:57-58`).
- Failure: a transient hook-write failure after successful delivery causes the bridge to re-submit — the agent executes the same instruction twice (duplicate side effects, duplicate spend); a corrupt Git index lets the reconciler kill and restart a pane whose worktree state it never actually inspected; a session shown as blocked in status still accepts input.
- Confidence: medium-high (delivery-then-failure is probe-proven; the double submission is a stated source-supported consequence, not a live transmission)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — the audit narrative marks the delivery ID as REQUIRED and names the counterexample, but none of the authors' Tension/Debt/Drift markers cover these

### F-15 — The typed-spawn tools are wired to an in-memory database by default: spawn_ready sees nothing and spawn_complete cannot find the bead
- Topics: AB-26.7, AB-26.6
- Evidence: `mcp.json:224` passes `AGENTBOX_BEADS_DB` through with an empty default, `typed-spawn.js:31-35` maps empty to `:memory:`, and each tool call opens a fresh connection — so "with the shipped default spawn_ready sees no children and spawn_complete cannot find the bead spawn_child made", contradicting the handler comment claiming a "persistent beads.db" (`substrate-tools.js:167`). Source tracing only; no runtime call was made.
- Failure: an operator builds a work-DAG through `spawn_child`; every later `spawn_ready` returns an empty list and `spawn_complete` errors NotFound — typed spawn appears to work (children are created) while the coordination surface is silently non-functional unless someone sets the env var.
- Confidence: high (the authors traced the exact code path)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, AB-26.7)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Which commit the production containers actually run** — AB-40 is pinned at `0ec2956…` while every other topic is `c57d9c0…`; whether the relay-consumer signature repair (F-05) is deployed cannot be determined from the distillation.
2. **Whether prompt/code egress is enforced at runtime or only recorded** — the egress register (AB-24.14) is a rendered compliance artifact with stale citations; nothing shown proves a runtime enforcement point reads `egress-policy.json`.
3. **Backup and restore coverage for the role, chain and identity keys** — the secret-backup service is listed as a source but never drawn (AB-30 debt), and AB-37.3 shows only that a *proposed* key name is omitted from its allowlist; the actual allowlist contents and any tested restore are not shown.
4. **Behaviour with two of anything** — two session-plane controllers, concurrent API and reconcile loops, concurrent beads actors, or concurrent Loom facade consumers: the material describes single-instance behaviour, and AB-39.4 shows controller and API lifetimes already diverging with one of each.
5. **Whether the staged-not-rebuilt Jev/System One projections are now live**, and whether the env-secret-inventory gate passes at the current pin — the last recorded pass (715 names, at `6500582`) predates a table the material itself counts at 745 names, and the gate "was not re-run for this review" (AB-36.6).

## agentbox#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — agentbox estate incident review

Scope note: no incident record exists in this material; what follows are the three failure chains this material most supports, then latent defects found while tracing them. Every claim below is tied to the citations the material gives; where I reason past the material I say so. All findings require code-level verification before action.

---

### R-1 — One compromised agent tool becomes the whole estate: shared devuser, ambient secrets, and a signing port that authorises by uid alone
- Topics: AB-36.3, AB-36.4, AB-36.5, AB-36.6, AB-36.7, AB-34.3, AB-34.6, AB-37.2
- Chain of events: an agent session runs as devuser (uid 1000). A tool it loads — an MCP server, an npm dependency, a skill script — executes in that uid. With `[security].role_isolation = false` (how it ships), nothing constrains it: PID 1's environment, including `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`, `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, the System One vendor keys and the ssh-agent socket, reaches every child via supervisord; the sidestr signer and faucet treasury keys are read from devuser-readable workspace paths; and the identity port, authorising purely on `SO_PEERCRED` uid, will for uid 1000 sign `forum_event` kinds 1, 42, 31923 as JunkieJarvis and hand over the live-mirror child secret (`mirror_key`, secret true). The port passes caller tags, content and timestamp through unchecked. Result: forged house-account messages, an exported signing key, management-API use, and chain-key theft — all before any boundary in ADR-2122 exists, because the flag is off and the only committed rehearsal receipt is STAGED with the flag false.
- Evidence: role_isolation ships false (`agentbox/agentbox.toml:2543`); with it off every chain program runs as devuser (`agentbox/flake.nix:3129`, `agentbox/flake.nix:3164`) and the faucet reads the workspace key (`config/sidechain/run-faucet.sh:27`, `agentbox.toml:1964`); the legacy workspace twins of chain keys are "never deleted and stay devuser-readable by design until a manual purge" (`docs/adr/ADR-2122...md:241-244`); 36 DEVUSER_CLASS names including `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`, `BRIDGE_TOKEN` and the ssh-agent socket stay ambient in both modes (`config/custody/env-classes.json:92`, `:94`, `:193`, `:201`, `:209`); the ACL grants devuser `forum_event` on junkiejarvis and `mirror_key` with secret true (`config/custody/identity-port-acl.json:34-43`, `:42`); the port passes content/tags/timestamp through (`services/nostr-pod-bridge/src/identity_port/port.rs:559-564` via AB-37.2) and the mesh audit qualifies that "UID identifies an account, ordinary event signing checks kind rather than catalogue semantics" (`estate-review/2026-11-01-mesh-signer-and-custody.md` via AB-36.4); ADR-2122 is `proposed`/`partial`/`inactive`; the only receipt is STAGED, flag false (`docs/estate-closeout/x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json:4`, `:8`).
- Warning signs visible today: the custody programme's own record says "until this change every secret in the container reached every process" and the change is off; the audit's three qualifications of the port boundary; W3b consumer cutover owed; the host Docker socket open (`docker-compose.override.yml:115-116`, degraded:docker-socket counted as failure in the ADR).
- Confidence: high — every precondition is stated in the material; the initial tool compromise itself is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Client corpus and session content leave to vendors through paths the egress register cannot see
- Topics: AB-24.14, AB-25.8, AB-26.1, AB-23 (as referenced from AB-24.9/AB-29)
- Chain of events: the dream engine's default night provider is Z.AI, a vendor, and the egress register itself records that row as `no-accepted-record` — "nothing accepts the corpus leaving". Independently, the Jev evaluators for ontology retrieval, ontology proposals and headroom evidence default to cloud backends (the shared proxy / TypeSafe), because `[system_one].local_only` is empty and the projector exports `*_LOCAL_ONLY=0` for every in-container consumer; the detached ontology-monitor child makes a Z.AI review call over the session's gathered concept-bearing work. The register that should surface all of this is stamped at an old commit (`0919dc39a`), its loom and zai rows cite manifest/flake lines that have since drifted to unrelated content, and `render-egress-register.js --check` only verifies a cited line falls inside the file — so the compliance surface renders green while pointing at nothing. Client material in the corpus or in sessions transits to external vendors with no accepted record and no detectable drift.
- Evidence: zai-night row "the dream engine's DEFAULT provider is Z.AI, a vendor; marker no-accepted-record" (`config/egress-policy.json:346-361`); night provider defaults to zai (`agentbox.toml:2622`, `services/dream-engine/src/config.rs:184`); local-only only when listed in `[system_one].local_only`, empty, so the proxy and TypeSafe are allowed (`services/agentbox-manifest/src/system_one.rs:580-593`; `mcp/servers/lib/ontology-jev.js:147`; `management-api/lib/headroom-evidence.js:27-29,116`; `config/hooks/lib/ontology-proposal-jev.cjs:152-153`); the monitor's child sends "concept-bearing work" to Z.AI (`config/hooks/ontology-monitor.cjs` sequence, AB-25.8); register stamp `0919dc39a` (`config/egress-policy.json:51`) with stale cites at `:231-232`, `:246`, `:355` and bounds-only checking (`scripts/ci/render-egress-register.js:110-119`).
- Warning signs visible today: a compliance register that is already partly re-pointed but whose `verified_commit` was not; the renderer's check structurally unable to catch the shift; the ZAI key held in devuser's ambient environment by the Q8 exception (`config/custody/env-classes.json:225-228`).
- Confidence: medium — the standing exposure is stated outright; that it becomes the incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Governed write pushes more than the governed commit: the canonical-corpus integrity seam
- Topics: AB-25.12, AB-25 (preamble), AB-04.13 (referenced)
- Chain of events: a signed kind-31403 forum decision triggers `applyOntologyDecision` → guarded `ontomark edit` → `commitPage`. `commitPage` makes a pathspec-limited commit, counts commits ahead of upstream, and pushes only when the governed commit is the sole one ahead. Between that count and the push, a second writer lands a commit on the same branch — the push carries both. The unreviewed commit reaches the canonical vault; downstream, the vault build, the Loom generation identity and every grounded answer treat the corpus as governed content. The race was reproduced with real local Git; nothing in the material shows it closed.
- Evidence: "a second writer between that count and the push still rides along; this was reproduced with real local Git" (`management-api/lib/ontology-apply.js:868-879`, `estate-review/2026-10-31-corpus-consumer.md#commit-and-attestation-boundaries`, AB-25.12 Tension); commit failure is recorded, never thrown (`ontology-apply.js:872`); the governed write door runs `ontomark` verbs with `vault propose` only for governance transport (`management-api/lib/ontology-apply.js:153-160`).
- Warning signs visible today: the reproduction itself; multiple concurrent writers exist in the estate (condense scheduler, session-plane worktrees, reconciliation) against one vault repository; ADR-2140's boot reconcile builds from HEAD, so whatever lands is what the door serves next.
- Confidence: high — reproduced in the material's own evidence.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — A failed busy-hook write still submitted the text; the bridge's fallback can submit it again
- Topics: AB-39.3
- Evidence: tmux submits text (`plane.rs:411`), then the hook write happens (`plane.rs:414-415`); a hook-write failure returns ok false (`api.rs:106`) *after* submission, and the client promise rejects on error reply or timeout (`client.cjs:9,18`, timeout leaves completion unknown `client.cjs:4`); the bridge catches the send error and falls back to tmux (`server.mjs:157,162,168`), which "may submit again" — "source-supported consequence, not a live transmission performed by the audit".
- Failure: a retry after an error reply re-enters the same instruction; for an irreversible command (a spend, a delete, a publish) the agent executes it twice with no delivery ID or staged receipt to deduplicate.
- Confidence: high (source-supported; not live-transmitted by the audit).
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-02 — secret-backup's fixed filename allowlist silently omits any key it does not already know
- Topics: AB-37.3, AB-36.1
- Evidence: backup scans a configured root (`services/secret-backup/src/main.rs:154,156`) against a fixed allowlist (`:58,66,178`); "other basenames are skipped" (`:179`); the audit shows synthetic federation-key filenames omitted even inside the scan root; the proposed node key "needs explicit inclusion, recovery-state inventory and an off-host restore rehearsal".
- Failure: a newly added role key (a second chain's signer, a mesh node key) is never backed up; a host loss makes it permanently unrecoverable — silent, because skipping produces no error.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-03 — The Loom door serves a stale generation by design while reconciliation fail-opens
- Topics: AB-24.13, AB-24.15, AB-24.3
- Evidence: a generation past `stale_after` is "STILL SERVED, honestly labelled — staleness is carried, never enforced" (`loom-domain/src/model.rs:459-462`, ADR-141 D2); reconcile failures (unreachable, deferred, refused, failed) are recorded and exit 0 (`scripts/hp-loom-reconcile.sh:15-17`), boot Phase 5d is background, timeout 1800, fail-open (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:1219-1229`); observed live: the door served `visionGraph@ae913f93…` while `015ca2c1f` was built, promoted and verified, because the baked `vault` 0.1.0 could not produce a promotable bundle until an image rebuild (`loom/docs/design/ADR-141...md:141-150`).
- Failure: production answers grounded on month-old corpus while every health signal is green; clients act on superseded knowledge.
- Confidence: high (an instance is recorded).
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, Drift)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — The signature-rejection repair is merged but deployment and roster consolidation are unverified
- Topics: AB-40
- Evidence: "It is merged source, awaiting deployment; it does not certify a running container"; "ABI-05 consumer/roster consolidation and deployment remain unverified"; the independent authority consumer already rejects verification exceptions (`management-api/lib/authority-consumer.js:159`), implying the two paths hold separate acceptance logic.
- Failure: a deployed container predating the merge dispatches a forum decision whose signature check threw; or a signer accepted by one path's roster is rejected — or accepted — by the other's during migration.
- Confidence: medium (code state stated; deployed-image state unknown).
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — The setup wizard binds unauthenticated to a non-loopback address with only a warning
- Topics: AB-30.2
- Evidence: non-loopback bind prints "red warning: unauthenticated, can rewrite config and use the mgmt key" and continues (`setup/server/src/main.rs:275-276`); the proxy loads the management key and forwards (`main.rs:193-211`); a fixed or non-loopback bind is an explicit `AGENTBOX_SETUP_BIND` opt-in (`main.rs:268-269`).
- Failure: an operator points the first-run wizard at the LAN during provisioning; anything on the network rewrites `agentbox.toml` (gates, `loom_url`, consultants) and issues management-API calls with the key.
- Confidence: high (behaviour stated; the operator choice is inferred).
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-06 — devuser→root is guarded only by the absence of setuid sudo and `no-new-privileges`
- Topics: AB-36.7, AB-36.2
- Evidence: a flag-off build keeps devuser in `wheel` (`flake.nix:4047`) with a `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in (`config/bake-devuser-privilege.sh:44-47`); the same flake asserts there is no runtime escalation path (`flake.nix:3814-3818`); the grant is inert only because `no-new-privileges` is set (`docker-compose.yml:129`) and no setuid sudo ships; "reverting either guard on a flag-off build turns devuser back into root, past every role boundary"; check (f) probes `sudo -n true` (`scripts/activation/role-isolation-rehearsal.sh:593-594`).
- Failure: a compose hardening change or image variant that drops `no-new-privileges` or ships setuid sudo silently restores passwordless root for every agent in the box.
- Confidence: high (guard structure stated; the trigger is inferred).
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)
- Severity: high

### F-07 — The port can be configured to let an agent key sign governance kinds
- Topics: AB-36.5
- Evidence: `AGENT_GOVERNANCE_KINDS 31400 panel and 31402 case` are grantable on `forum_event` to a key declared `agent_governance` (`identity_port/acl.rs:84`, `:209-210`, `:294-303`); not live today — the checked-in ACL declares only `core` and `junkiejarvis` (`identity-port-acl.json:17-30`); the mesh audit shows sign_event checks kind, not catalogue semantics.
- Failure: once an agent-governance key is declared, a compromised agent signs panel/case events that read to forum humans as legitimate proposals; nothing in the port validates content semantics.
- Confidence: high in the mechanism; the activation is future configuration.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-08 — `SIDESTR_ALLOW_UNPINNED=1` reopens agent-writable consensus code for an unbounded time
- Topics: AB-34.1
- Evidence: the override runs the workspace checkout at any commit (`run-producer.sh:85`), warns but never refuses (`:86-93`), and "nothing bounds how long it stays set" — while the workspace is devuser-writable and agents run as devuser; the default bake exists precisely because the old exposure let a dirty working tree pass the pin check (`run-producer.sh:9-16`).
- Failure: an operator sets the override for one run and leaves it; an agent session edits the checked-out spec; the chain produces and signs blocks under altered consensus rules.
- Confidence: medium (requires the operator override; the exposure window is stated as DEBT).
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-09 — A TUI save silently resets the operator's model-routing configuration to literals
- Topics: AB-28.10, AB-29.8
- Evidence: only `enabled` round-trips through TUI state; every other key is emitted as a fixed literal — provider `"openrouter"`, `quality_bar 0.50`, `cost_ceiling_usd_per_mtok 0`, `trajectory`, `assets_dir` (`tui_sections.rs:193-200`); "there is no read-back-and-preserve path for the non-enabled model-router keys yet", unlike the ADR-2031 consultant-model preserve.
- Failure: an operator's tightened cost ceiling or non-OpenRouter provider is silently reverted on the next settings save; work reroutes to an external provider with the cost control reset.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, as DOC-DRIFT)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — The spawn tools ship with a `:memory:` beads database, so the work-DAG is silently non-persistent
- Topics: AB-26.7, AB-26.6
- Evidence: `mcp.json:224` defaults `AGENTBOX_BEADS_DB` to empty; `typed-spawn.js:31-35` maps empty to `:memory:` before the adapter's workspace default applies; each tool call opens a fresh connection; the handler comment claims a persistent db (`substrate-tools.js:167`) — "source tracing only; no runtime call was made".
- Failure: `spawn_ready` returns nothing, `spawn_complete` cannot find the bead `spawn_child` made; duplicate work is spawned, ownership and blocker edges vanish, and the typed-spawn contract appears functional while persisting nothing.
- Confidence: high (source-traced).
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — A Git inspection failure is treated as "zero changes" in the restart dirty-check
- Topics: AB-39.1
- Evidence: "Inspect desired plan.dir — Git error becomes zero changes" (`plane.rs:325`); a clean result proceeds to kill the old pane and start a replacement (`plane.rs:268-277`); the audit's counterexample shows a prefix-only move refused by Git *before* the kill.
- Failure: a corrupted or locked worktree reads as clean; reconciliation kills a running agent whose worktree holds uncommitted work, with no hold raised.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — Cancelling one ComfyUI job can kill another client's job
- Topics: AB-38.3
- Evidence: the client posts an interrupt with `prompt_id` (`comfy_client.py:325`) but the pinned upstream handler checks a running snapshot before signalling; the audit reproduces "owned A finishes; B starts — upstream global interrupt can reach B"; request acknowledgement is not terminal history (`comfy_client.py:309,366`); the fix (atomic per-job cancellation) is marked REQUIRED.
- Failure: an operator cancels a long render; a job that started in the window is interrupted with no record tying the cancellation to it; lost render time and wrongly-blamed workflows.
- Confidence: high (reproduced controlled, without a live queue).
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — The upstream-pins bump rule is already violated, and the acceptance is baked
- Topics: AB-34.1
- Evidence: the rule is to bump a line only after the chain has restarted and produced a block on that commit (`config/sidechain/upstream-pins:5-7`); `blaketestnode` was bumped 2026-10-01 as an "operator-accepted active development head" — "acceptance rather than a produced block" (`upstream-pins:10`, commit `47e187934`); the bake carries that commit (`lib/sidestr-upstream.nix:58-62`).
- Failure: consensus-adjacent upstream code enters the image on operator acceptance alone; the precedent erodes the only control that keeps the running chain's code a repository fact.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — Identity-port receipts are append-only and not crash-durable
- Topics: AB-36.4, AB-37.2
- Evidence: receipts are one JSONL line per decision written to a 0640 file (`identity_port/receipts.rs:124,133`); an admitted request whose receipt cannot be written is withheld (`port.rs:324`) — but the mesh audit qualifies "appended receipts are not a crash-durable protocol inbox".
- Failure: a crash between signing and receipt flush leaves a valid house-account or governance signature in the wild with no audit record; an incident investigation cannot establish what the port signed.
- Confidence: high (audit qualification stated).
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — The model-delegation seam fails silently: a dead backend route with green health, and requests pinned to an empty model name
- Topics: AB-24.6, AB-24.7
- Evidence: "HP's old 192.168.2.48 is DEAD — a stale model-backend route black-holes every synthesis while /health still answers" (AB-24.7 divergence note); independently, the supervisor exports `LOOM_MODEL` empty (`flake.nix:2902`), `llm_config` passes it through (`estate.rs:106`), so a loom night "sends an empty model name and depends on the Loom answering an empty name with its loaded model" — observed working once on 2026-10-02, with the serde default applying only when the key is absent (`config.rs:171`).
- Failure: a backend swap or facade update that retires the old route or starts rejecting empty model names makes every nightly synthesis fail while health monitoring stays green.
- Confidence: high (both facts stated); the future break is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, as DIVERGENCE)
- Severity: medium

---

Not included because the material does not support them as incident-shaped, but flagged for the verifying engineer: the https-bridge `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` + credentials combination (AB-30.4 — browsers reject it, so it is a functional bug, not an exposure); the headroom MCP tools being registered nowhere, which leaves CCR sentinels in compressed memory results with no agent-facing retrieval path (AB-26.3, marked Debt); and the openmed `[privacy_filter.openmed.policy]` outbound=soft setting for clinical content (AB-27.8), which is fail-closed-gated off today but would warrant a review before any enablement.

## dreamlab-ai-website / critical

### F-01 — Verified poker hands trigger automatic wallet payment on fields the verifier never checks
- Topics: DW-10.4
- Evidence: The replay verifier checks "commitment, seed, actions, cards, result and deterministic house play" (`nostr-bbs-poker/src/verify.rs:151`), but its Claim "has no settlement amount, destination or supplied root"; the browser then pays "using supplied settlement, root and destination without deriving all from replay" (`nostr-bbs-forum-client/src/poker/live.rs:968`) and "Verified status allows automatic payment" (`live.rs:1036`). The authors state this is a source-only finding, not an executed exploit.
- Failure: A crafted or tampered Done reply carrying a legitimately replayable game record with an attacker-chosen settlement amount and destination address drives an automatic transfer from a member's browser wallet with no user confirmation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, DW-10.4 node D)

### F-02 — House balance and daily payout cap are checked without reservation; concurrent buy-ins overcommit them
- Topics: DW-10.2
- Evidence: Admission "check[s] member balance and house balance less already settled unpaid obligations" and "remaining daily payout cap then deal[s] without reserving active buy-ins" (`nostr-bbs-poker-citizen/src/table.rs:521,535`); "two simultaneous buy-ins can use the same available house balance and cap" (`table.rs:225`). Separately, "a non-hex 64-byte transaction ID can renew the payment claim timestamp" (`table.rs:399`), keeping an unproven payment claim alive. The authors' own fixtures "admit two 200-unit hands against 200 units of house balance and a 200-unit cap" — on the two configured *asset* tables (DREAM, BLAKES7), not practice chips.
- Failure: A member fires two seat requests concurrently and plays real-asset hands against house balance and cap that were each sized for one; a fabricated txid string indefinitely defers the payment claim. Exposure beyond the cap, not a demonstrated cap breach — the authors say exactly that.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, DW-10.2 nodes C and F)

### F-03 — Poker settlement effects run before persistence; a crash pays without recording, and a failed ledger write doesn't stop dealing
- Topics: DW-10.3
- Evidence: "Finished hand emits Done and optional Pay before its Persist effect" (`table.rs:846`); the runner "performs effects in queue order and clears house debt on producer acceptance" (`run.rs:460`) — so a crash between Pay and Persist executes a transfer the saved book never records. Restart "reconstructs Citizen from Ledger and starts with empty active-hand maps" (`table.rs:189`), dropping every in-progress hand. And "failed file write is logged without stopping subsequent dealing" (`run.rs:276`).
- Failure: Power loss or worker crash in the pay-before-save window produces a settled transfer with no persisted obligation (or a duplicate one after reconstruction); members mid-hand lose the game silently; a failing disk lets the game diverge from its own ledger indefinitely.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, DW-10.3 nodes C and F)

### F-04 — One key is both primary forum admin and governance publisher, and the one admin-key mirror CI cannot verify is the Cloudflare secret
- Topics: DW-03.3, DW-03.4, DW-03.5
- Evidence: `visionclaw-server` (11ed6422…663c) is "governance publisher AND primary admin" (`forum-config/README.md:22-38`; `dreamlab.toml:39-72` comment) — the split is "Open, staged", deliberately held because "the auth-worker ADMIN_PUBKEYS CF secret cannot be rotated from this repo or CI" (DW-03.4 note). The mirror checker lists that secret as `verifiable: false` (DW-03.5); executing the split later is "a five-location atomic change".
- Failure: Compromise of that single key yields full forum administration plus governance publication. On any rotation, an operator who updates the TOML and the two wrangler `[vars]` but fat-fingers the secret passes every CI mirror check (it only asserts the key is *absent* from `[vars]`) and ships relay/search accepting a key the auth worker rejects — split-brain admin with a green pipeline.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open/staged for the unsplit key); no for the unverifiable-secret rotation hazard, which is stated but carries no marker

### F-05 — A plaintext admin private key sits in git history forever, unrotated
- Topics: DW-07.2
- Evidence: The three deleted root-level seeders "embedded a plaintext ADMIN_PRIVKEY_HEX" (`CLOSEOUT-SECURITY-AUDIT.md:300`); after deletion by `3baf2fa4`, "the plaintext private key stays in git history from c0bcece; the owner judges it unused or already replaced, so no rotation followed" (`CLOSEOUT-SECURITY-AUDIT.md:116`). "Whether it is live cannot be decided from this repo."
- Failure: Anyone with repo read access (or a future publicisation/push-mirror mistake) extracts the key from history; if it matches any live whitelisted admin pubkey or the CF secret, that person becomes admin of the forum without any detectable change in the working tree.
- Confidence: high that the key is in history; medium that it authorises anything live
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-06 — First-user-is-admin bootstrap on an internet-facing auth worker
- Topics: DW-04.6, DW-07.3
- Evidence: Admin resolution's third layer: "first-user-is-admin bootstrap — GET /api/setup-status reports needsSetup when no is_admin=1 row exists" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:259`). The repo's own tooling references a `reset-db` operation that wipes whitelist rows ("a recipient declared under `[[ingress]]` … survives `reset-db`", `whitelist-admin-recipient.mjs:19-21`), and the nightly backup is raw export only (F-09), so a restore of a pre-admin snapshot has the same effect.
- Failure: Any event that empties or regresses the admin rows — an operator reset-db, a bad restore, a botched migration — silently re-arms the bootstrap on the live public deployment, and the next visitor to complete setup owns the forum.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the material shows the bootstrap condition and the row-wiping operations but not that the setup endpoint is unauthenticated on the live deployment)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — No tested restore path exists for the forum's only backup
- Topics: DW-07.5
- Evidence: The nightly cron exports D1 (dreamlab-relay, dreamlab-auth), R2 (pods, vectors) and KV to the NAS (`forum-backup.sh:26-35`); "This is the only backup/restore path for the forum found in this repo — there is no corresponding restore script, only the raw D1/R2/KV export."
- Failure: In the actual incident this backup exists for — corrupted D1, deleted R2 pods, fat-fingered KV — the operator has never rehearsed or scripted restoration of raw exports into live Cloudflare resources. RTO is unbounded; a botched manual restore can itself trigger F-06 by restoring a pre-admin snapshot. (The Durable Object's SQLite state is not listed in the export at all — see Not judgeable.)
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated plainly, no marker)

### F-08 — Forum and identity content egresses to a third-party AI vendor, cloud by default, on a switch the docs say is "local-only"
- Topics: DW-03.8, DW-07.4, DW-09.5
- Evidence: The overlay opts in `JEV_TRIAGE`, `JEV_SEARCH_RERANK`, `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK` with a 5-minute relay cron; "the auth worker's rationale check is cloud by default and private only when JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK_LOCAL_ONLY=1" (`auth-worker.wrangler.toml:61-66`; `SYSTEM-ONE.md:13-16` — the kit's per-consumer local-only switch is off by default). The inbox-watch module's "old local-only header comment" contradicts the executable default; decrypted enquiry text "up to 1500 characters" goes to the vendor endpoint in plaintext as request state (`enquiry-topic.mjs:27-32,86`; `jev.mjs:38-59`).
- Failure: Locked-zone and auth-worker content, and decrypted contact enquiries, are shipped to an external API by default; the module header tells an operator the opposite. A data-protection review conducted from the comments would be wrong.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN for the worker opt-in; no marker on the header-vs-default contradiction in DW-09.5)

### F-09 — The operator's mail digest permanently drops messages, and its health checks cannot detect the row loss ADR-2011 claims it reports
- Topics: DW-04.9, DW-07.8, DW-09.1, DW-09.4
- Evidence: The digest "itemises at most 25 messages" but "persists all fetched IDs as seen" — "undisplayed messages do not roll forward" (`digest.mjs:18-19`; `inbox-watch/run.mjs:327-328`); "any content containing probe phrase is omitted as a synthetic probe" (`run.mjs:200`) — a sender-controlled suppression; the pagination cursor can skip tied entries (`digest.mjs:74`). Meanwhile ADR-2011 "says the watch reports missing registry/whitelist rows, but configuration-backed ingress and merged disclosure can keep its checks green after row loss" (`provision-website-info.mjs:97`; `run.mjs:229`).
- Failure: A busy night or a probe-phrase collision silently buries real contact/signup/chat enquiries — the business's inbound pipeline — forever, while the nightly receipt stays green; a reset-db that deletes the monitor's rows is equally invisible. Every submission is also dual-wrapped to the monitor key, so this second copy is the only readable one for anyone but the admin.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt/Drift across DW-07.8, DW-09.1, DW-09.4)

### F-10 — Split pins: the house-seat service and the verifying client run different kit commits while ADR-2009 claims lockstep
- Topics: DW-10.1
- Evidence: "service pin dd454474 differs from website client pin 9c36b603" (`agentbox/lib/poker-citizen.nix:32` vs `deploy.yml:130`); "ADR-2009's exact-commit lockstep wording is stronger than the current separately pinned deployment". The 42 poker files were byte-identical across the two pins, but the authors state the comparison "did not certify the complete dependency graph or running binary provenance."
- Failure: The next kit repin on either side independently breaks the assumed lockstep: a client whose replay engine or message format diverges from the dealing service's build produces systematic verification failures or, worse, accepts games under subtly different rules — on real-asset tables.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT)

### F-11 — The fail-closed secret validator is dead code; the live deploy gate requires 2 of 4 secrets, warns on 2, and skips the auth worker's newest credential entirely
- Topics: DW-03.8, DW-05.1
- Evidence: `deploy_config.rs:67-78` says `NATIVE_POD_URL` is "gated here so a fresh deploy without it fails fast", but "no workflow calls `validate_required_secrets` or `validate_deploy_dir`" and the closeout audit "records the validator as test-only" (`CLOSEOUT-SECURITY-AUDIT.md:148-152`); the bash gate "requires two and warns on two" (`workers-deploy.yml:318-336`). The `set-worker-secrets.yml:8` header claims "All four are validated by the workers-deploy.yml pre-deploy gate" — false. And "Validate public System One credential" runs "for the search-worker and relay-worker matrix entries only" (`workers-deploy.yml:287-288`) although the auth worker now needs `TYPESAFE_API_KEY`, so "a missing key there degrades silently".
- Failure: A fresh or partially-seeded environment deploys green with critical secrets absent; the auth worker's rationale check fails at runtime with no deploy-time or receipt-level signal; operators reading the workflow headers believe validation happened.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in DW-03.8; Debt in DW-05.1; the false header is marked Drift)

### F-12 — Every client API call depends on raw `*.workers.dev` hostnames; the branded domains remain unprovisioned and once severed the whole client
- Topics: DW-01.3
- Evidence: The branded worker domains "are the documented end-state but are **not provisioned in DNS** — the client instead talks to raw `*.workers.dev` hosts baked into the build env (`deploy.yml:49-61`)" and "shipping the branded domains baked in previously severed every client API call" (`BASELINE-architecture.md:91-97`; `ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED` verified 2026-06-09, not re-checked as of 2026-08-31).
- Failure: The entire contact/signup/chat/forum surface hangs on the workers.dev shared subdomain setting; flipping it (or completing the documented DNS cut without redoing the env bake, per the precedent) breaks every client API call at once. The env-rebake step that bit before is a manual, unverified operation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as verified fact, no marker)

### F-13 — The SEO-watch publisher cannot actually publish: it commits, then fails its own check; and raw provider errors (including key echoes) reach the ledger and stderr unsanitised
- Topics: DW-08.5, DW-08.6
- Evidence: "inspect staged names AFTER commit — normal empty index fails expected-file check" (`propose.mjs:298-301`) with push/label/draft-PR "not reached in local success-to-commit fixture"; "ledger serialises raw advice error, error log is also unsanitised" (`run.mjs:253`) while the report is scrubbed — "a synthetic provider-key echo reaches the ledger and stderr but not the report". ADR-2010 "requires scrubbing" and tested, human-reviewed proposals.
- Failure: Every accepted proposal leaves a local commit and then errors, so the automation never opens the PR it exists to open (silent operability failure); concurrently, a provider error containing the API key is persisted unencrypted into the state file on the NAS.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT/DEBT in both diagrams)

### F-14 — Admin resolution reads `whitelist.is_admin` from a database the schema doesn't create it in, and D1-promoted admins are invisible to search
- Topics: DW-03.10, DW-03.11, DW-04.6
- Evidence: `SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:269` says the D1 path checks "whitelist.is_admin (relay D1)", but `whitelist` (`001_init.sql:266`) and `members` (`:160`) are created in the **dreamlab-auth** database via the auth worker's `migrations_dir` (`auth-worker.wrangler.toml:18`); the relay's own D1 declares no migrations in this overlay. "Which copy the kit's admin resolution reads cannot be settled from this repo." Separately, "the search-worker honours only its own `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` `[vars]` value — D1-promoted admins are not visible to it" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:272`).
- Failure: Promotion via `/api/whitelist/set-admin` works for the auth/relay path but the promoted admin silently fails admin-gated search operations — two admins with different effective powers depending on which surface they touch; if the kit does read a relay-D1 `whitelist` table, that copy diverges from the migrated auth copy with no reconciliation.
- Confidence: medium (the doc/dependency contradiction is high-confidence; the runtime consequence depends on kit code not in this repo)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open in DW-03.11; "Known gap" in DW-04.6)

### F-15 — The invariants register — the stated compliance surface — is breached and stale in multiple places at once
- Topics: DW-06.6, DW-06.9, DW-03.1, DW-01.5, DW-05.4
- Evidence: Invariant 7 "doc says only zone3 is encrypted" is marked BREACHED: `IDENTITY-zones.md:180-181` vs `dreamlab.toml:123,137,148` (zone2/3/4 `encrypted=true`) plus a master `[encryption].enabled` gate citing a kit ADR — "the governing doc is still unrevised" (D15). `BASELINE-architecture.md:117` cites a stale KIT_REF one repin behind the four live pin sites; `kit-compatibility-record.md:355` still marks a six-releases-old SHA "Current (canonical)"; `CLAUDE.md:14` still says "static dual-SPA" against three shipped frontends; pin-parity "compares only the SHA" so these rots never fail the gate.
- Failure: These ten invariants are declared the change-control surface ("any change touching them requires a governing-doc update plus a thin ADR"); an operator or auditor reasoning from them today gets the encryption model, the kit pin and the topology wrong. The 2026-07-20 cohorts regression shows this class of doc/ctl drift has already produced a live access failure once.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (BREACHED/Drift markers throughout — included because the *aggregate* control failure is larger than any single marked entry)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Liveness of the leaked history key (F-05):** the actual hex at `c0bcece` versus the live relay whitelist, `[admin].static_pubkeys` and the CF `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` secret. Need the key value and the deployed worker configs/secrets.
2. **The bootstrap attack path (F-06):** whether `/api/setup-status` and the setup flow are unauthenticated on the deployed auth worker, and what `reset-db` actually wipes. Need the kit's auth-worker source at `KIT_REF` (`9c36b603…`), which this repo does not vendor.
3. **Durable Object state in backups (F-07):** whether `NostrRelayDO`'s SQLite-backed state is covered by any export; `scripts/backup/` lists D1/R2/KV only, and the DO schema lives in kit code.
4. **The pin split's real blast radius (F-10):** a full dependency and behaviour diff between agentbox `dd454474` and website `9c36b603` beyond the 42 byte-identical poker files — shared crates, wire formats, settlement conventions.
5. **Anonymous ingress admission mode actually deployed (DW-04.5):** the relay's write-auth mode at the pinned kit revision — the operator docs' "no-AUTH/whitelist-only" wording is stale, but which admission posture (nip42-required vs configured-ingress) the live relay runs cannot be settled from this repo.

## dreamlab-ai-website / premortem

# Post-mortem — asset-table custody failure at the poker house seats

**Incident (as understood for this report):** a client member of an asset-backed table (DREAM or BLAKES7) suffered a custody loss: the house seat admitted and settled play against credit it had not actually received, a settlement was paid on fields the member's wallet had never checked against the verified game record, and every operational receipt stayed green throughout. **Scope note:** all findings below are grounded in the November‑2026 stamped material; twelve months of subsequent change is not visible to me, and every confidence line states where I am reasoning beyond what the material shows.

---

### R-1 — The house seat deals on renewable, unreserved credit, so simultaneous hands over-extend house balance and the daily payout cap
- Topics: DW-10.2, DW-10.1, DW-10.6
- Chain of events:
  1. A member requests a seat claiming payment, supplying a 64‑character **non-hex** string as the transaction id. Per the kit's own DEBT note, such an id "can renew the payment claim timestamp" (`nostr-bbs-poker-citizen/src/table.rs:399`), so the claim never ages past the grace period that would otherwise refuse it (`table.rs:260`).
  2. Admission checks "member balance and house balance **less already settled unpaid obligations**" (`table.rs:521`) — only *settled* obligations are counted; active buy-ins are not reserved (`table.rs:535`, DEBT: "deal without reserving active buy-ins").
  3. The member opens a second hand before the first settles; both admissions see the same available house balance and the same remaining daily cap — explicitly: "two simultaneous buy-ins can use the same available house balance and cap" (`table.rs:225`, DEBT).
  4. Both hands are dealt; payouts are computed per hand; aggregate exposure now exceeds the house balance and cap that were each checked individually.
  5. Because these are **asset** tables, not practice chips (`dreamlab.toml:320`; ADR‑2009 funds the house from treasury), the shortfall lands on the operator or on a member whose settled win cannot be paid.
- Evidence: DW-10.2 — `table.rs:225`, `:399`, `:521`, `:535`; the kit's own native fixtures "admit two 200‑unit hands against 200 units of house balance and a 200‑unit cap"; DW-10.1 confirms the tables carry real assets and separate house seats per chain (`agentbox.toml:2034`); DW-10.6 confirms no operational probe checks settlement adequacy.
- Warning signs visible today: the exposure is **demonstrated in the kit's own fixtures**, not merely suspected (DW-10.2: "They show exposure without reservation"); three DEBT labels sit on exactly this path; the area summary already separates "a completed hand, an accepted transfer and a recoverable record" as outcomes with "gaps between them" (DW-10 preamble).
- Confidence: high for the defect and the demonstrated exposure; escalation from exposure to an actual unpaid settlement is **inferred** — the material itself notes the fixtures show "exposure without reservation, not a demonstrated breach of the payout cap".
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The wallet auto-pays settlement fields that were never derived from the replay it just verified
- Topics: DW-10.4, DW-10.1
- Chain of events:
  1. A finished hand's Done reply carries commitment, record, root, settlement amount and opponent (`nostr-bbs-forum-client/src/poker/live.rs:840`).
  2. The browser matches its remembered nonce and replays the record through the verifier, which checks commitment, seed, actions, cards, result and deterministic house play (`live.rs:911`, `nostr-bbs-poker/src/verify.rs:151`).
  3. The verifier's Claim "has no settlement amount, destination or supplied root" (DW-10.4), and the payment path "uses supplied settlement, root and destination without deriving all from replay" (`live.rs:968`, DEBT).
  4. Verified status **allows automatic payment** (`live.rs:1036`); the wallet's only guard is per-hand dedup ("checks an existing hand payment before attempting another transfer", `live.rs:554`).
  5. Any divergence between the verified record and the payment fields — a house-side bug, a deal/verify divergence between the separately pinned client and service (DW-10.1: service pin `dd454474` vs client pin `9c36b603`, `poker-citizen.nix:32` vs `deploy.yml:130`), or a compromised house key — results in an automatic transfer of an amount, root or destination the replay never certified.
- Evidence: DW-10.4 nodes and DEBT at `live.rs:968`; DW-10.4's own statement: "the missing binding between replay and payment instructions" is a source-only finding; the pin skew is a stated DRIFT (DW-10.1).
- Warning signs visible today: the DEBT is explicit in the consumed kit source; DW-10.4 states "this review did not execute a malicious browser payout" — i.e., the path is untested against adversarial input; the two deployment pins already disagree, so client/service engine divergence is a live possibility.
- Confidence: high that the binding is missing (stated); medium that this specific path fired in the incident — exploitation requires a house-side fault or key compromise, which is **inferred**.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The persisted book and the live game diverge: payouts precede persistence, failed ledger writes don't stop dealing, and restart erases active hands
- Topics: DW-10.3, DW-10.2
- Chain of events:
  1. Active hands, offers and sessions live only in the Citizen's in-memory maps (`table.rs:148`).
  2. A finished hand emits Done and optional Pay **before** its Persist effect (`table.rs:846`); the runner performs effects in queue order and clears house debt on producer acceptance (`run.rs:460`).
  3. A failed ledger-file write "is logged without stopping subsequent dealing" (`run.rs:276`, DEBT) — the persisted book, which "covers settled obligations" (DW-10.3), goes stale while the table keeps dealing. This is exactly the input the admission arithmetic counts ("less already settled unpaid obligations", `table.rs:521`), so a stale book under-counts obligations and compounds R‑1.
  4. A restart reconstructs the Citizen from the Ledger with **empty active-hand maps** (`table.rs:189`, DEBT): any hand in progress — including a member's staked buy-in — vanishes.
  5. A crash in the emit/pay-before-persist window, or continued operation on a stale ledger, yields payouts the book does not record and hands the book does not know about: unrecorded or duplicated settlements, and members' stakes lost with the vanished hands.
- Evidence: DW-10.3 — `table.rs:148`, `:189`, `:846`, `:1029`, `run.rs:276`, `:460`; the material's own simulation: "an idle active hand survives a simulated month of ticks but disappears after reconstruction from the saved ledger"; "a send/pay-before-save crash window; no production crash or duplicate payment was induced".
- Warning signs visible today: the vanish-on-restart behaviour is demonstrated, not hypothetical; two DEBT labels sit on the persistence path; no probe (DW-10.6) checks ledger consistency or settlement records.
- Confidence: high for the defects (stated and partially demonstrated); the precise production loss/duplication shape is **inferred** — the material notes no production crash was induced.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Operational probes certify far more than they proved, so custody degradation was invisible
- Topics: DW-10.6
- Evidence: the profile provisioner resolves on the first OK "without matching the kind‑0 event ID" (`provision-poker-citizen.mjs:62`, DEBT) and "rejected publication still resolves instead of returning a failed exit status" (`:66`, DEBT); the live-hand probe's Done check "does not compare against" the remembered commitment (`test-poker-citizen.mjs:86`), checks only self-consistent secret/seed/nonce "without engine replay or settlement confirmation" (`:105`), and "next Offer prints played and settled even when the member still owes the transfer" (`:84`, DEBT). All reproduced with mocked transport (DW-10.6).
- Failure: the house service is unpublished, insolvent or owing transfers while every operational receipt is green — the detection blindfold behind R‑1/R‑3.
- Confidence: high — reproduced with synthetic keys and mocked transport.
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Client and house-service consume different kit pins; byte-identical poker files do not certify the dependency graph
- Topics: DW-10.1
- Evidence: agentbox service pin `dd454474` (`poker-citizen.nix:32`) vs website client pin `9c36b603` (`deploy.yml:130`), flagged DRIFT; 42 poker files byte-identical across the pins, but "equal poker source does not prove all dependencies equal" (DW-10.1).
- Failure: deal/verify divergence between house and browser — hands the house dealt fail client replay (settlements stuck behind manual action) or settle differently than verified; an enabling condition for R‑2.
- Confidence: medium — the skew is stated; behavioural divergence is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT)
- Severity: medium

### F-03 — The enquiry digest marks fetched-but-undisplayed messages as seen, and boundary ties are skipped: permanent silent enquiry loss
- Topics: DW-09.4, DW-07.8
- Evidence: "digest itemises first 25" (`digest.mjs:18`) but "all fetched IDs become persisted seen; undisplayed items do not roll into next digest" (`inbox-watch/run.mjs:389`, DEBT; persisted after relay acceptance, `run.mjs:327-328`); the material's own fixture: 30 messages → 25 displayed, all 30 marked seen, "reports zero new on its next run" (DW-09.4); separately, "next cursor is oldest second minus one; unseen ties at the boundary can be skipped" (`digest.mjs:74`, DEBT).
- Failure: a burst of client enquiries (or messages landing on a tied boundary second) are fetched once, never shown, never resurface — clients believe they made contact.
- Confidence: high — demonstrated by the 30‑message fixture.
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — A sender-controlled probe phrase suppresses a message from the digest
- Topics: DW-09.4
- Evidence: "any content containing probe phrase is omitted as a synthetic probe" (`inbox-watch/run.mjs:200`, DEBT).
- Failure: a sender who includes (or coincidentally matches) the watcher's probe phrase has their enquiry silently excluded from the operator digest.
- Confidence: high for the mechanism (stated); practical exploitability is inferred (depends on the phrase being guessable).
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)
- Severity: medium

### F-05 — The monitor copy of every enquiry is best-effort with silent no-op modes, and its pubkey is not in the CI-checked mirror set
- Topics: DW-09.3, DW-01.6, DW-03.5, DW-03.6, DW-04.9
- Evidence: the monitor wrap is best-effort and "missing/equal key is a no-op" (`src/lib/nostr.ts:352`); "copy failure does not change the visitor-facing primary result" (`nostr.ts:363`); the relay serves wraps only to their addressee, which is why the copy exists at all (`nostr.ts:342-347`, DW-04.9); DW‑01.6 calls `VITE_MONITOR_PUBKEY` "another hand-synced mirror (DW-03)", yet the seven enumerated mirror groups (DW-03.5/DW-03.6) do not include it, and the completeness sweep scans wrangler `[vars]` only (`config-mirrors.mjs:41-47`); the nightly probe "tests the monitor path, while actual visitor copies remain best effort", and the bundle substring checks "are not a browser form-submission test" (`run.mjs:253`, DW-09.3).
- Failure: a build ships with a missing or equal monitor key (or a copy-path regression); every contact, signup and chat copy silently no-ops; visitors see success; the nightly probe stays green because it exercises the monitor's own inbox, not the browser copy path.
- Confidence: high for the no-op semantics and the CI gap (both are direct joins of stated facts); the incident scenario is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-06 — One key is both primary admin and governance publisher, and rotating it is a five-site manual atomic change with one unverifiable site
- Topics: DW-03.4, DW-03.3, DW-03.5
- Evidence: `visionclaw-server` (11ed6422…663c) is governance publisher AND primary admin (`dreamlab.toml:275`, DW-03.3); the auth-worker `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` Cloudflare secret "cannot be rotated from this repo or CI" (DW-03.4, `dreamlab.toml:39-72` comment); `VITE_ADMIN_PUBKEY` is INTERIM, making the split "a five-location atomic change" (`deploy.yml:62-73`); before 2026‑09‑05 CI compared only one of the admin mirror sites (`config-mirrors.mjs:1-18`), and the secret site remains `verifiable: false` (DW-03.5).
- Failure: a VisionClaw-side compromise yields full forum admin; remediation requires a coordinated manual rotation during which a missed site (the CF secret, or search `[vars]`) leaves the compromised key privileged somewhere.
- Confidence: high for the structural exposure (all stated); the compromise scenario is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — "Open, staged: the admin/governance key split")

### F-07 — A plaintext admin private key remains in git history, unrotated, with liveness undetermined

### F-11 — Decrypted plaintext leaves the trust boundary to a vendor endpoint, with local-only as opt-in rather than default

What data the worker-side calls carry is **not stated in the material and must be verified**.

- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-06.6

- Failure: an operator following the governing doc mishandles zone2/zone4 content during response (e.g., treats it as non-encrypted), or a mirror flip of `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` silently disables zone enforcement while members believe they are posting E2EE.

### F-15 — The only backup copy is readable plaintext forum content on a NAS, with KV including SESSIONS exported
- Topics: DW-07.5
- Evidence: the script's own header: kind-1059 DMs are E2E at rest but kind-40/42/0 events are signed plaintext, so "the NAS copy is readable content — keep it on the trusted segment only" (`forum-backup.sh:16-18`); KV `SESSIONS/POD_META/SEARCH_CONFIG` are exported (`forum-backup.sh:31-35`); this is the only backup path, with no restore script (DW-07.5).

## estate / critical

### F-01 — Production forum edge runs a crypto dependency with an unfixed RUSTSEC advisory while the fix exists only on main
- Topics: ES-05.14, ES-11.4
- Evidence: ES-05.14 states forum main (`c39f41f`) is "fixed for RUSTSEC-2026-0387, with k256 0.14.0, p256 0.14.0… but forum production still runs kit `9c36b60`, with crypto-bigint 0.5.5 and k256 0.13.4, so main is fixed and the edge is not." The deploy pins that kit (`../dreamlab-ai-website/.github/workflows/workers-deploy.yml:44`); the website at `0e15728` still pins `9c36b60`, an ancestor of forum head (`rust-ci.yml:20` context, ES-11.4). No marker is attached — it is stated inside a paragraph whose opening claim is that a drift was "closed."
- Failure: the deployed relay worker — the estate's governance ingress that verifies signatures and enforces the rationale gate — carries known-vulnerable crypto crate versions. Any advisory-driven patch cycle, or any exploitation of RUSTSEC-2026-0387 against the edge, is unaddressed in production until someone re-pins and redeploys, and nothing in the tree flags that the edge lags main.
- Confidence: high (that the skew exists as stated; the material does not state the advisory's exploitability)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — Shipped compose defaults to a demo-open posture and a standalone tunnel file can put anonymous API reads on the public internet, invisibly to repo review and the port audit
- Topics: ES-10.3
- Evidence: compose defaults `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS: ${...:-1}` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:132`), `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS: ${...:-1}` (`:133`), `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE: ${...:-1}` (`:116`) — "anonymous /api reads ON, owner-less boot permitted." The standalone cloudflared "fronts WHATEVER profile the backend booted… anonymous /api reads reach the public internet via the Cloudflare tunnel" (`docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:3-8`); its route is a Cloudflare dashboard Public Hostname with "no local config.yml, so the exposed mapping is NOT reviewable from this repo" (`:5-6`) and it is "invisible to the ADR-2013 compose port-audit" (`:6-8`). ADR-2119's `VISIONCLAW_INGRESS=lan` "does not stop a hand-started standalone tunnel fronting a LAN-declared host" (ADR-2119:64-66).
- Failure: an operator brings up the dev stack with shipped defaults and separately runs the still-shipped standalone tunnel; the graph API becomes readable anonymously from the internet, and neither CI nor any committed file shows the exposure — it lives only in the Cloudflare dashboard.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT1, CFT note, LANGAP Open in ES-10.3)

### F-03 — PATCH against a Turtle/JSON-LD pod resource silently destroys its prior content
- Topics: ES-08.6
- Evidence: `Graph::parse_ntriples` errors on non-N-Triples input (`../solid-pod-rs/crates/solid-pod-rs/src/ldp.rs:817-828`, same function at the pinned `v0.5.0-alpha.17`); the handler turns that error into an empty graph (`../project/src/handlers/solid_proxy_handler.rs:786-789`), so "a PATCH against a Turtle or JSON-LD resource, which is what `putResource` writes by default (`ldpClient.ts:153-157`)… stores only the patch's own triples… and drops the previous content" (`solid_proxy_handler.rs:805-812`). The in-repo client never PATCHes; any other Solid client can.
- Failure: any standards-compliant Solid client issuing an N3 Patch or SPARQL-Update against a user's pod resource (written as JSON-LD/Turtle by the shipped client) replaces the whole resource with the patch's triples — silent, unrecoverable data loss on user pods (no pod backup exists; see F-05).
- Confidence: high (code path as described; the material says source-traced, not reproduced)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-04 — The agentbox→VisionClaw agent-event pipeline is non-functional in release: wrong envelope shape, no credential, and emitted fields dropped at ingest
- Topics: ES-02.2, ES-02.3, ES-03.1
- Evidence: the subscriber forwards the bare publisher event (`agent-event-ws-subscriber.js:144-151`) while the canonical `jsonrpc`/`method`/`params` envelope is built only by `createMcpNotification` (`agent-event-publisher.js:305-343`), which the subscriber never calls; `AgentActionNotification` requires those fields with no serde default (`src/agent_events/schema.rs:26-29`), so `process_frame` returns `Malformed` (`ingest.rs:101,149`) — "no forwarded frame from this path reaches the hub." Independently, the subscriber sends no auth (`agent-event-ws-subscriber.js:94`, `server.js:383`), and a release VisionClaw refuses the upgrade with 401 (`ingest.rs:291-292`). Additionally, `failure_mode` and `authority_class` are emitted (`agent-event-publisher.js:326,335`) but silently parsed away because the Rust mirror lacks the fields and does not deny unknown fields (`schema.rs:60-147`); `intent` (`schema.rs:143`) is never emitted.
- Failure: in a release deployment the entire cross-container agent-event/beam telemetry feature produces nothing — the socket either never authenticates or accepts and rejects every frame as `malformed_json` — while the publisher reports success (`broadcast_count`) and the subscriber retries on backoff, so the outage is silent and unalerted.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift ×2 in ES-02.2; Open in ES-02.3)

### F-05 — Erasure is a hole and recovery is absent: pod deletion never revokes RuVector embeddings, and neither store has a point-in-time backup, RPO or RTO
- Topics: ES-07.9, ES-08.9, ES-08.8
- Evidence: "pod-sync deletion has NO reverse tombstone. `deleteAgentMemory()` in the Pod does not revoke the RuVector-held agent memory: the embedding row persists and stays semantically searchable. Largest erasure hole. No point-in-time RuVector backup exists… no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO" (ES-07.9 D5). The call graph has "no reverse-tombstone dispatch" (`agentMemory.ts:191-199`) and "does not even attempt the secondary write, let alone log its absence" (ES-08.9, against DATA-authority-erasure Invariant 6). Separately: "no point-in-time backup for the pod store, `scripts/backup-sqlite.sh` covers SQLite only… no declared RPO or RTO" (ES-08.8).
- Failure: a user exercises erasure; the deleted agent memory remains retrievable by semantic search indefinitely. After store corruption or accidental deletion of pod or vector data, there is no restore path at all — the recovery objective has never been declared.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE D5; ES-08.8/ES-08.9 notes)

### F-06 — System One egress contradicts the Loom privacy invariant: compose arms cloud-bound JEV and memory-rerank, the code's own header says local-only, and the proxy will be a vendor-key spend point on a shared bridge
- Topics: ES-06.1, ES-06.5, ES-06.6, ES-07.3, ES-10.8, ES-01.3
- Evidence: the stated invariant is "It delegates ONLY to a LAN/local model, never to a cloud endpoint, so mail content never leaves the LAN" (ES-06.1 INV2). Yet compose sets `LOOM_JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD=1` reaching the agentbox proxy with "selected backend TypeSafe, with gate, rank and support on" (`docker-compose.unified.yml:387-397`, `jev.rs:353`), while "the façade's own header still justifies local-only by private corpus content (`jev.rs:30-34`)" (ES-06.6). For memory: "the query and capped value snippets of the shortlist go to the selected backend, cloud TypeSafe by default" (`memory-jev-rerank.js:16-18,107`), staged until image activation (ES-07.3). Also: the proxy binds `0.0.0.0` in-container when `network=true` (`agentbox.toml:892-893`), so "any container on visionclaw_network can reach it and spend the vendor key" (ADR-2139:298); and host services are already repointed at `agentbox:8094` while "the running container still dials loopback" (ES-01.3 Open).
- Failure: once the new agentbox image activates, mail-derived queries through the Loom and agent-memory query/snippet content leave the LAN to a third-party cloud endpoint by default — directly against the system's stated privacy guarantee — and every peer container on the shared bridge can spend the System One vendor key; in the interim window, repointed consumers call an endpoint that is not yet serving.
- Confidence: medium (memory-rerank content is explicit; what Loom JEV payloads carry is inferred from the code header's own justification; activation is staged)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — yes (Open in ES-06.6, Drift in ES-07.3, D6 in ES-10.8, Open in ES-01.3) for the configuration facts; no for the contradiction with the ES-06.1 LAN-only invariant, which no marker draws

### F-07 — Governance delivery ignores expiry and projection: an expired decision is still applied, and a restart can permanently suppress an unfinished one
- Topics: ES-13.1, ES-13.2
- Evidence: the agentbox apply path "applies without reading projection receipt or expiry" (`local-process-manager.js:419,444,461`); "no non-test agentbox source reads `stale_after`… A decision the forum has already closed as expired is still applied" (ES-13.1, TENSION). The expiry close itself exists on the forum (`cron.rs:764,794,834`). Separately, the consumer adds an event to the shared seen map before dispatch (`relay-consumer.js:464`), the earlier event's settle persists the whole map including the not-yet-run event (`:473,395`), and a restored consumer then rejects its redelivery (`:459`) — "persisted seen does not imply completed handler"; a fixture reproduced the suppression.
- Failure: a proposal expires on the forum but the queued decision is still consumed and the corpus is mutated contrary to the expiry outcome, with no veto anywhere. Independently, a restart between one event settling and the next completing silently drops that governance decision application forever, with no trace.
- Confidence: high (suppression reproduced by fixture per the material; expiry bypass is source-read)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION ×2; Drift on the stale `settle` comment)

### F-08 — Both production startup paths are broken or non-self-healing: one names a nonexistent binary, the other never restarts a crashed backend
- Topics: ES-09.7
- Evidence: `supervisord.production.conf:29` runs `/app/target/release/visionclaw-server`, but the image that reads this file puts the binary at `/app/visionclaw-server` (`Dockerfile.unified:406`, WORKDIR `:393`) — "from source, the Dockerfile.unified production target would fail its backend start." The compose production service builds `Dockerfile.production` instead (`docker-compose.unified.yml:256-258`), whose `start.sh` runs the backend on 4001 in the background with nginx foreground (`production-startup.sh:38-41,68-70`), no supervisord — "nothing on the compose production path restarts a backend that crashes after startup: nginx keeps the container alive, so `restart: unless-stopped` does not fire."
- Failure: on the actual compose production path, a backend crash leaves a healthy-looking container serving errors with no automatic recovery and no restart-policy trigger; on the other target the backend never starts at all. Either way, production is down until a human intervenes.
- Confidence: high (source-traced; the material says not run)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt for the path; Divergence for the no-restart path)

### F-09 — The agentbox container's trust boundary is a UID: any in-box process can forge `did:nostr` attribution, call the session socket, and the local governance ledger is unsigned
- Topics: ES-04.1, ES-04.5, ES-05.8
- Evidence: "a process inside the agentbox container can still stamp any pubkey over loopback (TODO-unified X-1)" (`agent-event-auth.js:36-39`); the `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` header is honoured from any loopback peer (ES-04.1). "The ACL authorises a UID, not a process: any devuser (uid 1000) process may call the session socket" (`session-plane-acl.json:6`, `api.rs:32`). The ADR-2164 task-token path emits agent actions with `did: null` under every policy (`agent-event-auth.js:143-145`). And the durable claim ledger that gates governed replay is described only as "Agentbox local ledger is unsigned" (ES-05.8 note).
- Failure: one compromised or buggy skill, hook, or tool inside the container can publish agent-events attributed to any identity, drive sessions as uid 1000, and rewrite the unsigned received-claim ledger that replay refusal depends on — all attribution and governance records produced inside the box are untrusted against anything else in the box.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — yes (Debt X-1; DIVERGENCE D3; Drift for ADR-2164); no for the unsigned-ledger fact, which is stated without a marker
