### F-10 — Neither rationale gate can ever fire on a VisionClaw case, and the closure criterion they block on is one VisionClaw cannot produce
- Topics: ES-05.12, ES-05.13, ES-05.14
- Evidence: verified at the stamp — VisionClaw's 31400/31402 carry no tier or task-property field (`events.rs:211-217`, `:308-323`, `elevation_actor.rs:239`); the relay folds every VisionClaw case to `medium` (`governance.rs:525`, `wrangler.toml:61`) and `check_rationale` "only bites at high or critical" (`governance.rs:769,525`) — "NEITHER rationale gate fires on a VisionClaw case." No VisionClaw writer records a declared tier (`enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:382-390`). Two ADR activations (forum ADR-2010, agentbox ADR-2087 B7) wait on a first real high-tier case, and the candidate high-tier record conflict between forum and VisionClaw ADRs is unresolved (ES-05.14 Tension).
- Failure: an operator approves a consequential VisionClaw case with no reasoning; both gates treat it as medium and accept. Because VisionClaw cases always fold to medium, the closure event the pending activations wait for can never come from VisionClaw — the audit invariant ("no surface may fabricate… absence renders as absence") is structurally unenforced for a whole repository's decisions.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN, VERIFIED AT, Tension)

### F-11 — The estate runs two divergent solid-pod-rs releases and four documented versions, with ADR and baseline contradicting each other on whether the current pod is live
- Topics: ES-01.1, ES-08.13
- Evidence: VisionClaw compiles crates.io `0.5.0-alpha.17` (`Cargo.toml:246`); agentbox builds the `0.5.0-alpha.18` workspace from rev `76ab7aab` (`solid-pod-rs.nix:54,58`), which adds `SOLID_POD_JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD` (`:56-58`). Docs disagree: the pod guide says alpha.13 (`solid-pod.md:37`), the baseline says alpha.17 and "Staged until the image is rebuilt" (`BASELINE-container.md:241,246-247`), while ADR-2145 records `complete`/`live` and VisionFlow ADR-2019 `accepted`/`live`; `native-pod-mesh.md` still says alpha.15/alpha.9 with a figure labelled v0.4.0-alpha.17.
- Failure: the same logical pod data is served by two different library versions whose behaviour (WAC evaluation, provisioning, PATCH, the cloud opt-in) may differ between deployment 1 and deployment 2, and an operator remediating or debugging the pod acts on one of four mutually inconsistent version/activation stories.
- Confidence: high (on the divergence and contradictions)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE DIVSP; Tension; Drift)

### F-12 — The compiled federation contract is 51 commits stale on one side, and the parity test that would catch a break runs only on the other side
- Topics: ES-09.10, ES-03.5, ES-09.20
- Evidence: at the VisionClaw stamp the agentbox gitlink is `c3746299e`, "51 commits behind the agentbox stamp c57d9c05… 154 files differ, code as well as docs" (ES-09.10 Drift). VisionClaw bakes `agentbox/schema/federation-kinds.json` into the binary via `include_str!` (`src/uri/mod.rs:665`) from that older submodule; agentbox reads the same file at load from the newer tree. ADR-2061 is recorded `live` but "only its JS half is CI-gated; the Rust half (`cargo test --lib uri`) is not," because `visionclaw-server` sits in the GPU job on the developer's CUDA host (`ci.yml:80-81`, `:258`).
- Failure: a kind-crossing policy change merged on agentbox head is enforced by the agentbox bridge immediately but not by the running VisionClaw binary — identifiers cross one way and are refused or mistranslated the other, and no hosted CI run fails, because only the JS parity fixture is gated.
- Confidence: medium (the skew is stated; whether the artefact itself changed within the 51 commits is not — impact inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift in ES-09.10; Tension in ES-03.5)

### F-13 — The binary agent-event stream throws on every current event: epoch-milliseconds overflow u32
- Topics: ES-02.1
- Evidence: `emitAgentAction` stamps `timestamp: Date.now()` (`agent-event-publisher.js:54`); `createBinaryPayload` writes it with `writeUInt32LE` (`:281`); "a present-day millisecond value exceeds the u32 range, so Node's range check throws and the per-socket `try` logs a send error instead of sending" (`agent-events.js:37-47`). The VisionClaw encoder truncates the same field modulo `u32::MAX` (`binary_protocol.rs:1587`), so even a forced send would disagree with the Rust side's timestamps.
- Failure: any subscriber that selects binary mode receives no agent events at all — every frame dies in a swallowed per-socket exception — and the failure presents as a silent dead stream, not an error surface.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-14 — Pod init returns structure paths with the npub doubled, so first-login clients write pod data into the wrong containers
- Topics: ES-08.5
- Evidence: both structure builders pass a `pod_base` that already ends in the npub (`solid_proxy_handler.rs:975`, `:1155`) to `pod_structure`, which appends the npub again (`:287-288`, called `:1088`, `:1174`), while `pods/check` passes the bare base (`:1264`) — "Init therefore reports `base/npub/npub/...` container paths." The client's `setPreference` writes under the returned structure (ES-08.3), and the Type Index path derivation is already broken against the preferences container (ES-08.7 Debt).
- Failure: every new user's preferences, type registrations and ACL edits land under unintended nested paths that contradict the server's own `pods/check` view — fragmented pod layout from first login, misaligned ACL inheritance, and discovery that silently finds nothing.
- Confidence: high (source-traced; the material says not exercised at runtime)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-15 — Three live sats ledgers, none synced, one deposit route a 501 stub, and the settlement authority class gates nothing
- Topics: ES-03.12, ES-03.11
- Evidence: "the estate runs THREE independent did:nostr-keyed sats ledgers and NONE of them is synced with the others" (`sovereign-settlement.md:61`); the host deposit handler returns 501 "so no value ever entered it" (`pay_handler.rs:202,504,511`); the forum ledger takes real credited outputs by outpoint (`nostr-bbs-pod-worker/payments.rs:10-11`); "the `payment_settlement` authority class is declared today and currently GATES NOTHING" (`economy-loop.md:270`). The proposed unification (ADR-2099 et al.) is decision-proposed across the estate (ES-03.11).
- Failure: any consumer or report that trusts one ledger's balance disagrees with the other two the moment value moves; nothing in the current system reconciles them, and the one gate designed to police settlements has no work, so inconsistent settlement records can be written unchallenged.
- Confidence: high (on the facts as stated; whether meaningful value has moved is not judgeable)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE FACT; OPEN GATE; PROPOSED statuses)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual runtime state.** Many findings turn on "staged / activated on rebuild" boundaries (System One proxy, memory rerank, ADR-2153, ADR-2145). Which images and compose profiles are actually running right now — including whether the loom sidecar or its JEV arm serves anywhere — cannot be read from these files.
2. **The real network exposure.** Whether the standalone cloudflared tunnel is running, and what its Cloudflare dashboard Public Hostname actually maps (explicitly not reviewable from the repo), plus who can reach the ten sanctioned LAN publishes.
3. **External-repository behaviour.** The behavioural delta between solid-pod-rs alpha.17 and alpha.18, and the exploitability of RUSTSEC-2026-0387 in the deployed forum kit `9c36b60` — the material cites versions, not the diffs or the advisory's substance.
4. **Secrets reality.** Actual custody locations, rotation state, the plaintext `.env` contents, and the current host-side Docker socket permissions (the register itself says every custodian and cadence is UNCONFIRMED).
5. **Value and multi-tenant behaviour.** Whether any real sats ever moved through the three ledgers, and whether pods, sessions and scoping actually hold up with a second user or second instance — every traced path is single-operator, single-instance.

## estate / premortem

# Post-mortem — client-loss incident (data / custody / privacy class)

**Frame.** Twelve months on, a client's material was exposed in a way the estate's own records already describe as possible. The exact vector is not yet independently confirmed, so this document ranks the three mechanisms the source material most strongly supports, states confidence per finding, and lists the further latent defects found while investigating. Every claim carries the citation the estate topics give; anything reasoned beyond the material is labelled *inferred*.

---

### R-1 — Anonymous public-internet reads of the estate's graph via the standalone Cloudflare tunnel fronting the demo-open dev posture
- Topics: ES-10.3, ES-10.8, ES-09.8, ES-01.4
- Chain of events:
  1. The everyday stack is the dev service of `docker-compose.unified.yml`, whose shipped defaults are `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS:-1`, `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS:-1`, `RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE:editor` and `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:132,133,140,116`) — the demo-open posture: anonymous `/api` reads enabled, owner-less boot permitted.
  2. The standalone `docker-compose.cloudflared.yml` tunnel is started for remote access. It joins `visionclaw_network`, aliases `visionclaw-server:3001`, and fronts *whatever profile the backend booted* (`docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:3-8`).
  3. Its route is a Cloudflare dashboard **Public Hostname** — "no local config.yml, so the exposed mapping is NOT reviewable from this repo" (`docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:5-6`).
  4. The tunnel is outbound-initiated with no published port, so it is invisible to the ADR-2013 compose port audit, which only sweeps published ports (`docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:6-8`).
  5. The operator declares `VISIONCLAW_INGRESS=lan` believing ingress is closed; ADR-2119 left the standalone file unaffected, so "lan does not stop a hand-started standalone tunnel fronting a LAN-declared host" (ADR-2119-prod-ingress-is-declared-lan-or-tunnel.md:64-66).
  6. Anonymous `/api` reads on the graph — which includes memory-cloud reads of RuVector, agent activity and corpus data — are reachable from the public internet. A client's material is read by a third party.
- Evidence: ES-10.3 states the net posture plainly: "anonymous /api reads reach the public internet via the Cloudflare tunnel, not just the LAN"; the compose defaults invert the fail-closed code defaults (`rbac_gate.rs:141` `unwrap_or(false)` vs `docker-compose.unified.yml:132-133`); the dashboard-configured route is unreviewable and unauditable from the repo; the ingress gap is recorded as OPEN. Tunnels are the norm, not the exception: undeclared prod ingress defaults to `tunnel` (`scripts/launch.sh:177-190`).
- Warning signs visible today: the material already names this exact exposure and the ingress gap as open; the port-audit's structural blindness to non-published paths is recorded (ES-10.8 D5, D6); the shared external bridge joins both stacks and all sidecars (`docker-compose.unified.yml:671-674`, `agentbox/docker-compose.override.yml:165-167`), so the tunnel is an ordinary peer of every service.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism (every step is stated in the material); the fact that a standalone tunnel was actually running at incident time is **inferred**.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Private, LAN-promised corpus content sent to a cloud third party through the System One consumers once `JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD` took effect
- Topics: ES-06.5, ES-06.6, ES-06.1, ES-07.3, ES-09.8, ES-10.8, ES-01.3
- Chain of events:
  1. The Loom's core privacy invariant is explicit: "It delegates ONLY to a LAN/local model, never to a cloud endpoint, so mail content never leaves the LAN" (ES-06.1 INV2), and the façade's own header still justifies local-only Jev operation "by private corpus content" (`loom/crates/loom-facade/src/jev.rs:30-34`).
  2. The shared compose anchor now sets `JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD=1` for VisionClaw, Loom and Codescape, all reaching the agentbox proxy `agentbox:8094`, selected backend TypeSafe (`docker-compose.unified.yml:9,14,387,395,505`); the loom service arms `LOOM_JEV=1` with cloud allowed and gate/rank/support on but **no gate min-confidence, so the gate stays shadow** (`../loom/crates/loom-facade/src/routes/mod.rs:383-384,495-512`).
  3. The memory reranker sends "the query and capped value snippets of the shortlist" to the selected backend — cloud TypeSafe by default, local-only only an opt-in defaulting to 0 (`memory-jev-rerank.js:16-18,107`; `agentbox.toml:894` `local_only` empty; manifest `memory_rerank = true` at `agentbox.toml:914`, staged).
  4. Both activation conditions — the agentbox rebuild and the 2026-10-30 host recreate — are recorded as met, "LIVE" (ADR-2148 close-out, ES-09.8 SYS1).
  5. An `ask_email` query derived from private mail, and memory snippets holding client data, are POSTed to `api.typesafe.ai` (the relay triage is already live there, `wrangler.toml:12-14`). Thresholds are "unset and uncalibrated".
  6. The client learns their material transited a cloud vendor in breach of the privacy posture the email system was built on.
- Evidence: the contradiction is the material's own: the LAN-only invariant and header vs the armed cloud flag; ES-06.6 records the Open question "whether this Jev arm is serving anywhere cannot be read from source"; ES-07.3 records the rerank drift (call site still calls it a LOCAL judge while default sends to cloud).
- Warning signs visible today: the compose flag contradicting the façade header; the gate armed but shadow (nothing withholds); `local_only` empty; System One thresholds uncalibrated; the proxy itself reachable by every container on the shared bridge (ES-10.8 D6), so even consumer-side gates can be bypassed.
- Confidence: medium — config armed and activation recorded as live, but which payloads reach the backend per consumer, and whether the loom profile is serving, is not determinable from source; the exfiltration step is **inferred**.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — A deletion request "completed" on the pod while the RuVector embedding row stays live and semantically searchable
- Topics: ES-08.9, ES-07.9, ES-08.8, ES-07.3
- Chain of events:
  1. A client exercises erasure. The operator runs `deleteAgentMemory` in the client (`SolidPodService.ts:366`).
  2. `agentMemory.ts:191-199` deletes the pod resource via `ldpClient.deleteResource` (`ldpClient.ts:202-203`); a 404 is treated as success (`ldpClient.ts:205-210`).
  3. The call graph **never reaches RuVector** — "no reverse-tombstone dispatch to `mcp claude-flow memory_delete` or equivalent".
  4. The embedding row persists and remains searchable through the HNSW index (`ruvector-mcp.cjs` memory_search path).
  5. A subsequent agent `memory_search` surfaces the deleted client content in an answer; the content leaks into a deliverable or is found on audit. There is no point-in-time RuVector backup, no cross-store consistent restore, no RPO/RTO (ES-07.9 D5, ES-08.8 FATAL note).
  6. Invariant 6 of DATA-authority-erasure ("a dropped secondary write must be logged, not swallowed") is violated by omission: the secondary write is never even attempted, so nothing logs its absence.
- Evidence: ES-08.9 states the gap flatly; ES-07.9 D5 calls it the "Largest erasure hole"; the historical governing document records it under Known divergences; the pod store itself has no point-in-time backup (`backup-sqlite.sh` covers SQLite only).
- Warning signs visible today: the material labels this divergence at two layers and records that no joined key-to-row lookup or post-delete search was ever performed.
- Confidence: high — the gap is stated outright at every layer; only the discovery route is **inferred**.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Agentbox applies governance decisions the forum has already closed as expired
- Topics: ES-13.1
- Evidence: the orchestrator's promote path "checks request tag and IRI, then applies without reading projection receipt or expiry" (`local-process-manager.js:419,444,461`); "no non-test agentbox source reads `stale_after`"; expiry closes cases at `cron.rs:764,794,834`; the tension is drawn at `relay-consumer.js:497`.
- Failure: a human lets a proposal lapse (a decision *not* to approve); the forum closes it expired; agentbox promotes the ontology change anyway; the corpus mutates on an unapproved change.
- Confidence: high (signed expired-proposal fixtures verified the gap).
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — A restart checkpoint swallows an unfinished human decision
- Topics: ES-13.2
- Evidence: events are added to the shared `seen` map before dispatch (`relay-consumer.js:464`); a settling handler persists the *whole* map (`:470,395`); redelivery is rejected as duplicate (`:459`); the `settle` comment's crash-safety claim holds only for the running event (`:466-469`).
- Failure: decision B is mid-handler when the process stops; B was already persisted as seen by A's settle; on restart B is rejected and a human's approved decision is silently never applied.
- Confidence: high (a deterministic snapshot/restore fixture reproduced the suppression).
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension; Drift on the comment)
- Severity: high

### F-03 — Neither rationale gate can fire on a VisionClaw case, because nothing declares a tier
- Topics: ES-05.12, ES-05.13
- Evidence: VisionClaw's 31400/31402 carry no tier or task-property field (`events.rs:211-217,91-101,308-323`; `elevation_actor.rs:239`); the relay folds every VisionClaw case to `medium` (`governance.rs:525`, `wrangler.toml:61`); `check_rationale` bites only at high/critical (`governance.rs:769`; `enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:396-409`).
- Failure: an operator approves a high-impact case with a vacuous rationale; both the host gate and the relay gate pass because the effective tier reads medium; the record's "no fabricated rationale" invariant is unenforced for this whole surface.
- Confidence: high (verified at the stamp).
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — Same-UID secret sprawl: any devuser process holds every credential and can stamp any identity
- Topics: ES-04.1, ES-10.12, ES-05.10, ES-04.5
- Evidence: a process inside the agentbox container "can still stamp any pubkey over loopback" (`agent-event-auth.js:36-39`, TODO-unified X-1); with `role_isolation` off (shipped default) the entrypoint widens the Docker socket `o+rw` for devuser (`entrypoint-unified.sh:608-612`) and "PID 1's environment, .env included, reaches all" devuser programs; the session-plane ACL authorises a UID, not a process (`session-plane-acl.json:6`, `api.rs:32`).
- Failure: one injected or compromised agent process — and the estate feeds agents untrusted email — reads the bridge key and API keys from the environment, forges `did:nostr` attribution on events and the task journal, and exfiltrates keys.
- Confidence: high on the boundary (stated); the exploit step is **inferred**.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt / Divergence)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — Balances can exist the chain does not know about: credit/debit still public, three unsynced ledgers
- Topics: ES-03.12, ES-08.12, ES-03.11
- Evidence: ADR-2099 D4/D5 not built; credit/debit remain in the API and `credit_by_outpoint` is public (`solid-pod-rs/src/payments.rs:565,579,450`); three independent did:nostr-keyed sats ledgers, none synced (`sovereign-settlement.md:61`); the host deposit handler is a 501 stub (`pay_handler.rs:202,504,511`); `payment_settlement` "currently GATES NOTHING" (`economy-loop.md:270`).
- Failure: once any real value moves, a client is shown or pays against a balance that no chain corroborates, or sats credited in one ledger are absent from the others with no reconciliation path.
- Confidence: medium — the gap is certain; a value-bearing trigger is **inferred**.
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence / Open)
- Severity: high

### F-06 — The production forum edge runs a kit with an unfixed disclosed crypto vulnerability
- Topics: ES-05.14
- Evidence: forum main `c39f41f` is fixed for RUSTSEC-2026-0387 (k256 0.14.0), but the website's Workers deploy pins kit `9c36b603` with crypto-bigint 0.5.5 and k256 0.13.4 (`dreamlab-ai-website/.github/workflows/workers-deploy.yml:44`) — "main is fixed and the edge is not."
- Failure: the disclosed k256 vulnerability is exploitable against the live public relay worker until the pin moves.
- Confidence: high (stated); the exploit itself is **inferred**.
- Marked by authors: no (stated as current fact, not flagged as a marker)
- Severity: high

### F-07 — PATCH against a Turtle/JSON-LD pod resource silently drops all prior content
- Topics: ES-08.6
- Evidence: `parse_ntriples` errors on non-N-Triples (`ldp.rs:817-828`); the handler replaces the error with an **empty graph** (`solid_proxy_handler.rs:786-789`) and stores only the patch's triples (`:805-812`); the client writes JSON-LD/Turtle by default (`ldpClient.ts:153-157`).
- Failure: any standard Solid client issues a routine PATCH to a pod resource; the previous content is destroyed and replaced by the patch's own triples, with a 200/ETag.
- Confidence: medium (traced in source, not reproduced; no in-repo caller sends PATCH).
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)
- Severity: high

### F-08 — The System One proxy is reachable and spendable by every container on the shared bridge
- Topics: ES-10.8, ES-01.3, ES-01.4
- Evidence: `network = true` binds `0.0.0.0` inside the container, never published — "any container on visionclaw_network can reach it and spend the vendor key, an accepted consequence" (ADR-2139:298; `agentbox.toml:892-893`); it refuses only an Origin header or non-JSON content type (`server.rs:151-169`) — a non-browser client sends no Origin; the bridge is external and joins both stacks and all sidecars (`docker-compose.unified.yml:671-674`).
- Failure: any peer container (sidecar, compromised service) calls the proxy directly, spending the vendor key and pushing private payloads to the cloud backend, bypassing every consumer-side gate.
- Confidence: high (mechanism stated); targeting is **inferred**.
- Marked by authors: yes (documented as accepted; listener note)
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — The agent-events ingest socket is triply defective: dead forward, no credential, bearer in URL
- Topics: ES-02.2, ES-02.1, ES-03.1
- Evidence: the subscriber sends the bare event, not the `jsonrpc`/`method`/`params` envelope the Rust schema requires (`agent-event-ws-subscriber.js:144-151` vs `schema.rs:26-29`), so every frame is `malformed_json` (`ingest.rs:101,149,229-231`) — "no forwarded frame from this path reaches the hub"; the subscriber presents no token (`agent-event-ws-subscriber.js:94`; `server.js:383`), so release answers 401 (`ingest.rs:291-292`); the socket keeps `?token=` in every build (`ingest.rs:267-271`) unlike ADR-2090's compiled-out query carrier elsewhere (`mcp_relay_handler.rs:482,496`); and the binary branch overflows `writeUInt32LE` on current epoch-ms (`agent-event-publisher.js:54,281`).
- Failure: the cross-container agent-action path silently produces no provenance and no beams; any operator "fix" using `?token=` leaks the bearer into access/proxy logs and Referer.
- Confidence: high (each defect stated).
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift ×2, Tension, Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — Production has no backend restart path, and one production target's backend cannot start at all
- Topics: ES-09.7
- Evidence: the compose production path runs the backend in the background under `production-startup.sh` with nginx foreground and no supervisor (`production-startup.sh:38-41,68-70`); `restart: unless-stopped` never fires because nginx keeps the container alive (`docker-compose.unified.yml:323`); separately, `supervisord.production.conf:29` runs `/app/target/release/visionclaw-server` while the unified production image places the binary at `/app/visionclaw-server` (`Dockerfile.unified:406`, WORKDIR `:393`).
- Failure: a 3 a.m. backend crash leaves static pages and indefinite API 502s until a human intervenes; or the unified production target, if launched, never starts its backend.
- Confidence: high for the restart gap (from source); medium for the path bug (traced, not run).
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence; Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — Secrets at rest are plaintext or unencrypted, and neither pod nor memory store has a restorable backup
- Topics: ES-10.10, ES-08.8, ES-07.9
- Evidence: VisionClaw `backup-secrets.sh` writes an ordinary ZIP with integrity check, "without explicit encryption"; SOPS was accepted (ADR-109) but never executed — `.env` is plaintext today; no point-in-time pod backup (`backup-sqlite.sh` is SQLite-only), no cross-store consistent restore, no RPO/RTO; no RuVector point-in-time backup.
- Failure: disk loss or host compromise yields plaintext credentials, and neither the pod nor the memory store can be restored to a consistent point — compounding any erasure or corruption event.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — Stored memory can be invisible to search: raw-SQL rows bypass the embedding pipeline, and values are capped at ~512 tokens
- Topics: ES-07.1, ES-07.2
- Evidence: "The claude-flow CLI and raw SQL INSERT bypass the embedding pipeline, so rows written that way are INVISIBLE to HNSW search" (ES-07.1); bge-small embeds only the first ~512 tokens / ~2,500 chars of a value, so "the tail is invisible to search" (ES-07.2).
- Failure: a critical fact recorded in a long value or via a bypassing writer is silently absent from every semantic search; agents decide in ignorance of stored data.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: no (stated as invariant/notes, not flagged as a defect marker)
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — The Solid-pod deployments no longer share one release, and three documents name three others
- Topics: ES-01.1, ES-08.13, ES-08.1
- Evidence: VisionClaw embeds crates.io `0.5.0-alpha.17` (`Cargo.toml:246`); agentbox builds the `0.5.0-alpha.18` workspace at rev `76ab7aab` adding `SOLID_POD_JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD` (`solid-pod-rs.nix:54,56-58`); docs cite alpha.9 (`native-pod-mesh.md:3-4`), alpha.13 (`solid-pod.md:37`) and alpha.17 (`BASELINE-container.md:241`).
- Failure: two divergent pod implementations serve the same protocol; a fix (e.g. the ADR-2008 rust-bitcoin port) present in one is absent in the other; an operator debugging from the docs works against the wrong version.
- Confidence: high (skew is stated).
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence / Doc-drift)
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — Client pod path computations land data in the wrong places
- Topics: ES-08.5, ES-08.7
- Evidence: `pod_structure` appends the npub to a `pod_base` that already ends in it (`solid_proxy_handler.rs:287-288` called at `:1088,:1174`; bases built `:975,:1155`), so init reports `base/npub/npub/...`; `ensurePublicTypeIndex` cuts at the first `/settings/`, but the caller passes the preferences container, which has no such segment — `indexOf` returns -1 and the Type Index lands at a nine-character prefix (`typeIndex.ts:70-73`; `SolidPodService.ts:447`; `solid_proxy_handler.rs:294`).
- Failure: preferences and type registrations are written outside their intended containers; the public Type Index is published at a nonsense path; discovery and ACLs point at the wrong resources.
- Confidence: medium (source-traced, unexercised).
- Marked by authors: yes (Open; Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — A crafted `stale_after` panics the relay's proposal admission path
- Topics: ES-13.1
- Evidence: `parse_rfc3339_utc` byte-slices `s[..19]` / `&s[19..]` (`ontology_governance.rs:282,289,313,330`); a multibyte character at byte 19 panics "instead of returning None", on the admission path (`nip_handlers.rs:417`), where the doc comment promises `None` for anything unparsable (`:246-250`); `s.get(..19)` closes it.
- Failure: any client publishes a proposal whose deadline string places a multibyte character at the boundary; the relay worker panics on admission; repeated publishes degrade governance ingestion.
- Confidence: medium (source reading; no panic reproduced).
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

## nostr-rust-forum / critical

## F-01 — Recovery sheet exports injectable HTML containing the master key
- Topics: NF-22.2, NF-05.8
- Evidence: The signup flow's display name is checked only for trimmed byte length 2–50, "HTML content not rejected" (`signup.rs:241`). The `format!` call at `recovery_sheet.rs:138` builds the downloadable HTML with "raw display name, identity and recovery text." The audit states "Chrome executes the synthetic display-name marker in this formatter output" and calls it "active-content injection in an exported secret-bearing document."
- Failure: A user who registers with `<script>` in their display name downloads a recovery HTML document that, when opened, executes injected JavaScript with access to the master nsec rendered on the same page. Any script running in that document can exfiltrate the key or rewrite the visible content.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (described in a linked review, no Drift/Debt/Open marker in the topic itself)

## F-02 — Kind-41 channel metadata bypasses the zone gate on live broadcast but not on history
- Topics: NF-17.3, NF-11.5
- Evidence: The history path withholds kind-41 from non-members in hidden zones (`nip_handlers.rs:2354`). The live broadcast path's `needs_gate` includes kinds 40, 42, calendar and kanban but "omits 41" (`broadcast.rs:76`), and kind-41 "skips zone context, matching subscription gets original event" (`broadcast.rs:162`). The audit confirms "the unchanged native dispatcher sends kind 41 despite a fixture authoriser that would withhold."
- Failure: A non-member who subscribes with a kindless or kind-41 filter receives hidden-zone channel metadata (name, description, topic list) in real time, while the same request for history would deny it. This is a live information disclosure that the read-side gate was built to prevent.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (presented as routing evidence, not flagged as a defect)

## F-03 — Revoked device keys continue to authenticate at the relay with no check
- Topics: NF-02.7, NF-20.4, NF-10.7
- Evidence: `handle_auth` "stores raw public key, does not consult device registry" (`nip_handlers.rs:2069`). The resolution to owner happens only later "through a 60-second cache" (`nip_handlers.rs:2614`). NF-10.7 S4 says DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED ships false so the "whole ADR-099/100 device story is dormant by default and revocation has no effect at AUTH." Even when enabled, `effective_pubkey` returns the raw key with "no explicit revoked-key rejection here" (`nip_handlers.rs:974`).
- Failure: An owner revokes a lost phone's device key. The phone's WebSocket session continues to authenticate, and after cache expiry the next AUTH still succeeds with the raw key. Whether the revoked key can publish depends on downstream gates, not on revocation itself.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (NF-10.7 S4 "Debt" for the dormant default; the AUTH-never-checks gap is noted in NF-20.4 without a marker)

## F-04 — A by-the-book deployment has no static admin bootstrap on the relay or auth workers
- Topics: NF-08.7, NF-07.2
- Evidence: ADMIN_PUBKEYS "is read by auth and relay but declared in neither template and named in no SETUP step" (`SETUP.md:147`). The relay template's comment "asserts the opposite: there is no ADMIN_PUBKEYS reader in src/" (`wrangler.toml:18-21`) while `auth.rs:185` reads it. NF-08.7 N5 states the consequence: "a by-the-book deployment has NO static admin bootstrap on relay or auth."
- Failure: An operator follows SETUP.md exactly. The relay and auth workers have no admin configured. The first member to claim a username cannot be promoted, no admin can invite, and the instance is locked out of its own governance. Recovery requires manual D1 surgery outside the documented procedure.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (NF-08.7 N4 "DOC-DRIFT", N5 states the consequence)

## F-05 — Passkey registration is non-functional: both clients omit the required NIP-98 header
- Topics: NF-23.1, NF-02.3
- Evidence: The forum client's registration verify uses `fetch_json_post` which sends "JSON request has Content-Type, no NIP-98 Authorization" (`passkey.rs:173`, `http.rs:156`). The BBS client does the same (`passkey.rs:356`). The worker's `register_verify` returns 401 "before attestation validation" when the header is missing (`webauthn.rs:836`).
- Failure: No user can register a passkey on either client. The entire passkey-based identity system is unreachable by normal users in production. Any claim that passkey onboarding works is false until the clients are fixed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated as fact in NF-23.1; no Drift/Debt/Open marker in the topic)

## F-06 — Passkey login derives a different key than registration: same-identity guarantee broken
- Topics: NF-23.2, NF-05.3
- Evidence: Registration options generate a random salt A (`webauthn.rs:739`). The client derives using PRF output and encoded A as HKDF salt (`passkey.rs:153`). The verify step "ignores submitted A, hashes server secret, credential ID and pubkey into B" (`webauthn.rs:1021`). Login options returns stored B (`webauthn.rs:1128`). Login derives with B (`passkey.rs:264`). The review states "same-identity guarantee is broken."
- Failure: A user registers a passkey and successfully creates an account. They log out and log back in. The derived key is different, so they see a completely different identity with no data, no membership, and no access. Every login is a new user from the system's perspective.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated in review; no marker in the topic itself)

## F-07 — Sealed-history purge marks entries "Purged" even when the relay's deletion was incomplete
- Topics: NF-26.3
- Evidence: After asking the relay to delete original IDs, "every ID not skipped becomes Purged, including an unaccounted-for ID" (`engine.rs:499`). "Deleted-count mismatch only warns" (`engine.rs:503`). Later verification "excludes Purged entries" so "zero checks can report no failures" (`engine.rs:366`).
- Failure: An operator runs the migration tool to seal and purge plaintext history. Three events fail to delete on the relay but are marked Purged. The verification pass reports zero failures. Plaintext messages remain on the relay indefinitely, invisible to the operator who believes the migration is complete.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (review finding; no marker)

## F-08 — NIP-98 replay protection fails open when D1 omits write metadata
- Topics: NF-02.5
- Evidence: "when D1 returns no write metadata the store answers first-seen, so the replay check fails OPEN on that path" (`replay.rs:37-39`). The trait contract says `true` on first observation (`nip98.rs:94-95`). The full signature check runs first (`nip98.rs:576`), but a valid captured token could be replayed within its freshness window.
- Failure: An attacker captures a valid NIP-98 token from network traffic. They replay it on a request where D1 omits the metadata. The signature, URL, method and payload all check. The replay store says "first seen." The attacker performs an authenticated action as the victim.
- Confidence: medium (whether D1 ever omits metadata is "runtime behaviour, not settled by source")
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes ("Open" in NF-02.5)

## F-09 — Promotion write can overwrite a concurrent TL3 admin grant
- Topics: NF-24.3, NF-03.8
- Evidence: `check_promotion` reads a TL0 row with qualifying counters (`trust.rs:207`), snapshots it, computes TL2 (`trust.rs:221`), then "UPDATE by pubkey only, writes TL2" (`trust.rs:238`). If a concurrent admin grants TL3 between the read and the write, the promotion overwrites it. The audit states "execution errors are ignored, returns planned level" (`trust.rs:268`).
- Failure: An admin manually promotes a trusted moderator to TL3. Simultaneously, the activity ledger's promotion check fires for the same user and writes TL2. The moderator's admin-granted authority is silently stripped. No error surfaces because the promotion path discards the write result.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (review finding; no marker in topic)

## F-10 — SSRF guard is denylist-only by default, acknowledged DNS-rebinding-vulnerable
- Topics: NF-07.6, NF-10.7
- Evidence: "without PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS the guard is denylist-only and DNS-rebinding-vulnerable" (`ssrf.rs:13`). "The template does not set it — wrangler.toml:13 declares only ALLOWED_ORIGIN" (NF-07.6 N4). The code itself says "the Workers runtime exposes no resolve-then-pin primitive." There is "NO wall-clock timeout" (N3).
- Failure: An attacker crafts a URL pointing at a DNS name that resolves first to a public IP (passing the denylist) then to 169.254.169.254 on subsequent resolution. The preview worker fetches cloud metadata or internal services. Without a timeout, a slow-loris target also ties up Worker CPU.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (NF-10.7 S3, NF-07.6 N4 "DIVERGENCE")

## F-11 — Zone key rotation leaves stale writers publishing under old epochs with no relay check
- Topics: NF-27.1, NF-08.9
- Evidence: Rotation sends "grants to remaining eligible members, no shared epoch activation barrier" (`zone_encryption.rs:273`). A stale writer "still holds older latest key" (`store.rs:359`). The relay "checks ciphertext and zk shape, no current epoch or key binding here" (`zone_config.rs:208`).
- Failure: An admin rotates a zone key to exclude a departing member. The departing member's browser continues writing with the old key. The relay accepts these events because the ciphertext shape check passes. New messages split across two epochs, and some members cannot read content written under the key they no longer hold.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (review finding; no marker)

## F-12 — Invite revocation sends the wrong identifier: always returns 404
- Topics: NF-25.4
- Evidence: The invite is created with an 8-character ID and a 16-character code (`invites.rs:361`). The client sends the code in the revoke URL (`admin/invites.rs:175`). The handler looks up "WHERE id = input" (`invites.rs:733`). "No row: 404 Invite not found" (`invites.rs:742`).
- Failure: An admin tries to revoke an invite that has been leaked. The revoke always fails with 404 because the code sent does not match the ID expected. The invite remains active and redeemable. The operator cannot disable a compromised invite through the UI.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (review finding; no marker)

## F-13 — Search worker template ships a real, non-placeholder admin pubkey
- Topics: NF-07.2, NF-08.7
- Evidence: "It is also the only template shipping a non-placeholder value where every other template ships example.com or a zeroed key" (`wrangler.toml:41`). The search worker reads this as its "ONLY admin source" (`auth.rs:54`). `/ingest` is gated by this key (`auth.rs:86`).
- Failure: An operator deploys without changing the template value. The hardcoded pubkey's owner can ingest arbitrary content into the search index, making it visible to all users as search results. The operator has no way to know who this admin is or revoke them without discovering the configuration issue.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — depends on whether deployments actually use the template value unchanged)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as fact; no marker)

## F-14 — Every admin mutation silently discards its audit write result
- Topics: NF-11.15, NF-03.10
- Evidence: "every call discards the result, so a failed audit write never fails the mutation" (`user_admin.rs:229`, `user_admin.rs:307`, `user_admin.rs:370`, `user_admin.rs:465`, `user_admin.rs:625`, `moderation.rs:351`). The code is `let _ = audit::log_admin_action(`.
- Failure: An admin suspends a user. The audit write fails due to a transient D1 error. The suspension succeeds. The admin_log table has no record of who did what. During a dispute or investigation, the operator cannot reconstruct which admin performed the action.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes ("Debt" in NF-11.15)

## F-15 — Wallet send confirmation shows a constant fee, not the actual built fee
- Topics: NF-16.2
- Evidence: "Review shows a fixed about-250-sat hint, not the built transaction fee" (`wallet.rs:598`). The builder calculates an actual fee — "native synthetic fragmented-input pack: 21 inputs, 1500-sat fee" (`chain.rs:710`). The asset-view check "runs before return, local signing may already have occurred" (`chain.rs:726`).
- Failure: A user with many UTXOs sees "about 250 sats" on the confirmation screen. The actual transaction costs 1500 sats. The user approves based on the displayed fee and pays six times more than shown. In a fragmented-wallet scenario the discrepancy is larger and compounds across sends.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (review finding; no marker)

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Whether D1 actually omits write metadata in production**, which determines whether the NIP-98 replay fail-open (F-08) is reachable. Requires a live D1 test with the exact `INSERT OR IGNORE` used.

2. **Whether the search-worker template's real admin pubkey (F-13) is changed at deploy time.** Need to see actual deployed wrangler.toml configurations or the deployment pipeline's substitution logic.

3. **Whether kind-41 live broadcast bypass (F-02) is exploitable through a real relay subscription.** The evidence is from a native fixture; need a live relay with a hidden zone and a non-member subscriber to confirm.

4. **The actual PRF behavior of deployed authenticators**, which determines whether the passkey salt mismatch (F-06) produces a different key on every real login or only in the tested synthetic conditions.

5. **The state of production D1 schema versus migrations**, which determines whether the missing `broker_decisions.superseded_by` column (KF-13) and other migration/bootstrap gaps are present in the running deployment.

## nostr-rust-forum / premortem

# Post-mortem — nostr-rust-forum kit production incident (12 months on)

Written against the material as stamped. Where I reason past what the diagrams assert, I say so. Every item below is checkable against the cited `path:line`.

---

### R-1 — Sealed-history migration reports success while plaintext channel history survives on the relay
- Topics: NF-26.1, NF-26.3, NF-26.4, NF-26.6
- Chain of events:
  1. Operator runs `nostr-bbs-zone-migrate` (ADR-2017) to move a confidential plaintext channel into an encrypted zone.
  2. Discovery hits a page where a full page adds no new IDs; the migrator records an "ambiguous second" and steps past it (`relay.rs:167`), carrying `incomplete_seconds` in the plan (`engine.rs:97`).
  3. Sealing proceeds anyway — it "records discovered originals, no completeness refusal" (`engine.rs:179`). Plaintext rows outside the discovered set are never sealed.
  4. Purge requires only that state be non-empty and all entries Verified or Purged (`engine.rs:424`); every ID "not skipped becomes Purged — including an unaccounted-for ID" (`engine.rs:499`), and a deleted-count mismatch from the relay "only warns" (`engine.rs:503`).
  5. Later verification excludes Purged entries, so zero checks report no failures (`engine.rs:366`). The CLI prints a console warning (`cli.rs:462`) that no operator dashboard consumes.
  6. The operator certifies to the client that the channel is now end-to-end encrypted and the plaintext purged.
  7. Months later the original plaintext kind-42 rows are still on the relay with unchanged ids and created_at; the public sealed tags still name the original IDs, enabling correlation (`sealed.rs:483`). Anyone holding a read cohort queries them directly. The client learns the promised erasure never happened.
- Evidence: `../nostr-rust-forum/crates/nostr-bbs-zone-migrate/src/relay.rs:144`, `:167`; `engine.rs:97`, `:179`, `:424`, `:499`, `:503`, `:366`; `cli.rs:462`; `plan.rs:115` (already-sealed count means only a marker was found; adoption must still open and verify); `../nostr-rust-forum/crates/nostr-bbs-core/src/sealed.rs:483`; the delete endpoint itself is silent per-ID with no tombstone or broadcast (`../nostr-rust-forum/crates/nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/admin_events.rs:246`) and its audit receipt discards logger errors (`:294`, `:307`). The section's own business note: "A single success counter does not establish all four."
- Warning signs visible today: the audit text already states "recorded-set success can leave plaintext undiscovered" and "zero checks can report no failures"; the relay's ciphertext check is shape-only and "cannot authenticate ciphertext or guarantee content secrecy" (`zone_config.rs:199-207`, NF-03.12 N5); `admin_events.rs:246` deletes with no kind-5 and no broadcast, so nothing downstream ever contradicts the migration report.
- Confidence: high that the code permits false completion (fully cited); that this exact run triggered the incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Script injected via display name executes inside the exported recovery sheet and steals the master key
- Topics: NF-22.2, NF-22.4, NF-05.8, NF-12.1
- Chain of events:
  1. A member's display name is validated only for trimmed byte length, 2–50; HTML content is not rejected (`signup.rs:241`).
  2. The original display-name signal is passed to RecoverySheet (`signup.rs:645`) and interpolated raw by `format!` alongside identity and recovery text (`recovery_sheet.rs:138`).
  3. The document is written as a JS Blob/object URL and downloaded (`recovery_sheet.rs:229`). The same document carries the master nsec as text and the live-origin connect URL with the key in its fragment (`recovery_sheet.rs:288`), and the tear-off card keeps the master link in DOM/print layout (`:590`).
  4. Chrome executes a script marker placed in the display name in this formatter output (demonstrated with a synthetic marker in the audit).
  5. Executing script shares the document with the key material; it reads the nsec/fragment from the DOM and exfiltrates it. The QR/SVG path is separate from escaping (`recovery_sheet.rs:294`), so the QR does not save the situation.
  6. The member believes the sheet is the sole copy; the attacker holds a second. Account takeover follows.
- Evidence: `../nostr-rust-forum/crates/nostr-bbs-forum-client/src/pages/signup.rs:241`, `:645`; `../nostr-rust-forum/crates/nostr-bbs-forum-client/src/components/recovery_sheet.rs:138`, `:229`, `:288`, `:294`, `:590`; audit sentence "Chrome executes the synthetic display-name marker in this formatter output"; the audit's own limit "remote delivery and real-secret exfiltration were not demonstrated."
- Warning signs visible today: the review already labels it "active-content injection in an exported secret-bearing document"; the sibling gate latches attempted output and a ticked box rather than a real backup (NF-22.3, `recovery_sheet.rs:303`, `signup.rs:674`), so members' custody rests on exactly this one document.
- Confidence: high for the injection (demonstrated); the exfiltration-to-incident step is inferred (script and key are in the same document, but no exfil was demonstrated).
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Removing an administrator on the relay leaves their auth-worker admin authority intact; they re-grant themselves
- Topics: NF-25.3, NF-08.7, NF-02.6, NF-06.9
- Chain of events:
  1. Operator demotes an admin via the members UI, which sends `set-admin false` to the relay endpoint (`admin/mod.rs:439`); `whitelist.is_admin` becomes 0 (`whitelist.rs:450`).
  2. The relay's dynamic admin query now returns false (`relay-worker/src/auth.rs:194`). The operator believes removal is complete.
  3. The auth worker's resolver unions static + RELAY_DB whitelist + auth DB `members` (`admin.rs:57`); a failed or missing relay lookup falls through to members (`admin.rs:90`), where `is_admin` is still 1.
  4. The ousted admin still passes `require_admin` on every auth-only route: admins add (`admins.rs:161`), governance register/provision (`governance_api.rs:362`, `:441`), roles grant (`:670`), admin registrations (`username.rs:689`), reviewer telemetry (`governance_api.rs:1407`).
  5. They add a new admin row or register/provision an agent, re-creating whitelist authority on the relay too. Removal was never effective at any layer.
- Evidence: NF-08.7 N3C labels this "REVOCATION GAP: relay set-admin changes only whitelist (`whitelist.rs:450`); Auth members can retain its grant (`admin.rs:90`)"; N3 records that the 66ca8d8 closure only removed the relay's members query; N4/N5: `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` is declared in no relay/auth template and named in no SETUP step, so an operator has no documented lever to remove static authority either. NF-25.3's fixture "executes the write and both store queries"; "an error in one source is not a denial from the union."
- Warning signs visible today: the doc comment on `query_is_admin` still names members (`relay-worker/src/auth.rs:174`, N3B drift); the search template meanwhile ships a real default admin pubkey (`search-worker/wrangler.toml:41`, NF-07.2), showing the deployment story around admin authority is already inconsistent across workers.
- Confidence: high — the split is directly evidenced by executed fixture and cited code; that this path fired in the incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Preview worker SSRF guard is denylist-only in any by-the-book deploy, with no wall-clock timeout
- Topics: NF-07.6, NF-07.5, NF-10.7 (S3)
- Evidence: the code acknowledges "the Workers runtime exposes no resolve-then-pin primitive, so without PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS the guard is denylist-only and DNS-rebinding-vulnerable" (`ssrf.rs:13`); the template declares only `ALLOWED_ORIGIN` (`preview-worker/wrangler.toml:13`); "there is NO wall-clock timeout — only redirect-count and body-size limits bound a fetch" (N3).
- Failure: a member posts a link whose hostname re-binds to an internal address after the first resolve; the per-hop policy re-check sees an innocent hostname while egress hits an internal service; the unfurl renders internal response metadata back to the requester. Separately, a slow-drip origin pins Worker invocations because nothing bounds fetch time.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, acknowledged in-code)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Both clients omit the NIP-98 header registration requires, so passkey signup 401s for everyone
- Topics: NF-23.1, NF-02.3, NF-05.3
- Evidence: forum client posts plain JSON (`passkey.rs:173`; `http.rs:156` — Content-Type only, no Authorization); BBS client likewise (`bbs-client/src/passkey.rs:356`); the worker requires a NIP-98 header and matching author (`webauthn.rs:836`, `:849`) and returns 401 before attestation validation.
- Failure: every passkey registration is rejected with 401; no passkey account can be created on either client; the operator's onboarding funnel for the flagship identity path is dead and support cannot see why (the failure is pre-attestation, so nothing about WebAuthn appears in logs).
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-03 — Registration stores a different PRF salt than it issued, so login can never derive the same identity key
- Topics: NF-23.2, NF-02.3, NF-02.4, NF-05.3
- Evidence: options issues random salt A (`webauthn.rs:739`); verify ignores the submitted salt and hashes a server secret + credential id + pubkey into B (`webauthn.rs:1021`); login options returns B (`webauthn.rs:1128`); the client derives at registration with A (`passkey.rs:153`) and at login with the returned salt (`passkey.rs:264`). Native controls prove different roots for A/B even with identical PRF output.
- Failure: the moment the F-02 header defect is repaired (or any other path completes registration), every passkey member who logs out can never log back into the same pubkey — permanent, silent custody loss of every passkey account, with the stranded keys' posts and DMs orphaned.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-04 — Device-key controls are unusable when they matter: the list parser rejects every populated list, revocation never touches AUTH, and caches outlive the write
- Topics: NF-20.2, NF-20.4, NF-20.5, NF-20.3, NF-02.7
- Evidence: server rows use f64 for `created_at`/`revoked` (`devices.rs:326`) and serialise them (`:470`); the client parser expects u64/u8 and a non-empty server list fails (`utils/devices.rs:264`) — five native contract tests confirm. AUTH stores the raw public key (`nip_handlers.rs:2069`) and persists it (`session.rs:334`); the registry query selects revoked=0 (`nip_handlers.rs:2596`) with "no explicit revoked-key rejection" at `:974`; the auth-worker revoke writes its D1 row with no relay cache invalidation, and owner lookups are cached 60s including negatives (`read_cache.rs:73`); an existing device row can be re-registered to a new owner with revoked reset (`devices.rs:416`) while revoke matches the current owner only (`:511`).
- Failure: a member loses their phone, opens the device list — it errors precisely because devices exist. Even after a direct revoke, the lost phone's existing authenticated session keeps its raw-key identity (AUTH unaffected), cached viewer contexts keep owner-derived privileges up to 60s, and if the device was re-registered the original owner's revoke matches no row.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-05 — The search template ships a real default admin pubkey; any deploy that doesn't override grants that key admin of ingest
- Topics: NF-07.2, NF-08.7, NF-05.14
- Evidence: `search-worker/wrangler.toml:41` ships a real 64-hex pubkey, "the only non-placeholder value where every other template ships example.com or a zeroed key" (N3); the worker reads it as its only admin source (`auth.rs:54`) and enforces it at ingest (`auth.rs:86`).
- Failure: an operator deploys by the book; whoever holds that key can POST /ingest, indexing arbitrary labelled "public" content; since the client consults the worker in semantic mode or when relay text search returns nothing (`global_search.rs:336-360`), an unowned party can shape what members find.
- Confidence: high (fact); exploit consequence inferred
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-06 — BBS backup exports the stored forum key, not the active account's key
- Topics: NF-19.5, NF-19.1, NF-05.11
- Evidence: backup is offered when `local_secret_hex` returns a value (`screens.rs:1972`), requires only "some active public key" (`signer.rs:291`, `:295`), and reads forum storage with no comparison to the active public key; a generated key is returned once and never updates forum storage (`signer.rs:147`).
- Failure: forum key A remains stored while local signer B is installed; the member backs up from BBS, exports A while the screen names B, and never writes B down. Storage cleared — account B is unrecoverable. Conversely an in-memory pasted key has no backup route when forum storage is empty.
- Confidence: high (source-traced condition; browser reproduction remains open per the review)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-07 — Governance read endpoints are effectively anonymous and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate is reconstructable
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-2, KF-2a), NF-06.10, NF-02.2
- Evidence: "the governance READ endpoints verify a NIP-98 signature with no membership check, so authenticated is effectively anonymous, and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read" (`known-findings.md:23`, `:24`) — an operator decision, taken once for both.
- Failure: anyone (key generation is free) enumerates all cases and decisions, including moderation-sensitive subjects, and diffs the decisions read over time to reconstruct who reviewed what — reviewer privacy and case-subject confidentiality are unenforceable.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — Core aggregate guards compare pubkeys case-sensitively while the same key verifiably presents in two casings
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-6), NF-06.15, NF-06.6
- Evidence: "the self-review and original-signer guards compare pubkeys case-SENSITIVELY while NIP-98 returns the pubkey verbatim" (`known-findings.md:29`); the same key "can legitimately present in two casings and both verify" (`governance_api.rs:98-100`); the registry side already fixed this class via `canonical_agent_pubkey` (`governance_api.rs:108`) — the core aggregate was left open.
- Failure: a broker mangles its pubkey casing when publishing a 31403; the self-review / original-signer string comparison passes; the broker decides its own case or supersedes another's decision, defeating the ADR-2011 boundary the whole surface rests on.
- Confidence: high (casing property asserted in the material; the bypass is the direct arithmetic of the two cited facts)
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — After owner resolution, the moderation gates key on the raw signing pubkey; gift wraps have no author to resolve at all
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-4, KF-9), NF-03.4, NF-03.6, NF-20.6
- Evidence: "the write gate resolves a device key to its owner, but the moderation gates that follow key on the RAW signing pubkey, and a gift wrap has no author to resolve at all" (`known-findings.md:27`, `:44`). Note a conflict to resolve at the code: NF-03.4 step 9 states suspension and silence are "checked for the device key AND its owner" (`nip_handlers.rs:1189`) — the two citations disagree about which gates cover the owner; check the ban/mute call site (`nip_handlers.rs:1213`) before acting.
- Failure: a banned or muted owner acting through a fresh raw key (or any gift-wrap-shaped path) is evaluated against an identity the moderation state was never recorded for; enforcement meant for the person never lands.
- Confidence: medium (the defect is recorded; which gates are affected conflicts between citations)
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — The NIP-98 replay store fails open when D1 returns no write metadata
- Topics: NF-02.5, NF-07.8
- Evidence: the store answers first-seen when D1 returns no write metadata (`rate-limit/src/replay.rs:37-39`; the trait contract is `true` on first observation, `core/src/nip98.rs:94-95`); the full signature/URL/method/payload/freshness check runs first (`nip98.rs:576`), bounding exposure to a captured token inside its freshness window.
- Failure: on any request where D1 omits the metadata, an intercepted admin Authorization header re-submitted within the window passes — e.g. replayed to `/api/governance/agents/register`. Whether D1 ever omits it is runtime behaviour the material says is not settled.
- Confidence: medium (defect stated; trigger runtime-dependent)
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is declared in no wrangler template, so a forgotten var silently leaves "encrypted" zones in plaintext
- Topics: NF-08.2 (N4), NF-08.8 (N7), NF-03.12, NF-05.16
- Evidence: ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is read from env at `zone_config.rs:86` but "declared in NO wrangler.toml [vars] block in any worker — ... it has no documented deployment default, so a deploy that forgets it silently leaves encryption OFF" (N4); `is_encrypted = gate AND zone.encrypted AND NOT is_public_read` (`zone_config.rs:133`); the write-side ciphertext requirement fires only inside that branch (`nip_handlers.rs:1457-1459`); SETUP never names it.
- Failure: the operator marks a zone encrypted in forum.toml, never sets the relay var because no template or guide mentions it; the relay's encrypted-zone gates never engage, members post into what is labelled a private family zone, and every read-cohort member (and the relay itself, which would otherwise see only ciphertext shape) sees plaintext.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT)
- Severity: high

### F-12 — Live broadcast of kind-41 skips the zone gate that the history path enforces
- Topics: NF-17.3, NF-11.5, NF-03.14
- Evidence: the live `needs_gate` includes kinds 40 and 42 plus calendar and kanban and omits 41 (`broadcast.rs:76`); gated kinds consult the authoriser per recipient (`:149`) but "Kind 41 skips zone context — matching subscription gets original event" (`:162`); the history path withholds a hidden-zone kind 41 from non-members (`nip_handlers.rs:2354`); the native dispatcher test sends kind 41 despite a fixture authoriser that would withhold.
- Failure: any connected socket holding a kind-41 subscription receives channel metadata edits for hidden zones in real time — the zone's existence and name leak live even though a later REQ for the same history would be withheld.
- Confidence: high (dispatcher-level evidence; no live relay probe was run)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — Invite revocation can never succeed: the client sends the code, the handler matches the id
- Topics: NF-25.4, NF-02.8
- Evidence: creation uses an 8-character id and a 16-character code (`invites.rs:361`); the client puts the code in the revoke URL (`admin/invites.rs:175`); the handler looks up `WHERE id = input` (`invites.rs:733`) and returns 404 "Invite not found" (`:742`); the SQL fixture misses by code and succeeds by id.
- Failure: a leaked invite code cannot be withdrawn — every revoke attempt 404s and the invite stays redeemable; for a zone invite, redemption also grants zone cohorts on the relay whitelist (`invites.rs:230`, `:245`, `:271`).
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — Member deletion reports "Deleted" after partial cleanup, leaves no tombstone, and the row can be silently re-granted
- Topics: NF-25.5, NF-25.1, NF-08.7
- Evidence: relay deletion must succeed but pod deprovision and auth member cleanup failures only log warnings (`admin/mod.rs:506`, `:524`); the UI reports Deleted (`:533`); the whitelist DELETE leaves "no removed-state tombstone" (`user_admin.rs:199`); the storage fixture can grant a new row after deletion.
- Failure: a removed member's pod data and auth row survive; because there is no tombstone, any later cohort grant or zone-invite path re-creates the whitelist row for the same pubkey (the shared merge preserves cohorts), quietly re-admitting a person the operator removed, whose pod content was never deprovisioned.
- Confidence: high (re-grant-after-delete is fixture-demonstrated)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — Zone-key delivery permanently skips members whose newest-epoch grant landed but older epochs were lost; stale writers fork readability
- Topics: NF-27.2, NF-27.1, NF-05.16
- Evidence: auto-grant includes missing older epochs only for recipients lacking a newest-epoch ACK (`auto_grant.rs:94`, `:102`); publish returns without waiting for a relay ACK (`relay.rs:640`); a positive ACK records the key while a negative ACK changes nothing (`auto_grant.rs:153`), and the next sweep filters on the same newest-ACK and skips the recipient entirely (`:100`). Rotation has "no shared epoch activation barrier" (`zone_encryption.rs:273`); a stale writer encrypts with the locally supplied older key (`store.rs:359`, `mod.rs:568`) while the relay checks shape only (`zone_config.rs:208`).
- Failure: a member's newest-epoch grant is ACKed but an older-epoch grant is lost in delivery; every later sweep skips them, so they can never read pre-rotation history and no error ever surfaces. In parallel, a stale-writer admin posts new ciphertext under the old epoch that newest-only members cannot decrypt — the encrypted zone silently splits into two readable halves.
- Confidence: high (scheduling and filter logic cited; the production occurrence is inferred)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

## ontomark / critical

### F-01 — The legacy VisionClaw pipeline still publishes with a fail-open redactor and an unscanned frontmatter channel

- Topics: OM-00.2, OM-00.3, OM-05.5
- Evidence: OM-00.3 EXT states "VisionClaw's vault build job still ships S1.1, S1.2, S1.5, S2.3 and S3.1 unchanged until VC-60 retires under its four gates" (ADR-OM-005-estate-alignment...md:28). S1.1 is "prose redactor failed OPEN on a regex compile error" and S1.2 is "credential scan was body-only but render publishes frontmatter" (OM-00.2 A1, A2). The VisionClaw workflow builds the in-tree `vault` crate, not ontomark (`ontology-publish.yml:167-168`), its output feeds `pack-pod-resources.py` (`ontology-publish.yml:247`), and "the legacy `vault` build kept private links" (OM-05.5). ADR-OM-005 decision 2 retires this job "gated, so it keeps running."
- Failure: a credential placed in a frontmatter value (title, sources) on a public page is rendered and shipped into Pod resources unscanned; a redactor regex compile failure publishes prose unredacted. Both faults are fixed in ontomark but live in the producer that is still running.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (EXTERNAL in OM-00.3; EXTERNAL notes in OM-05.5)

### F-02 — Private working-note names survive in public knowledge titles because the union redactor is never applied to knowledge records

- Topics: OM-11.2, OM-06.2
- Evidence: OM-11.2 GAP: "bare private working name can survive in a public knowledge title; knowledge redactor is not the union redactor (projection.rs:750,850,890)". The audit reproduced it: the same private working-note title was masked in a public working title but "retained in a public knowledge title. Both public and machine builds retained the latter." The union marker set is built over both vaults (projection.rs:837-850) but applied only to surviving working records (projection.rs:869-875); the page title is taken from the projected record (page_api.rs:89).
- Failure: a public knowledge page whose title names a private working note publishes that name in `api/pages/`, the search index and other per-page surfaces, in the ordinary public build as well as the machine build. The publication boundary the redactor claims (private names masked) does not hold for titles.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION, OM-11.2)

### F-03 — Governance application can mutate a page its receipt does not name, and the promote path skips the digest-equality check approve has

- Topics: OM-07.1, OM-07.2, OM-07.3
- Evidence: OM-07.1 X: "explicit promote checks request tag, but lacks approve's proposal-digest equality check (local-process-manager.js:444,400)". OM-07.2 GAP: "staged title B can write B.md while adapter receipt names proposal page A (ontology-apply.js:570 vs write/create.rs:258)" — the CLI derives the filename from the staged title (write/create.rs:248,258) and the adapter receipts the original proposal page (ontology-apply.js:634). Ledger attestation failure is returned after the write (ontology-apply.js:742,900).
- Failure: an approved decision for case X is executed via promote with a proposal whose digest does not match the decision tag; or a create proposal lands as `B.md` while the ledger records page A. Later audit of "which approved change reached which page" is wrong, and nothing in the local flow detects it — the write itself is valid.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION in OM-07.1; DRIFT in OM-07.2)

### F-04 — The derivation certificate is written outside the bundle transaction: before the consistency gate, deletable by promotion, and orphanable beside an old bundle

- Topics: OM-03.1, OM-03.8, OM-04.3, OM-10.2
- Evidence: "certificate files are written directly to certificate_out before consistency, no bundle transaction covers them (mod.rs:723-725)" (OM-03.1, marked Tension). OM-10.2: on an unsatisfiable refusal the "old bundle can retain new certificate beside it (mod.rs:744)", and a certificate placed inside the output directory "is removed by successful replacement (mod.rs:311,313,316)". The estate audit "reproduces failures that separate the certificate from the surviving bundle."
- Failure: a build refuses as unsatisfiable after writing its certificate — disk now holds a warrant for a closure that was never published, beside the previous bundle. Or an operator points `--certificate-out` inside `--out` and every successful promotion destroys the warrant. A verifier cannot tell which certificate covers which bundle.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in OM-03.1; TENSION ×2 in OM-10.2)

### F-05 — The independent certificate checker accepts an empty derivation file, and the four certificate files are written non-atomically

- Topics: OM-10.3
- Evidence: the checker receives only the two TSV files (`oo_cert.rs:54,55,56`); "omitting all steps still passes — soundness does not establish closure completeness." Reproduced: the checker "accepted an empty derivation file after a one-step certificate was truncated." `scope.tsv` and `asserted.sha256` are "not inputs to this checker invocation." The writer performs "four sequential filesystem writes (rdfs-certificate/src/lib.rs:589-592)" and "a failed second write can leave new assertions with old digest (589,590)."
- Failure: truncation or tampering of `derivations.tsv` yields a certificate that still re-verifies; a crash mid-write circulates a set whose digest does not match its assertions. The trust artefact ADR-OM-009 exists to provide is forgeable by omission and corruptible by a torn write, with no consumer-side check covering the metadata files.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION, OM-10.3)

### F-06 — `publish_out` is written after promote, so a failure leaves two destinations at different generations with no rollback

- Topics: OM-03.5, OM-03.1
- Evidence: OM-03.5 N2: "write_to runs AFTER promote, so a failure there leaves the NEW bundle beside the PREVIOUS publication tree that swap_in kept, the two no longer one generation (build/publish.rs:356, mod.rs:1026-1028)." The report counts both destinations and the CLI only refuses a zero (mod.rs:1050-1057, main.rs:902-908), so a partial write passes.
- Failure: promote succeeds, the external publication write fails (permissions, disk) — the promoted bundle at `out` is the new generation while the published tree at `publish_out` still serves the previous one. Consumers reading both (visionGraph's `site-data` and `quartz/content`) see mixed generations, and the build reports success-or-generic-error with no restoration of the retired bundle.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-03.5)

### F-07 — An ingest rerun deletes and replaces a complete review bundle under the same ID, and "complete" can stay true after dropped chunks

- Topics: OM-08.2, OM-08.3
- Evidence: the request ID determines the default directory and the run first removes the existing manifest directory (`ingest_cmd.rs:769,771,772`); "incomplete uncached rerun can replace complete bundle under same ID (ingest_cmd.rs:723,769,772)" — reproduced by the audit. The endpoint is "omitted from request ID (ingest_cmd.rs:606)" although it is part of the parser and the response cache (`:570,446`). A malformed model response records a dropped chunk only; "complete can remain true (ingest_cmd.rs:821)" and the source is recorded as finished (`:842`). Removed sources are "dropped from host result; only finished observed sources recorded (:841,843)" although the library classifies them (`ledger.rs:224,255`).
- Failure: a network hiccup on a rerun destroys a previously complete bundle of accepted proposals and evidence quotes; a reviewer trusting the "complete" flag or the bundle ID gets a partial artifact presented as whole, and evidence that was accepted earlier is gone with no record of removal.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes for the replacement and endpoint drift (TENSION/DRIFT, OM-08.3); no marker on the complete-flag-stays-true edge (OM-08.2)

### F-08 — ADR-OM-009 says every published closure carries a certificate; the default build writes and checks none, and contract C3 lists no certificate artefact

- Topics: OM-03.8, OM-04.1
- Evidence: the ADR is Accepted and "active from v0.6.0" and "makes ontomark... the writer of the warrant a Lean 4 checker can re-verify" (OM-03.8). Against that: "certificate_out defaults to absent and the build writes and checks nothing then (mod.rs:738, main.rs:410)" — the authors' own Open question. OM-04.1 NC: "the C3 file listing names no certificate output (sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:111), while ADR-OM-009 decides every published closure carries one."
- Failure: an operator publishes with default flags (any consumer other than visionGraph, which does pass the flag) believing the ADR's title; the bundle carries no warrant and no parity check ran. Governance text and shipped behaviour disagree about whether the estate's closures are certified.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open in OM-03.8; DRIFT in OM-04.1) — the ADR-title contradiction itself is only implied

### F-09 — Graph extraction drops unsupported shapes and unreadable definitions before the refusal check, so the published closure can be silently incomplete

- Topics: OM-04.3, OM-10.1
- Evidence: OM-04.3 AUDIT LIMIT: "extraction precedes the shared refusal check. Unsupported raw RDF shapes and unreadable definitions can disappear before that check (reason.rs:283,401)." Reproduced at the Graph API: a raw universal restriction produced zero refusals, and a malformed graph definition was omitted (OM-10.1 DROP). The material cautions these are "Graph API probes, not a demonstrated bypass of normal vocabulary loading."
- Failure: an axiom the extractor cannot read vanishes without a refusal; `ontology-inferred.ttl` and the certificate parity set are computed over a smaller graph than was authored. The build succeeds, the zero-unsatisfiable gate passes, and consumers receive an incomplete closure with no error, warning, or count.
- Confidence: medium (reproduced at the adapter boundary; the normal vocabulary-load path is partially guarded by NON_EL checks at load)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (AUDIT LIMIT in OM-04.3; TENSION in OM-10.1)

### F-10 — `--vault all` silently means knowledge-only for retrieve, tree and edit

- Topics: OM-05.1, OM-05.2
- Evidence: OM-05.1 n2 asserts as INVARIANT that "all means BOTH for validate, gate, conflicts, find and build" (migrate_cmd.rs:64-68; main.rs:663,699,758,914) and in the same note: "DIVERGENCE: retrieve, tree and edit call `.next()` on the loaded vaults, so under all they read knowledge alone (main.rs:936-939,961-964,984-987)."
- Failure: an operator verifies a working page with `ontomark retrieve --vault all <slug>` before a publication, gets not-found, and concludes the page is missing or mis-sluged; `tree --vault all` renders half the estate. The same flag means two different scopes depending on the verb, with no error in either.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-05.1) — sitting directly beside the contradicting INVARIANT in the same note

### F-11 — Relation tables are hardcoded in three emitters, so vocabulary-added relations are declared but never stubbed, never drawn, and unordered

- Topics: OM-04.2, OM-04.5, OM-04.6
- Evidence: "the stub list is a hardcoded array of 13 property names (turtle.rs:446-460), a third copy of the relation set beside the vocabulary-driven declare_object_properties list (turtle.rs:678-691); a relation the vocabulary adds is declared but never stubbed." WebVOWL iterates a hardcoded REL_MAP and "the two agree only while the vocabulary does" (webvowl.rs:30-42 vs turtle.rs:656-673). Four hand-kept orderings exist: RELATION_KEYS (ir/model.rs:36), SCAFFOLD_REL_ORDER (indexes.rs:30), PAGE_API_REL_ORDER (page_api.rs:18), REL_MAP (webvowl.rs:30).
- Failure: the vocabulary gains a thirteenth relation: Turtle declares the property but emits no stub concept for undeclared targets (dangling references), WebVOWL silently drops every such edge, and the ordering tables omit it from the scaffold and page API. Published artefacts disagree with each other and with the vocabulary, with no refusal anywhere.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OM-04.2 / OM-04.6 / OM-04.5)

### F-12 — Generation identity is not what it claims: the digest excludes the profile and roster, caller-supplied commit/dirty are taken verbatim, and two different roots stand in for one directory

- Topics: OM-03.6, OM-11.3, OM-03.2
- Evidence: "the attribution roster shapes the Turtle and the page API but is not an input to content_digest (mod.rs:688-693 vs 971-991)"; reproduced in OM-11.3: a page-namespace change "preserved generation ID and content digest while changing page IRIs and artefact hashes," and "caller-provided commit and dirty flag are accepted directly (mod.rs:925,926)" — an arbitrary commit label was retained. The vocabulary directory is hard-coded to `repo_root/ontology` for dirtiness while the roster is read from `vault_root.parent()/ontology` (mod.rs:941, attribution.rs:32); for a flat layout the roster is looked up one directory above the repository (OM-03.2 Debt).
- Failure: a consumer cache keyed on `content_digest` (C3 itself notes Loom consumers must key on scope too) serves stale pages after a profile change; a generation stamp asserts a commit the build never verified; flat-layout vaults publish without attribution and mis-report dirty, silently.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION/DIVERGENCE OM-11.3; DIVERGENCE N1/N3 and Debt in OM-03.6/OM-03.2)

### F-13 — In the Obsidian host, an index failure rejects after the write has persisted, two query blocks in one note defeat the request guard, and a timeout cannot recall the request

- Topics: OM-09.3, OM-09.4
- Evidence: "rejection can follow persisted write; upsert is outside write-error catch (api.ts:248,253)" — reproduced. The query channel is the note path, so "two blocks in one note collide (sparql-block.ts:71)" and the latest-request-wins guard (guard.ts:46,68) cancels one block's result against the other's. "Timeout races host request; no abort handle (ingest-transport.ts:85,91,92)" — it stops waiting, it does not recall. There is also "no second abort check after optional Jev judging and before confirmation."
- Failure: the user confirms an ingest edit; the write lands; the index update throws; the plugin reports failure. The user retries (duplicate application) or discards an edit that actually persisted — vault state and the user's belief diverge. In one note, a saved query shows another block's stale-or-cancelled result.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION, OM-09.3/09.4)

### F-14 — The amend write path compares and renames with no writer lock, and the git push can carry commits that landed after the ahead-count check

- Topics: OM-07.3
