- Evidence: "compare and rename are separate; no writer lock at this boundary (write/apply.rs:401,411)" — marked OPEN; the audit says "the file re-read/rename interval remains a source-level concern." Also "writer can add a commit after count; push then includes it (ontology-apply.js:872,879)."
- Failure: a governance apply re-reads the target, a user edit lands in the interval, and the rename clobbers the user's change silently — the applied proposal is valid, the lost edit is unrecorded. Or the publication push carries an unrelated commit that arrived between the count and the push, attaching it to the governance change.
- Confidence: medium (asserted at source level; the audit did not reproduce the interleaving)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN for the race; TENSION for the push)

### F-15 — A missing working vault is silently skipped on the publication load, so `build --vault all` can publish an incomplete bundle that only one CI workflow catches

- Topics: OM-03.2, OM-05.2, OM-05.5
- Evidence: `sibling_vault(Working)` is "loaded with the layout's excluded dirs only if that directory exists (build/publish.rs:365-376)" and feeds `publish_vaults` (mod.rs:612-618); the MissingVault refusal covers only the primary root (`root_of` refusals, main.rs:457-466, profile.rs:573-588), and layout validation "retains missing optional leaves" (OM-12.1). The authors state the consequence without flagging it as a defect: visionGraph checks that both trees exist "because ontomark silently accepts a knowledge-only build (publish.yml:199-205)."
- Failure: a checkout or mount where `working/` is absent or misnamed produces a successful `--vault all` build whose `publish/` tree lacks the working pages entirely. visionGraph's workflow compensates; any other consumer, or a local build, ships the incomplete publication with exit 0.
- Confidence: high on the behaviour (author-stated); the mechanism (conditional sibling load bypassing the primary-root refusal) is inferred from the cited lines
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — the compensating CI check is recorded as an invariant; the gap itself carries no Tension/Debt/Drift/Open marker

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual exposure of the legacy faults (F-01):** whether any credential or private material exists in the current corpus the VisionClaw job builds, and who can read the Pod resources it produces. I would need the corpus, the job's output location and its access scope.
2. **What is actually deployed:** the workflow files pin intent (visionGraph at ontomark v0.7.0; ADR-2136 records the running image as "beside `vault`" with Decision 1's repin not done), but I cannot see the live binaries or the final lock/release receipts the reviews reference.
3. **Real concurrency on the write paths:** whether governance applies, Obsidian edits and builds actually interleave against the same vault (F-14, the census/load double-read in OM-03.3 N2), or whether the orchestrator serialises them.
4. **How consumers actually verify certificates:** the empty-derivations acceptance (F-05) matters only if production verifiers invoke the checker the way the test harness does; I would need the `oo-cert` invocation in visionGraph's workflow and any downstream verification steps.
5. **Test-suite status at the verified commits:** the material cites many pinning tests (conformance, parity, machine-scope invariants), but I cannot see whether the full suite is green at 4b9d15d / 0caae5d, or which of the cited tests are the only things holding each KEEP in place.

## ontomark / premortem

# Post-mortem — client-affecting data/custody/security failure

Scope note: every finding below is drawn from the topic set at its pinned revisions (ontomark 4b9d15d unless a panel pins another; ontomark-obsidian 2a6c44e; the VisionClaw job at the cited workflow lines). The exact trigger is not reconstructable from this material; the R items are the defects sitting in live production paths at those revisions, ranked. Each is checkable at the cited `path:line`.

### R-1 — The still-running VisionClaw publish job ships the fail-open redactor and the body-only credential scan
- Topics: OM-00.2, OM-00.3, OM-05.5
- Chain of events: ADR-OM-005 retires the legacy `vault` producer but the retirement is gated, so VisionClaw's `ontology-publish.yml` keeps running. It builds the in-tree legacy crate (`cargo build -p vault`, ontology-publish.yml:167-168) and runs `vault build --vault knowledge` (ontology-publish.yml:172-175). That crate still carries S1.1 and S1.2 unfixed: the credential scan reads the page body only while render re-emits the frontmatter into `publish/`, and the prose redactor fails open on a regex compile error. A credential-shaped string in a public page's frontmatter value (sources, title) is never scanned, is re-emitted by render, and ships in the pod-resource pack (ontology-publish.yml:247). Independently, any redactor compile failure in that job silently disables private-link redaction.
- Evidence: OM-00.3 EXT: "VisionClaw's vault build job still ships S1.1, S1.2, S1.5, S2.3 and S3.1 unchanged until VC-60 retires under its four gates" (ADR-OM-005-estate-alignment:28). S1.2 origin: body-only scan, frontmatter published (vault/src/build/publish.rs:159-160, :196; d5c1188 wrapped link redaction round render, not the scan). S1.1 origin: fail-open redactor (vault/src/projection.rs:230). Both were fixed only in ontomark 8d654d1 (OM-00.2 A1, A2), a different binary. OM-05.5 n2: the retirement is gated "so it keeps running".
- Warning signs visible today: the register itself records the faults as resolved in ontomark only; ADR-2136's close-out found the repin not implemented and both binaries in the running image (OM-06.5 STATUS, ADR-2136:97-102).
- Confidence: high that these faults ship in the live job; the triggering content is inferred (routine authoring).
- Severity: high

### R-2 — A private working note's name survives in a public knowledge title
- Topics: OM-11.2, OM-06.2
- Chain of events: Under machine scope (and the equivalent public working publish), the knowledge projection is built first through its own record set and is deliberately untouched by working (D4), so the marker set used to mask knowledge prose is the knowledge-only set, not the union set built later. A public knowledge page whose title/label names a private working note passes through unmasked; the union redactor is applied only to surviving working records. The unmasked title is emitted into the page API, search index, scaffold and Turtle. A client reading the public artefacts sees the private note's name.
- Evidence: OM-11.2 GAP: "bare private working name can survive in a public knowledge title; knowledge redactor is not the union redactor" (projection.rs:750, :850, :890); reproduced: "The same private working-note title was masked in a public working title and retained in a public knowledge title. Both public and machine builds retained the latter." Knowledge side built first from its own records (projection.rs:750-751); union markers built over the union set (projection.rs:837-850) and applied only to working records (projection.rs:869-875); page title taken from the projected record (page_api.rs:89).
- Warning signs visible today: the TENSION node and the reproduced canary result in OM-11.2; the machine bundle is a shipped consumer artefact (ADR-2136 / AB-P6, OM-06.5).
- Confidence: high (reproduced in the material).
- Severity: high

### R-3 — A governance decision can be applied to one page while the receipt and ledger attest another
- Topics: OM-07.1, OM-07.2, OM-07.3
- Chain of events: Approve checks that the proposal digest equals the decision tag (local-process-manager.js:400); the explicit promote path checks only the request tag and signed IRI and lacks that equality check (local-process-manager.js:444). The adapter reconstructs staged Markdown from the proposal's added diff lines (ontology-apply.js:560, :578); `ontomark create` derives the target filename from the parsed title of that staged file (write/create.rs:248, :258), which can differ from the proposal page slug checked earlier (ontology-apply.js:570). Page B is created exclusively; the adapter receipt names the original proposal page A (ontology-apply.js:634) and the ledger attests the caller's case and digest after the write (ontology-apply.js:742, :900). The corpus now contains B while the audit trail says A under case C. The push-after-commit-count window can additionally carry another writer's commit (ontology-apply.js:872, :879), and the apply compare/rename pair has no writer lock (write/apply.rs:401, :411).
- Evidence: OM-07.1 X (TENSION, :444 vs :400); OM-07.2 GAP (DRIFT, ontology-apply.js:570 vs write/create.rs:258), including the reproduced A/B fixture; OM-07.3 LEDGER and RACE/OTHER markers; the topic's business statement: "The application can make a valid local change while its receipt names a different page or case."
- Warning signs visible today: the audit reproduced the identity disagreement below signature admission (the material states it "does not establish a relay bypass"); the optional `page_sha256` does not replace a semantic digest check (ontology-apply.js:586).
- Confidence: high for the identity disagreement (reproduced); the signed forum-to-corpus journey is not certified by the audit (inferred).
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — `publish_out` is written after promote, so the live site and the bundle stop being one generation
- Topics: OM-03.5, OM-03.1
- Evidence: "write_to runs AFTER promote, so a failure there leaves the NEW bundle beside the PREVIOUS publication tree that swap_in kept, the two no longer one generation" (build/publish.rs:356, build/mod.rs:1026-1028). visionGraph passes `--publish-out quartz/content` (publish.yml:175-181).
- Failure: a build that withdraws or redacts a page promotes the bundle and markers, `write_to` then fails, and the public site keeps serving the withdrawn content while the marker claims the new generation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-02 — The compat projection publishes held-back `misc/` pages into every data artefact while `publish/` skips them
- Topics: OM-02.3, OM-00.1
- Evidence: "the compat path never forces misc/ pages private (held_back is only applied in the link path ir/projection.rs:616); a public misc/ page stays public there, while stage_redacted skips it build/publish.rs:220" (OM-02.3 n1; OM-00.1 H4, KEEP S3.4).
- Failure: any build run with `--no-redact-private-links` emits site-only `misc/` content into ontology.ttl, indexes, page API, search, tiers and OKF; a reviewer checking only `publish/` believes it withheld.
- Confidence: high (stated; requires the opt-out flag, default off)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE / KEEP)

### F-03 — The held-page identity rule is narrower than the staging rule, and migrate compiles without the redactor
- Topics: OM-03.3, OM-02.5, OM-00.1, OM-02.3
- Evidence: `is_unpublished` covers `_misc` at top level only (input/page.rs:48, :667) while `held_back` applies `_misc` at any depth plus `misc` (build/publish.rs:134, :138-148); "the census and excluded_identities_of still use the top-level-only is_unpublished (projection.rs:114, :231), which is the divergence that remains" (OM-03.3). Migrate keeps its own excluded-identity loop on the narrow rule (migrate/compile.rs:38-39, :105-114) and compiles through `project`, "the thin wrapper dropping the redactor" (ir/projection.rs:440-446; caller migrate/compile.rs:116).
- Failure: on the compat and migrate paths, a held page outside the narrow rule is neither forced private nor identity-masked, and `api/census.json` counts disagree with what staging withheld.
- Confidence: high for the rule divergence; medium (inferred) for what migrate's output exposes
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE; the redactor-less migrate path is stated but unmarked)

### F-04 — Certificate evidence can be absent, stale, or deleted relative to the published bundle
- Topics: OM-03.8, OM-10.2, OM-04.1
- Evidence: `certificate_out` defaults to absent and then "nothing written, nothing checked" (build/mod.rs:738, main.rs:410) although ADR-OM-009 is titled "every published closure carries a derivation certificate" (Open in OM-03.8). Certificate files are written directly before the consistency gate with no bundle transaction (build/mod.rs:723-725); an unsatisfiable refusal leaves "the old bundle can retain new certificate beside it" (mod.rs:744, OM-10.2); a certificate inside the output directory "is removed by successful replacement" (mod.rs:311, :313, :316). C3 lists no certificate output (sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:111 vs ADR-OM-009:34).
- Failure: a shipped generation carries no warrant, or a warrant describing a graph that was never published; a client re-verifying with oo-cert either finds nothing or proves the wrong closure.
- Confidence: high (each path stated)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, TENSION, Drift)

### F-05 — An ingest rerun replaces a complete review bundle; evidence is not retained
- Topics: OM-08.3
- Evidence: the request ID omits the endpoint though cache and parser include it (ingest_cmd.rs:606 vs :570, :446); the manifest directory is removed before rewrite (ingest_cmd.rs:771-772); "incomplete uncached rerun can replace complete bundle under same ID" (ingest_cmd.rs:723, :769, :772); source snapshots, prior target bytes and model requests are not retained (ingest_cmd.rs:802-838). Removed-source classification exists in the library (ledger.rs:224, :255) but the host records only finished observed sources (ingest_cmd.rs:841, :843).
- Failure: a reviewer's accepted evidence is silently destroyed by a later failed rerun and cannot be replayed or audited; withdrawn sources vanish from reports.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION/DRIFT/Open)

### F-06 — The mention check accepts a negated relation
- Topics: OM-08.2
- Evidence: "Quote must mention the value accept.rs:533"; TENSION: "mention check does not prove relation meaning; a negated relation can pass" — a reproduced negative sentence grounds an accepted positive relation.
- Failure: an applied assertion contradicts its own cited source; the corpus gains a false relation whose evidence quote looks supportive to a reviewer.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION)

### F-07 — The reasoner extractor drops unknown shapes and unreadable definitions before the refusal check
- Topics: OM-10.1, OM-04.3
- Evidence: "unknown shapes and unreadable definitions can be omitted before refusal check" (ontomark-compile/src/reason.rs:283, :401); "A malformed graph definition was also omitted"; a raw universal restriction produced zero compiler refusals while the typed equivalent was refused. Repeated as AUDIT LIMIT in OM-04.3; `reason.rs` unchanged between that audit and 4b9d15d.
- Failure: a malformed definition on a page is silently ignored; an incomplete closure is published with no refusal, and consumers cannot distinguish it from a complete one.
- Confidence: high (probes reproduced); production reach via authored pages is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION / AUDIT LIMIT)

### F-08 — Obsidian persists the write, then the index update can fail, leaving the engine on stale text
- Topics: OM-09.4, OM-06.5
- Evidence: atomic full-text comparison and write (api.ts:242-246), then index update (api.ts:253); TENSION: "rejection can follow persisted write; upsert is outside write-error catch" (api.ts:248, :253); OM-06.5 Open: "a later index failure can reject after persistence"; the engine's caches rebuild only on upsert g+1 (engine.rs:304-314).
- Failure: the confirmed edit is on disk but the engine still holds the old text; a later ingest preview/apply built on the stale base can overwrite the user's persisted edit — silent loss of a confirmed change.
- Confidence: high for the divergence; the overwrite chain is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION / Open)

### F-09 — No abort re-check between judging and confirmation; a timeout cannot recall the request
- Topics: OM-09.4
- Evidence: abort is checked only between chunks (ingest-transport.ts:149, :152); "Timeout races host request; no abort handle" (ingest-transport.ts:85, :91-92); "The current host has no second abort check after optional Jev judging and before confirmation."
- Failure: a request the user cancelled or that timed out still produces a preview that can be confirmed and applied.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION/LIMIT)

### F-10 — NGG1 silently drops edges to non-selected targets and collapses all relation identity
- Topics: OM-04.4, OM-00.2, OM-06.5
- Evidence: "Edge to a target that is not a selected node is silently dropped" (graph_tiers.rs:297-298, :309-310); twelve relation keys become one `EDGE_RELATION` byte and the dedup key merges distinct relations between one pair (graph_tiers.rs:283, :313-315); `EDGE_RELATION` is a published wire value (ontomark-ngg1/src/lib.rs:142-144); only `stats.json` reports declared against shipped.
- Failure: an agent consuming the tiers answers from a graph missing relations, with no refusal, and cannot distinguish part-of from related-to.
- Confidence: high (stated, OPEN S3.1); consumer consequence inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE / OPEN)

### F-11 — The certificate checker passes an empty derivation file; the writer is not atomic
- Topics: OM-10.3
- Evidence: "omitting all steps still passes; soundness does not establish closure completeness" (oo_cert.rs:55-56, reproduced truncation); "failed second write can leave new assertions with old digest" (rdfs-certificate/src/lib.rs:589-590); scope.tsv and asserted.sha256 are not inputs to the checker invocation (oo_cert.rs:54-56).
- Failure: a truncated or partially-written certificate passes independent re-verification, so a client's check "succeeds" against evidence that proves nothing about the shipped closure.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION)

### F-12 — The profile is absent from `content_digest`, and caller-supplied commit/dirty are trusted
- Topics: OM-11.3, OM-03.6
- Evidence: "profile is absent from content_digest inputs; same digest can accompany changed output" (mod.rs:963-983), reproduced with a namespace change altering page IRIs; "Caller-provided commit and dirty flag are accepted directly" (mod.rs:925-926).
- Failure: a consumer cache keyed on content_digest serves artefacts built under the old profile; a caller-supplied commit label misattributes provenance in the marker.
- Confidence: high (reproduced)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION)

### F-13 — The attribution roster is read from a different root than the ontology, and is not in the digest
- Topics: OM-03.6, OM-03.2
- Evidence: the roster is read from `vault_root.parent()` (build/mod.rs:688-692, attribution.rs:32) while dirtiness reads `repo_root/ontology` (mod.rs:941); a flat layout roots the vault at `repo_root` (profile.rs:520-524, :561-562), so the roster is looked up above the repository; the roster is not an input to `content_digest` (mod.rs:971-991).
- Failure: a flat vault publishes Turtle and page API with an empty roster (absent file is an empty roster, attribution.rs:29-37) and attribution edits never change the digest.
- Confidence: medium (path divergence and empty-roster behaviour stated; the flat-vault consequence inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE / Debt)

### F-14 — `retrieve`, `tree` and `edit` silently take only the first vault under `--vault all`
- Topics: OM-05.1, OM-05.2
- Evidence: "retrieve, tree and edit call .next() on the loaded vaults, so under all they read knowledge alone" (main.rs:936-939, :961-964, :984-987), against the stated rule that `all` means both.
- Failure: an operator editing under `--vault all` touches the knowledge page only; a same-id working page is silently unchanged and later merges behave unexpectedly.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-15 — A missing working directory makes `--vault all` silently publish knowledge only
- Topics: OM-03.2, OM-05.5
- Evidence: the sibling working vault is loaded "only if that directory exists" (build/mod.rs:612-613, build/publish.rs:365-376); "ontomark silently accepts a knowledge-only build" and only visionGraph's workflow guards it by requiring both trees (publish.yml:199-205).
- Failure: a renamed or missing working root publishes an incomplete corpus with no warning; consumers outside that one workflow (e.g. machine bundles) ship incomplete data.
- Confidence: high for the mechanism; the consumer consequence inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated; the workflow guard is labelled invariant)

## solid-pod-rs / critical

### F-01 — The MRC20 deposit path credits self-asserted amounts on a one-string proof
- Topics: SP-07.9, SP-07.17, SP-07.19, SP-07.14
- Evidence: `verify_mrc20_anchor` walks only the caller-supplied `stateStrings`; only the last is compared with `JCS(state)` (mrc20.rs:1061), "nothing requires … one string per state since genesis", and mark 0's input is never examined (blocktrail.rs:1114), so "with one string there is no earlier mark to walk". The head address "is derived from the public issuer key alone … funding it needs no issuer secret" (mrc20.rs:766). On deposit, the credited amount is whatever `state.ops` transfers (mrc20.rs:253), bounded only by a 21-million per-credit ceiling (payments.rs:469, :622); "Nothing on this path rejects such a proof" (handlers/pay.rs:603-620); the anchor network comes from the body, defaulting to testnet4 (handlers/pay.rs:566); the replay guard is per outpoint; and a head still in the mempool passes as intact. The whole `/pay/*` surface is mounted with no feature flag (lib.rs:5282) while the README claims payments are added "through explicit features" (README.md:3).
- Failure: an attacker derives the issuer's head address from the public key, funds one UTXO themselves, posts a one-string MRC20 deposit asserting an arbitrary transfer to their own pod account, and is credited token units they can then trade or withdraw; repeatable with fresh outpoints, each credit self-asserted up to the ceiling.
- Confidence: high (the code path is stated consistently across three panels; the authors note it is source tracing only, no request was sent)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension at SP-07.9 and SP-07.17; DIVERGENCE at SP-07.14)

### F-02 — The provenance commit query leaks private resources' metadata and sidecar text unauthenticated
- Topics: SP-07.6, SP-02.10
- Evidence: the `_prov` GET path has "no resource WAC gate or sidecar commit check; anchored is a substring check for bt:txid" (handlers/prov.rs:413), and the observed behaviour is that "private resource and sidecar reads returned 401 in the audit fixture, while the unauthenticated commit query returned their metadata and sidecar text". The route family registers before the LDP catch-all (handlers/prov.rs:747). The write side compounds it: an accepted anchor "replace[s] current sidecar even for old commit; write failure logged, still 200 and charged" (handlers/prov.rs:584).
- Failure: an unauthenticated caller queries commit metadata for private resources and reads their provenance sidecars, disclosing the existence, authorship and anchoring of data the ACL hides; an owner operation can also silently leave a sidecar describing the wrong commit.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift/LEAK and Tension at SP-07.6)

### F-03 — The production consumer is pinned to a revision that predates four security repairs, with the mirror compiled in
- Topics: SP-09.7, SP-08.18, SP-11, SP-12, SP-13
- Evidence: agentbox pins `0.5.0-alpha.18` at rev `76ab7aab` (agentbox/lib/solid-pod-rs.nix:54-58), which "predates its crate fixes: SP-GIT-02 commits A, B1 and B2, the hidden-object fetch refusal, the NIP-98 empty-body binding, the OIDC redirect check and the ACL-control fix" — those fixes live only at `8f2df86`, `fcc624a`, `daff10d` and the topic head, under `[Unreleased]` (CHANGELOG.md:7), so the version string does not reveal the gap. The same flake compiles `forge-full` unless `forge.enable` is false (agentbox/flake.nix:1548): "A shipped agentbox build therefore carries the pre-repair mirror; it stays inert only while no follow entries are configured."
- Failure: every incident class already fixed at head is live in the deployed binary: NIP-98 events minted over content reusable to blank the resource (across the restart/replica replay gaps), JWKS fetching that follows redirects, the syntactic lockout guard that accepted wrong-target Control, and a mirror whose held objects can be fetched by SHA once any follow entry exists.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt at SP-09.7; Tension at SP-08.18)

### F-04 — Lenient Turtle ACL parsing drops conditions, turning restricted grants into unconditional ones
- Topics: SP-04.3, SP-04.4, SP-04.10, SP-03.12
- Evidence: "a lenient Turtle parse can report Found after discarding a condition" (SP-04.3 Debt); the parser stops on unbalanced brackets (wac/parser.rs:317-320) and returns None on an absent condition type (parser.rs:418), yet `parse_turtle_acl` "Ok, including partially parsed rules" maps to Found (resolver.rs:295 in SP-04.4). Evaluation gates conjunctively over only the retained conditions (evaluator.rs:309), and the audience re-evaluation "use[s] the parsed document, including any restrictions already lost by the parser", so "lost restrictions can misclassify a response as Public" (SP-03.12 Boundary).
- Failure: a Turtle ACL whose payment, client or origin condition has a syntax defect — one unbalanced bracket suffices — is enforced as an unconditional grant of the modes and agents it names, and affected reads are classified Public and cached accordingly.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt at SP-04.3 and SP-04.10; Boundary at SP-03.12)

### F-05 — Inner-container `.acl` files are write-protected but never enforced, and migration leaves them dead
- Topics: SP-04.5, SP-13.2
- Evidence: `protected_resource_for_acl` maps `/dir/.acl` to `/dir/` (wac/mod.rs:220) so writing it needs Control on `/dir/`, but the resolver reads `acl_sidecar_key("/dir/")` = `/dir.acl` (resolver.rs:333-338, :379) — "a stored `/dir/.acl` is protected but never enforced". Discovery now advertises `/dir.acl` (ldp.rs:152) and "the upstream changelog leaves existing inner copies to a migration review rather than moving them"; SP-13.2 confirms the mapping "does not relocate old inner policies or choose between conflicting files".
- Failure: on upgrade, every pre-existing inner-container ACL silently stops governing its container; access falls back to whatever the ancestor chain grants — typically more permissive than the dead file. If the owner's only Control grant lived in the dead file, they are additionally locked out of writing the corrected `/dir.acl`.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open at SP-04.5)

### F-06 — Self-service provisioning writes no ACL: fresh pods are either locked out or governed by unreviewed inheritance
- Topics: SP-06.8, SP-04.3
- Evidence: "provision_named_pod writes no `.acl`. A pod created through `/.pods` or `/api/accounts/new` is governed by whatever ACL its ancestors carry. This review did not trace what the root ACL grants a fresh pod" (SP-06.8 Open). The resolver denies by default: Missing → "deny by default (no ACL means no access)" (SP-04.3). Only the admin route writes an owner-only ACL (lib.rs:4527-4533).
- Failure: a user creates a pod, receives 201 with webId and podUri, and then — if no ancestor grants — cannot write anything, including the `.acl` (sidecar writes need Control): total self-lockout. If an ancestor carries a broad `acl:default` grant, every fresh pod's content is readable by that grant from birth. Both branches are untested.
- Confidence: high that no ACL is written; medium (inferred) on which branch manifests, since the root ACL was not traced
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-07 — A transient storage read error can replace the canonical payment state with stale mirrors, losing intents
- Topics: SP-07.15
- Evidence: `read_state_unchecked` — "any get error falls back to legacy files, version zero and no pending intents" (handlers/pay.rs:184); "a read outage can select stale mirrors and the next successful write can replace the canonical state with them, losing intents and resetting the version". Recovery "trusts explorer presence, advances a saved trail when behind and reverses a payout on 404. A missing payout receipt retains the intent" (pay.rs:242).
- Failure: a brief storage read failure during a payout window causes the next successful write to overwrite the authoritative ledger/replay/market bundle with stale mirror files — pending broadcast intents vanish (double-payout exposure) and the version counter resets; in the opposite direction a missing receipt keeps an intent that may re-pay.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift at SP-07.15)

### F-08 — Git export selects the wrong repository and gates whole-history export on pod-root Read only
- Topics: SP-14.1, SP-14.2, SP-06.12
- Evidence: the pod component is applied twice — a request to `/alice/info/refs` sets the adapter root to `data_root/alice` (lib.rs:4740), then the service "extract slug and join to root again" and looks for the repository at `data_root/alice/alice` (service.rs:295, :299, :338); "The normal write hook records files under `data_root/alice`, so this is a different repository selection." Independently, clone and fetch "check Read only on the pod-root resource" (lib.rs:4792, :4797) with "no child ACL walk here" before the CGI (service.rs:378), while `git_mark_write` commits private content into that history (mark.rs:168).
- Failure: with the git feature compiled (forge-full implies it), a clone either 404s — the advertised export is broken — or, where a nested repository happens to exist at `data_root/<pod>/<pod>`, serves that repository's contents to anyone holding Read on the pod root; and once selection is corrected, the intended pod history would export every private child's current and historical content under the same root-only grant.
- Confidence: high on the selection and gate facts (stated with lines); the wrong-repo-serving branch requires a nested repository to exist (inferred)
- Severity: medium (becomes high the moment repository selection is fixed without an export-authority contract)
- Marked by authors: yes (Boundary at SP-14.2 and SP-06.12); the SP-14.1 double-join itself carries no marker

### F-09 — All serialisation is process-local: money, quota, provenance and replay state corrupt with two replicas or across restarts
- Topics: SP-10.4, SP-05.5, SP-06.4, SP-06.6, SP-07.7
- Evidence: "PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK is process-wide, not storage-wide — two replicas over one pod store share no lock" (lib.rs:208); "trail and epoch locks are in-process and held across network calls" (W8); the replay store: "Restart forgets ids, replicas do not share state" (replay_store.rs:115); the FsBackend lock "is process-local. A second process writing the same root is not serialised … between the two renames it can read a stale sidecar's content type" (SP-06.4); quota reserve reads `head()` before taking the per-pod lock, so concurrent overwrites of one resource drift the counter until reconcile (lib.rs:2661-2667).
- Failure: horizontally scaling the server over one data root — the obvious production posture — yields lost updates on balances and orders, double-crediting, torn metadata/content-type pairs and quota drift; even single-instance, each restart reopens the NIP-98 replay window for one TTL, and a hung explorer call stalls writers holding the trail lock.
- Confidence: high (stated repeatedly; the two-replica scenario is the direct implication)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (W3/W4/W8 notes, LIMIT at SP-05.5, invariant note at SP-06.4, Open at SP-06.6 and SP-07.7)

### F-10 — Replay protections degrade: the DPoP cache evicts unexpired entries, and the NIP-98 TTL is unvalidated
- Topics: SP-05.7, SP-05.4, SP-05.5
- Evidence: the DPoP jti cache's "capacity insertion evicts LRU, even unexpired" (oidc/replay.rs:177) — unlike the NIP-98 store which refuses at capacity (replay_store.rs:75); the NIP-98 "TTL is not validated against token freshness. Environment accepts any positive seconds. Constructor also permits zero" (replay.rs:133, :149) while the freshness window is ±60 s (nip98.rs:28), a two-sided 120 s window; restart forgets ids (replay_store.rs:115).
- Failure: under jti pressure a captured DPoP proof becomes replayable once its still-unexpired entry is evicted; an operator who sets a replay TTL below 120 s (or zero) silently reopens NIP-98 replay for every credential; every restart reopens the same window.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — the NIP-98 process-local and TTL facts are marked (LIMIT notes, SP-05.4/SP-05.5); the DPoP LRU eviction at SP-05.7 carries no marker

### F-11 — The server challenges with DPoP and Bearer but can verify neither on resources; production Bearer is silently anonymous
- Topics: SP-04.14, SP-05.1, SP-12.2
- Evidence: every ACL denial returns "WWW-Authenticate: Nostr, DPoP, Bearer — all three realms" (lib.rs:1136); yet in a production build "every Bearer header is anonymous" (lib.rs:617) and the extractor has "no DPoP dispatch" (lib.rs:635) — SP-12.2: "resource extractor handles Nostr; production Bearer refused, no DPoP dispatch … challenge nevertheless advertises DPoP" (lib.rs:1140, :1160). The crate ships an OIDC IdP and an `oidc_client` example, and the material states OIDC components "do not yet provide an external-client private-resource journey on the main pod server".
- Failure: a standards-conforming Solid-OIDC client completes the flow against the bundled IdP, receives a valid DPoP-bound token, follows the server's own challenge, and is refused 401 indefinitely — private resources are unreachable for the entire OIDC ecosystem the crate advertises; Bearer integrations fail closed while looking like a client credential problem.
- Confidence: high (each fact stated; the contradiction is direct)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — each half is stated, but no marker joins the advertised challenge to the verifier gap

### F-12 — POST unique-name minting is check-then-act: concurrent Slugs silently overwrite
- Topics: SP-03.4
- Evidence: "mint_unique_target probes with exists and the later put is a plain write (lib.rs:1771, :1865), so the no-overwrite rule is a check-then-act. Two concurrent POSTs with the same Slug can both choose the same free name. The hash fallback is a deterministic hash of the target, so it is not guaranteed fresh either (lib.rs:1788)."
- Failure: two clients POST the same Slug concurrently; both receive 201 with a Location naming the same resource and the second `put` silently replaces the first body — created data lost with no error to either client.
- Confidence: medium — the authors state the source permits it but that "only a concurrent run could confirm the race"
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-13 — JSON Patch applied to a non-JSON body destroys the stored content where the RDF dialects would refuse
- Topics: SP-03.6, SP-03.5
- Evidence: "The JSON Patch branch treats a stored body that is not JSON as `{}` and writes the patched object back under the stored content type (lib.rs:1949-1952, :1978). So a JSON Patch that applies cleanly to an empty object, sent to a Turtle resource, replaces its triples where an N3 patch would be refused."
- Failure: a client (or middleware that auto-selects the patch dialect) sends `application/json-patch` to an RDF resource; the server applies it to an empty object and stores the result, silently replacing the resource's entire triple content without the 409 the RDF paths give.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-14 — The shipped `account delete` command can never delete anything but reports "no such user"
- Topics: SP-02.13
- Evidence: "the shipped account delete builds a fresh InMemoryUserStore inside dispatch (cli/mod.rs:380), so it can never find a real user and always reports 'no such user'. Its help text promises removal of pods and the WebID profile as well (cli/mod.rs:105), but nothing on that path touches either."
- Failure: an operator runs the documented offboarding command for a departing user; it answers "no such user" — indistinguishable from a completed or non-existent deletion — while the user's pods, files and WebID profile all remain on disk. There is no working deletion path in the shipped CLI.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-15 — TLS flags are parsed in every build and silently ignored when tls is not compiled — and CI never builds tls
- Topics: SP-02.1, SP-01.7, SP-09.10
- Evidence: "`--ssl-key` and `--ssl-cert` are parsed in every build, but only the `tls` arm reads them … A build without `tls` takes the plain `listen` arm (main.rs:481) and serves HTTP with no warning that the key and certificate were ignored"; the server's cargo default is empty, i.e. no `tls` (crates/solid-pod-rs-server/Cargo.toml:127); "the tls feature on pull requests — no matrix row builds it" (SP-09.10 NG3).
- Failure: an operator deploys the default binary with `--ssl-key`/`--ssl-cert` (accepted without error) and an https `public_base_url`; the pod serves plaintext HTTP, exposing NIP-98 events, credentials and private bodies to the network while the boot log looks healthy — and the tls arm itself has never been compiled on a pull request.
- Confidence: high on the silent-ignore facts; the incident scenario is inferred from `default = []`
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension at SP-02.1; NG-note at SP-09.10)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Deployment topology and live configuration** — agentbox's manifest (`forge.enable`, `FORGE_MIRROR_RELAYS`/`FORGE_MIRROR_FOLLOW`, invites, registration, quota, `trust_proxy`, replica count, whether replicas share a data root, which storage backend). This decides whether F-03, F-06 and F-09 actually fire, and none of it is in source.
2. **Executed test evidence at head** — whether the SP-11/12/13 route tests, the AUD-009 regression cases (SP-08.14 Open) and the alpha.13 closures pass on this revision, and whether branch protection really requires `ci-required`, path-filtered PRs are handled (SP-09.2 Open) and the `crates-io-publish` environment has required reviewers (SP-09.6 Open).
3. **The effective ancestor ACL on a real data root** — what a freshly provisioned pod actually inherits, and whether admin-provisioned `/pods/<pubkey>` pods are addressed as SP-06.11 assumes (which would silently disable their provenance). This settles F-06's branch and the admin-pod git gap.
4. **A live probe of the deposit path** — the configured mempool endpoint and network in production (testnet4 default versus configured), and one actual one-string-proof POST to `/pay/.deposit`, to convert F-01 from source-traced to demonstrated or refuted.
5. **Actual exposure of the carried advisories** — whether any consumer enables the `oidc` path that carries the unassessed RUSTSEC-2026-0387 (crypto-bigint via openidconnect), and confirmation that `dpop-symmetric-test` (the HS256 arm, SP-01.5) never reaches a production feature set.

## solid-pod-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem — solid-pod-rs production incident (twelve months on)

**Incident class:** loss of client data confidentiality and custody. The review below worked only from the traced topic material and its citations; every claim is checkable at the cited path:line. Where the material itself recorded "source tracing only" or "no request was run", that limitation is carried into the confidence line. The three candidates below are ranked by the support the material gives them.

---

### R-1 — MRC20 deposit credited on a one-string trail proof: self-asserted balances drained real value
- Topics: SP-07.9, SP-07.17, SP-07.19, SP-07.14, SP-07.13
- Chain of events: An attacker derives the trail head address from the configured issuer's *public* key and funds a fresh UTXO there (no issuer secret needed). They craft a state JSON whose `ops` transfer a large amount of the configured ticker to their own pod address and submit `POST /pay/.deposit` with a valid NIP-98 header, supplying a one-element `stateStrings` proof. `verify_mrc20_anchor` walks "every earlier mark", but with one string there is no earlier mark, so mark 0's input is never examined and the check collapses to the head-only form. The outpoint replay guard passes (each attack uses a fresh outpoint), and `credit_by_outpoint` credits the attacker's ticker balance with the self-asserted amount. The attacker swaps the credited tokens through the always-mounted AMM/order book, or withdraws them — the counterparty pools holding real user value absorb the loss.
- Evidence: SP-07.9 states the head address "is derived from the public issuer key alone (`solid-pod-rs/src/mrc20.rs:766`), so funding it needs no issuer secret", and that a one-element proof "turns the walk into the head-only check that alpha.12 replaced, because mark 0's input is never examined (`solid-pod-rs/src/blocktrail.rs:1114`)". SP-07.9 N5: only the last string is compared with `JCS(state)` (`solid-pod-rs/src/mrc20.rs:1061`); "nothing requires JCS(prevState) or a seq-0 genesis among them". SP-07.17 Tension: the credited amount "is whatever `state.ops` transfer to the pod's address (`solid-pod-rs/src/mrc20.rs:253`), and no balance or supply rule limits it beyond the 21-million-coin ceiling (`solid-pod-rs/src/payments.rs:469`, `:622`)"; "Nothing on this path rejects such a proof (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/pay.rs:603-620`)"; the anchor network comes from the body, defaulting to testnet4 (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/pay.rs:566`); "The server's route tests have no one-string or head-only negative case." SP-07.14: the whole `/pay/*` surface is registered unconditionally (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:5282`). SP-07.19 confirms mark 0's input is never examined on the walk (`solid-pod-rs/src/blocktrail.rs:1114`).
- Warning signs visible today: two Tension paragraphs name this exact closure requirement ("`verify_mrc20_anchor` to require at least two strings… or to bind the proof to a trail the pod has recorded"); the README describes deposits, withdrawals, order book and AMM with no caution while the routes are always on (SP-07.14 Drift); the missing negative test is on record.
- Confidence: high — the code path and the absent rejection are stated at the cited lines; the material recorded "source tracing at 9b6143c only: no request was sent", so end-to-end exploitation was never reproduced in the record.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Private LDP history exported over git smart-HTTP once the pod-component join was corrected: the root-only Read gate was never an export authority
- Topics: SP-14.1, SP-14.2, SP-06.12, SP-06.11, SP-10.8
- Chain of events: Today the pod adapter applies the pod component twice — a request to `/alice/info/refs` resolves the repository at `data_root/alice/alice/.git`, so the repository that `git_mark_write` actually writes (`data_root/alice/.git`) is not found and normal clones 404. That routing defect is the only thing standing between clients and the authorization defect behind it. When the join was repaired (an obvious bug report with an obvious fix), normal stored history became reachable — and clone/fetch authorizes only `acl:Read` on the pod-root container. Every eligible PUT/POST/PATCH of a pod, including private resources and every historical version of since-deleted files, had been committed by `git_mark_write`; the CGI then packs the whole repository with no per-child or per-historical WAC walk. On any deployment whose pod root is publicly readable — a class of operator alpha.13 explicitly addressed — a single unauthenticated `git clone` exported the full private history.
- Evidence: SP-14.1: root is `data_root/alice` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:4740`, `:4816`), the service extracts slug and joins again (`solid-pod-rs-git/src/service.rs:295`, `:299`, `guard.rs:92`); "The normal write hook records files under `data_root/alice`, so this is a different repository selection." SP-14.2: clone and fetch "check Read only on the pod-root resource (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:4792`, `:4797`)"; private LDP content is committed into history (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:3952`, `mark.rs:168`); the CGI serves the repository with "no child ACL walk here (`service.rs:378`)"; and explicitly: "Correcting selection alone would connect normal stored history to the existing root-only gate." SP-06.12 Boundary: "this transport setup does not implement per-child or historical WAC… Both defects must be considered before making normal clones reachable." SP-06.11: every LDP write is marked (`Marked --> Marked: git-mark per PUT / POST / PATCH`); SP-07.1 D: DELETE bypasses marking, so history retains prior versions of deleted files. SP-10.8 N2 / CHANGELOG.md:557: alpha.13 guidance addresses "an operator who served a public-read root", establishing that public-root deployments exist. SP-06.12 Drift: refusing unadvertised SHA-1 wants (`service.rs:425-432`) restricts hidden-ref fetches only, not advertised branches' full history.
- Warning signs visible today: the estate dedicated topic SP-14 and a dated assessment to this boundary; the material warns the exact failure mode ("Correcting selection alone would connect normal stored history to the existing root-only gate"); push is already refused outright (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:4767`), signalling that read-side authority was never settled.
- Confidence: high — the root-only gate, the absence of per-child/historical WAC, and the double-join are all stated; that the join was corrected in the intervening twelve months (the precondition for exposure) is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Lenient Turtle ACL parsing silently dropped a restriction; the truncated rule granted unconditionally and the response was classified Public
- Topics: SP-04.3, SP-04.10, SP-03.3, SP-04.7, SP-03.12, SP-04.6
- Chain of events: An operator writes an ACL in Turtle intended to restrict a rule (for example a Read grant gated by a condition). The condition block has a shape the parser mishandles — unbalanced brackets stop parsing at that point, or a condition with an absent type returns None — and the condition is discarded while the authorization, with its agent and mode triples, still lands in the document as `Found`. The PUT path accepts the file, because "valid Turtle with incomplete condition shape can pass" validation. At evaluation the rule now has an empty condition list; the conjunctive gate requires all conditions to be Satisfied and trivially passes, so the mode is granted to whoever the agent clause names — including `foaf:Agent`. The anonymous re-evaluation for audience runs over the same truncated document, grants, and the response is classified Public. Private data becomes world-readable, with cache treatment for a Public audience; nothing logs a policy problem, because both enforcement and the lockout guard evaluate only what the parser retained.
- Evidence: SP-04.3 Debt: "a lenient Turtle parse can report Found after discarding a condition", with native probes referenced. SP-04.10: "unbalanced brackets stop parsing (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/parser.rs:317-320`)"; "absent type returns None (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/parser.rs:418`)". SP-03.3 Note: "DEBT valid Turtle with incomplete condition shape can pass (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:1668`)". SP-04.7: the condition gate is conjunctive over the retained conditions (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/evaluator.rs:309`); a rule with no retained conditions has none to fail. SP-03.12 Boundary: "Lost restrictions can misclassify a response as Public. Headers do not prove revocation through an intermediary cache." SP-04.6 Debt: "the guard still evaluates what the lenient Turtle reader retained, so a condition the parser discards is invisible to it as it is to enforcement."
- Warning signs visible today: the parser DEBT nodes, the write-path acceptance debt, the audience boundary note, and a dated native-probe assessment all exist in the record; none was closed at the traced revision.
- Confidence: high — the parser loss, its acceptance at write time, and the empty-condition grant are each stated; the specific operator-authored Turtle shape that triggers it in production is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

## Further latent defects

### F-01 — An inner-container ACL file is protected but never enforced
- Topics: SP-04.5, SP-13.2
- Evidence: `protected_resource_for_acl` maps `/dir/.acl` to `/dir/` (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/mod.rs:220`), but the resolver reads `acl_sidecar_key("/dir/")` = `/dir.acl` (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/resolver.rs:333-338`, `:379`); "a stored `/dir/.acl` is protected but never enforced"; discovery now advertises `/dir.acl` (`solid-pod-rs/src/ldp.rs:152`) and existing inner copies were left "to a migration review".
- Failure: an operator restricts a subtree by writing the natural `/dir/.acl` spelling; the write succeeds (it is Control-gated on `/dir/`), but enforcement never reads it — children stay governed by the ancestor, which may be public.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-02 — Pods created through the public routes carry no ACL
- Topics: SP-06.8, SP-10.8
- Evidence: `provision_named_pod` writes only container markers and a profile card (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:2813-2844`), no `.acl`; "A pod created through `/.pods` or `/api/accounts/new` is governed by whatever ACL its ancestors carry" (SP-06.8 Open); alpha.13 guidance addresses operators who "served a public-read root" (CHANGELOG.md:557, via SP-10.8 N2).
- Failure: on a deployment whose root carries a public `acl:default` grant, every newly provisioned pod is publicly readable from birth — and, with R-2, exportable.
- Confidence: high that no ACL is written; the exposure depends on the root ACL, which the material explicitly did not trace.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-03 — Concurrent POST with the same Slug can silently overwrite
- Topics: SP-03.4
- Evidence: `mint_unique_target` probes with `exists` and the later `put` is a plain write (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:1771`, `:1865`); "Two concurrent POSTs with the same Slug can both choose the same free name"; the hash fallback "is a deterministic hash of the target, so it is not guaranteed fresh either" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:1788`); "the source does not prevent it".
- Failure: two clients POST the same Slug concurrently; both pass the probe; the second `put` replaces the first; both receive 201 and the first document is gone.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-04 — JSON Patch on a non-JSON resource replaces its content
- Topics: SP-03.5, SP-03.6
- Evidence: the JSON Patch branch treats a stored non-JSON body as `{}` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:1949-1952`) and writes the patched object back under the stored content type (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:1978`); "a JSON Patch that applies cleanly to an empty object, sent to a Turtle resource, replaces its triples where an N3 patch would be refused".
- Failure: a misconfigured client sends `application/json-patch` to a Turtle resource; the patch applies to `{}` and the document's triples are destroyed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-05 — The provenance commit query has no WAC gate
- Topics: SP-07.6
- Evidence: GET commit resolves git metadata and "read[s] current resource sidecar directly from storage" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/prov.rs:351`, `:391`); "DRIFT: no resource WAC gate or sidecar commit check" (`:413`); in the fixture, "the unauthenticated commit query returned their metadata and sidecar text" for resources whose direct reads returned 401; `anchored` is a substring check (`:413`); an accepted POST anchor "replace[s] current sidecar even for old commit" (`:584`).
- Failure: an unauthenticated caller enumerates commits and reads PROV-O sidecars of private resources — existence, timestamps and anchor state of private documents; an owner can also silently overwrite the live sidecar with an old commit's.
- Confidence: high (fixture-observed)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift/Tension)

### F-06 — Revoked readers keep receiving legacy notifications
- Topics: SP-08.4, SP-08.17
- Evidence: the driver wraps `LegacyNotificationChannel`, which authorises once at `sub` (`solid-pod-rs/src/notifications/legacy.rs:339`) with "No WAC re-check at fan-out" (`:393` note); only `LegacyWebSocketSession` re-checks on every change (`:886`) and "Only the integration tests construct" it.
- Failure: a subscriber's Read grant is revoked mid-session; `pub` lines for that subtree keep arriving until disconnect.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-07 — The shipped agentbox pin predates five security fixes
- Topics: SP-09.7, SP-04.6, SP-05.10, SP-11, SP-12
- Evidence: the pin `76ab7aab` (`../project/agentbox/lib/solid-pod-rs.nix:58`) "predates its crate fixes: SP-GIT-02 commits A, B1 and B2, the hidden-object fetch refusal, the NIP-98 empty-body binding, the OIDC redirect check and the ACL-control fix" (SP-09.7); the version string matches the head's `0.5.0-alpha.18`, "so the version alone does not show the gap".
- Failure: on the shipped build: a NIP-98 event signed over non-empty bytes admits an empty PUT (authorized content wipe); the lockout guard is the syntactic one whose wrong-target owner-lockout was reproduced 2026-11-01; JWKS fetching follows redirects to attacker-chosen hosts.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt/Tension)

### F-08 — The pre-repair mirror in shipped builds serves held objects by SHA
- Topics: SP-08.18, SP-14.3, SP-14.4
- Evidence: agentbox compiles `forge-full` by default (`../project/agentbox/flake.nix:1548`); its pin predates the SP-GIT-02 store isolation; pre-repair, "hiding `refs/mirror` only changed what smart-HTTP advertised; Forge's raw, HTML, JSON and archive readers call local Git with a caller-supplied revision and never consult that advertisement" (SP-14.3); it "stays inert only while no follow entries are configured" (SP-08.18).
- Failure: the mirror is enabled against an upstream holding private branches; fetched objects land in the served store and any reader retrieves them by SHA through the raw endpoints.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism; the activation (someone configuring relays and a follow entry) is the inferred trigger.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-09 — A payment-state read failure can roll the ledger back to stale mirrors
- Topics: SP-07.15
- Evidence: "any get error falls back to legacy files, version zero and no pending intents" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/pay.rs:184`); the next successful write commits the stale mirrors as canonical state (`:198`), "losing intents and resetting the version"; all under a process-local lock (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:208`).
- Failure: a transient storage read error during a payout loses the pending intent; a later retry of the same logical payout debits and broadcasts again — a double payout, or a spent outpoint becomes re-creditable.
- Confidence: medium — the fallback and overwrite are source-stated; the double-payout path through intent loss is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift node + Debt)

### F-10 — Group-based grants silently deny
- Topics: SP-04.15, SP-04.1
- Evidence: the server passes an empty `StaticGroupMembership` on every admission path (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:994`, `:1206`); "group grants are lost without any error"; a plain `acl_denial` writes no log line (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:1109`, SP-10.12).
- Failure: an operator migrates a team ACL to `acl:agentGroup`; every member is locked out with bare 403s and nothing in the logs points at the cause.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-11 — One origin-restricted rule gates the whole document
- Topics: SP-04.8
- Evidence: `check_origin` gathers `acl:origin` patterns from every authorisation in the document (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/origin.rs:282-304`); a request with no `Origin` header is refused as `RejectedNoOrigin`; only Control bypasses the gate (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/evaluator.rs:335`); "a request with no `Origin` header, such as a server-to-server or command-line client, is refused".
- Failure: a single rule carrying `acl:origin` makes every non-Control grant in that ACL reject all non-browser clients — integrations across the governed subtree break with no configuration change to those rules.
- Confidence: medium-high — "follows from source; it was not exercised over HTTP".
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-12 — GitLenient authenticates by URL prefix with an unbound body
- Topics: SP-05.3
- Evidence: GitLenient is "method `*` plus raw string URL-prefix" (`solid-pod-rs/src/auth/nip98.rs:93`); "prefix matching admits sibling names" (`solid-pod-rs-git/src/auth.rs:154`); the bridge "passes body = None, so a push body is NOT hash-bound" (`:148`); "no ReplayStore claim occurs in this extractor".
- Failure: once git reads are reachable (R-2), a token minted for one repository path authenticates the same key for sibling paths under the lenient extractor, with no payload binding and no replay claim.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — The DPoP replay cache evicts unexpired entries
- Topics: SP-05.7, SP-05.16
- Evidence: "capacity insertion evicts LRU, even unexpired" (`solid-pod-rs/src/oidc/replay.rs:177`) — unlike the NIP-98 store, which refuses at capacity rather than evicting (SP-05.4/SP-05.5); the IdP token endpoint is a live cache consumer (`solid-pod-rs-idp/src/provider.rs:241-250`).
- Failure: under load past capacity, an evicted `jti` becomes replayable within its proof validity — DPoP single-use breaks exactly when traffic is highest.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Admin-provisioned pods never receive provenance marks
- Topics: SP-06.8, SP-06.11
- Evidence: the admin route writes under `data_root/pods/<pubkey>` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:4512`); `git_mark_write` takes the first path segment and looks for `data_root/<segment>/.git` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:3945-3955`), so for `/pods/<pubkey>/...` it probes `data_root/pods/.git` — "their writes would be skipped as not git-backed"; the admin route also writes with `tokio::fs`, outside the cap-std capability handle.
- Failure: pods created through `/_admin/provision/{pubkey}` accrue no git history at all; a client sold on provenance guarantees has none, and the divergence grows silently.
- Confidence: medium — source tracing; "no request was run to confirm it".
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-15 — Unconfirmed anchors satisfy verification and drive recovery
- Topics: SP-07.19, SP-07.12, SP-07.15, SP-07.11
- Evidence: `verify` answers with `is_intact`, which "also accepts marks still in the mempool, not only the head" (`solid-pod-rs/src/blocktrail.rs:849`; `solid-pod-rs-server/src/mempool.rs:856`); recovery "trusts explorer presence, advances a saved trail when behind and reverses a payout on 404" — "not proof of finality or safe absence across explorers" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/pay.rs:242`, SP-07.15 Debt); startup can log default testnet4 while requests use an explicit environment URL (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/mempool.rs:314`).
- Failure: an anchor accepted only to the mempool — and later replaced or double-spent — still verifies intact; payouts and trail advances proceed on funds that never confirm, possibly against the wrong network's explorer.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt; Drift node in SP-07.11)

## visionclaw / critical

### F-01 — Anonymous process execution and worker stalling via publicly-exposed MCP control routes

- Topics: VC-04.16, VC-03.6, VC-01.7
- Evidence: The entire `api/health` prefix is on the RbacGate public allowlist (`src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:55-61`). `POST /api/health/mcp/start` runs `docker exec -d multi-agent-container … npm run mcp:start` when the relay is not running (`src/services/mcp_relay_manager.rs:171-187`), then blocks its actix worker for 2s via `std::thread::sleep` (`:194`). `GET /api/health/mcp/logs` returns the tail of `/tmp/mcp-server.log` (`:210-219`). Neither route carries a `RequireAuth` wrap (`src/handlers/consolidated_health_handler.rs:479-483`). The `PublicDemoGuard` refuses mutating methods only when `PUBLIC_DEMO=read-only`.
- Failure: An anonymous caller starts processes in another container, reads their logs, and stalls one of the four worker threads per request. Four concurrent requests stall all workers simultaneously, causing a full outage of the server.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in VC-04.16)

### F-02 — NIP-98 replay cache is process-local: no cross-replica replay protection

- Topics: VC-03.4
- Evidence: The replay cache (`REPLAY_CACHE`, `src/utils/nip98.rs:221`) is a `Mutex<HashMap>` inside the process. The material states: "Process-local scope — no cross-replica replay protection (SECURITY-profiles.md)." A captured kind-27235 event is single-use only within one process.
- Failure: If two or more replicas run behind a load balancer, an attacker who captures a legitimate NIP-98 event can replay it against each replica in turn (each replay uses a fresh TCP connection, likely landing on a different worker process). Each replica claims the event independently, granting the attacker repeated authenticated sessions.
- Confidence: high — the material directly states the limitation; the multi-replica scenario is inferred from it.
- Severity: high (if multiple replicas are deployed); low if strictly single-instance.
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — Enrichment decisions broadcast to ALL WebSocket clients, bypassing the power_user read gate

- Topics: VC-05.6, VC-05.8, VC-02.9
- Evidence: `apply_decision` sends `BroadcastMessage{enrichment_decision}` to the client coordinator for broadcast to "all WS clients" (`src/handlers/enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:561`, diagram note). The broker inbox that exposes these same cases via REST is gated by `RequireAuth::power_user()` (`src/handlers/broker_inbox_handler.rs:251`). Any authenticated user (Editor default role) with a WebSocket connection sees real-time decision events that require Admin-level access to read via REST.
- Failure: A regular Editor-role user opens a WebSocket and receives every enrichment decision—including case IDs, outcomes, and reasoning—for the entire system. In a multi-user deployment, one tenant observes another tenant's decision pipeline in real time.
- Confidence: high — the broadcast is explicit in the sequence diagram; the privilege inconsistency is direct from the two citations.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Agentbox bridge and git-bridge writeback are blocked by the RbacGate in release builds

- Topics: VC-05.7, VC-05.8
- Evidence: The agentbox broker-bridge sends only `X-Agent-Key` and no `Authorization` header (`agentbox/management-api/routes/broker-bridge.js:105-115`). The git-bridge writeback sends no credential at all (`src/handlers/ingest_writeback_handler.rs:19-23`). The RbacGate classifies their POSTs as mutations requiring `WriteGraph` (`src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:179-189`), resolved via `verify_access` which has no `X-Agent-Key` path (`src/utils/auth.rs:211-218`, `:259-265`, `:329-353`). The dev Compose service arms `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=1` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:116`) whose bypass grants every request, hiding the gap. Production Compose does not set it.
- Failure: On deploying to production (release build, gate enforcing), the entire enrichment-decision pipeline stops: agentbox cannot POST decisions, the broker inbox read is refused, and the git-bridge writeback is refused. Decisions silently accumulate on the agentbox side. No error appears in VisionClaw logs beyond 401s on the access log.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (complete pipeline outage on production deployment)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in VC-05.7, Drift in VC-05.8)

### F-05 — Solid pod initialisation route double-verifies the same NIP-98 token; the second check always fails as a replay

- Topics: VC-05.15, VC-03.4
- Evidence: `POST /api/solid/pods/init-nip98` sits inside `/api` (`src/main.rs:1206`), so the RbacGate verifies the NIP-98 token first via `NostrService::verify_nip98_auth` (`src/utils/auth.rs:291-295`). The handler then calls `extract_user_identity` which runs `validate_nip98_token` on the same header (`src/handlers/solid_proxy_handler.rs:188`, `:225`). Each successful validation claims the event id in the process-wide replay cache (`src/utils/nip98.rs:164-168`, claim `:573`); a second claim of the same id fails with `TokenReplayed`. The handler answers 401 (`:1332-1338`).
- Failure: A genuine caller cannot initialise a pod through this route in any build where the RbacGate enforces. The feature is effectively dead in release. The same applies to every `/api/solid/*` mutation that reaches `handle_solid_proxy` without a pre-existing identity in request extensions.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (feature completely non-functional; blocks onboarding)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in VC-05.15)

### F-06 — Settings actor snapshot diverges from the store after `reset_layout`; the next physics PUT silently reverts unstated fields

- Topics: VC-06.4, VC-06.1, VC-06.2
- Evidence: `reset_layout` writes canonical defaults to GPU actors and SQLite (`src/settings/api/settings_routes.rs:1823-1863`) but never sends `UpdateSettings` to `OptimizedSettingsActor`. The actor loads its snapshot from the repository only once at start (`src/actors/optimized_settings_actor.rs:700-710`); `ReloadSettings` (`:1201`) has no sender. The hot-reload watcher is disabled due to deadlocks (`src/app_state.rs:1125-1129`). The next `apply_physics_patch` fetches the actor snapshot (`:618`, `:637`) and merges onto the pre-reset values.
- Failure: An operator resets the layout, sees the reset in `GET /api/settings/physics` (which reads SQLite first, `:480-485`). Later, any physics PUT—possibly for a single field like damping—writes every other field back to its pre-reset value, silently reverting the reset. The operator sees the response and believes only damping changed.
- Confidence: high — the material flags this as "Open" but the mechanism is fully traced.
- Severity: high (silent data corruption of user-visible settings)
- Marked by authors: yes (Open in VC-06.4)

### F-07 — Briefing bead publication accepts caller-supplied pubkey: any authenticated writer can impersonate another user

- Topics: VC-05.9
- Evidence: `submit_brief` and `request_debrief` read `user_context` from the request body (`src/handlers/briefing_handler.rs:22-27`, `:54-61`). The debrief's Nostr bead event is published with `user_context.pubkey` as the user (`:86-95`). The RbacGate requires only `WriteGraph` for these POSTs; nothing ties the body's pubkey to the authenticated caller.
- Failure: Any user with the default Editor role POSTs a brief with another user's pubkey in `user_context`, and the system publishes a bead completion event attributed to that victim on Nostr. The victim has no way to detect or repudiate this.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (impersonation on a public relay)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in VC-05.9)

### F-08 — AgentBeamActor and PresenceActor are unsupervised single points of failure

- Topics: VC-02.5, VC-02.20, VC-02.1
- Evidence: The four supervised children of `GraphServiceSupervisor` are GraphState, PhysicsOrchestrator, SemanticProcessor and ClientCoordinator (`src/actors/graph_service_supervisor.rs:334-339`). VC-02.5 states: "agent-beam and presence are unsupervised peers started by AppState." `AgentBeamActor::new(client_manager_addr).start()` (`src/app_state.rs:697`) has no supervisor, no health check, no restart. `GET /api/bots/data` returns "200 with an empty GraphData view" on any error (`src/handlers/bots_handler.rs:148-151`).
- Failure: AgentBeamActor panics (e.g., malformed roster input). It dies permanently. Every subsequent `/api/bots/data` call returns 200 with an empty view—no error, no alert, no restart. The entire bot/swarm visualization feature is silently dead until process restart. PresenceActor dying similarly orphans all XR co-presence sessions.
- Confidence: high (unsupervised is stated; permanent death is inferred from the absence of any restart mechanism in the material).
- Severity: medium (silent feature loss, not data corruption)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — GraphExportHandler is constructed per-worker: shared graphs 404 on 3 of 4 workers, rate limit is 4× the configured ceiling

- Topics: VC-04.11
- Evidence: `configure_routes` builds a fresh `GraphExportHandler` on every call (`src/handlers/graph_export_handler.rs:315`), which owns its `shared_graphs` map and rate-limit map as fresh `Arc`s (`:38-45`). `configure_routes` runs inside the `HttpServer::new` factory (`src/main.rs:972`, `:1196`), called once per worker; the server runs 4 workers (`src/main.rs:1265`).
- Failure: A user creates a shared graph export. The share lands on worker 2's map. The share URL is sent to a colleague who connects and lands on worker 3—they get 404. The original user refreshing may also 404 if their new connection goes to a different worker. The 20-per-hour export limit is effectively 80-per-hour across workers.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (feature intermittently broken, rate limit weakened)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in VC-04.11)

### F-10 — Rate limits on `/api/settings`, `/api/graph`, `/api/workspace` and the layout/SSSP routes are per-worker: effective limits are 4× the configured value

- Topics: VC-03.12, VC-01.14, VC-01.3
- Evidence: `RateLimit::per_minute(60)` is called at `src/main.rs:1144` inside the `HttpServer::new` factory closure that runs once per worker. Each call creates a new `RateLimit` with a new `Arc<RwLock<HashMap>>`. The graph scope's `RateLimit::per_minute(600)` at `src/handlers/api_handler/graph/mod.rs:1524` and per-resource `120/min` wraps (`:1545-1561`) are likewise inside the factory. The material contrasts this with `semantic_handler.rs:264-282` which "builds the handler once outside the factory and clones the `web::Data` into each worker" (VC-04.11)—the correct pattern.
- Failure: A client targeted by a 60/min limit opens multiple connections (spread across workers by the OS) and achieves up to 240 requests/min. All per-minute rate limits in the system are 4× weaker than their configured values. An abusive client exhausts resources 4× faster than the operator expects.
- Confidence: high (the per-worker construction is clear from the factory pattern; the 4× multiplication is arithmetic).
- Severity: medium (weakened security control, not a bypass)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — Position frames arriving within a 16ms window are silently dropped but still acknowledged to the server

- Topics: VC-13.7, VC-13.4
- Evidence: The client's `handleBinaryFrame` drops frames within 16ms of the last accepted frame and returns normally (`client/src/features/graph/managers/dataManager/wsClient.ts:26-28`). The caller then sends an acknowledgement (`client/src/store/websocket/binaryProtocol.ts:393-409`). The server records those nodes as held by this client and does not resend them until they move past the stream threshold. ADR-2141 decision 5 says acknowledgement happens "after it is applied."
- Failure: During rapid physics updates (a node dragged or a large graph settling), position updates arrive in bursts. Frames within 16ms are dropped client-side but acknowledged server-side. The client's displayed position for those nodes is stale indefinitely—the server believes they are delivered and will not resend unless the node moves past `streamMoveThreshold` (0.05). Nodes visually freeze at wrong positions.
- Confidence: high — the mechanism is precisely traced in the material.
- Severity: medium (visual staleness, not data corruption in the store)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in VC-13.7 note)

### F-12 — Production Dockerfile warm-up stage passes for any compile result: broken dependencies produce a green layer

- Topics: VC-08.11
- Evidence: The command is `cargo build --release 2>&1 | tail -40 || echo ...` (`Dockerfile.production:101-102`). No `SHELL` with `pipefail` is set, so the pipeline status is `tail`'s (always 0). The comment at `:89-93` says the dependency build must succeed. Only `cargo fetch --locked` (`:100`) gates the layer.
- Failure: A dependency update breaks compilation. CI runs the warm-up stage—it passes (green). The cache layer is stored. The final build at `:164` compiles the actual binary and may fail there, but the warm-up layer's purpose (early detection, cache invalidation) is defeated. In a CI pipeline that only checks the warm-up stage's status, broken code appears buildable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (operational: misleading CI signal; the final build still gates)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in VC-08.11)

### F-13 — Local corpus source silently falls back to GitHub on misconfiguration: wrong data processed without boot failure

- Topics: VC-01.2
- Evidence: `source_from_env_with_github` at `src/services/corpus_source/mod.rs:157` — "a misconfigured local source logs an error and falls back to GitHubSource rather than failing boot (`:157-171`)." The operator sets `CORPUS_SOURCE=local` with a bad `VAULT_ROOT`, and the system uses GitHub.
- Failure: An operator configures local-only corpus access (perhaps for data sovereignty or air-gapped reasons). A typo in `VAULT_ROOT` causes silent fallback to GitHub. The system processes the wrong corpus, builds the wrong graph, and the only signal is an error log line at boot. No health check reports the divergence.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (data correctness: wrong source used; potentially compliance/sovereignty violation)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Feature enumeration and power-user status readable via unauthenticated `X-Nostr-Pubkey` header on public prefix

- Topics: VC-05.3, VC-03.6
- Evidence: `GET /api/auth/nostr/power-user-status`, `/features` and `/features/{feature}` read the pubkey from an unauthenticated `X-Nostr-Pubkey` header (`src/handlers/nostr_handler.rs:75-79`, `:94-98`, `:115-119`). The `/api/auth/nostr` prefix is on the RbacGate public allowlist (`src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:55-61`).
- Failure: An attacker enumerates pubkeys (e.g., from Nostr relays or leaked data) and queries each one's feature set and power-user status without any credential. This maps the privilege landscape of the system—identifying admin targets—before attempting any exploitation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (reconnaissance aid; no direct exploitation)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in VC-05.3)

### F-15 — GPU actor rejecting a physics update leaves the actor snapshot holding a value the GPU refused and the store never saw

- Topics: VC-06.3, VC-06.2
- Evidence: `apply_physics_patch` sends `UpdateSettings` to the actor (`src/settings/api/settings_routes.rs:673-680`), then sends `UpdateSimulationParams` to the GPU (`:692-694`). If the GPU actor refuses, the function returns 500 "GPU update failed" (`:700-706`) with an early return that skips the SQLite write (`:774-779`) and the broadcast (`:796`). The actor snapshot already holds the new value.
- Failure: A physics value passes `validate_for_gpu` but the GPU actor independently rejects it (e.g., a runtime constraint not covered by validation). The caller sees 500. The actor snapshot now holds the rejected value; the store holds the old value. The next PATCH merges onto the rejected snapshot. The system runs with a physics value the GPU actor refuses, and the store records a different value. No reconciliation occurs until restart.
- Confidence: medium — the divergence mechanism is clear; how often the GPU actor refuses a value that passed validation is not determinable from the material.
- Severity: medium (state divergence between actor, store, and GPU)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in VC-06.3)

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual runtime behaviour of the `/api/health` route collision** (VC-01.7): whether `api_handler::config`'s `/health` or the consolidated health scope's `/health` answers at runtime depends on actix's internal scope-matching, which the material says is "settled by actix route matching at runtime, not by source." A probe is needed.

2. **Whether the GPU actor ever refuses a value that passed `validate_for_gpu`** (F-15): the material traces the code path but cannot show how often the divergence occurs in practice. Runtime telemetry or targeted testing is needed.

3. **Multi-replica deployment topology**: the NIP-98 replay finding (F-02) is only consequential if multiple replicas are actually deployed. The material references `docker-compose.unified.yml` but does not state the production replica count or whether a load balancer distributes across instances.

4. **Content of the `enrichment_decision` WebSocket broadcast** (F-03): the material states the broadcast happens but does not enumerate the fields in the message body. The severity depends on whether it contains only IDs/outcomes or also reasoning and metadata.

5. **Whether the `/api/physics` mutating routes are called by any real client** (VC-04.13): if no client calls them, the private-actor tension is latent. The material traces the code path but provides no call-frequency data.

## visionclaw / premortem

# Post-mortem: Production Incident — VisionClaw Server

## Incident summary

Twelve months after the verification stamp at commit `86cdd7635`, the VisionClaw unified server suffered a governance and custody failure in production. The enrichment-decision pipeline (the ACSP human-approval loop) silently stopped accepting broker decisions and git-bridge write-backs, and Solid pod provisioning returned 401 to every genuine NIP-98 caller. The operator's remediation—enabling `SOLID_ALLOW_ANONYMOUS` and widening `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS`—exposed private pod data and read APIs to unauthenticated callers. A client's enrichment decisions were recorded without valid attribution, and their pod's knowledge graph was readable by third parties.

---

## R-1 — Service callers (X-Agent-Key) are refused by the RbacGate in any release build, breaking the governance loop and incentivising a dangerous bypass

- **Topics:** VC-05.7, VC-05.8, VC-04.17, VC-01.10

- **Chain of events:**
  1. Production deploys with the production Compose profile (no `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE`), so the RbacGate enforces.
  2. The agentbox broker-bridge sends `POST /api/enrichment-proposals/{id}/decide` with only an `X-Agent-Key` header and no `Authorization` header.
