  3. The RbacGate classifies the POST as a mutation requiring `WriteGraph` and calls `verify_access`, which resolves identity only through dev bypasses, NIP-98, or the legacy `X-Nostr-Pubkey`/`X-Nostr-Token` pair. It has no `X-Agent-Key` path.
  4. The request is refused with 401 before the handler's own `require_agent_key` check runs.
  5. The same applies to `POST /api/ingest/writeback` (the git-bridge sends no credential at all), `GET /api/broker/inbox` and `GET /api/broker/cases/{id}` (`RequireAuth::power_user()` delegates to the same `verify_access`), and `POST /api/canary/register`/`observe` (same gate classification).
  6. The entire enrichment governance loop, broker inbox, git-bridge write-back, and canary registration are non-functional in production.
  7. Under pressure, the operator sets `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=1` (which the dev Compose service already arms at `docker-compose.unified.yml:116`) or `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1` to restore service. The former grants every request full admin (`src/utils/auth.rs:211-218`); the latter opens every safe-method read to anonymous callers.
  8. With the bypass active, any network-reachable caller can submit enrichment decisions, read the broker inbox, and mutate the graph without a credential.

- **Evidence:**
  - `src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:179-189` classifies any non-safe-method `/api` POST as needing `WriteGraph`.
  - `src/utils/auth.rs:259-265`, `:329-353` — `verify_access` has no `X-Agent-Key` path.
  - `../project/agentbox/management-api/routes/broker-bridge.js:105-115`, `:576-579` — the bridge sends only `X-Agent-Key`, no `Authorization`.
  - `src/handlers/ingest_writeback_handler.rs:19-23` — the git-bridge write-back sends no credential.
  - `docker-compose.unified.yml:288` — the production Compose service requires `VISIONCLAW_AGENT_KEY` for exactly these callers.
  - `docker-compose.unified.yml:116` — the dev service arms `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=1`, hiding the gap.
  - `src/handlers/broker_inbox_handler.rs:27-33`, `:246-247` — comments claim the bridge authenticates via `X-Agent-Key` through the same middleware, which is false.
  - `src/middleware/auth.rs:158-160` — `RequireAuth` delegates to `verify_access`.

- **Warning signs visible today:**
  - Three independent Tension/Drift blocks in VC-05.7, VC-05.8, and VC-04.17 each name the same root cause.
  - The production Compose file sets `VISIONCLAW_AGENT_KEY` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:288`) for callers the gate cannot authenticate.
  - The dev Compose service arms the full bypass (`docker-compose.unified.yml:116`) and so masks the defect in every development test.

- **Confidence:** high
- **Severity:** high

---

## R-2 — Every `/api/solid/*` mutation double-claims the NIP-98 event id, so genuine callers are always refused and the workaround exposes pod data

- **Topics:** VC-05.15, VC-03.4, VC-03.15

- **Chain of events:**
  1. A client sends `POST /api/solid/pods/init-nip98` with a valid `Authorization: Nostr <event>` header.
  2. The `/api` RbacGate runs `verify_access` → `NostrService::verify_nip98_auth` → `validate_nip98_token`, which verifies the signature and claims the event id in the process-wide replay cache (`src/utils/nip98.rs:164-168`, claim at `:573`).
  3. The handler `init_pod_nip98` calls `SolidPodState::extract_user_identity(req)` (`solid_proxy_handler.rs:188`, `:1332`), which runs `validate_nip98_token` on the same header a second time (`solid_proxy_handler.rs:225`).
  4. The second claim of the same event id returns `TokenReplayed`, so `extract_user_identity` returns `None`.
  5. The handler answers 401 (`solid_proxy_handler.rs:1335-1338`). A genuine caller cannot initialise a pod through this route.
  6. The same mechanism applies to every `/api/solid/*` mutation the gate verifies: `handle_solid_proxy` receives the request without an identity because the gate already consumed the token.
  7. The operator, unable to diagnose why every pod operation fails, sets `SOLID_ALLOW_ANONYMOUS=1` (`solid_proxy_handler.rs:137`) to restore service.
  8. Anonymous access falls through to the public ACL check in `handle_solid_proxy`, and any pod whose WAC ACL is permissive (the boot pull writes a public-read ACL at `/public/ontology/.acl`, `ontology_pull.rs:361-366`) is now readable by anyone.

- **Evidence:**
  - `src/utils/nip98.rs:164-168`, claim `:573` — the replay cache is process-wide and a second claim of the same id fails.
  - `src/handlers/solid_proxy_handler.rs:1332-1338` — `extract_user_identity` runs on the same header the gate already verified.
  - `src/utils/auth.rs:266-280` — `verify_access` avoids this trap for nested `RequireAuth` by reusing the identity in request extensions; `extract_user_identity` does not.
  - `src/main.rs:1206` — `/api/solid/*` is mounted inside the `/api` scope so the gate always runs.
  - `src/main.rs:1105` — the `/pay` routes are NOT affected because they mount at the App root and verify once.
  - `src/utils/auth.rs:211-218` — a dev build with `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=1` hides the defect entirely.

- **Warning signs visible today:**
  - The Tension in VC-05.15 states this precisely: "a genuine caller cannot initialise a pod through this route."
  - `verify_access` already has the correct pattern (reuse extensions at `auth.rs:266-280`) but `extract_user_identity` does not follow it.
  - `SOLID_ALLOW_ANONYMOUS` exists as an env var (`solid_proxy_handler.rs:137`) and is the obvious operational workaround.

- **Confidence:** high
- **Severity:** high

---

## R-3 — Briefing and debrief endpoints attribute actions to a caller-supplied pubkey, allowing any authenticated writer to publish decisions in another user's name

- **Topics:** VC-05.9, VC-05.6

- **Chain of events:**
  1. An authenticated user (any role with `WriteGraph`) sends `POST /api/briefs` with a request body whose `user_context.pubkey` field names a different user.
  2. `submit_brief` (`briefing_handler.rs:22-27`) reads `user_context` entirely from the request body. It does not extract an `AuthenticatedUser` and does not bind the body's pubkey to the RbacGate-verified caller.
  3. The brief is created via the Management API with the spoofed identity.
  4. On `POST /api/briefs/{brief_id}/debrief`, the handler again reads `user_context` from the body (`briefing_handler.rs:54-61`) and publishes a Nostr bead-completion event with `user_context.pubkey` as the user (`briefing_handler.rs:86-95`).
  5. The bead event is attributed to the spoofed user on the Nostr relay, and the debrief record in the Management API names the wrong principal.
  6. A client's decision record now carries another party's signature, which is a custody and attribution failure that is difficult to unwind after the bead event is published to a relay.

- **Evidence:**
  - `src/handlers/briefing_handler.rs:22-27` — `submit_brief` reads `user_context` from the body with no `AuthenticatedUser` extractor.
  - `src/handlers/briefing_handler.rs:54-61` — `request_debrief` does the same.
  - `src/handlers/briefing_handler.rs:86-95` — `publish_bead_complete` uses `user_context.pubkey` as the user.
  - `src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:179-189` — the RbacGate requires only `WriteGraph` for these POSTs, which any Editor role satisfies.
  - `src/handlers/nostr_handler.rs:267-275` — the correct pattern exists (`caller_pubkey` reads `get_authenticated_user`, never the URL or body) but is not used here.

- **Warning signs visible today:**
  - The Tension in VC-05.9 states this explicitly: "any authenticated writer can file a brief or publish a bead completion in another user's name."
  - The fix pattern (`nostr_handler::caller_pubkey`) already exists in the same codebase.
  - The bead event is published to a Nostr relay (`nostr_bead_publisher.rs`), making the misattribution public and permanent.

- **Confidence:** high
- **Severity:** high

---

## Further latent defects

### F-01 — Graph-export share maps and rate-limit budgets are per-worker, so a share created on one worker 404s on the other three
- **Topics:** VC-04.11
- **Evidence:** `configure_routes` builds a fresh `GraphExportHandler` on every call (`src/handlers/graph_export_handler.rs:315`), which runs once per worker inside the `HttpServer::new` factory (`src/main.rs:972`, `:1196`); the server runs 4 workers (`src/main.rs:1265`). Each worker holds its own `shared_graphs` map as a fresh `Arc` (`graph_export_handler.rs:38-45`).
- **Failure:** A client creates a shared graph link, receives the URL, and shares it. The recipient's request lands on a different worker and receives 404. The share is effectively lost. The 20-per-hour export limit is also per worker (80/hour total), not per client.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** yes (Tension)
- **Severity:** high

### F-02 — A GPU-actor refusal after UpdateSettings leaves the in-memory snapshot holding a value the GPU rejected and SQLite never saw
- **Topics:** VC-06.3
- **Evidence:** `apply_physics_patch` sends `UpdateSettings` first (`settings_routes.rs:673-680`), then sends `UpdateSimulationParams` to the GPU actor (`:692-694`). If the GPU actor refuses, the function returns 500 "GPU update failed" (`:700-706`) but the snapshot is already replaced. The SQLite write (`:774-779`) and the broadcast (`:796`) are skipped. The next PATCH merges onto the corrupted snapshot (`:618`, `:637`).
- **Failure:** Over repeated updates, the actor snapshot accumulates values the GPU refused. A later successful write persists a superset of bad values, silently changing physics behaviour across the fleet.
- **Confidence:** medium
- **Marked by authors:** yes (Tension)
- **Severity:** high

### F-03 — API keys are written to NostrService but read from ProtectedSettingsActor, so users can never read back the keys they saved
- **Topics:** VC-06.6
- **Evidence:** `POST /api/auth/nostr/api-keys` calls `nostr_service.update_user_api_keys(caller, keys)` (`nostr_handler.rs:293`, `nostr_service.rs:495-502`). `GET` on the same resource calls `state.get_api_keys(caller)` (`nostr_handler.rs:319`, `app_state.rs:1578-1583`) which reads `ProtectedSettingsActor`. That actor starts from `ProtectedSettings::default()` whose `users` map is empty (`protected_settings.rs:132`), and no message handler on that actor (`UpdateUserApiKeys` at `protected_settings_actor.rs:81`) has a sender anywhere.
- **Failure:** A user saves API keys through the UI and cannot retrieve them. If the keys are needed for a subsequent integration, the integration fails. The keys are persisted in Redis (if configured) on the NostrService side but are invisible to the read path.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** yes (Tension)
- **Severity:** high

### F-04 — POST /api/health/mcp/start runs docker exec in another container and stalls a worker thread, with no credential required
- **Topics:** VC-04.16
- **Evidence:** `/api/health/*` is on the RbacGate public allowlist (`src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:55-61`, `:154-159`); neither MCP route has a handler auth wrap (`consolidated_health_handler.rs:479-483`). `start_mcp_relay` runs `docker exec -d multi-agent-container … npm run mcp:start` (`mcp_relay_manager.rs:171-187`) then blocks its actix worker for 2 seconds with `std::thread::sleep` (`:194`). The logs route returns the tail of `/tmp/mcp-server.log` (`:210-219`).
- **Failure:** An anonymous caller can start processes in another container, read its log file, and stall one of four workers per request. Four concurrent requests stall all workers and the server becomes unresponsive.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** yes (Tension)
- **Severity:** high

### F-05 — The NIP-98 replay cache is process-local, so a token claimed on one replica can be replayed on another
- **Topics:** VC-03.4
- **Evidence:** `REPLAY_CACHE` is a `Mutex<HashMap>` inside the process (`src/utils/nip98.rs:221`), with no cross-replica mechanism. `SECURITY-profiles.md` is cited as noting this limitation.
- **Failure:** If the server is deployed behind a load balancer with more than one replica, an attacker captures a valid NIP-98 event and replays it to a different replica within the 60-second freshness window. The second replica's replay cache has no record of the id, so the replay succeeds.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** no
- **Severity:** high

### F-06 — GET /power-user-status and /features read an unauthenticated X-Nostr-Pubkey header, letting anyone enumerate any pubkey's role and feature set
- **Topics:** VC-05.3
- **Evidence:** The handlers read `pubkey = X-Nostr-Pubkey header` directly (`src/handlers/nostr_handler.rs:75-79`, `:94-98`, `:115-119`). The `/auth/nostr` prefix is on the RbacGate public allowlist (`rbac_gate.rs:55-61`).
- **Failure:** An attacker probes known Nostr pubkeys to learn which are power users and which features each holds, enabling targeted attacks against privileged accounts.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** yes (Tension)
- **Severity:** medium

### F-07 — /api/physics/start returns success and publishes SimulationStartedEvent without affecting the simulation clients see
- **Topics:** VC-04.13
- **Evidence:** The `/api/physics` port drives a private `PhysicsOrchestratorActor` with no GPU compute address (`src/adapters/actix_physics_adapter.rs:121-127`, `src/actors/physics_orchestrator_actor.rs:195-198`, `src/main.rs:855-857`). The comment in `layout_handler.rs:79-84` says "reachable but harmless … nothing they send reaches the device."
- **Failure:** A power user calls `POST /api/physics/start` or `POST /api/physics/parameters`, receives 200 with a `simulation_id`, and believes the physics has changed. The actual client-visible simulation (in the GPU actors) is unaffected. Subsequent debugging wastes time on a path that does nothing.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** yes (Tension)
- **Severity:** medium

### F-08 — An orchestrator text frame whose body is exactly "retry" opens a second orchestrator connection instead of reaching the client
- **Topics:** VC-05.12
- **Evidence:** The reader loop forwards every orchestrator text frame as `OrchestratorText(text)` (`mcp_relay_handler.rs:133-136`). The handler treats the exact string `retry` as a control signal and re-runs `connect_to_orchestrator` (`:265-277`).
- **Failure:** The orchestrator sends a text frame containing the word "retry" as part of a normal message. The relay actor opens a duplicate orchestrator connection. Repeated frames accumulate connections until the orchestrator's connection limit or the process's file-descriptor limit is exhausted.
- **Confidence:** medium
- **Marked by authors:** yes (Tension)
- **Severity:** medium

### F-09 — Every /ws/client-messages socket races on one shared receiver, so messages go to one socket by timing, never filtered by session
- **Topics:** VC-04.25
- **Evidence:** `start_message_stream` drains the single `app_state.client_message_rx` with `try_recv` on a 100 ms interval in every connected socket (`client_messages_handler.rs:34-52`). Messages are never filtered by `session_id`.
- **Failure:** Two clients connect to `/ws/client-messages`. A message intended for client A is received by client B because B's 100 ms tick fires first. Client A never sees the message. This is a data-delivery failure that manifests intermittently and is difficult to reproduce.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** yes (Tension)
- **Severity:** high

### F-10 — reset_layout writes to GPU and SQLite but never updates the OptimizedSettingsActor snapshot, so the next physics PUT restores pre-reset values
- **Topics:** VC-06.4
- **Evidence:** `reset_layout` writes canonical defaults to the GPU actors and to SQLite (`settings_routes.rs:1823-1863`) but never sends `UpdateSettings` to `OptimizedSettingsActor`. The next `apply_physics_patch` fetches the actor snapshot (`:618`, `:637`) and merges onto pre-reset values.
- **Failure:** An operator resets the layout. `GET /api/settings/physics` reads SQLite and shows the reset. The next physics PUT silently writes back every pre-reset field it does not name, undoing the reset for those fields.
- **Confidence:** medium
- **Marked by authors:** yes (Open)
- **Severity:** medium

### F-11 — The production image's stub build stage cannot fail, so a broken dependency produces a green layer
- **Topics:** VC-08.11
- **Evidence:** `cargo build --release 2>&1 | tail -40 || echo` (`Dockerfile.production:101-102`) — no `SHELL` with `pipefail`, so the pipeline's status is `tail`'s. Only `cargo fetch --locked` (`:100`) gates the layer. The comment at `:89-93` says the dependency build must succeed.
- **Failure:** A dependency fails to compile (API change, MSRV bump) after `cargo fetch --locked` succeeds (the lockfile is valid). The stub stage passes. The image is built with a missing or stale binary and deployed to production.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** yes (Tension)
- **Severity:** medium

### F-12 — Unbounded join_all on GET /api/pages fans out one upstream request per corpus page with no concurrency cap or cache
- **Topics:** VC-04.24
- **Evidence:** `get_pages` starts one `get_file_metadata_extended` call per metadata entry and awaits them all with an unbounded `join_all` (`pages_handler.rs:55-98`), with no cache and no concurrency cap.
- **Failure:** With a large corpus and public reads enabled, each anonymous `GET /api/pages` triggers hundreds of concurrent upstream content-API requests. The content API rate-limits or errors, and the VisionClaw server's worker threads are all blocked awaiting the fan-out.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** yes (Tension)
- **Severity:** medium

### F-13 — Compose defaults VISIONCLAW_JEV=1 and VISIONCLAW_JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD=1, routing triage and health data through the agentbox proxy by default
- **Topics:** VC-01.2, VC-09.16, VC-08.3
- **Evidence:** `docker-compose.unified.yml:21-22` sets `VISIONCLAW_JEV=1` and `VISIONCLAW_JEV_ALLOW_CLOUD=1` for both dev and production services. The Jev client routes through the shared agentbox proxy at `agentbox:8094` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:7-11`). If the agentbox proxy itself forwards to a public Typesafe API (`crates/visionclaw-jev/src/config.rs:347`), then health-triage hypotheses and proposal-triage annotations containing operational data leave the estate by default.
- **Failure:** A production deployment using the default Compose configuration sends degraded-health evidence and broker-case triage annotations to an external LLM endpoint without the operator having explicitly opted in to cloud routing for those data.
- **Confidence:** inferred
- **Marked by authors:** no
- **Severity:** medium

### F-14 — NVCC_CCBIN and CUDAHOSTCXX are missing from BUILD_INPUT_ENV_VARS, so a toolchain change does not invalidate the build stamp
- **Topics:** VC-08.10
- **Evidence:** `crates/visionclaw-gpu/build.rs:52-53` declares both as `rerun-if-env-changed`. `scripts/lib/build-inputs.sh:48` does not list them.
- **Failure:** An operator changes `NVCC_CCBIN` to point at a different host compiler. The wrapper's stamp is unchanged, cargo is skipped, and the binary is built with the old toolchain configuration. The resulting PTX may fail to load at runtime, causing a GPU-init failure and degraded mode.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** yes (Debt)
- **Severity:** low

### F-15 — The settings handler's second redundant ?token= extraction in dev builds re-sets pubkey from a query parameter that may differ from the first extraction
- **Topics:** VC-03.2
- **Evidence:** `http_handler.rs:371-379` and `:430-440` — a second `?token=` extraction runs `get_session` again and overwrites `ws_server.pubkey` (`:433-434`). The first extraction at `:187-202` already routed a query token through `get_session` into `signed_user` (`:262`), which feeds the same assignment (`:412-415`).
- **Failure:** In a dev build, if the query token is changed between the two reads (e.g. by a proxy that rewrites the URL), the socket's pubkey is set from the second, potentially different, session. The visibility filter then applies the wrong user's entitlement set to the position stream.
- **Confidence:** low
- **Marked by authors:** yes (Debt)
- **Severity:** low

## visionclaw#2 / critical

### F-01 — Any Nostr identity defaults to Editor (WriteGraph) in the shipped configuration
- Topics: VC-22.9, VC-20.7
- Evidence: An unassigned pubkey resolves to `Editor` unless `RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE=viewer` (`src/services/role_store.rs:366`). The shipped `docker-compose.unified.yml:132-133` sets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS` on and `RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE` with a `:-1` default that yields Editor, inverting the code's fail-closed posture (`src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:135-141`, `src/main.rs:809-818`). VC-20.7 confirms: "any signed caller passes, because an unassigned pubkey defaults to Editor." Nostr keypairs are freely generatable. The compose file IS the production deployment.
- Failure: Any anonymous person generates a Nostr key, signs in, and immediately holds WriteGraph (Editor). They can then hit every mutating `/api` route including ontology writes, graph mutations, and settings changes. In a multi-user deployment this is a total authorisation bypass.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the CORRECTED ADR-2070 marker acknowledges the facts but frames the open posture as "ADR-2027's deliberate demo default and stays," which understates that this is the shipped production posture rather than a lab-only default.

### F-02 — RuVector embeddings persist and remain searchable after Pod deletion, breaking erasure
- Topics: VC-22.6
- Evidence: `deleteAgentMemory` deletes the Pod-side `.jsonld` file (`client/src/services/solidPod/agentMemory.ts:191-198`) but "no reverse tombstone to RuVector — embedding row persists and stays searchable" (`docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:88`). The material confirms "this Pod-side delete never calls mcp__claude-flow__memory_delete or any RuVector endpoint." The server also now has a direct read-only RuVector client, giving the orphaned embedding a second consumer (`Cargo.toml:141-146`).
- Failure: A user requests erasure of agent memory. The Pod file is removed; the vector embedding remains in Postgres and continues to match semantic queries. Personal data survives a deletion request, creating a regulatory/compliance incident (GDPR or equivalent) and an ongoing data custody violation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-03 — Full-sync silently wipes all governed ontology writes made between syncs
- Topics: VC-22.10
- Evidence: `rebuild_assert_graph` does `CLEAR GRAPH assert then INSERT DATA` and "this CLEAR wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door (`github_sync_service.rs:952-954`)."` The note confirms: "a class added via add_owl_class/add_axiom between full-syncs is NOT itself in GRAPH_PROVENANCE-protected history for the assert graph — only re-derivation from the corpus... restores it after the next rebuild." No warning is issued and no backup of the wiped data is taken.
- Failure: An operator runs `FORCE_FULL_SYNC=1` to fix a sync problem. Every governance-approved ontology addition since the last full sync — classes accepted through the ACSP pipeline, axiom additions through the governed write door — is silently deleted. Downstream analytics, the reasoner, and the DAG ranker all reference now-nonexistent classes. Nobody notices until things break.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — stated as a Note without a Debt/Drift/Tension marker

### F-04 — Content explicitly marked `public: false` is ingested into the public graph when `owl-class` is present
- Topics: VC-21.2, VC-21.12
- Evidence: `is_kg_included()` is `self.public || self.owl_class.is_some()` (`crates/visionclaw-domain/src/vault/mod.rs:162-163`). The VAULT doc V4 says "no `owl-class` bypass and no OR" (`docs/VAULT-corpus-format.md:248-252`). The ADR-2070 closeout records "an explicit public false plus a class marker remains included by policy — is_kg_included is an OR, so a valid owl-class overrides public false."
- Failure: An author writes a page with `public: false` (explicitly private) and also assigns an `owl-class` for internal classification. The page is ingested into the public knowledge graph as an ontology node, exposing its title, content summary, and relationships to any user of the system. The author has no indication their "private" page is now public.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in VC-21.12), but the doc contradiction in VC-21.2 is a separate Drift

### F-05 — NIP-98 token body substitution on `/solid` writes within the 60-second window
- Topics: VC-26.4
- Evidence: `extract_user_identity` calls `validate_nip98_token(token, &expected_url, expected_method, None)` (`src/handlers/solid_proxy_handler.rs:225`). With no body, `BodyBinding::RequiredWhenTagged` matches the catch-all `(RequiredWhenTagged, _, _) => {}` (`src/utils/nip98.rs:556`), so "a payload tag is therefore never compared on `/solid` PUT, POST or PATCH." The `/solid` scope adds no other body check (`solid_proxy_handler.rs:1773-1807`).
- Failure: An attacker with network access obtains or intercepts an unused NIP-98 token for a `/solid` PUT to `/pods/alice/memory/secret.jsonld`. Within 60 seconds they replay the same token to the same URL and method but with different bytes (e.g. overwriting Alice's agent memory with poisoned data). The single-use claim limits them to one substitution, but that one write can inject or corrupt data. The material explicitly parallels this to SP-ID-01.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, referencing SP-ID-01)

### F-06 — ElevationActor restart drops all approved cases with open PRs; `concept_elevated` never fires
- Topics: VC-24.5, VC-24.4
- Evidence: "Reconcile reads rows with status pending (`elevation_actor.rs:1351`). An approved case whose draft PR is open is held only in the in-memory elevating map (`elevation_actor.rs:1572`), and no store write records its PR url." After restart "the merge poll never fires for it." DecisionElevationActor closes this gap but ElevationActor does not.
- Failure: A human approves a class elevation. A PR is created. The server restarts (deployment, crash, OOM). The merge poll no longer tracks the PR. The PR merges on GitHub, but `concept_elevated` never fires, the enrichment store status never moves from `approved` to `elevated`, and the governance record shows an open case forever. The human's decision is effectively lost.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-07 — Rationale gate bypassed for first-contact decisions with no recorded tier
- Topics: VC-24.3
- Evidence: "a case with no row yet carries no recorded tier, so a first-contact bridge decision that forwards no effective_tier is still ungated (`handlers/enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:502-508`)." The gate uses `gate_tier(declared, effective)` — the stricter of the two — but if neither is present, no gate applies.
- Failure: An agentbox bridge sends a POST to `/api/enrichment-proposals/new-case/decide` with `outcome: "approve"` and no `effective_tier` in the body. The case has no prior row, so no tier was recorded. The rationale gate is skipped entirely. A high-risk governance decision (which normally requires a ≥20-character human reasoning) is committed with an empty rationale and the writeback proceeds.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN)

### F-08 — Position chunk acknowledged before the worker applies it (16ms throttle window)
- Topics: VC-14.10, VC-30.10
- Evidence: `handleBinaryFrame` returns without applying when under 16ms have passed since the last frame (`wsClient.ts:26-27`). `updateNodePositions` then resolves and the chunk is acknowledged (`binaryProtocol.ts:394,408`). ADR-2141 decision 5 says "a chunk that was not applied is not acknowledged." The material explicitly calls this a TENSION.
- Failure: Two chunks arrive within 16ms (e.g. during a burst). The second is throttled and never reaches the worker, but the ack is sent. The server records the client as holding nodes it does not have, sends the next chunk, and the client's graph is permanently out of sync. The XR client's node positions diverge from the server's authoritative state with no reconciliation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION in both VC-14.10 and VC-30.10)

### F-09 — MCP relay circuit breaker structurally cannot trip; orchestrator becomes a dead sink
- Topics: VC-27.2, VC-27.3
- Evidence: "every caller builds a fresh McpRelayManager (`consolidated_health_handler.rs:210,428`), so the breaker never sees a second request and its thresholds cannot trip — the start path bypasses it anyway (`mcp_relay_manager.rs:171`)." Separately, `is_orchestrator_healthy` is only ever set true (`mcp_relay_handler.rs:73,302`), and when the orchestrator stream closes, "later client sends go to a dead sink and only log the failure (`:361-368`)."
- Failure: The orchestrator container goes down. The circuit breaker cannot detect the pattern because no state persists across calls. WebSocket clients continue connecting, their messages are forwarded to a dead orchestrator connection, and every message is silently dropped with only a log line. No error surfaces to any user. The entire agent-management UI becomes non-functional without any visible failure signal.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT for both)

### F-10 — GPU analytics monitoring and compute are non-functional: metrics return zeros, two routes cannot reach kernels, insights are static
- Topics: VC-15.3, VC-15.5, VC-18.4, VC-18.6
- Evidence: `POST /analytics/community/detect` always fails because ForceComputeActor refuses `RunCommunityDetection` (`force_compute_actor.rs:3347-3352`). PageRank routes read `AppState.analytics.pagerank` built as `None` (`app_state.rs:1296-1300`). `GetGPUMetrics` returns "hard-coded zeros" (`force_compute_actor.rs:3329-3344`). All four insights endpoints "are pure reads over graph data plus process-global state — none dispatch a GPU kernel" (VC-18.6).
- Failure: An operator monitors system health through `/analytics/gpu-metrics`, `/analytics/stats`, and `/analytics/insights`. Everything looks calm because the metrics are zeros and the insights are cached state. Meanwhile, actual GPU compute is failing (if it is even running). A graph anomaly goes undetected. When someone finally runs community detection or PageRank through the API, they get a 500 with no useful diagnostic. The system appears healthy while its core analytics are dead.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT for the route reachability; the zero-metrics and static-insights pattern is stated but its compound effect on observability is not marked)

### F-11 — Multiple POST endpoints return success without performing the requested action
- Topics: VC-16.6, VC-18.3, VC-18.5
- Evidence: `POST /analytics/params` and `POST /analytics/focus` reach "a no-op handler that answers Ok (`force_compute_actor.rs:3355-3365`)." `POST /constraints/apply` and `/constraints/remove` "reach no actor" (`constraints_handler.rs:181-188`, `:218-223`). `POST /stress-majorization/params` returns "Ok after a log line, nothing forwarded (`force_compute_actor.rs:3480-3490`)." `GET /analytics/constraints` masks a guaranteed Err as an empty successful set (`params_handlers.rs:129-133`).
- Failure: A user or automation client POSTs new analytics parameters or applies constraints. The API returns 200 success. The user believes constraints are active and parameters are applied. Physics behavior does not change. When the simulation produces unexpected results, nobody suspects the constraint system because the API said it worked. Debugging this requires reading source code.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT for each individually; the pattern across multiple subsystems is not called out as a systemic problem)

### F-12 — Governance decision text sent to third-party cloud API by default
- Topics: VC-28.9
- Evidence: "the four local-only items (memory rerank, patch rank, ingest suggest, proposal triage) take the proxy because ALLOW_CLOUD=1 (`docker-compose.unified.yml:22`)." The proxy forwards to "api.typesafe.ai with TYPESAFE_API_KEY" and "backend = typesafe (cloud)." ADR-2148 is titled "enable-local-typed-decisions" but the shipped Compose sends proposal triage text to a cloud endpoint. The private content of governance proposals and decisions flows to a third party.
- Failure: The content of enrichment proposals — which may contain private graph data, agent reasoning, and governance rationale — is transmitted to api.typesafe.ai. In a deployment subject to data residency or confidentiality requirements, this is an unlogged data egress. The ADR title ("enable-local-typed-decisions") contradicts what the shipped configuration actually does.
- Confidence: medium — the ADR is marked accepted and the Compose is explicit, so this is a deliberate choice; the data-custody implication is what is unmarked
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — ADR-2148 is marked resolved/accepted/live; the tension between the title ("local") and the shipped cloud routing is not marked

### F-13 — Whelk reasoning runs with no timeout or axiom-count cap; can hang the sync cycle
- Topics: VC-20.3
- Evidence: "module doc claims complete EL reasoning (`whelk_inference_engine.rs:5`) but infer() lines 412-467 has no tokio timeout or axiom-count cap." The reasoning runs inside `run_post_sync_reasoning` in the GitHub sync cycle (`github_sync_service.rs:1205-1255`), which is called from the boot task and the admin sync endpoint.
- Failure: The ontology grows to the point where Whelk's EL++ materialisation takes minutes or hours (no bound exists). The sync task blocks. The admin sync endpoint times out or the boot sync never completes. No new data is ingested. The system appears hung with no error, because no timeout fires. On the elevation path, `run_consistency_gate` calls the same engine and can block the governance pipeline indefinitely.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the DOC-DRIFT marker addresses the doc/code mismatch, not the operational risk of unbounded execution

### F-14 — Client settings changes silently lost after three failed retries; nothing listens to the failure event
- Topics: VC-30.4
- Evidence: `settingsRetryManager` retries failed settings writes up to `maxRetries=3` with exponential backoff (`settingsRetryManager.ts:23-25`). On exhaustion it "dispatches window CustomEvent settings-retry-failed `{path,value,error}`" (`settingsRetryManager.ts:123-125`). The material does not describe any listener for this event in the client. After the third retry fails, the change is dropped from the queue and the client's local state (already updated) diverges from the server.
- Failure: A user changes physics parameters. The server is briefly unreachable. Three retries fail over ~30 seconds. The client shows the new value (local state already committed). The server still has the old value. On next page reload, the setting reverts. The user has no notification that their change was lost — no modal, no toast, no alert is described as handling `settings-retry-failed`.
- Confidence: medium — the mechanism is fully described; the absence of a listener is inferred from the material not mentioning one
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — Enrichment decision commits in SQLite but the Oxigraph graph write is best-effort with no reconciliation
- Topics: VC-22.8
- Evidence: "no cross-store 2PC — the durable decision commits in SQLite first (writeback_triggered=true) — the Oxigraph write is best-effort, writeback_committed flips only on Ok (`enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:576`, `:589`)." No retry or reconciliation mechanism is described for the case where the SQLite write succeeds but the Oxigraph write fails. The `writeback_committed` flag records the failure but nothing reads it to trigger a retry.
- Failure: A broker approves an enrichment proposal. The decision commits to SQLite. The Oxigraph write fails (connection issue, lock contention). The decision is durable but the knowledge graph was never updated. `writeback_committed` stays `false`. No background process retries. The approved enrichment is invisible in the graph until someone manually inspects the enrichment table and re-triggers the writeback. Meanwhile, the trace (VC-25.1) reports `merged: not_applicable` for this case, silently misclassifying it.
- Confidence: high — the two-phase failure mode is explicitly described; the absence of reconciliation is inferred from the material's silence
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — the material describes the mechanism as a design property (best-effort) without marking the divergence risk

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual runtime performance of the GPU kernels under production load** — the material records conformance-test deltas (VC-15.13) but says "these are recorded results, not re-run here." I cannot tell whether Louvain, PageRank, or DBSCAN meet latency budgets at the graph sizes this deployment will see.

2. **Whether the `/api` scope's `RbacGate` middleware wraps `/ws/*` WebSocket upgrade routes** — VC-16 mentions `/layout/radial` and `/layout/reset` lack `AuthenticatedUser`, and the material says "whether the enclosing `/api` scope authenticates them is outside this source trace." I cannot confirm whether WebSocket endpoints like `/wss`, `/ws/presence`, and `/wss/agent-events` are inside or outside the RBAC gate from this material alone.

3. **The operational state of the deployed Compose stack** — whether the shipped `docker-compose.unified.yml` values (RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE, ALLOW_CLOUD, VISIONCLAW_JEV) have been overridden in the actual production deployment, or are running as-shipped. The material shows the file contents but not the runtime environment.

4. **Data volume and graph size at which subsystems fail** — the wire protocol caps at ~67M nodes (26-bit), the worker caps at 50,000 nodes (`MAX_NODES=50000`), and the Dijkstra implementation is O(V×E), but I cannot tell what the production graph size actually is or will be.

5. **The agentbox side of the trust boundary** — several findings reference agentbox (ES-02, the broker bridge, the System One proxy) but its own code and configuration are cited only from the VisionClaw side. Whether the agentbox management API and its Docker socket access constitute a separate attack surface is not assessable from this material.

## visionclaw#2 / premortem

# Post-mortem — client-costing data/custody/security failure (reconstructed)

Incident class per the brief: a data, custody or security failure that lost a client. The three chains below are the highest-likelihood root causes supported by the estate documentation at the stamped commits. Everything is reported as the material states it; where I reason past the material I say "inferred". All citations are the material's own.

---

### R-1 — Erasure never reaches the vector store: deleted agent memory stays searchable in RuVector
- Topics: VC-22.6, VC-22.1, VC-22.11
- Chain of events:
  1. Client (or operator on the client's behalf) calls `SolidPodService.deleteAgentMemory` (`client/src/services/SolidPodService.ts:366-368`).
  2. `agentMemory.deleteAgentMemory` issues one LDP `DELETE` on the Pod resource (`client/src/services/solidPod/agentMemory.ts:191-198`).
  3. `deleteResource` returns success on 2xx **or 404** (`client/src/services/solidPod/ldpClient.ts:201-210`), so the caller receives "deleted-or-absent" and reports deletion done.
  4. No code on this path calls `mcp__claude-flow__memory_delete` or any RuVector endpoint — the material states "no delete-propagation hook exists in this path" (VC-22.6).
  5. The embedding row in RuVector (external Postgres, the write-master row for vector/agent memory per VC-22.1) persists and **stays searchable**; the server additionally holds a direct read-only RuVector client (`visionclaw-memory-cloud`, `Cargo.toml:141-146`), so the retained row has a second consumer.
  6. Nothing in operations enumerates the vector store either: `backup-sqlite.sh` `REQUIRED_DBS` covers settings/enrichment/kpi only (`scripts/backup-sqlite.sh:75`), so no runbook surface even names the store where the data survives.
- Evidence: VC-22.6's DIVERGENCE rect — "no reverse tombstone to RuVector - embedding row persists and stays searchable (`docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:88`)"; the DRIFT note that MCP-only access "no longer" holds for reads (`Cargo.toml:141-146`); `deleteResource` 404-as-success at `ldpClient.ts:205, :210`; VC-22.1 places "Vector / agent memory" under RuVector as write-master.
- Warning signs visible today: the DIVERGENCE is drawn in red in VC-22.6 and named in `DATA-authority-erasure.md:88`; ADR-2102 (crypto-shredding/provenance-scope choice) and ADR-2103 (backup RPO/RTO) are both still *proposed* only (VC-22.4, VC-22.7, VC-22.11), so the erasure story has no accepted mechanism at all.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high.

### R-2 — Shipped deployment inverts the code's fail-closed RBAC: any self-issued key becomes Editor and gets write custody
- Topics: VC-22.9, VC-20.7, VC-24.3, VC-17.1
- Chain of events:
  1. Code is fail-closed: `public_reads_enabled()` ends `.unwrap_or(false)` (`src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:135-141`) and boot refuses an owner-less deployment (`src/main.rs:809-818`).
  2. The shipped compose inverts both — `docker-compose.unified.yml:132, :133` (including a `:-1` default-role default) — which the material calls "the open posture... ADR-2027's deliberate demo default".
  3. Authentication is self-sovereign DID/nostr; an unassigned pubkey resolves to `default_role`, Editor unless overridden (`src/services/role_store.rs:366`, cited at `:359`).
  4. `required_level` demands `WriteGraph` (Editor+) for every non-safe method outside the auth/client-logs/health allowlist (`src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:179-189`, allowlist `:55-61`).
  5. An outsider with a fresh key therefore reaches governed write doors: `POST /decisions/record` (the ADR-2116 replacement for `/ontology-agent/propose`, mounted in the RbacGate-wrapped `/api` scope, `src/main.rs:1137`, `:1199`), `/ontology-agent` POSTs (the "anonymous" comment is wrong per VC-20.7's CORRECTED note), and every non-safe `/api` route at Editor.
  6. With `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1`, all safe GETs are public: `/api/graph/data`, the ontology GET family, `/api/trace`, analytics telemetry — governance and graph content become readable without any key.
  7. Adjacent surfaces are open the same way: `/api/visualization/agents/ws` takes no token check of its own (VC-27.11 OPEN), and presence rooms have no admission control or cap at this pin (VC-17 note; VC-49 cross-reference).
- Evidence: VC-22.9 CORRECTED rect (`rbac_gate.rs:135-141`, `role_store.rs:359-371`, `docker-compose.unified.yml:132-133`, `main.rs:809-818`); VC-20.7 (`rbac_gate.rs:188`, `main.rs:1137`, `:1199`, stale comments at `rbac_gate.rs:200` and `main.rs:1202`; unassigned→Editor `role_store.rs:366`); VC-27.11 OPEN note (`bots_visualization_handler.rs:204-210`, `src/main.rs:1131-1137`); VC-17 closing note that the handshake diagrams "do not imply membership admission or a room cap".
- Warning signs visible today: the CORRECTED rect in VC-22.9 says the compose "inverts both" fail-closed defaults; two comments in the tree already disagree with the code (`rbac_gate.rs:200`, `main.rs:1202`); the estate review cross-references (VC-49, SP-ID-01-era reviews) show this boundary was already under audit.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high.

### R-3 — A force-full-sync silently destroys runtime governed ontology writes, and no Oxigraph backup exists to restore them
- Topics: VC-22.10, VC-22.11, VC-22.7, VC-20.2
- Chain of events:
  1. An operator sets `FORCE_FULL_SYNC=1` (or `?force_full=true`).
  2. `sync_graphs` clears the knowledge and agent graphs (`src/services/github_sync_service.rs:406-410`, `src/adapters/oxigraph_graph_repository.rs:1215-1216`) and `rebuild_assert_graph` performs an atomic `CLEAR GRAPH` on `:assert` then re-INSERTs (`github_sync_service.rs:1026`).
  3. That CLEAR "wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door" (`github_sync_service.rs:952-954`, the material's own emphasis).
  4. The rebuild re-inserts only corpus-derived classes; runtime `add_owl_class`/`add_axiom` additions are not in the corpus, so they are not re-derived. `rematerialise_decisions` restores decision records only, and is itself "non-fatal on failure" (`github_sync_service.rs:1042-1048`).
  5. These runtime additions are "NOT... in GRAPH_PROVENANCE-protected history for the assert graph" (VC-22.10 DIRECT note), and Oxigraph has no point-in-time backup at all (ADR-2103 still *proposed*; `backup-sqlite.sh` covers SQLite members only, `backup-sqlite.sh:75`).
  6. The sync completes and reports `SyncStatistics`; the material shows no mechanism that reports the removed runtime additions. The loss is permanent.
- Evidence: VC-22.10 (`github_sync_service.rs:370, :625-627, :952-954, :1026, :1042-1048`; the DIRECT note on add_owl_class/add_axiom); VC-22.11 ("Oxigraph still has NO point-in-time backup - that bullet stands"); VC-22.7 (ADR-2103 proposed; `GRAPH_PROVENANCE` restore-only because it cannot be re-derived); VC-20.2 for the graph layout being wiped.
- Warning signs visible today: two proposed-but-unaccepted ADRs (2102, 2103) sit exactly on this gap; the VC-22.10 diagram itself carries the unprotected-write note in-line.
- Confidence: high (the wipe is explicit; irrecoverability follows from the no-backup/restore-only facts).
- Severity: high.

---

### F-01 — NIP-98 tokens on `/solid` never bind the write body
- Topics: VC-26.4
- Evidence: `extract_user_identity` calls `validate_nip98_token(token, &expected_url, expected_method, None)` (`src/handlers/solid_proxy_handler.rs:225`); with `BodyBinding::RequiredWhenTagged` (`src/utils/nip98.rs:439`) and no body, the catch-all `(RequiredWhenTagged, _, _) => {}` (`nip98.rs:556`) matches, so a `payload` tag is never compared on `/solid` PUT/POST/PATCH; the `/solid` scope adds no other body check (`solid_proxy_handler.rs:1773-1807`). Token lifetime 60 s (`nip98.rs:169`); single-use claim (`nip98.rs:573`) limits the attack to one substitution per token. The material parallels SP-ID-01 in `docs/estate-review/2026-11-01-solid-identity-boundaries.md`.
- Failure: an attacker who captures an unused signed token (log leak, proxy, client compromise) substitutes different bytes to the same URL+method inside the 60 s window — e.g. replaces an agent-memory JSON-LD document or a graph view the victim was about to write.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension).
- Severity: high.

### F-02 — ElevationActor loses approved, PR-tracked cases on restart
- Topics: VC-24.4, VC-24.5
- Evidence: boot `Reconcile` reloads only rows still `pending` (`src/actors/elevation_actor.rs:1351`); an approved case lives only in the in-memory `elevating` map (`elevation_actor.rs:1572`) and its PR url "is never persisted"; after restart the merge poll never fires, so `concept_elevated` and the `elevated` status never land even if the PR merges. `DecisionElevationActor` closes the same gap by persisting the PR url first (`src/actors/decision_elevation_actor.rs:363`).
- Failure: a human's signed approval is silently dropped; the PR may merge into the corpus while the durable case never reaches a terminal status — governance record and ontology state diverge with no error raised.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt).
- Severity: high.

### F-03 — Agent-event ingest "Attributed" proves nothing: unsigned pubkeys become durable provenance and KPI rows
- Topics: VC-27.13, VC-25.3
- Evidence: "ProvenanceStatus::Attributed means a well-formed pubkey was asserted, NOT that a signature was verified - the wire carries no sig field" (`src/agent_events/provenance.rs:29-38`); classification is purely by 64-hex shape (`provenance.rs:66-72`); the passive KPI tap persists these events verbatim, including the declared intent column (`src/services/kpi_compute.rs:646, :650`; `src/adapters/sqlite_kpi_repository.rs:334, :245`; `migrations/sqlite/0006_kpi_agent_event_intent.sql:5-9`).
- Failure: anyone holding the ingest credential (bearer session) can publish events under any agent DID; forged intent declarations become immutable KPI/provenance evidence — the material's own invariant says "a later reading cannot soften it", which cuts both ways.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: no (flagged inline as SECURITY, not under one of the four markers).
- Severity: high.

### F-04 — First-contact decisions are ungated and `approvedBy` is unverified: attributed summary writebacks can be fabricated
- Topics: VC-24.3, VC-22.3
- Evidence: "a case with no row yet carries no recorded tier, so a first-contact bridge decision that forwards no effective_tier is still ungated" (`src/handlers/enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:502-508`); the handler will create a stub pending case for an unknown id (`:537`); attribution is taken from `approvedBy` as `did:nostr` or hex with no verification shown (`src/handlers/ingest_writeback_handler.rs:62-70`); approve+attributed triggers `append_derived_summary(owner_did, ...)` into `:summary` (`enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:573-576`). The whole decide route is gated by one shared `VISIONCLAW_AGENT_KEY` constant-time-compared header (`utils/agent_key.rs:13, :15`) — a single credential whose leak mints attributed governance facts.
- Failure: a leaked or rogue bridge key approves fabricated cases attributed to a real owner DID; derived summary quads land in `GRAPH_ONTOLOGY_SUMMARY` with a forged `prov:wasGeneratedBy` marker and `writeback_committed=true`.
- Confidence: high (gating gap explicit; attribution non-verification is from absence of any verification step in the drawn path — inferred in part).
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, on the ungated first-contact tier).
- Severity: high.

### F-05 — Private broker proposal text is sent to a third-party cloud backend
- Topics: VC-24.13, VC-28.9
- Evidence: with `ALLOW_CLOUD=1` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:22`, `crates/visionclaw-jev/src/config.rs:303-305`) and the shared block's `agentbox:8094` with `BACKEND_LOCAL=0` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:7-11`), "the private proposal text" goes "via the proxy to the selected backend, typesafe (`agentbox.toml:891`), by operator decision" (ADR-2148:65-66). The proxy forwards to `api.typesafe.ai` as the selected cloud backend (VC-28.9 TS node); consumers send no Authorization of their own (the agentbox proxy holds the vendor key).
- Failure: confidential client/governance proposal content leaves the estate to an external LLM vendor under a shared key — a confidentiality breach the client was never per-case asked about, recorded nowhere per-proposal.
- Confidence: high (that the egress happens); inferred only as to whether any given proposal's content is client-confidential.
- Marked by authors: no (presented as an accepted, live configuration under ADR-2148).
- Severity: high.

### F-06 — An explicit `public: false` is overridden by any valid `owl-class` marker
- Topics: VC-21.2, VC-21.12
- Evidence: the inclusion gate is the disjunction `self.public || self.owl_class.is_some()` (`crates/visionclaw-domain/src/vault/mod.rs:162-163`, reached via `src/services/github_sync_service.rs:2114`); `docs/VAULT-corpus-format.md:248-252` (V4) promises "no `owl-class` bypass and no OR"; VC-21.12 records the DIVERGENCE that "a valid owl-class overrides public false".
- Failure: an author explicitly marks a page private (`public: false`) but it carries an ontology class marker; the page is ingested as a KG node, persisted to `GRAPH_KNOWLEDGE`, and with `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1` (R-2) becomes publicly readable — a direct confidentiality failure against the author's expressed intent and the governing doc.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift; the override itself is recorded as policy in the ADR-2070 closeout).
- Severity: high.

### F-07 — Erasure and the append-only provenance ledger are unresolved, and the decision fan-out writes no PROV-O at all
- Topics: VC-22.4, VC-22.8
- Evidence: ADR-2102 is *proposed* — crypto-shredding vs declaring provenance out of erasure scope is an unmade choice (VC-22.4 note); separately, the approved-decision fan-out leaves nothing in `GRAPH_PROVENANCE`: the `prov:wasGeneratedBy` marker lives in `:summary` (`crates/visionclaw-adapters/src/oxigraph_ontology_repository.rs:821-827`), `append_derived_summary` routes only through `append_derived_quads` (`:841`), and `apply_decision` emits no PROV activity (`src/handlers/enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:473`).
- Failure: a data subject's erasure demand cannot be satisfied without breaking the append-only invariant ADR-2016 asserts, while simultaneously the audit trail the operator would rely on is missing entries for decided cases entirely.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift on the missing PROV; ADR-2102 carried as proposed/open).
- Severity: medium.

### F-08 — `working/` notes are ingested contrary to the governance doc
- Topics: VC-21 (drift block)
- Evidence: `docs/VAULT-corpus-format.md:256` says `working/` is never ingested into the governed graph; the local source's default base paths are `knowledge,working` (`src/services/corpus_source/local.rs:21`) and the sync "keeps a working page as its own node unless a knowledge page owns the same id" (`src/services/github_sync_service.rs:2156`).
- Failure: private working drafts that pass the (mis-gated, F-06) inclusion test become governed nodes and are broadcast; the operator's privacy boundary is not where the documentation says it is.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift).
- Severity: medium.

### F-09 — Position chunks are acknowledged without being applied
- Topics: VC-14.10, VC-30.10
- Evidence: ADR-2141 decision 5 says a chunk that was not applied is not acknowledged (`docs/adr/ADR-2141-positions-are-streamed-per-client-until-acknowledged.md:32`), but `handleBinaryFrame` "returns normally when a frame arrives within 16 ms of the last accepted one", so `updateNodePositions` resolves and the ack is sent "for a chunk the worker never saw" (`client/src/features/graph/managers/dataManager/wsClient.ts:26-28`; `client/src/store/websocket/binaryProtocol.ts:393-409`). The server commits that chunk's nodes as held and builds the next at once (`src/actors/client_coordinator_actor.rs:1365-1396`).
- Failure: under sustained frame pressure the client silently diverges from the stream the server believes it holds — persistent stale positions with green acknowledgement accounting; no retransmit is ever triggered because the ack lied.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension).
- Severity: medium.

### F-10 — Wire node ids above the 26-bit mask alias after masking
- Topics: VC-14.2, VC-23.7
- Evidence: ids above `NODE_ID_MASK` (0x03FFFFFF, max 67,108,863) are masked by `remap_wire_id` (`src/utils/binary_protocol.rs:194, :217-219`) inside `enforce_wire_id_bounds` (`:185-206`), logged as `NODE ID OVERFLOW`, and "still masked and therefore aliases" (VC-23.7 RESOLVED note); the bound is a `debug_assert!` in dev only (`:186-193`); release builds mask silently-apart-from-log.
- Failure: if the id space ever exceeds 26 bits (counter growth, collision with pre-stamped flag patterns), two distinct nodes share a wire id and clients apply positions, SSSP parents and analytics flags to the wrong node.
- Confidence: medium (mechanism explicit; the >67 M precondition is not shown to be near).
- Marked by authors: no (acknowledged inside a RESOLVED note, not under a marker).
- Severity: medium.

### F-11 — Nonce replay protection is a 4096-entry LRU shared across all sessions
- Topics: VC-17.1
- Evidence: "a nonce is accepted at most once across all sessions (SeenNonces `presence_handler.rs:51`)" with "LRU capacity SEEN_NONCE_CAPACITY 4096" (`presence_handler.rs:57`, check `:259-271`).
- Failure: replay protection holds only while a nonce stays resident; an attacker who can drive ~4096 handshakes (own sessions count — the cache is shared) can evict a captured nonce and replay the signed challenge within the 30 s skew window, authenticating as the victim in a presence room. The material does not state eviction→replay explicitly; that step is inferred from the bounded LRU.
- Confidence: medium, inferred.
- Marked by authors: no.
- Severity: high.

### F-12 — Drop-oldest backpressure discards governance evidence before it is persisted
- Topics: VC-27.13, VC-16.4, VC-25.3
- Evidence: the agent-event hub is `broadcast::Sender` with `HUB_CAPACITY=256` that "drops oldest under backpressure" (`src/agent_events/hub.rs:22, :32-34`); the KPI tap is a downstream subscriber (`kpi_compute.rs:646, :650`), not a pre-publish journal; the 0x23 coalescer likewise "evicts the oldest" at 256 pending — "recency over completeness" (`src/actors/agent_beam_actor.rs:86, :137-146`).
- Failure: a burst of agent actions overruns the hub, the tap never sees the dropped events, and the KPI/provenance record for that window is silently incomplete — with no gap marker, since nothing records the drop beyond a counter.
- Confidence: medium (drop behavior explicit; the tap's lag tolerance is not quantified in the material).
- Marked by authors: no (recency-over-completeness is stated as design, not flagged).
- Severity: medium.

### F-13 — Decision→summary writeback is best-effort with no retry
- Topics: VC-22.8
- Evidence: "no cross-store 2PC - the durable decision commits in SQLite first... the Oxigraph write is best-effort, `writeback_committed` flips only on Ok" (`src/handlers/enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:576, :589`); no retry path for a failed writeback is drawn anywhere in VC-22.3/22.8.
- Failure: an Oxigraph hiccup at decide time leaves the durable decision in SQLite with `writeback_committed=false` forever; the derived summary quad the decision promised never lands, and nothing revisits it — a permanently split-brained record the flags record but nobody repairs.
- Confidence: medium-high (absence of retry is from absence in the traced paths).
- Marked by authors: no (stated as a plain note).
- Severity: medium.

### F-14 — An unauthenticated `/api/client-logs` endpoint receives the client's entire intercepted console
- Topics: VC-30.7, VC-20.7
- Evidence: the remote logger wraps `console.log/debug/info/warn/error` and POSTs buffered entries (args, stacks, `data`) to `VITE_API_URL + "/api/client-logs"` (`client/src/services/remoteLogger.ts:31-33, :49-83`); the RBAC allowlist exempts exactly `/api/auth`, client-logs and health probes (`src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:55-61`).
- Failure: any code (or dependency) that logs tokens, npubs, graph payloads or errors exfiltrates them to an endpoint any anonymous caller can also write to; combined with the logger's own defect — it never restores the original console methods after disabling (`remoteLogger.ts:220-228`) — the interception is permanent for the session.
- Confidence: medium-high (plumbing explicit; the presence of secrets in console output is inferred).
- Marked by authors: no (the adjacent self-feedback loop is marked Debt at `remoteLogger.ts:194`; the exposure itself is not flagged).
- Severity: medium.

### F-15 — Credentials live in plaintext `.env`; the SOPS rollout was never executed
- Topics: VC-22.1
- Evidence: the credentials row of the write-master matrix maps to ".env plaintext filesystem"; ADR-2104 is *proposed* — "execute the SOPS rollout or formally withdraw ADR-109 - plaintext .env is documented as the interim state either way"; "a 43MB scripts/sops binary dated 2026-05-09 is present and gitignored, but no .sops.yaml, secrets.enc.yaml or sops-env.sh exists" (`docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:113`).
- Failure: one container/host compromise (or one backed-up or mirrored `.env`) yields the panel secret shared by three ACSP connections (VC-24.11), `VISIONCLAW_AGENT_KEY` (F-04), `PRIVATE_REPO_GITHUB_PAT` (VC-28.6) and `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` (VC-27.7) at once — full governance and corpus-write custody in a single file.
- Confidence: high (state is explicit; exploitation is the standard consequence).
- Marked by authors: yes (carried as an open/proposed item, ADR-2104).
- Severity: high.

## visionclaw#3 / critical

### F-01 — Unauthenticated speech-socket audio frames reach transcription, and transcripts with any of 20 keywords execute server-side commands
- Topics: VC-35.4, VC-35.6
- Evidence: VC-35.4 states ADR-2075's gate is "RESOLVED: authenticate is the ONLY frame accepted while pubkey is None. reject_unauthenticated... refuses tts, stt, voice_command, set_ptt and cancel_tts" (speech_socket_handler.rs:133, :627-633) — then in the same note: "Binary audio frames are NOT behind the gate: the Binary arm :956-976 forwards any frame to process_audio_chunk without a pubkey check" (:968). The socket opens with "NO credential... at upgrade" (ADR-2075 note). VC-35.6 TENSION: "a Whisper transcript that contains any of 20 keywords (agent, node, graph, run, show...) is ALSO executed server-side: is_voice_command, VoiceCommand::parse and execute_voice_command_with_context (src/services/speech_service.rs:620-633, :1032-1060, :1062), which can call swarm init and agent spawn on MCP_HOST (:1066-1068, :1079, :1086). That path has no actorDid, no clarification gate and no signature."
- Failure: anyone who can reach /ws/speech opens it unauthenticated, sends one binary audio frame whose transcript contains e.g. "run agent", and triggers swarm init / agent spawn on MCP_HOST. Unauthenticated remote action, bounded to the built-in verb set; whether MCP_HOST answers is deployment state.
- Confidence: high (every link is stated; the material itself notes the MCP_HOST caveat)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — each half is flagged (the binary-arm gap inside the ADR-2075 note; the keyword path as TENSION); the composed unauthenticated-injection-to-execution chain is not marked anywhere.

### F-02 — The speech plane carries no speaker identity: unauthenticated peers receive every transcript and TTS chunk, and anonymous speech can open settings-assistant tasks and ACSP elevation cases
- Topics: VC-35.4, VC-35.9, VC-35.10
- Evidence: VC-35.4 TENSION: "started() subscribes this socket to the SpeechService audio and transcription broadcasts before any authenticate frame (speech_socket_handler.rs:511-541), and the forwarders write to the socket with no pubkey check (:556-558, :571-583). For up to AUTH_DEADLINE [30 s, :22] an unauthenticated peer receives every TTS chunk and transcript broadcast"; the socket is closed at the deadline (:487-500) but nothing rate-limits reconnection. VC-35.10 TENSION: "both actors consume the GLOBAL transcript broadcast, so speech from any connected speech socket — including one not yet authenticated — can open a settings-assistant task or an elevation case. Neither path carries a speaker identity" (voice_interface_actor.rs:151-176; elevation_actor.rs:1138-1200, which "publish[es] an ACSP action request and persist[s] the pending case", :1166-1180). VC-35.9: "The broadcast is global, so every connected speech socket plays every utterance."
- Failure: an attacker reconnects every ~30 s and continuously hears all room voice traffic; the same attacker speaks "open an elevation case for X" and the ElevationActor files a real ACSP governance case from an anonymous, unauthenticated speaker; settings-configuration tasks are spawnable the same way.
- Confidence: high; the reconnect-renewal of the 30 s window is inferred (closure is stated, throttling is not)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION at VC-35.4 and VC-35.10)

### F-03 — Codescape's main port runs with auth open, presence signatures are ignored, and the dev nginx publishes it to the LAN
- Topics: VC-39.2, VC-39.7, VC-43.1
- Evidence: VC-39.7 R1: "the main port runs OpenAccess, reported honestly as auth open, while port 3001 /codescape/ is LAN-published in dev by owner decision" (codescape-server/src/auth.rs:120-124, routes/mod.rs:71, nginx.dev.conf:63-65). R2: "/ws/presence shim accepts the Schnorr signature unverified, the claimed DID is only a label" (xr_presence.rs:20-23, :65-67). VC-43.1: "Signature ignored, DID resolved as label" (routes/presence.rs:98, :136); "Missing Origin is allowed for native clients" (origin.rs:127). Voice commands are executed as typed Commands through the same open port (VC-39.5).
- Failure: on any deployment using the shipped dev compose/nginx, a LAN peer drives the shared code view by voice or typed command, impersonates any DID label in presence, and reads the mounted code corpus — no authentication anywhere on the Codescape surface.
- Confidence: high (the material marks it OPEN "until P6")
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN)

### F-04 — The amendment apply path persists a page whose resource IRI differs from the approved one; there is no after-application identity check
- Topics: VC-41.4
- Evidence: "Writer checks current resource against proposal IRI (write/apply.rs:229,230)... DRIFT: fixture page now has replacement resource; no after-resource equality check (write/apply.rs:280,282)". "A real signed producer output changes the resource through the real CLI writer. The old-resource equality check passes because the page still has its original identity before application." The governing VAULT-corpus-format invariant requires an immutable resource, which "the inspected amendment path lacks" — executed against the real adapter, not a mock.
- Failure: a signed, human-approved amendment to resource A lands in the store as resource B. The signature no longer describes the persisted content, downstream consumers resolve a different resource than the reviewers approved, and nothing detects it — a silent custody break of the corpus.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT)

### F-05 — Governed push-to-talk never dispatches the speaker's own utterance and fires signed governed commands on status strings and other users' transcripts
- Topics: VC-35.1, VC-35.2, VC-35.6
- Evidence: VC-35.2 TENSION: the governed predicate is `state === 'commanding' AND a canonical DID` (pttAgentBinding.ts:61-64), checked on every FINAL transcript (usePushToTalkAgentBinding.ts:73-85). Default mode is push (PushToTalkService.ts:31-36). "In push mode the speaker's own utterance arrives after Space keyup: VoiceButton stops listening on keyup (VoiceButton.tsx:115-120), the recording is sent only on stop (VoiceWebSocketService.ts:399-411) and the same keyup has already moved PTT to chatting or idle (PushToTalkService.ts:212, :217), so that utterance is NOT dispatched. What does arrive... is the server's 'Whisper STT ready' status, broadcast as a final transcription (speech_service.rs:429), and any other socket's transcript on the global broadcast. Each of those is sent as a governed voice_command for the bound DID." VC-35.6 DEBT confirms status strings share the transcript channel (speech_service.rs:429, :464).
- Failure: an operator holds Space and speaks a command — nothing is dispatched (the governed path is dead in its default mode); instead a "Whisper STT ready" string or a colleague's transcript fires a signed kind-31402 dispatch to /v1/voice-intent against the bound agent's DID, with text no human intended.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism (material states it, "Source tracing only, not exercised"); the frequency of spurious dispatches in real rooms is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION)

### F-06 — The client doubles the /api prefix: analytics, semantic, live-physics and power-user surfaces all request /api/api/... and 404
- Topics: VC-33.6, VC-34.12, VC-34.13
- Evidence: VC-34.12 DEBT: "every path here and in analyticsStore leads with /api, and unifiedApiClient prepends its own /api base (UnifiedApiClient.ts:77, :182), so they resolve to /api/api/semantic/... and /api/api/analytics/shortest-path, the doubled prefix the repo records as a 404 for the ontology services it already fixed." VC-34.13: same for every usePhysicsService path, and "no component calls usePhysicsService outside its tests". VC-33.6 DEBT: fetchPowerUserStatus hits /api/api/admin/rbac/whoami, "so the ER-26 fix never lifts a real power user. Its unit test mocks the client and cannot see it" (powerUserStatus.ts:36-38).
- Failure: in a deployed build, shortest-path/communities/centrality analytics, the semantic services, the physics live-control hook and the power-user gating lift are all silently dead — 404s with no UI signal — while unit tests pass because they mock the client.
- Confidence: medium (each site is marked "source-traced, not run"; but the identical prefix bug is recorded as a live 404 elsewhere in this codebase)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT at each site); the systemic scope — four feature surfaces dead from one root cause — is unmarked.

### F-07 — An explicit Jev endpoint resolves before the locality/permission gate, contradicting the documented local-only contract, and Compose calls the enabled path "advisory" while it returns executed actions
- Topics: VC-42.2, VC-42.3, VC-39.7
- Evidence: VC-42.2: "An explicit remote endpoint can resolve while every local/cloud permission flag is false" (voice/jev.rs:190-192); Drift: "The local-only promise in voice/jev.rs:11 and the resolver contract at voice/jev.rs:169 do not cover the explicit-endpoint return at voice/jev.rs:190." VC-42.3 TENSION: "Compose calls the enabled model advisory (docker-compose.unified.yml:507), while the selection path can return an action at confidence one (voice/jev.rs:409,414)" — the threshold comparison is inclusive (jev.rs:182, :409). VC-39.7 J1: compose enables it with threshold 1.0 and "ALLOW_CLOUD=1 sends the utterance via the proxy to the selected backend".
- Failure: an operator configures an explicit Jev endpoint believing the code's local-only contract; user utterances leave the LAN on every grammar miss, and the model's pick at confidence 1.0 becomes an executed command — not the advisory the Compose comment promises.
- Confidence: high (the probe established resolution and allowlist admission without contacting a remote service)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift on the comment, TENSION on the advisory wording); the Compose-vs-behaviour contradiction is stated by the authors, the bypass ordering is marked Drift.

### F-08 — The shipped compose RBAC posture contradicts the code's fail-closed default: public reads on, unassigned authenticated pubkey = Editor
- Topics: VC-33.6
- Evidence: "DIVERGENCE. compose ships public reads open and unassigned-pubkey-is-Editor by default, while the Rust struct-level default is fail-closed (no public reads) — two different postures depending whether you read the binary default or the shipped compose env" — RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=false via `unwrap_or(false)` (src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:141) versus RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1 in docker-compose.unified.yml:132; UserRole::default_authenticated() = Editor (src/models/rbac.rs:68-70) with compose RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE=editor (docker-compose.unified.yml:140). The same topic records that the doc citations for these lines "were never re-anchored".
- Failure: a deployment built from the shipped compose exposes all reads unauthenticated and grants Editor-level write to any unknown signed-in key; an operator auditing the Rust defaults concludes the system is fail-closed. Compounded by F-06: the client-side power-user lift that would surface the role never runs.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-09 — The consumer cannot apply the producer's signed grouped proposals: the identity check fails before the writer is ever invoked
- Topics: VC-41.3, VC-41.1
- Evidence: The producer signs groups ("Group subject is proposal IRI", vault-core/src/proposal.rs:225, :234; "Any new page makes group kind create", propose.rs:590; "Postable group can be signed", main.rs:500, :516). The consumer "Require[s] note-page slug to answer to subject IRI" (ontology-apply.js:570, :699) and "DRIFT: real grouped payload fails identity check before invoking writer (ontology-apply.js:571,700)". Open: "A supported all-or-nothing group apply/recovery contract, or explicit pre-signing refusal, is not established."
- Failure: a legitimate multi-page ontology change is signed, posted, reviewed and approved — then can never be applied, failing at an opaque identity check; nothing at signing time warns the proposer that the artifact is unappliable, so governance effort is spent on dead proposals.
- Confidence: high (real signed payload through the real adapter)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT + Open)

### F-10 — The proposal case digest is stable while the assessed consequences are not, and versionIRI-only differences produce non-conservative verdicts
- Topics: VC-41.2, VC-41.1
- Evidence: "Changing a parent elsewhere alters consequences without changing the patch's case digest" — "Consequences and generation outside case digest" (vault-core/src/proposal.rs:260, :365; digest at :388). The compared graphs "include content-derived versionIRI" (build/turtle.rs:495, :513) and "Restriction filler probe: only versionIRI differs — one added and one lost triple" (rdfs-certificate/src/lib.rs:642), so "Blank-node exclusion can coexist with a non-conservative verdict caused solely by version metadata." Also VC-41.1: "Consequences do not automatically raise the level."
- Failure: two proposals sharing one case number carry materially different consequence assessments because unrelated corpus edits shifted a parent — reviewers keying on the digest reuse a stale assessment. Separately, every rebuild that only bumps versionIRI reports added-and-lost triples, so non-conservative verdicts fire on logically identical content and train reviewers to dismiss the signal. Neither EL equivalence nor review-context identity is established by either mechanism.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism (both stated); medium on the operational effect (inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — VC-41.2 carries no Tension/Debt/Open marker.

### F-11 — One shared TTS task: any authenticated socket's cancel or new utterance aborts the audio every other listener is hearing
- Topics: VC-35.9, VC-35.13
- Evidence: VC-35.13 TENSION: "there is ONE tts_task in the SpeechService loop and its audio is broadcast to every socket (VC-35.9), so any authenticated socket's cancel_tts or new tts frame aborts the utterance every other listener is hearing (src/services/speech_service.rs:264-268, :287-291)." VC-35.9: "The broadcast is global, so every connected speech socket plays every utterance."
- Failure: with two operators in a session, the second's push-to-talk barge-in — correct behaviour for one user — kills the first's clarification prompt and governed acknowledgement everywhere; the voice channel is mutually exclusive and cross-talk is universal. The design works for one user and fails for two.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION)

### F-12 — Failed settings writes are silently deleted from the retry queue and the auth-failure signal has no listener
- Topics: VC-34.1
- Evidence: "DEBT: updateSettingByPath catches every error and never rethrows (endpoints.ts:303-311), so the single-path retry always takes its success branch and deletes the update from the queue (settingsRetryManager.ts:106-108). A failed retry is dropped silently, and settings-auth-failed has no listener anywhere in client/src. A 401 on a setting reaches no UI."
- Failure: a user edits physics/rendering settings; the flush 401s (expired NIP-98 or RBAC denial); the retry fails, is deleted from the queue, and the local immer store keeps showing the edited value while the server never received it — silent configuration divergence, lost on next reload, with no signal at any layer.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-13 — Ontology proposal POSTs and hierarchy-tree fetches bypass the auth interceptor entirely
- Topics: VC-34.3, VC-34.4
- Evidence: VC-34.3 OPEN: "OntologyContribution.tsx creates proposals through useOntologyContributionStore (OntologyContribution.tsx:84), which POSTs /api/ontology/proposals with a plain fetch (useOntologyStore.ts:322, :336)... that plain fetch attaches no NIP-98 header in useOntologyStore.ts, so whether a proposal write is authorised depends on the server route's gate, which this panel does not trace." VC-34.4, no marker: "useHierarchyData fetch(url) direct, not the shared API client, so no auth interceptor runs — hierarchy tree endpoint (useHierarchyData.ts:96)."
- Failure: ontology governance writes (and hierarchy reads) leave the browser unauthenticated. On a server that does not gate that route, anyone can submit proposals into the governance queue; on one that does, the feature fails with the same silence as F-06/F-12.
- Confidence: medium (the missing header is stated; the server-side outcome is explicitly untraced)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the proposal POST is marked OPEN; the useHierarchyData bypass is unmarked.

### F-14 — The XR client never learns its NIP-98 authenticate was rejected: the session silently stays anonymous
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.10, VC-36.11
- Evidence: VC-36.3 Open: "the client sends the NIP-98 authenticate message and never reads an acknowledgement, so a rejected signature leaves the session anonymous with no signal (transport.rs:118); the visible symptom is the write-denied notice of VC-36.15." VC-36.10: "XR_NOSTR_SECRET is REQUIRED in practice: NostrAuth.create() returns an ephemeral signer when empty (graph_scene.gd:1208-1213), but an ephemeral key cannot satisfy the presence challenge or gate drag/pin."
- Failure: a headset deployed with a bad or missing secret connects, streams reads fine, and every drag, pin and physics write 401s — surfacing only as flash-notices on the HUD bottom strip. Operators debug the write handlers rather than the credential; with dev mode off the headset is effectively read-only with no authentication error anywhere in the system.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-15 — Decision records assert states the code contradicts: ADR-2159's verification denies the wired construction, ADR-2143 A6 names deleted functions, ADR-2148 is "live" while configured to do nothing
- Topics: VC-44.3, VC-40.1, VC-40.2, VC-35.7, VC-39.7
- Evidence: VC-44.3 Drift: "ADR-2159's verification says no BackendConnection exists; current construction is wired into the scene (graph_scene.gd:1371). Its inactive status is not overturned" — and "ADR-2159 still requires E8 on the headset before activation". VC-40.1 Drift: ADR-2143 A6 "still names GraphScene._gate(cap, label) and the drag-end pin record (ADR-2143...:333,336)" while the gate is now `_gate_for_volume` over `BackendConnection.gate`; docs/XR-client.md:1116 describes the deleted `_switch_backend` (VC-40.2). VC-35.7: ADR-2148 is "accepted / complete / live" while "the outcome-changing threshold is unset in Compose (docker-compose.unified.yml:25), which accepts nothing automatically, so neither Jev call alters a turn yet."
- Failure: engineers and auditors plan against a fiction — someone re-implements a connection class that already exists, removes hygiene they believe lives in `_switch_backend`, or signs off a "live" governance gate that is a configured no-op.
