The concurrent-connection transport that actually ships is the one whose own activation gate (headset acceptance E8) is unmet.
- Confidence: high on the contradictions; medium on the operational consequence (inferred)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift at each site); the pattern — records systematically lagging the code across at least four ADRs — is unmarked as a whole.

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual deployment posture**: which compose file, build profile (release vs dev-auth) and env values the production hosts run — VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE, RBAC_PUBLIC_READS/RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE, nginx exposure, Codescape profile. F-03, F-07 and F-08 hinge entirely on this and the material shows only the shipped defaults.
2. **Server-side gates on the unauthenticated client paths**: whether /api/ontology/proposals and the hierarchy route are gated (F-13), and whether MCP_HOST is reachable from the speech host (F-01). The material explicitly does not trace these.
3. **Runtime confirmation of the source-traced breakage**: whether any interceptor normalizes the doubled /api prefix before send (F-06), and the actual transcript timing in a real browser (F-05) — the material marks both "source tracing only, not exercised".
4. **Test and CI coverage for the defect paths**: which of these behaviours (pre-auth speech frames, PTT transcript dispatch, proposal group apply, resource-identity persistence) have tests that would catch a regression — the material cites test files for some findings and none for others.
5. **Multi-user and load behaviour in production**: real concurrent TTS listeners (F-11), presence queue depth under load, headset acceptance evidence (ADR-2159 E8), and whether the retained-PTX/CPU-fallback diagnostic (VC-42.4) matches what runs — all marked unexercised in the material.

## visionclaw#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — candidate root causes and latent defects

Incident class assumed: a security, custody or data-integrity failure that lost a client. The three root causes below are ranked candidates drawn from defects this corpus documents explicitly; each chain is reconstructed from the cited facts, and the deployment step that turned a documented hole into an incident is labelled inferred where the material does not show it.

---

### R-1 — The speech socket admits anonymous peers into an ungoverned server-side command path and into every other user's transcripts
- Topics: VC-35.4, VC-35.6, VC-35.9, VC-35.10, VC-35.13
- Chain of events: A peer on the network opens `/ws/speech`. ADR-2075 checks no credential at upgrade — "the socket opens anonymous and useless" for JSON commands only. On `started()` the socket is subscribed to the SpeechService audio and transcription broadcasts *before* any authenticate frame, and the forwarders write with no pubkey check, so for up to `AUTH_DEADLINE` (30 s) — and repeatedly, by reconnecting — the anonymous peer receives every TTS chunk and every transcript broadcast to all sockets. Independently, binary frames bypass the auth gate entirely: the Binary arm forwards any frame to `process_audio_chunk` without a pubkey check. The attacker sends audio; Whisper transcribes it; `is_voice_command` / `VoiceCommand::parse` / `execute_voice_command_with_context` executes it server-side — swarm init and agent spawn on `MCP_HOST` — with no actorDid, no clarification gate and no signature. In parallel, any transcript on the global broadcast reaches `VoiceInterfaceActor` and `ElevationActor` with no speaker identity, so an unauthenticated socket's speech can open a settings-assistant task or a persisted elevation case. Authenticated victims' TTS audio is itself broadcast to every connected socket.
- Evidence: "started() subscribes this socket to the SpeechService audio and transcription broadcasts before any authenticate frame (speech_socket_handler.rs:511-541), and the forwarders write to the socket with no pubkey check (:556-558, :571-583). For up to AUTH_DEADLINE an unauthenticated peer receives every TTS chunk and transcript broadcast to all sockets. ADR-2075 guards inbound commands only" (VC-35.4); "Binary audio frames are NOT behind the gate: the Binary arm :956-976 forwards any frame to process_audio_chunk without a pubkey check"; "authenticate is the ONLY frame accepted while pubkey is None … reject_unauthenticated (speech_socket_handler.rs:133, applied :627-633) refuses tts, stt, voice_command, set_ptt and cancel_tts" — i.e. the gate covers exactly the frames an attacker does not need. "a Whisper transcript that contains any of 20 keywords (agent, node, graph, run, show…) is ALSO executed server-side: is_voice_command, VoiceCommand::parse and execute_voice_command_with_context (src/services/speech_service.rs:620-633, :1032-1060, :1062), which can call swarm init and agent spawn on MCP_HOST (:1066-1068, :1079, :1086). That path has no actorDid, no clarification gate and no signature" (VC-35.6); "both actors consume the GLOBAL transcript broadcast, so speech from any connected speech socket - including one not yet authenticated (VC-35.4) - can open a settings-assistant task or an elevation case. Neither path carries a speaker identity" (VC-35.10); "The broadcast is global, so every connected speech socket plays every utterance" (VC-35.9); `AUTH_DEADLINE` = 30 s, speech_socket_handler.rs:22, :487-500.
- Warning signs visible today: the authors' own TENSION notes at VC-35.4, VC-35.6 and VC-35.10 state each half of this chain verbatim; the status-string pollution of the transcript channel ("Whisper STT ready", speech_service.rs:429) already demonstrates the broadcast carries non-utterance content to all sockets.
- Confidence: high — every gate omission is stated in the material; only the presence of a hostile peer on the network is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The unified compose boots an armed, peer-agnostic admin bypass by default, and separately grants Editor to any authenticated key with public reads on
- Topics: VC-36.15, VC-33.6, VC-33.3
- Chain of events: The dev/unified compose sets `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE: "${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}"` — on unless the operator opts out. A dev or dev-auth build honours it: `dev_full_bypass_active()` returns true on value `1`, every request is granted as `DEV_MODE_PUBKEY` = "dev-mode-local-admin", and the REST chokepoint path is documented "LAN-local FULL bypass, peer-agnostic: no NIP-98, no token, no peer check" (src/utils/auth.rs:203-218). Release hygiene refuses the variable, but both services load `env_file .env`, "so the var must never sit in a .env shared with prod - sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked". A service run for a client engagement on this compose therefore grants full admin — settings writes (fanned to every viewer via `settingsUpdated`), physics and graph writes, drag/pin — to anyone who can reach it; the documented headset rig already points at a LAN backend (`XR_BACKEND_WS=ws://192.168.2.132`). Even with the bypass off, the same compose sets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1` and `RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE=editor`, so an unassigned authenticated pubkey resolves to Editor via `UserRole::default_authenticated()` — a write-capable role for any stranger with a Nostr key, while the Rust struct-level default is fail-closed.
- Evidence: `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE: "${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}" docker-compose.unified.yml:116 - … ADR-2108 flipped the default from :-0 to :-1 …, so launch.sh up dev boots an ARMED backend and the operator opts OUT` (VC-36.15); `warn banner "VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=1 - LAN-LOCAL AUTH BYPASS ACTIVE" project/src/main.rs:296`; `Every request granted as DEV_MODE_PUBKEY = "dev-mode-local-admin" src/utils/auth.rs:96, project/src/main.rs:299`; `LAN-local FULL bypass, peer-agnostic: no NIP-98, no token, no peer check. src/utils/auth.rs:203-218` (VC-36.15); `.env` caveat at project/src/main.rs:109, docker-compose.unified.yml:113-115; release refusal project/src/main.rs:136, :161; `docker-compose.unified.yml:132 sets RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1 … An unassigned authenticated pubkey resolves to Editor via UserRole::default_authenticated() src/models/rbac.rs:68-70, src/services/role_store.rs:188,196-198` and "two different postures depending whether you read the binary default or the shipped compose env" (VC-33.6); headset LAN target at VC-36.16.
- Warning signs visible today: the armed-by-default flip itself; the compose/Rust posture divergence the authors flag as DIVERGENCE; the compose RBAC citation lines that were "never re-anchored" (VC-33.6 DOC-DRIFT), so an operator reading the baseline can misread the shipped posture.
- Confidence: high for the documented mechanism; inferred that a dev-profile service was reachable outside the trusted rail when the incident occurred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Codescape serves the estate's member repositories on a port that cannot authenticate, and its voice path can leave the LAN outside the documented local-only contract
- Topics: VC-39.2, VC-39.7, VC-43.1, VC-42.2, VC-42.3
- Chain of events: The compose Codescape service mounts read-only views of the estate scene and ten member repositories (docker-compose.unified.yml:492, :538-619) — including client code — and serves them on a main port that runs OpenAccess: `require_auth` true is a *startup refusal* (codescape-server/src/auth.rs:137-138), so the server cannot run authenticated at all, and the capability surface reports "auth open" (routes/mod.rs:71, auth.rs:120-124). Port 4100 is loopback-published by default (docker-compose.unified.yml:641), but dev nginx publishes `/codescape/` on LAN port 3001 (nginx.dev.conf:63-65 — "the owner's dev-only waiver"), and direct LAN publication is one environment variable (`CODESCAPE_PUBLISH_ADDR`, VC-39.2). Presence identity is a label — the Schnorr signature is accepted unverified (codescape-server/src/xr_presence.rs:20-23; routes/presence.rs:98, :136). On the voice side, the Jev option is enabled in compose with cloud permission granted: `ALLOW_CLOUD=1` sends the utterance via the proxy to the selected backend (agentbox.toml:891), and the explicit-endpoint branch of the resolver returns *before* the local/cloud permission check (voice/jev.rs:190), which the local-only promise at voice/jev.rs:11 does not cover. An unauthenticated visitor reads a client's source through the open graph surface, or a visitor's spoken utterance is routed to a cloud endpoint outside any documented boundary.
- Evidence: "OPEN until P6: the main port runs OpenAccess, reported honestly as auth open, while port 3001 /codescape/ is LAN-published in dev by owner decision codescape-server/src/auth.rs:120-124 codescape-server/src/routes/mod.rs:71 nginx.dev.conf:63-65" (VC-39.7); "the compose peer now maps the estate scene and ten member repositories read-only" (VC-39.2, docker-compose.unified.yml:492, :538-619); "the /ws/presence shim accepts the Schnorr signature unverified, the claimed DID is only a label" (VC-39.7; VC-43.1); "an explicit remote endpoint can resolve while every local/cloud permission flag is false" and "The local-only promise in voice/jev.rs:11 … do[es] not cover the explicit-endpoint return at voice/jev.rs:190" (VC-42.2); "ALLOW_CLOUD=1 sends the utterance via the proxy to the selected backend (agentbox.toml:891)" (VC-39.7).
- Warning signs visible today: VC-39.7 records the open-access state and the egress boundary as rejected invariants ("compose non-publication is not an egress boundary, open until P2"); the estate-wide mounts were added to a service whose auth posture is open by design.
- Confidence: medium — the open-access state, the mounts and the resolver order are stated facts; which network path (dev nginx, `CODESCAPE_PUBLISH_ADDR`, or the cloud route) carried the incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — The passkey-derived Nostr private key is simultaneously claimed never to touch sessionStorage and cleaned out of sessionStorage at logout
- Topics: VC-33.9, VC-33.5, VC-33.4
- Evidence: VC-33.9 declares `LocalPrivateKeyMaterial` "storage in-memory only, never sessionStorage … definedAt nostrAuthService.ts:14,200", while VC-33.5's logout performs `removeItem nostr_privkey, nostr_passkey_pubkey, nostr_prf nostrAuthService.ts:463-465` — items that can only exist in sessionStorage if something wrote them there. The PRF output (`nostr_prf`) is the HKDF input that derives the key (`deriveNostrKey(prfOutput)`, passkeyService.ts:60-73), so persisting it is persisting key material.
- Failure: if the key or PRF output is in sessionStorage, any XSS or shared-machine access exfiltrates the operator's Nostr signing key — full identity custody loss, including the ability to sign kind-31402 intents and NIP-98 requests. If it is not, the logout cleanup is dead code and the class diagram in VC-33.9 misdescribes the most security-relevant store in the client. One of the two statements is wrong; the engineer should determine which.
- Confidence: high that the contradiction exists; which side is true is unresolved by the material.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — The shipped compose resolves any authenticated stranger to Editor with public reads on
- Topics: VC-33.6
- Evidence: "compose ships public reads open and unassigned-pubkey-is-Editor by default, while the Rust struct-level default is fail-closed … docker-compose.unified.yml:132 sets RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1 … RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE, docker-compose.unified.yml:140 … UserRole::default_authenticated() src/models/rbac.rs:68-70, src/services/role_store.rs:188,196-198".
- Failure: on any deployment provisioned from the unified compose, a third party with any NIP-07 key authenticates and receives Editor — a write-capable role; the server-side per-request RbacGate then permits the writes, and the client's binary isPowerUser surface (VC-33.6 DIVERGENCE) never warns anyone.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-03 — An approved amendment silently replaces a corpus page's resource, violating the immutable-resource invariant
- Topics: VC-41.4
- Evidence: the writer checks the *current* resource against the proposal IRI before applying (`write/apply.rs:229-230`), applies the patch and re-parses (`:242, :258`), checks only introduced errors and persists (`:280, :282`); "DRIFT: fixture page now has replacement resource / no after-resource equality check (write/apply.rs:280,282)" — demonstrated with a real signed producer output through the real CLI writer.
- Failure: a signed, human-approved amendment changes the resource IRI of an existing page; consumers addressing the old IRI break silently and the provenance chain is corrupted after a legitimate governance step. This is corpus custody loss executed by the system's own happy path.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT)

### F-04 — A signed grouped proposal carries one human judgement that the apply consumer can never apply
- Topics: VC-41.3
- Evidence: the producer makes the group subject the proposal IRI (`vault-core/src/proposal.rs:225, :234`), any new page forces group kind create (`vault/src/propose.rs:590`), and the group is postable and signable (`vault/src/main.rs:500, :516`); the consumer requires a note-page slug answering to the subject IRI and "DRIFT: real grouped payload fails identity check before invoking writer (ontology-apply.js:571, :700)", with further guards refusing grouped application (:574, :702).
- Failure: a multi-page change is approved at the forum and signed, then can never be applied — the decision record and the corpus permanently diverge, or an operator works around it by applying pages individually, splitting the single judgement ADR-2116 grouping exists to preserve. No all-or-nothing group contract or pre-signing refusal exists ("Open" at VC-41.3).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT and Open)

### F-05 — Server status strings and other users' transcripts are dispatched as signed, governed voice commands
- Topics: VC-35.2, VC-35.6
- Evidence: "status strings share the transcript channel: 'Whisper STT ready', 'Transcription stopped' (src/services/speech_service.rs:429, :464) reach clients as final transcriptions" (VC-35.6 DEBT); the governed predicate fires on every *final* transcript while commanding and bound (`pttAgentBinding.ts:61-64`, `usePushToTalkAgentBinding.ts:73-85`), and "any other socket's transcript on the global broadcast … is sent as a governed voice_command for the bound DID" (VC-35.2 TENSION), which the server signs as kind-31402 to `/v1/voice-intent` (speech_socket_handler.rs:318-319).
- Failure: a status string or a bystander's transcript becomes a signed intent event attributed to the operator's DID — fabricated governance evidence in the audit trail, or an unintended action executed on an agent under the operator's identity.
- Confidence: medium — the dispatch path is source-traced; the material itself says "Source tracing only, not exercised".
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION and DEBT)

### F-06 — A failed settings write, including a 401, is silently dropped and never reaches any UI
- Topics: VC-34.1
- Evidence: "updateSettingByPath catches every error and never rethrows (endpoints.ts:303-311), so the single-path retry always takes its success branch and deletes the update from the queue (settingsRetryManager.ts:106-108). A failed retry is dropped silently, and settings-auth-failed has no listener anywhere in client/src. A 401 on a setting reaches no UI".
- Failure: the operator edits settings (filters, constraints, quality gates), the debounced PUT fails, the client reports success by omission, and the server never receives the change — silent loss of configuration intent and cross-viewer divergence, including security-relevant settings such as node filters.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-07 — The Jev resolver returns an explicit endpoint before the locality gate, and the host enables cloud routing
- Topics: VC-42.2, VC-42.3, VC-39.7
- Evidence: "Explicit endpoint configured? → Return resolved endpoint (voice/jev.rs:192)" precedes the permission branch (voice/jev.rs:187, :200-201); "The local-only promise in voice/jev.rs:11 and the resolver contract at voice/jev.rs:169 do not cover the explicit-endpoint return at voice/jev.rs:190" (VC-42.2 Drift); "The current host intentionally enables cloud routing (docker-compose.unified.yml:505)" and "ALLOW_CLOUD=1 sends the utterance via the proxy to the selected backend (agentbox.toml:891)".
- Failure: spoken utterances are transmitted to a cloud model endpoint that no documented privacy boundary covers — a data-egress breach of user speech.
- Confidence: high for the resolution order; the actual cloud call was not executed in the probe, so the live egress is high-likelihood rather than observed.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift and Tension)

### F-08 — The doubled /api prefix makes power-user status, analytics and physics surfaces 404 in production
- Topics: VC-33.6, VC-34.12, VC-34.13
- Evidence: the shared client prepends `/api` (UnifiedApiClient.ts:77, :182), `fetchPowerUserStatus` requests `/api/admin/rbac/whoami` → `/api/api/…`, the repo records the doubled prefix as a 404, the catch returns null and "the ER-26 fix never lifts a real power user" (powerUserStatus.ts:28, :36-38); the same doubling affects every semantic/analytics path (VC-34.12 DEBT) and every physics path (VC-34.13 DEBT).
- Failure: power-user gating in the UI never reflects reality after any real sign-in, and the analytics/physics panels fail with 404s; operators act on the belief that a user is (or is not) privileged when the client never knew.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-09 — The XR client never reads the NIP-98 authenticate acknowledgement, and a failed signature still receives the full read stream
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.15
- Evidence: "the client sends the NIP-98 authenticate message and never reads an acknowledgement, so a rejected signature leaves the session anonymous with no signal (xr-client/rust/src/transport.rs:118)"; on refused credentials "drag/pin refused, read stream still served" (VC-36.15).
- Failure: an operator believes the headset is authenticated when it is anonymous; the session still receives the complete position stream and topology, so the graph's content flows to an identity the server rejected, with the only visible symptom being a write-denied notice.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-10 — Ontology proposals are submitted by a plain fetch with no authentication header
- Topics: VC-34.3
- Evidence: "that plain fetch attaches no NIP-98 header in useOntologyStore.ts, so whether a proposal write is authorised depends on the server route's gate, which this panel does not trace" (useOntologyStore.ts:322, :336).
- Failure: if the `/api/ontology/proposals` route is not gated, any anonymous party can submit proposals into the ACSP queue, poisoning the governance pipeline; if it is gated, legitimate contributions fail on closed deployments. Either outcome is unverified because the gate was never traced.
- Confidence: medium — the missing header is a stated fact; the consequence depends on an untraced server gate.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN)

### F-11 — A max-retry stop() permanently kills agent polling while the subscriber count says it is running
- Topics: VC-34.9
- Evidence: "the max-retry stop() releases a subscriber slot no subscriber gave up. With two subscribers the count drops to one, isPolling stays true and no timer runs, so polling is dead, and a later start() sees count 2, not 1, and never restarts it (AgentPollingService.ts:126-128, :141-143, :324-327)".

- Severity: medium

### F-12 — Codescape presence identity is unclaimed, and roster updates are lossy on a shared bounded queue

### F-15 — An over-range wire id is remapped onto another live node in release builds

- Evidence: `enforce_wire_id_bounds` fails fast only via debug_assert; in release "remap_wire_id masks the id into 26 bits and an error! log names the class and both ids (:194-204, ADR-2024). The masked id still aliases another node, so an over-range id is loud, not harmless" (src/utils/binary_protocol.rs:185-206).
- Failure: with ids past the 26-bit space (or a mis-stamped class bit pattern), one node's positions and analytics are written and rendered under a different node's id in every client — silent cross-node data corruption that continues after the error log line.
- Confidence: high for the documented behaviour; triggering requires an over-range id, so the production occurrence is conditional.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

## visionclaw#4 / critical

### F-01 — Payment replay protection is process-local, bounded and wiped by restart
- Topics: VC-55.1
- Evidence: The single-use mechanism is "claim signed id under mutex, bounded process-local cache" (`src/utils/nip98.rs:227`, `:295`). The file's own closing prose states "the cache does not span restarts or replicas", "a freshly signed retry can still repeat an operation", and "separate storage commits … remain open" — no durable idempotency is claimed anywhere. The charge itself is a "durable ledger debit" (`src/handlers/pay_handler.rs:303`), so the debit persists while the replay guard does not.
- Failure: A captured signed payment request (from a proxy, log or client) is replayed after a restart, after cache eviction under load, or against a second replica; the duplicate sell/swap/liquidity operation authenticates as valid and debits twice.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — stated only as "remain open" in closing prose; no Tension/Debt/Drift marker

### F-02 — The XR client authenticates only after the release gate that demands authentication
- Topics: VC-48.1
- Evidence: The transport "opens socket with URL string" (`transport.rs:95`) and only "after upgrade: request topology then send authenticate text" (`transport.rs:99,115,118`). In release, the server "requires Origin" (`http_handler.rs:67,129,131,139`) and verifies "signed subprotocol or legacy bearer at upgrade" (`http_handler.rs:146,151,181,183`), refusing "unauthenticated release upgrade" (`http_handler.rs:314,321,322`). The material's own conclusion: "a secret used for the post-upgrade text message cannot satisfy earlier release Origin and authentication checks."
- Failure: On a release deployment every XR connection is refused at upgrade — the XR interface is down. The only workaround visible in the material is permissive development mode, which removes the gate for all clients.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, VC-48.1)

### F-03 — Any verified identity can join any presence room, and no session is ever re-checked
- Topics: VC-49.1, VC-49.3
- Evidence: The join path runs identity/duplicate checks, parses the legacy room URN, and sends the verified DID in JoinRoom with "no membership gate or cap" (`presence_handler.rs:273`, `:281,290`, `:305,306`; `room.rs:42,45`, `:51,52`). The 10-second recheck and "stale membership closes sessions" exist only as PROPOSED nodes (`IDENTITY-authority-chain.md:240`); the text confirms "the 90-second presence deadline still needs implementation."
- Failure: A user removed from a space — or never admitted — joins any room and receives member presence indefinitely; removal has no effect because nothing re-evaluates admission during a live session.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (mentioned as a business limitation; no marker)

### F-04 — Visibility defaults ON in code while its documentation says OFF
- Topics: VC-48.2
- Evidence: "Visibility flag defaults ON; explicit false tokens opt out" (`visibility_filter.rs:26,30,32,38`), the parser "defaults it ON and fails closed" (`visibility_filter.rs:21,32`), and initial load "filters by public or caller owner key" (`types.rs:277,280,289,295`). The marked drift: "the initial-load filter's comment says the flag defaults OFF (`types.rs:274`)". One of the two is the intended default; the material does not say which.
- Failure: If the documented OFF is the intent, every record whose author never wrote an explicit false token is returned as public to any caller on initial load — wide silent exposure with no error anywhere. If ON is the intent, the governing documentation misleads every operator reasoning about privacy.
- Confidence: medium (the contradiction is stated; the exposure direction is inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift) — but only as a comment mismatch; the exposure consequence is unmarked

### F-05 — Held node ids outlive the topology generation they came from
- Topics: VC-53.2, VC-53.1
- Evidence: Topology "bumps connection generation" and the scene clears selection and stale links (`backend_connection.gd:359,360,361`; `graph_scene.gd:4224,4227,4231,4234,4235`), but hand state "has id and volume, no generation" and drive "uses retained id without generation check" (`graph_scene.gd:845,846`, `:3441,3450,3452`) — while the connection itself "documents generation-scoped identity" (`backend_connection.gd:18,19`). VC-53.1 adds that all volumes share one id domain that is "not a general multi-server identity scheme" (`graph_scene.gd:94,95`).
- Failure: A grab in flight across a reconnect keeps writing drag updates under an old-generation id. Depending on whether the server reuses ids across generations (not stated), the grab silently dies or addresses whatever now owns that id — visible to every other client. The same shared id domain blocks adding a second backend source without collisions.
- Confidence: medium (the stale send is stated; the wrong-node outcome is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, VC-53.2)

### F-06 — Two release paths disagree about ownership, leaving held state inconsistent
- Topics: VC-52.2
- Evidence: Release sends a "node-only Released notification" (`position_updates.rs:164-171`) and the orchestrator "removes id without owner check" (`physics_orchestrator_actor.rs:1189-1195`), while the GPU path sends holder and node ids and the registry "ignores a stale owner's release" (`position_updates.rs:173-178`; `hold_registry.rs:68-70`). The marked gap: "GPU may still hold a node after orchestrator clears its held set"; the backstop "only runs within compute handling" and "does not reject closed holders" (`force_compute_actor.rs:1919-1932`).
- Failure: A late release from a previous holder clears the orchestrator's held set for a node someone else still holds: auto-pause/wake resumes against a node the GPU still pins, and if compute handling is not running the pin persists until the backstop — the node appears stuck or jumps for other users.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension; also called "known ADR gap")

### F-07 — The drag acceptance script passes a peer that pins the node and goes silent
- Topics: VC-52.4
- Evidence: The release check subtracts the dragger's release-time snapshot from "observer's latest position" (`drag-physics-acceptance.mjs:319-320`, `:359-364`, `:386-388`); the rest check passes on "zero settle milliseconds" when there is "no motion after release" (`:230`, `:353-355`, `:381-384`); "neither check requires a fresh post-release position observation" (`:355,388`). The negative control — peer leaves the node pinned at 150 and sends zero post-release positions — passes "all five checks".
- Failure: A deployment whose peer stops delivering positions after release, or leaves nodes pinned, is certified green by the acceptance gate; the regression ships and users see permanently stuck nodes. Anything else that leans on this script for drag correctness is unverified.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, in-diagram, VC-52.4)

### F-08 — XR pinning is dead: two pin sets have readers and no writer
- Topics: VC-53.6
- Evidence: Grab end "records no pin" (`graph_scene.gd:3456,3459,3466`) and connection drag end "records nothing" (`backend_connection.gd:272,273`); the marked debt is "no path adds to either set" (`backend_connection.gd:49,50,51`), so the HUD count, list, CSV and Unpin All iterate sets that are "always empty" (`graph_scene.gd:958,2092,2105,2153,2383`; `backend_connection.gd:52,292,299,301`). Marked drift: the comment "still says drag-end adds" (`graph_scene.gd:955,956`) though the writer was removed by ADR-2172 F3 and the replacement sender is only follow-up F5.
- Failure: Headset users cannot pin nodes at all; the pin UI exists, always reports zero, and raises no error, so the absence reads as user failure rather than a missing feature.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt and Drift, VC-53.6)

### F-09 — The portal re-arm threshold is in local units, so the metre promise scales with the graph
- Topics: VC-47.2
- Evidence: Distance is the "local excess-vector length" after inverse transform (`space_portals.gd:596,597,598,599`) compared "directly with REARM_DISTANCE 0.3" (`space_portals.gd:51,617`); measured: "half scale re-arms at a physical 0.2 m gap; double scale remains disarmed at 0.4 m". Marked tension: "The documented re-arm distance is in metres, while the implementation compares a local-coordinate length."
- Failure: At small arrangement scales, portals re-arm on incidental head movement, producing repeated unintended entry/exit transitions; at large scales the user retreats well past the documented 0.3 m with the portal still disarmed, appearing broken.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, VC-47.2)

### F-10 — Frame-budget enforcement runs on incomplete call demand and tolerates overrun
- Topics: VC-51.2, VC-51.1
- Evidence: The live pass measures "other triangles, but no other-call remainder" (`frame_budget_pass.gd:102,125`) while the "benchmark separately calibrates other calls" (`benchmark.gd:230,572`) — scene and benchmark "build demand differently"; even with inputs, "nine constructed frames also exceed the clamped price by 24 triangles". Separately, the "call guard permits zero-added-call tiers above limit" and "existing call overrun is reported but does not withdraw a zero-added-call allowance" (`frame_budget.rs:320`).
- Failure: Whole-frame draw-call compliance is certified by a controller whose input omits calls the benchmark counts, and overruns that are detected do not withdraw allowances — a scene can run over budget with green instrumentation.

### F-11 — Missing GPU timing readings freeze the quality governor at the highest tier
- Topics: VC-51.3
- Evidence: "Zero samples do not advance the governor" while "positive sample resets zero run" (`eye_candy.gd:169,170,171`, `:369,370`, `:378`); the fallback requires "more than 180 zeros" to select frame mode (`eye_candy.gd:371,373,374,375`), so sparse — not absent — readings never trip it. Fixture result: "forty seconds of sparse loaded samples can leave tier 4 requested while neither control window completes"; the coverage rule is "separate from the existing consecutive-zero fallback and manual quality pin."
- Failure: On a headset/driver combination that intermittently reports no GPU milliseconds, automatic quality reduction never triggers under sustained load; the client holds the highest-detail tier and drops frames for as long as the gaps persist, with no alarm.

- Marked by authors: no

The probe's raw-string fixture compiled as valid Rust and "the example becomes a bound annotation and two target links". Separately, the marked debt: "Portal membranes and fade meshes are absent from the scene's explicit draw-call sum" (`graph_scene.gd:3180,3181`) because the budget collector "has no portal-layer count" (`graph_scene.gd:3149,3183`).

- Evidence: A snapshot "labelled with repository and revision produces records with neither value" — "Repository empty and revision None" (`link_index.rs:281,284`) because "the stream omits these provenance values". Separately, "only first 32 sorted refs per node reach wire metadata" (`annotations.rs:239`) and "omitted producer refs cannot be counted by a consumer that never receives them"; with 33 bound refs and all targets present, the probe "reports zero unresolved" (`link_index.rs:289,290`).
