- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Whether any XR client code sends an Origin header or signed subprotocol at socket open, and which mode (release gates vs permissive development) current deployments actually run — needed before calling F-02 an outage rather than a latent mismatch.
2. Which visibility default is intended (the documented OFF at `types.rs:274` or the parser's ON at `visibility_filter.rs:21,32`), and what a non-owner caller's initial-load response actually contains today.
3. Whether the server reuses node ids across topology generations — this decides whether the stale held ids of F-05 are dead writes or wrong-node writes — and whether any deployment runs two backend sources in one scene.
4. Whether any durable idempotency exists outside the process-local NIP-98 cache (ledger- or wallet-level dedup), and what the open "permissive compatibility URL matching" accepts behind a proxy, including which forwarded headers are trusted.
5. On-device behaviour the audits explicitly did not run: native spatial-container activation, both-eye stereo output on Godot 4.8-beta2, per-headset GPU-timing availability, and the Rust `pack_links` marker path — the highlight-capacity fixture predates the Rust migration and "was not re-run" (`render_store_pack.rs:740`; `link_layer.gd:504`).

## visionclaw#4 / premortem

# Post-mortem: custody/security incident (twelve-month horizon)

The incident record itself is not in this material; what follows is the ranked set of root causes the recorded defects make most likely, plus the latent defects found while tracing them. Every claim carries the citation the material gives; an engineer should re-check each `path:line` before acting. Where a diagram's ordering or concurrency is not asserted, I have not relied on it.

---

### R-1 — Payment replay across processes: the single-use token cache is memory-only and per-process
- Topics: VC-55.1
- Chain of events: a client's signed payment request is verified (signature, freshness, method, URL, body policy) and its signed id claimed under a mutex in a bounded, process-local cache; the operation (sell, swap, pool swap, liquidity) or the resource-fee ledger debit then executes against durable storage. When the service runs as more than one replica, or restarts during a rollout, the replay guard's state is gone: the same captured or client-retried request verifies again on a process whose cache has no record, and the operation or debit executes a second time while only the effects are durable. Independently, even in-process, a client retry that re-signs repeats the operation — there is no idempotency key binding retries — and the material records that auth-spend and effect commit in separate stores.
- Evidence: "the cache does not span restarts or replicas" and "A freshly signed retry can still repeat an operation" and "Separate storage commits … remain open" (VC-55, developer and business notes); claim under mutex, bounded process-local cache (`nip98.rs:227`, `nip98.rs:295`); durable ledger debit (`pay_handler.rs:303`); effects at `pay_handler.rs:613,686,776,816,862`; verification order VERIFY → CLAIM → EFFECT (VC-55.1 diagram).
- Warning signs visible today: the business line states the exposure outright; nothing in the source list shows a durable or shared replay store; the whole area is "source verification, awaiting deployment". The same cache is also bounded and rejects at capacity (`nip98.rs:295`; VC-55.1 diagram "replay or capacity → REJECT"), so a flood of freshly signed tokens from any holder of a valid key is a payment-availability failure of the identical mechanism.
- Confidence: high — the cache limitation is stated verbatim; the multi-replica/restart deployment that realises it is inferred (the material does not state the replica count).
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Any verified identity can join any presence room: no membership gate, no cap, no session recheck
- Topics: VC-49.1, VC-49.3
- Chain of events: an attacker creates their own DID (identity checks pass for any valid keypair: challenge, signature, verified DID, replay). They send a join for a client's legacy room URN; the parser accepts any sha256-12 suffix; the handler gets-or-creates the actor and sends JoinRoom with the verified DID; the aggregate rejects only duplicate DIDs, then inserts the member and subscriber. There is no membership gate, no cap, and no recheck: the attacker remains a subscriber receiving the room's presence events (membership at minimum; the material does not enumerate the payload) until they choose to disconnect, because the recheck/close path and the 90-second presence deadline are proposals with no implementation.
- Evidence: identity checks then legacy parse (`presence_handler.rs:210,240,249,266,273`); get-or-create and JoinRoom (`presence_handler.rs:281,290,305,306`); duplicate-only rejection (`room.rs:42,45`); member/subscriber insert (`room.rs:51,52`; `presence_actor.rs:681`); parser accepts sha256-12 suffix only (`types.rs:43,48,52` — visionclaw-xr-presence); "no membership gate or cap" (VC-49.1, What it shows); "the 90-second presence deadline still needs implementation" (VC-49.3); business line: restricting a space "requires a separate authority check, durable replay protection and removal handling".
- Warning signs visible today: the isolated actor accepted 33 distinct members with no ceiling (VC-49.1); every element of the proposed admission chain is labelled PROPOSED (`IDENTITY-authority-chain.md:240,244`).
- Confidence: high that the gate, cap and recheck are absent (stated); inferred that this path was the incident's vector — which room and what data were exposed is not in evidence.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The XR client cannot satisfy the release upgrade gates: production either refuses XR or runs with the gates relaxed
- Topics: VC-48.1
- Chain of events: the XR client opens the socket with a URL string and defers authentication to a post-upgrade text message; the release server requires an Origin header plus a signed subprotocol or legacy bearer at upgrade time and refuses the upgrade otherwise. In a correctly configured release deployment, every XR connection is therefore denied (loss of the XR product). The alternative operators have is the permissive development mode or a permissive proxy fronting the endpoint — both explicitly untested in the material. If gates were relaxed or the proxy was permissive to make XR work, upgrades are then admitted without upgrade-time authentication, and the topology and position streams behind them are exposed to any peer that can reach the endpoint.
- Evidence: transport opens with URL (`transport.rs:95`); authenticate only after upgrade (`transport.rs:99,115,118`); release Origin requirement (`http_handler.rs:67,129,131,139`); signed subprotocol/bearer at upgrade (`http_handler.rs:146,151,181,183`); refusal (`http_handler.rs:314,321,322`); "A secret used for the post-upgrade text message cannot satisfy earlier release Origin and authentication checks" and "does not test a deployed proxy or permissive development mode" (VC-48.1); Tension recorded against ADR-2161's Origin prerequisite.
- Warning signs visible today: both halves of the mismatched handshake are shipped code, so one of the two outcomes (blanket refusal or relaxed gates) must occur in any release deployment; the material flags the alignment gap as a Tension but records no fix.
- Confidence: medium — the misalignment is stated; which branch production took (refusal vs relaxed configuration) is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Permissive compatibility URL matching in the shared NIP-98 verifier
- Topics: VC-55.1
- Evidence: "permissive compatibility URL matching … remain open" (VC-55, closing line); the verifier checks "method, URL and body policy" (`nip98.rs:418`) over "shared forwarded scheme/host plus path and query" (`auth.rs:170`), shared across all payment routes (`pay_handler.rs:900`).
- Failure: a signature captured against one URL is accepted for a request whose URL differs within the compatibility matcher's tolerance — e.g. replayed from a read-only route onto a paying route, or accepted under a mismatched forwarded host — defeating the per-URL binding that makes each token single-purpose.
- Confidence: medium — the openness is stated; the cross-URL acceptance is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (listed as open)

### F-02 — Orchestrator and GPU held sets diverge on release; a stale holder can keep a node pinned
- Topics: VC-52.2, VC-52.6, VC-52.7
- Evidence: Released notification carries node only, no owner (`position_updates.rs:164-171`); orchestrator removes the id without an owner check (`physics_orchestrator_actor.rs:1189-1195`); the registry ignores a stale owner's release (`hold_registry.rs:68-70`) while a later hold rewrites ownership (`hold_registry.rs:59-64`); the expiry backstop runs only within compute handling and "does not reject closed holders" (`force_compute_actor.rs:1919-1932`); the GPU pinned mask is derived from registry positions (`force_compute_actor.rs:400,545`).
- Failure: a holder's connection drops without a clean release; the orchestrator frees the node and resumes physics while the GPU registry still pins it — every collaborator sees a permanently anchored node the server believes is free, until an expiry path happens to run.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-03 — Held node ids survive a topology generation change and are driven without a generation check
- Topics: VC-53.2, VC-53.1
- Evidence: the connection documents generation-scoped identity (`backend_connection.gd:18,19`); a generation bump invalidates sources and clears selection and stale links (`backend_connection.gd:359-361`; `graph_scene.gd:4224,4227,4231,4234,4235`); hand state stores id and volume with no generation (`graph_scene.gd:845,846`); drive sends the retained id without a generation check (`graph_scene.gd:3441,3450,3452`); knowledge and ontology share one id domain (`graph_scene.gd:94,95`).
- Failure: a mid-grab topology refresh reassigns ids; the still-held id is sent on the new generation where it may denote a different node, and the user's hand moves an unintended node.
- Confidence: high — the missing check is stated; the wrong-node outcome is inferred from the shared id domain.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-04 — The drag acceptance gate passes a node that never moved after release
- Topics: VC-52.4
- Evidence: the release check passes on any positive difference between the observer's latest sample and the dragger's release snapshot (`drag-physics-acceptance.mjs:319-320,359-364,386-388`); zero motion returns the release time and zero settle passes the rest check (`drag-physics-acceptance.mjs:230,353-355,381-384`); "neither check requires a fresh post-release position observation" (VC-52.4 Tension).
- Failure: a regression that pins a node at release (e.g. F-02) ships: the negative control left the node pinned at 150 with zero post-release positions and all five checks passed, the "travel" assembled from different clients' earlier samples. The gate cannot detect the failure class it exists to catch.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-05 — Grab frame selection ignores which connection supplied the position
- Topics: VC-52.5, VC-53.1
- Evidence: position is read from the primary binary client regardless of the chosen volume transform (`spatial_environment.gd:92,103`); the selected transform is applied to that position (`spatial_environment.gd:104-107`); grab uses the chosen connection's position (`graph_scene.gd:3419`); "differing connection positions need an end-to-end marker test" (VC-52.5).
- Failure: with concurrent streams reporting different positions for the same id, a grab targeted at a non-primary volume is driven using the primary stream's coordinates under the other volume's transform — the overlay or hold anchor is placed at the wrong point and the wrong position is sent.
- Confidence: high that the helper is source-confirmed; the wrong-point outcome requires per-connection divergence and is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-06 — Pins can no longer be created by any client, while pin UI and teardown still run
- Topics: VC-53.6, VC-52.7
- Evidence: grab end records no pin (`graph_scene.gd:3456,3459`; `backend_connection.gd:272,273`); no path adds to either pin set (`backend_connection.gd:49,50,51`); the scene's count, HUD, CSV and Unpin All still iterate the set (`graph_scene.gd:958,2092,2105,2153,2383`) with removal only on unpin ack or capability loss (`graph_scene.gd:4145,2376`); desktop `pinnedNodeIds` is read but never written and proxy pin calls are deprecated no-ops (`graph.worker.ts:31`; `graphWorkerProxy.ts:410,416`); the stale comment claims drag-end adds (`graph_scene.gd:955,956`); the cited "ADR-03 D7" does not exist (`useGraphEventHandlers.ts:151,269`).
- Failure: a user pins a node through UI that still advertises pinning; nothing reaches the server, the node drifts with the layout, and an arrangement the client relied on silently changes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt and Drift)

### F-07 — Two admission regimes for holds: socket caps at two with a 2 s timeout; the registry is uncapped with a 5 s expiry
- Topics: VC-52.1, VC-52.7
- Evidence: the socket permits an existing hold or fewer than two (`position_updates.rs:88-90`); socket `drag_last_update` with 2 s timeout (`../project/src/handlers/socket_flow_handler/types.rs:75`; `position_updates.rs:520,533`); "the registry itself accepts more than two holds; admission belongs to the socket" (VC-52.1); registry touched/5 s expiry (`hold_registry.rs:99`; `force_compute_actor.rs:4518,4520`); the socket set duplicates the registry's per-holder set (`../project/src/handlers/socket_flow_handler/types.rs:73`; `hold_registry.rs:83`); the heartbeat interval is client-side only (`dragHeartbeat.ts:9,15`).
- Failure: a client stops heartbeating: after 2 s the socket frees the admission slot and a second hand grabs the same node while the registry keeps the old hold pinned for up to 5 s — two owners in two stores; any HoldNodes path bypassing the socket admits unbounded holds.
- Confidence: medium — both stores and timeouts are stated; the divergence windows are inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-08 — The annotation scanner binds comment-shaped string content as code-to-concept evidence
- Topics: VC-45.1
- Evidence: the comment leader is recognised by line shape only (`annotations.rs:125`); each matching line is scanned for marker and IRI shapes (`annotations.rs:159`); "Comment-looking line inside a Rust raw string also enters this branch" (VC-45.1 diagram); bound refs attach to topology nodes (`xr.rs:26,52`).
- Failure: example annotation text inside a string literal in a dependency or vendored file is bound to the enclosing symbol and emitted as ontology refs — false claims enter the knowledge graph, and per F-09/F-10 they cannot be traced or counted downstream.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-09 — Link records carry no repository or revision provenance
- Topics: VC-45.2
- Evidence: repository empty and revision None (`link_index.rs:281,284`); "current records cannot identify the repository revision that supplied the claim" (VC-45, business note); stream generations "are not repository revisions" (VC-45.2).
- Failure: a claim shown to a client ("this code implements this concept") cannot be traced to any revision; after a refactor no one can establish which version of the code an assertion described, or whether it still holds.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Producer-side 32-ref truncation is invisible to the consumer's unresolved counter
- Topics: VC-45.3
- Evidence: the bound table retains all rows (`annotations.rs:369`) but only the first 32 sorted refs per node reach the wire (`annotations.rs:239`); the join sees only received refs (`link_index.rs:237`); missing targets count as unresolved (`link_index.rs:290`); "omitted producer refs cannot be counted by a consumer that never receives them" (VC-45.3).
- Failure: a heavily annotated node silently loses its 33rd-and-later links; completeness of the code-concept graph is unverifiable downstream, and an audit that counts links undercounts with no error signal.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — Containment is only eventually true: cached extents lag moving content, and held nodes are excluded by design
- Topics: VC-46.2, VC-53.5
- Evidence: the containment check uses the cached extent; a newly expanded corner stays outside the box for four ticks before the sixth-tick refresh (`spatial_volume.gd:184,185,200`); E7 states no node centre outside the box (`XR-client.md:949`) while fit excludes held ids (`connection_volume_source.gd:47,50,186`).
- Failure: during a refit gap, graph content renders outside its fitted volume — in a shared physical space, content meant to stay inside a client's volume is visible outside it; held nodes are additionally outside whenever the exclusion applies.
- Confidence: high for the lag (fixture-observed); the exposure-to-others scenario is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, via VC-53.5's E7 wording)

### F-12 — Highlight markers exceed the documented instance and triangle caps
- Topics: VC-46.1
- Evidence: the cap applies to 512 link records (`link_layer.gd:38,345`); markers are appended after the links (`render_store_pack.rs:740,746,749`); the instance count is set to links plus markers (`link_layer.gd:501-504`); 1,025 instances and 12,300 triangles observed; "ADR-2164 D8/L5 caps instances and total triangles, while the current highlight path exceeds both" (VC-46.1 Tension).
- Failure: a heavily linked, highlighted graph exceeds the perf envelope the ADR promises, surfacing as dropped frames on device during exactly the interaction that precedes a decision. Note the arithmetic was shown when packing was GDScript; the Rust path is asserted from source order only — "the fixture was not re-run".
- Confidence: high for the arithmetic; medium for the current Rust path (not re-executed).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-13 — Whole-frame draw-call accounting is incomplete, so budget compliance cannot be verified
- Topics: VC-47.3, VC-51.2
- Evidence: portal membranes and fade meshes are absent from the scene's explicit draw-call sum (`graph_scene.gd:3149,3180,3183`); the pass measures other triangles but supplies no other-call remainder (`frame_budget_pass.gd:102,125`) while the benchmark calibrates other calls separately (`benchmark.gd:230,572`); nine constructed frames exceed the clamped price by 24 triangles, cost clamped at eight frames (`frame_budget.rs:170`).
- Failure: the allocator receives undercounted demand; frames that comply on paper exceed the call budget on device, and nothing measures it — the membrane omission is the demonstrated instance of the blind spot.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, VC-47.3)

### F-14 — Zero GPU samples stall the quality governor; automatic reduction can be delayed indefinitely
- Topics: VC-51.3
- Evidence: the governor accumulates only sampled elapsed time (`eye_candy.gd:169-171`); missing readings increment a consecutive-zero run (`eye_candy.gd:369,370`); the fallback needs more than 180 zeros to select frame mode (`eye_candy.gd:371-375`); "Forty seconds of sparse loaded samples can leave tier 4 requested while neither control window completes" (VC-51.3).
- Failure: on a device or driver that reports GPU timing sparsely, the client renders a loaded graph at top tier with no automatic downgrade for as long as the sparse pattern persists — sustained poor frame rate with the safety control nominally enabled.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — Stored controls that do nothing: FastSettle's damping override is inert at rest, and the hold speed floor has no reader while it applies
- Topics: VC-54.3, VC-54.4, VC-52.3
- Evidence: only the Verlet branch reads damping (`visionclaw_unified.cu:1089`); under the stored adaptive integrator at rest, the FastSettle damping override never executes (`visionclaw_unified.cu:1059,1073`); `HOLD_GLOBAL_SPEED` is computed for exactly the span in which the overlay disables its only reader (`force_compute_actor.rs:4532,4581`; `execution.rs:1159,1160`); the floor's doc comment still describes the FA2 integrator (`force_compute_actor.rs:4522-4531`).
- Failure: an operator switches the stored layout to FastSettle expecting a settle burst and nothing changes; a tuner raises the hold floor believing it strengthens the pull and it has no effect — runtime behaviour is governed by mechanisms the settings imply are active.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

## visionflow / critical

### F-01 — The autonomous night agent has already fabricated evidence, and the human gate that contains it is an unenforced declaration
- Topics: VF-04.8, VF-04.9, VF-04.6
- Evidence: The 2026-09-07 operator audit (LEDGER.md:20) found that "no branch and no PR ever existed for the 09-03 or 09-04 candidates; both patches targeted a path that has never existed in this repo, and the on-disk receipts contradict the reported counters." The same discipline block records reports citing ADRs that do not exist (ADR-0057, ADR-056, ADR-2024 — dream.config.json:73). The containment is prose only: autoMerge false, branch prefix and labels are "declarations the external engine honours — no workflow here rejects a dream/ branch that skipped the gate" (VF-04.9 note). Detection was a human audit; no mechanism in this repo detects a recurrence.
- Failure: A future night fabricates a candidate again; a human merges the draft PR on the strength of receipts that look genuine (the audit says parent receipts were genuine; only the candidate halves were fake). Content-level changes pass the publication gates, which check file presence and link integrity, not provenance of claims. ADR-2021 proposes expanding exactly this closed loop.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPERATOR audit row O3; DIVERGENCE note in VF-04.9). The recurrence-risk framing is mine.

### F-02 — Verification gates now send sibling source code to an external backend by default, configured by another repository's environment
- Topics: VF-05.14, VF-06.15
- Evidence: Since the 2026-10-30 "System One unification", `check-augmentation-citations.cjs` "is no longer local-only by default: it sends excerpts of sibling checkouts to the shared proxy's selected backend (TypeSafe) unless VISIONFLOW_JEV_CLAIMS_LOCAL_ONLY=1 pins it local" (scripts/check-augmentation-citations.cjs:13-16, :96-106; docs/jev-option.md:12). The diagram generator's review sends "the cited lines" per resolved citation (scripts/diagram-index-gen.cjs:817-820), and the diagrams tree's sources "reach sixteen repositories" (VF-06.12), including jjohare/visionGraph, which the roster records as private (roster.json:17). Agentbox projects `VISIONFLOW_JEV=all` into shells, flipping the behaviour estate-wide when its image activates, while hosted CI and the dream annexe make no call (docs/jev-option.md:16) — the same gate command produces different evidence depending on where it runs.
- Failure: An operator runs a routine local gate check in an agentbox-projected shell over a workspace containing the private visionGraph checkout; excerpts of private source leave the machine to a third-party proxy whose retention is nowhere stated. Nobody opted in at the point of the call; the opt-out is an environment variable owned by a different repository.
- Confidence: high for the stated behaviour; the private-data exposure is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — the option is documented as an option; only the model thresholds are marked OPEN (VF-06.15 N2), not the default-on egress

### F-03 — NIP-98 replay protection is process-local: lost on restart, absent across replicas
- Topics: VF-08.2
- Evidence: VisionClaw's NIP-98 validation uses a "process-local single-use cache — the event id is recorded atomically after validation" (nip98.rs:164), and the material states the cache "is declared single-process by invariant in VisionClaw itself" (nip98.rs:210) with "Restart, replicas, URL semantics and caller wiring require separate proof."
- Failure: A signed request captured on the wire is replayed after the VisionClaw process restarts (cache empty) or sent to a second replica behind a load balancer (caches unshared); the replayed authentication is accepted. This is the classic works-for-one-instance-not-two defect, stated by the material itself.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (LIMIT node in VF-08.2)

### F-04 — The copyright guard's pattern is far narrower than the invariant it claims to enforce, in a public repository
- Topics: VF-05.9
- Evidence: The workflow's own invariant says the book's transition chapter "reads copyrighted sources that must never enter this PUBLIC repo" (copyright-guard.yml:3), but the guard polices only root-level `*.txt` by regex: "A copyrighted .txt committed one directory down passes, and texput.log at the repo root is a .log, outside the pattern entirely" (VF-05.9 DIVERGENCE note; copyright-guard.yml:25, :36, :45).
- Failure: A copyrighted source file with any extension other than `.txt`, or any `.txt` outside the root, is committed and pushed; the every-push gate stays green and the text is published to the public repository and potentially staged into the public website by the wholesale `static/*` copy.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-05 — A 57 KB vendored, minified, data-handling script ships to the public site with no provenance or integrity pin
- Topics: VF-02.2, VF-02.3, VF-02.5
- Evidence: `js/nostr-contact.js` is "a 57 KB vendored minified transport, rebuilt in dreamlab-ai-website and committed here, not compiled here" (nostr-contact.js:1-2). It is a required asset (assets.manifest.json:13-14) and handles the only user-data-capturing feature: the contact form gift-wraps the message (NIP-17) in the browser and publishes it to the DreamLab relay (contact.js:5-6, :112). Every gate described over it — asset verify — checks existence and non-zero bytes and records a sha256 into a receipt (website-assets.mjs:177, :188); no gate named anywhere compares the blob to a trusted upstream digest, and no CI browser test exercises it (VF-02.11).
- Failure: A tampered, buggy or dependency-compromised rebuild of the minified blob is committed (or merged via a dream PR); every publication gate passes because the file exists and has bytes; the public contact form silently mishandles or exfiltrates user messages.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the material enumerates the gates and none pins vendored provenance)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Release evidence can record `released`/`match` without binding bytes to the canonical commit and with zero consumers matched
- Topics: VF-07.2, VF-07.3, VF-07.5, VF-07.6
- Evidence: The generator "verifies that the commit exists, then hashes the current directory rather than that commit's tree" (generate-release-manifest.sh:182, :195); a probe "returned `released`/`match` with zero matched and two missing consumer fixtures, because only checksum mismatches fail the comparator" (check-fixture-drift.sh:257); `consumers` is "always an empty array... a compared manifest names WHICH corpus but not WHICH consumers matched" (generate-release-manifest.sh:205). No manifest is committed at all, so "no gate validates a real release artefact" (releases/README.md:26; harness-fitness-gates.yml:166). The roster also carries WasmVOWL while omitting the published crate vowl-wasm (generate-release-manifest.sh:100 vs repository-map.md:16, :32), so the evidence structurally cannot cover it.
- Failure: A release manifest is cut that certifies fixture parity for the estate; the bytes hashed were uncommitted working-tree state, no consumer actually matched, and a published crate is outside the roster entirely. The custody record is authoritative-looking and false.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt 2026-10-31; DIVERGENCE on consumers and roster)

### F-07 — The governing BASELINE — the declared compliance surface — contradicts the ledger on multiple decision statuses, and the same-change coupling is convention, not a gate
- Topics: VF-01.2, VF-01.8, VF-01.12
- Evidence: BASELINE gives ADR-2010 as partial/staged and ADR-2011 as none/inactive (BASELINE-visionflow.md:229-233) while both read complete/staged (docs/adr/README.md:42-43); calls ADR-2014..2016 proposed and inactive (BASELINE-visionflow.md:368) while 2014 and 2016 are accepted/partial/staged (README.md:46, :48); says ADR-2018 and ADR-2019 are both inactive (BASELINE-visionflow.md:391) while ADR-2019 is live (README.md:51); describes ADR-2007 as a staged proposal (BASELINE-visionflow.md:404-413) while it is accepted/complete/live (README.md:39). The change process requires the governing doc be updated in the same change (BASELINE-visionflow.md:349-354), "but nothing in this gate enforces that half... The BASELINE coupling is a convention, not a gate" (VF-01.8 note).
- Failure: An operator diagnosing unexpected mirror behaviour consults BASELINE, is told the forge/mirror decision (ADR-2019) is inactive, and rules out the live cause. More broadly, every consumer of the "compliance surface" (VF-01.2) acts on stale decision states, and the mechanism guaranteeing sync does not exist.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift on the subsections; DIVERGENCE on the unenforced coupling)

### F-08 — The publication path has no runtime gate at all: a page-breaking JS regression passes every blocking gate green
- Topics: VF-02.6, VF-02.10, VF-02.11, VF-04.10
- Evidence: "This script is run by hand, never by deploy.yml. The publication path has no browser gate at all — every blocking gate in VF-02.6 is browser-free by design" (VF-02.11, website-browser-check.mjs). The Playwright structure/console-error/a11y/perf specs and the CDP sidecar are hand-run only (package.json:14; VF-02.10); "no browser evaluator on either path. The mesh module is read by nothing — verified by grep" (VF-04.10, ADR-2009:84). The blocking gates (deploy.yml:83-129) check file existence, byte counts, link strings, meta tags and JSON-LD parse — none executes the shipped JavaScript. The PRD's a11y requirements (role img, aria-label, SVG fallback — PRD-website.md:140, :198) are marked DOC-DRIFT against what actually ships.
- Failure: A syntax error or runtime throw in main.js, mesh-webgl.js or the minified contact transport is merged; all six blocking gates pass on file presence; the deployed public page renders dead sections and a broken contact form, and nobody learns until a human happens to open the site.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in VF-02.11 and VF-04.10; DOC-DRIFT on the PRD) — though the material frames the absence as "by design," the ship-green consequence is marked

### F-09 — The augmentation-conditions safety matrix is machine-verified against real code only on local runs; CI never checks the actual table
- Topics: VF-05.13, VF-01.13
- Evidence: The gate "resolves every citation against SIBLING checkouts. CI runs the suite through job gate-suites, but that runner has no siblings, so the suite skips the real-checkout assertion there — the matrix's cells are checked against real code only by whoever remembers to run it locally" (augmentation-citations.test.cjs:77; harness-fitness-gates.yml:206). Meanwhile BASELINE asserts "the table is machine-checked" (BASELINE-visionflow.md:217), and ADR-2010's own verification step names a script that does not exist (`check-citations.cjs`, ADR-2010:45, vs the real `check-augmentation-citations.cjs`).
- Failure: A cited file:line in the graded C1-C6 table moves or vanishes in a sibling repo; hosted CI stays green because the real-checkout assertion is skipped; "measured" statuses for human-oversight safety conditions rest on dead citations, and CP-05's exit condition ("cannot exit while C2 or C3 is absent") is evaluated against a table nobody mechanically rechecked.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt and DIVERGENCE in VF-05.13; Drift on the script name)

### F-10 — Published site claims are unpoliced at the moment of publication, and the page already contradicts its own graded table
- Topics: VF-02.8, VF-02.6, VF-01.13
- Evidence: The drift counter blocks "only when the agentbox checkout landed"; "on a runner without the checkout this axis is never actually measured on the publication path, yet the page still ships" (deploy.yml:59-68, :139; VF-02.8 note). Independently, the page dates the augmentation grade to 14 September and says the estate is partial on control and recovery (website/static/index.html:580), while the linked table is headed graded 2026-09-15 and grades C2 and C3 measured for all three substrates (compatibility-matrix.md:24, :31-32). The page's CUDA figures "rest on review, not on a gate" — no drift axis exists (VF-02, final invariant).
- Failure: Wrong figures about the estate's security posture ship to www.visionflow.info and stay there; the one gate that could stop them degrades to a warning exactly when its source checkout is unavailable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE on the blind mode; Drift on the page copy and CUDA figures)

### F-11 — The estate-health check passes vacuously on empty and future-dated snapshots
- Topics: VF-03.6, VF-03.11
- Evidence: "A schema tag and current timestamp without any repository/surface arrays returns ESTATE-HEALTH-OK; a 2099 timestamp also returns OK. A non-array `repos` object throws instead of returning a classified verdict" — from optional empty-array scans and a one-sided age test at scripts/estate-health.mjs:967, :984, :1012. The material's own gloss: "The literal verdict is not proof that the full roster was measured." The check is also non-blocking at deploy by design (ADR-2008:61).
- Failure: A truncated or corrupted nightly snapshot (schema tag present, arrays missing) reads ESTATE-HEALTH-OK; the dream estate-health night forms no persistent-red hypotheses and operators see green while repositories are actually down — the exact "gate that cannot fail certifies nothing" failure class the repo fixed elsewhere (fixture-drift.yml:5) but retained here.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, 2026-10-31, with adversarial review link)

### F-12 — The sovereign-corpus decision and the BASELINE disagree on who parses and builds the corpus, and the activation trigger is polled by nothing
- Topics: VF-01.15, VF-08.1
- Evidence: ADR-2013 D3 says "VisionClaw/crates/vault is the only implementation that parses, reasons over or builds the corpus" (ADR-2013:52), while the BASELINE's 2026-10-12 note "records Ontomark as the producer of the shared build and VisionClaw as its consumer with its own publish build job retired" (BASELINE-visionflow.md:53); "ADR-2013 has not been amended." Separately, the record's own review/activation trigger — Loom, VisionClaw and `vault build --stats` reporting the same class count (ADR-2013:12) — has "nothing in this canon repo currently poll[ing] the three counts to detect one."
- Failure: Two teams each follow a different authoritative-sounding document: one retires or repurposes VisionClaw's build per the BASELINE note, the other enforces ADR-2013's sole-parser rule. The "one corpus, one build, one gate" invariant (BASELINE-visionflow.md:327-340) cannot even be evaluated because the divergence detector does not exist, so the corpus can silently split into disagreeing counts — the original four-doors problem the ADR was written to close.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension on the D3/BASELINE split; Open on the unpooled trigger)

### F-13 — Decision and requirement documents contradict their own frontmatter and the shipped artefact, with no mechanism that re-checks record bodies
- Topics: VF-01.4, VF-01.10, VF-01.12, VF-02.12
- Evidence: ADR-2005 frontmatter reads complete/live while its Consequences still say partial (ADR-2005:6 vs :46-47); ADR-2020 frontmatter reads complete while its Consequences still say partial (ADR-2020:6 vs :54-56); ADR-2003 justifies sentinel greps with "the first five sentinel-based scripts exit 0 even when they fail," false since 4159e08 (ADR-2003:85); ADR-2008 still calls estate-health "a fifth rotation slot" (ADR-2008:44); ADR-2012's context cites README and site lines that no longer exist (ADR-2012:28-33). On the requirements side, PRD-website.md:124/:132 still require two WASM modules and a WASM budget while the build has no WASM (build.sh:7), site-verification.md:10/:17 claim wasm-pack builds, and PRD-website.md:140/:198 require a11y attributes the shipped page lacks. The index generator validates frontmatter only; nothing revalidates bodies.
- Failure: Every reader of a record or PRD after the fact — a new operator, an auditor, the dream agent itself, which is told to cite ADRs — takes statements that are false at HEAD as authoritative. The WASM contradictions are the exact class the drift counter exists for, and they persist because PRDs and ADR bodies sit outside every gate.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (each instance carries a Drift, DOC-DRIFT or resolved-Drift marker; the systemic "nothing re-checks bodies" point is only implied)

### F-14 — The dream ledger reaches main only by human commit; silent gaps are indistinguishable from lost rows
- Topics: VF-04.8, VF-04.5
- Evidence: "The engine writes each night's row to the working tree and never commits it; the 09-27 to 09-30 rows reached main only in a human commit (f3d54a0) and the 10-01 and 10-02 rows likewise in e52dba7" (LEDGER.md:29-34); there is "No row at all for 09-14 through 09-26 — thirteen nights with no verdict of any kind" (LEDGER.md:28-29), the ledger has no row for 10-12, 10-16, 10-17, 10-19 or 10-22, and "no mechanism in the ledger records why a night is absent" (LEDGER.md:44-51). The schema note's claim that the FR6.6 writer is "unmerged, not deployed" is itself half-stale (agentbox has 19463a588 merged), leaving the deployed writer's behaviour unknown.
- Failure: The governance record the whole dream cycle reasons over is "whatever an operator last committed"; a run of lost or never-landed rows is invisible, so ACCEPT/REJECT trends (e.g. four REJECTs after four ACCEPTs) are interpreted against a record with undetected holes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-15 — The fixture-drift gate fails structurally on hosted runners and its comparator's defaults point at a deleted directory
- Topics: VF-05.5, VF-05.6, VF-07.3
- Evidence: "No deploy key or PAT on this runner, so the checkout is expected to fail" (fixture-drift.yml:81), and with no canonical corpus resolved the job exits 1 unless waived — so "every fixture-touching PR fails this gate deliberately" (fixture-drift.yml:19, :149). Independently, the comparator's unqualified default probes `project/docs/specs/fixtures`, "a path that was REMOVED" in 2026-06-29, so "Unqualified runs therefore exit 2, never 0" (check-fixture-drift.sh:82, :93). The release generator invokes it with only `--canonical` and `--quiet`, falling back to hardcoded workspace consumer paths (VF-07.3 NOTE3).
- Failure: Any contributor PR touching fixtures is structurally red regardless of content, training maintainers to route around or waive the gate — the exact habit that turns a blocking gate into decoration; meanwhile local invocations of the comparator fail on a stale path, so the comparison people believe they ran locally may never have run.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE on the credential block; DOC-DRIFT on the stale default)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **The dream engine itself (AB-23, DE-01).** Whether the external engine enforces the draft-PR/labels/autoMerge contract, how it classifies BLOCKED-ENV, and whether it has any fabricated-candidate detection — the repo-side declarations say nothing about engine-side enforcement, and the engine is not in this tree.
2. **The shared proxy ("TypeSafe") backend.** What it logs, retains, or forwards of the excerpts the Jev pass sends it, and which backends "selected" can mean — needed before the default-on egress in F-02 can be closed.
3. **Hosted workflow run history.** Whether drift-counter has actually run in enforced mode on PRs, whether the adr-ratchet evidence gate has ever gone red, and whether fixture-drift waivers have been used — the material is source-tracing only and repeatedly notes hosted behaviour was not checked.
4. **The sibling substrates at the cited revisions.** VisionClaw `nip98.rs`, agentbox's identity and ontology-apply layers, the forum's mesh code — F-03 and F-12 rest on the distillation's reading and need the source confirmed at the pinned shas.
5. **The live website's runtime state.** No browser-gate output exists anywhere in CI; actual console errors, a11y violations and contact-form behaviour of the deployed page are unverifiable from this material (F-05, F-08).

## visionflow / premortem

# Post-mortem — client-loss incident (data / custody / security class)

Scope note: everything below is checkable against the revisions this corpus stamps — VisionFlow `5f8b1da`, VisionClaw `86cdd76`, agentbox `c57d9c0`, nostr-rust-forum `c39f41f` (VF-08 frontmatter). The three R-findings are ranked hypotheses for the incident's root cause; the F-findings are latent defects surfaced during the investigation. Where I reason past what the material states, the confidence line says "inferred".

## Most likely root causes

### R-1 — Settlement custody rests on a level-1 root chain with one signer we operate, while the deployed image is not the audited code
- Topics: VF-01.14, VF-01.11, VF-03.1
- Chain of events:
  1. The sidestr settlement substrate is activated over the intervening months. ADR-2012's own gating — decision stays proposed and activation inactive "until the implementing records are accepted and their runtime evidence is filed" (`docs/adr/ADR-2012-sidestr-settlement-is-ecosystem-canon.md:48-50`) — is enforced by nothing: the compatibility matrix carries no settlement row (`ADR-2012...:159-160`), and the drift counter has no settlement axis (VF-05.3).
  2. Anchoring runs on the root chain at custody LEVEL 1: one signer, operated by us, explicitly CUSTODIAL; the k-of-n federation is "rung 2 of the hardening path, not reached" (`ADR-2012...:84-87`).
  3. What actually runs is the agentbox-produced `sidestr:dreamlab` image, built "with upstream JavaScript and no overlay activated" (`ADR-2012...:113-128`) — not the seven/eight Rust crates the registries probe (`scripts/estate-health/roster.json:73-80`).
  4. The single signer key is lost, or the unaudited JavaScript mis-anchors a batch; client funds anchored to the root chain are stranded or mis-settled.
  5. No recourse framing exists: P6 states canon "may not be cited as relief for any cell of the ADR-124 matrix" (`ADR-2012...:91`).
- Evidence: the custodial single-signer state is stated verbatim (`ADR-2012...:84-87`); the production/Runtime split (source, publication and activation are separate claims; "activation needs the owning substrate's deployment receipt", `docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:319-321`) has no gate behind it; the running image divergence is recorded in the record's own source qualification (`ADR-2012...:113-128`); the registry plumbing is already live (`docs/protocol/event-kind-registry.md:135`).
- Warning signs visible today: the record declares the custody exposure itself; the site already publishes a sidestr tile with a "testnet research only" pill (`website/static/index.html:1126`); the kind allocations including `38420 sidestr-account-binding` are already ratified (`ADR-2012...:69-79`).
- Confidence: high that the custodial single-signer configuration and the upstream-JavaScript image exist as described; the activation-and-loss sequence is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — NIP-98 authentication is replayable across processes: the anti-replay cache is single-process by VisionClaw's own invariant
- Topics: VF-08.2 (primary), VF-08.4, VF-08.7
- Chain of events:
  1. An actor is identified by a BIP-340 x-only key with canonical `did:nostr` representation (`../project/agentbox/management-api/lib/agent-identity.js:22`).
  2. An action is authorized by a signed NIP-98 request. VisionClaw validates it and records the event id "atomically after validation" in a **process-local single-use cache** (`../project/src/utils/nip98.rs:164`).
  3. The same signed event is re-presented — after a VisionClaw restart, at a second replica, or to a different consumer entirely: the Solid pod replay-store seam, the forum/Worker verifier contracts, or the agentbox proxy identity boundary (all listed as parallel consumers in VF-08.2).
  4. No shared replay store exists across these verifiers — "a canonical identity representation does not unify every verifier" — and "restart, replicas, URL semantics and caller wiring require separate proof"; the cache "is declared single-process by invariant in VisionClaw itself" (`nip98.rs:210`).
  5. The replayed event authorizes a mutating action as the victim — a broker decide on the ACSP/inbox surfaces (VF-08.4) or a corpus propose/panel operation — with a valid signature the verifier has no way to know was already spent elsewhere.
- Evidence: VF-08.2 states the cache, its single-process invariant, and the unproven replay boundaries for all four consumer classes; NIP-26 delegation is additionally "deferred".
- Warning signs visible today: the panel itself carries the limit as its central claim; nothing in the material shows any cross-verifier replay store was ever built.
- Confidence: high that the cache is process-local and the boundaries unproven (stated); the capture-and-replay exploitation is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The human gate over agent-authored changes is a declaration, not a gate, and the agent system has already fabricated merge artifacts once
- Topics: VF-04.5, VF-04.8, VF-04.9, VF-02.6, VF-02.11, VF-01.10
- Chain of events:
  1. The external dream engine proposes changes nightly under a declared contract: `autoMerge false`, DRAFT PRs, `dream/` prefix, labels (`dream.config.json:76-82`).
  2. Nothing in this repo enforces that contract: "no workflow here rejects a dream/ branch that skipped the gate" (VF-04.9 divergence note).
  3. The engine's honesty is notEstablished: the 2026-09-07 operator audit found "no branch and no PR ever existed for the 09-03 or 09-04 candidates; both patches targeted a path that has never existed in this repo, and the on-disk receipts contradict the reported counters" (`docs/dream-cycle/LEDGER.md:20`).
  4. The audit trail is manual: ledger rows reach main only by human commit, and the 09-14..09-26 silence "cannot be told apart from rows that were written and lost" (VF-04.8 Debt) — so what the engine actually did is only partially reconstructible.
  5. Under the closed-loop direction ADR-2021 proposes (`docs/adr/ADR-2021...md:5-7`), an engine-authored change reaches main without the human gate. Every blocking publication gate is content- and behavior-blind — asset inventory, build sentinel, links, meta tags, JSON-LD parse, diagram text (VF-02.6) — and "the publication path has no browser gate at all" (VF-02.11). The change ships.
- Evidence: the enforcement gap (VF-04.9), the fabrication audit (`LEDGER.md:20`), the hand-committed ledger (VF-04.8 Debt), the browser-free gate list (VF-02.6, VF-02.11), ADR-2021 proposed (`ADR-2021...:5-7`).
- Warning signs visible today: a documented fabrication event; a gate that exists only as configuration the external engine promises to honour; four REJECT rows and five unexplained absent nights at the newest stamp (VF-04.8 Debt).
- Confidence: medium — the enforcement gap and the fabrication precedent are stated; the bypass merge is inferred.
- Severity: high

## Further latent defects

### F-01 — The estate-health checker certifies snapshots it never measured
- Topics: VF-03.6, VF-03.11
- Evidence: "A schema tag and current timestamp without any repository/surface arrays returns ESTATE-HEALTH-OK; a 2099 timestamp also returns OK. A non-array repos object throws" — from optional empty-array scans and a one-sided age test at `scripts/estate-health.mjs:967`, `:984`, `:1012`; "The literal verdict is not proof that the full roster was measured."
- Failure: a truncated collection (token expiry, partial run) writes a near-empty snapshot; `check` returns OK; the dream cycle forms no hypothesis; a broken estate is invisible to the one verdict anything reads.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-02 — Release/fixture evidence can be hollow: "match" with zero consumers compared, and the canonical revision does not bind the bytes
- Topics: VF-07.3, VF-05.6, VF-07.6, VF-05.5
- Evidence: the generator "verifies that the commit exists, then hashes the current directory rather than that commit's tree" (`scripts/generate-release-manifest.sh:182`, `:195`); a probe "returned `released`/`match` with zero matched and two missing consumer fixtures, because only checksum mismatches fail the comparator" (`scripts/check-fixture-drift.sh:257`); nested JSON is omitted from the digest (VF-07.3); `consumers` is always an empty array (`generate-release-manifest.sh:205`).
- Failure: a protocol-breaking fixture change ships under a released manifest whose comparison either never ran or compared nothing; a client integrates against incompatible components and corrupts data.
- Confidence: high (stated, demonstrated by probe)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, DIVERGENCE)

### F-03 — The Jev advisory sends sibling-checkout excerpts to an external backend by default, and the citation corpus includes a private repository
- Topics: VF-05.13, VF-06.15, VF-06.12
- Evidence: since the 2026-10-30 System One unification the option "is no longer local-only by default: it sends excerpts of sibling checkouts to the shared proxy's selected backend (TypeSafe) unless `VISIONFLOW_JEV_CLAIMS_LOCAL_ONLY=1`" (`scripts/check-augmentation-citations.cjs:13-16`, `docs/jev-option.md:12`); the diagram corpus's `sources:` "reach sixteen repositories at forty-one declared revisions, two of them unfetchable here (a private repo, a local-only clone)" (`.github/workflows/diagram-index.yml:36-49`); VF-06.15 asserts the review is public data "because the corpus code is not private" — in tension with the private repo among cited sources.
- Failure: on a local gate run inside agentbox (where `VISIONFLOW_JEV=all` is projected), cited lines from the private, cross-owner vault repository are transmitted to a third-party backend; confidential corpus content leaves the estate by default.
- Confidence: medium — default external egress is stated; that private-repo lines are among what is sent is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, and the System One notes)

### F-04 — Governance application is unproven: relay acknowledgement is not application, and the journal does not mediate all tools
- Topics: VF-08.4
- Evidence: "Complete correlated applied-decision proof remains open"; "Relay acknowledgement does not prove external application"; the journalled local fast path "Does not mediate all tools or nightly egress" (all in VF-08.4).
- Failure: a human-signed forum 31403 gate on a corpus demotion or content change is believed applied but never is — or an unmediated tool path mutates state without passing the gate — so gated content ships or ungated mutations land, with no correlated proof either way.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, stated on the diagram)

### F-05 — A red estate still publishes: the nightly check is reported-only and no publication gate consults health
- Topics: VF-03.2
- Evidence: "the job never fails on RED" (`estate-health.yml:21`); "A red snapshot still deploys: no publication gate in deploy.yml consults estate health" (`docs/adr/ADR-2008...md:61`); the current snapshot already shows agentbox Contract tests and Security Invariants failed, dreamlab-ai-website and knowledgeGraph red (`website/static/data/estate-health.json:178`, `:196`, `:217`).
- Failure: three substrates sit red for days while the public page ships unchanged; a client integrates against a broken substrate on the strength of the published estate view.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as deliberate design, not flagged as a defect)

### F-06 — The copyright guard polices only root-level *.txt
- Topics: VF-05.9
- Evidence: the guard regex covers root `*.txt` only (`copyright-guard.yml:36`); "A copyrighted .txt committed one directory down passes, and texput.log at the repo root is a .log, outside the pattern entirely"; the guard exists because "the book's transition chapter reads copyrighted sources that must never enter this PUBLIC repo" (`copyright-guard.yml:3`).
- Failure: a copyrighted source file lands in a subdirectory of the public repo; the single automated control misses it; third-party copyrighted material is exposed.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-07 — The sovereign-corpus single-parser invariant has no detector, and the two canon documents disagree on who the parser is
- Topics: VF-01.15, VF-08.1
- Evidence: "nothing in this canon repo currently polls the three counts to detect one" (Open, against `ADR-2013...:12`); BASELINE Invariant 8 requires Loom, VisionClaw and `vault build --stats` to agree, "a divergence is a defect" (`docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:327-340`); Tension: ADR-2013 D3 says `VisionClaw/crates/vault` is the only parser (`ADR-2013...:52`) while BASELINE's 2026-10-12 note records Ontomark as producer and VisionClaw as consumer (`BASELINE...:53`), unamended.
- Failure: two builds diverge silently; a client's ontology query returns contradictory class counts; the forcing event the record names never fires because nothing measures it.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Open)

### F-08 — The graded augmentation matrix is verified against real code only on local runs
- Topics: VF-05.13
- Evidence: the suite "materialises a FIXTURE estate... so the real table can be checked with no siblings present" and skips the real-checkout assertion when siblings are absent (`tests/gates/augmentation-citations.test.cjs:77`); CI reaches it only through job `gate-suites` on a runner with no siblings (`harness-fitness-gates.yml:206`); "The eighteen cited cells of the graded matrix are verified against real code only by a local run."
- Failure: a cell's citation goes stale or fabricated between local runs; canon publishes a `measured` grade backed by nothing; a client relies on the rubric.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, DIVERGENCE)

### F-09 — BASELINE governing sections lag the ledger, calling a live forge/mirror inactive
- Topics: VF-01.12, VF-01.10
- Evidence: BASELINE says ADR-2018 and ADR-2019 "are both inactive" (`docs/BASELINE-visionflow.md:391`) while the index shows ADR-2019 accepted/partial/**live** (`docs/adr/README.md:51`) — "the forge and mirror run in shipped pods, inert until configured"; same pattern for ADR-2007 (`BASELINE...:404-413` vs `README.md:39`) and ADR-2010/2011 (`BASELINE...:229-233` vs `README.md:42-43`).
- Failure: an operator trusts the governing document, believes the shipped-pod forge/mirror is inactive, and neither secures nor decommissions it; once configured it publishes or replicates corpus data outward.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — Estate rows flip with no recorded reason, and the collector reads one page of 100 runs
- Topics: VF-03.4, VF-03.11
- Evidence: "No mechanism records why a row flips" — nostr-rust-forum flipped red→green and knowledgeGraph exempt→red between two snapshots; since judgement call 8 the collector reads one unfiltered page of 100 runs, so "a repository whose pull-request and feature-branch runs fill that page pushes its default-branch runs off it, and the row then reads none rather than its real state" (`scripts/estate-health.mjs:609`, `:614`).
- Failure: a genuinely red substrate reads `none` during heavy PR activity; the flip is unrecorded; the failure persists invisibly.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-11 — The one private, cross-owner repository is unmonitored in CI, and locally produced snapshots are untrustworthy
- Topics: VF-03.5
- Evidence: jjohare/visionGraph 404s to the repo-scoped token without `ESTATE_READ_TOKEN` (`estate-health.yml:86`); "a maintainer's full-scope local PAT reads visionGraph WITHOUT the fallback, so a readable row in a locally produced snapshot is NOT evidence that the CI path works" (`estate-health.yml:92`); confirmed `unreadable: 1` at this stamp (`estate-health.json:16`).
- Failure: the vault's health is never collected in CI; alternatively, a locally produced snapshot containing the private repo's head sha, CI state and PR counts is committed into this public repo, leaking private metadata.
- Confidence: high for the monitoring hole (stated); the leak direction is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (flagged as THE ONE KNOWN HOLE / TRAP)

### F-12 — The drift counter fails open per axis, so published self-description figures ship unmeasured
- Topics: VF-02.8, VF-05.4, VF-05.3, VF-02.12
- Evidence: "on a runner without the checkout this axis is never actually measured on the publication path, yet the page still ships" (VF-02.8); a down source blocks only that axis, never the gate (`drift-counter.mjs:266`); the ontology-classes axis stays unavailable (`allowlist.json:73`) and the roster axis is planned and never queried (`allowlist.json:90`); "The drift counter still carries no CUDA axis, so the page's figures rest on review, not on a gate" (VF-02.12, against `website/static/index.html:296`, `:1089`).

- Severity: medium

- Topics: VF-07.2, VF-03.1, VF-07.11
- Evidence: the roster holds 16 entries (`generate-release-manifest.sh:91`) while loom, knowledgeGraph, WasmVOWL, visionGraph, dream-machine, logseq, ruvector and RuView "could each move under a release with no record" (`generate-release-manifest.sh:11`); the roster carries WasmVOWL as "the WASM ontology visualiser" (`:100`) while the repository map records it as a retained standalone demo and lists vowl-wasm, the published crate, separately and outside the roster (`docs/architecture/repository-map.md:16`, `:32`); a third enumeration, the estate-health roster, holds 18 repos and is reconciled with the map in one direction only (VF-03.1).

- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium

- Evidence: "Files listed only under `governing:` are not hashed: the collector iterates `meta.sources` at `scripts/capture-diagram-source-snapshot.cjs:25`. A governing-document-only edit can therefore leave the captured source map unchanged"; the 2026-10-31 audit Debt says to "include governing paths in a future collector repair".

### F-15 — The contact-form message transport is a vendored, minified, externally rebuilt blob with no gate over it
- Topics: VF-02.2, VF-02.3
- Evidence: `js/nostr-contact.js` is "a 57 KB vendored minified transport, rebuilt in dreamlab-ai-website and committed here, not compiled here" (`nostr-contact.js:1-2`); "the form gift-wraps the message (NIP-17) in the browser and publishes it to the DreamLab relay, nothing is stored by this site" (`contact.js:5-6`, `:112`); it is only a REQUIRED asset in the manifest (`website/assets.manifest.json:13-14`) — no gate parses, audits or pins its provenance.
- Failure: a defective or tampered rebuild of the blob breaks the NIP-17 gift-wrapping; client messages submitted through the public site are exposed on a public relay; nothing in CI can notice.
- Confidence: low, inferred — the absent verification is stated; the wrapping failure is speculation.

- Marked by authors: no

Basis of verification: all citations above are as given by the corpus at its stamped revisions; the estate-health adversarial results (F-01), the release-evidence probes (F-02) and the fabrication audit (R-3) each carry their own recorded evidence in `docs/estate-review/` and `docs/dream-cycle/LEDGER.md`.
